# M12 — what the general critique of DBeaver shows

**Status (2026-11-24): K1 to K62 and K64 to K66 are done** (K63 is not started), each started at the owner's word (2026-11-17 to 2026-11-23; K17
to K20, K22 and K23 in worktrees of their own, two at a time); every item has its "Done:" line below. The captures of K1 to K9 and K11, K13 to K21 and K24 are still to take. The `CLAUDE.md` §4 K
section and `docs/GOAL-3.md` (texts proposed in the appendix) stay the owner's files, not edited by the agent.

Triage: `docs/COMPETITOR-ISSUES.md`, section "A general critique of DBeaver, 2026-11-17" (C1-C22). Source:
`docs/research/dbeaver-critique.md`. Read `docs/plans/README.md` first (rules, commands, map of the code, UI
kit and UI test notes): everything there applies. State when written: 2026-11-17, commit `496e3a4`, v0.1.0
released, the owner runs the installed app daily (D-075).

## Order and the cut line

Priority: what protects data and production first, then what a daily user meets most, then the rest.

**Why the cut is there.** K1-K10 are each small or medium (about two to three days of agent work in all) and
close every hole where a user can lose data or act on the wrong server without being told: a transaction
lost on quit, a write stuck behind a lock with no way out, a tab running in another database than it shows, a
script bound to production on open, a `DELETE` that deletes everything without the question, an app that
vanishes after a downgrade, an error that hides the server's fix. K11 and K12 are the editor quality a daily
user notices most, but today they fail safe (no completion offered; a broken `CREATE` the server refuses) and
K11 alone is a day of work; they open the next milestone. K13-K16 are convenience or rare cases.

## Rules of this milestone

- `docs/plans/README.md` rules, unchanged: at most **2 subagents** at once, `nice -n 10`, `--test-threads=3`,
  `uptime` and `df -h /` before heavy steps, `target/` under about 20 GB, only `kerno-*` containers, scratch
  objects `kerno_ui_*` / `kerno_test_*`, never stop Kerno by name (the owner's installed app runs beside),
  instances of yours with `KERNO_DATA_DIR=<scratch>` and `KERNO_SECRET_STORE=memory`, commit locally only.
- New UI tests go in `crates/kerno/src/ui_tests/critique.rs` (one `mod critique;` line in `ui_tests.rs`);
  core tests in the module they test. Test names or doc comments quote the C number (`/// C15: …`).
- Parallel pairs that do not share files: (K1, K7), (K2, K3), (K4, K6), (K8, K9); K5 after K4 (both touch the
  transaction state display), K10 last.
- Each item ends with: its tests green with `KERNO_REQUIRE_CONTAINERS=1`, its row in `docs/FEATURES.md` (new
  section "K. v1.2: the critique of DBeaver"), a "Done:" line under the item here, the C row of
  `docs/COMPETITOR-ISSUES.md` updated, and the captures it lists (light and dark, `docs/screenshots/final/`,
  taken by the main session at the end).

---

## K1 — Quitting asks when a transaction or pending work would be lost (C15)

**Now.** `main.rs:188` binds `Quit` to `cx.quit()`; `main.rs:208-212` quits when the last window closes. An
open transaction, pending data edits, unsaved script text in a file tab: all dropped without a word (the
server rolls back when the socket closes). The update install already asks through
`Workspace::end_then` (`workspace.rs:4343`; `ui_tests::updates::quitting_to_update_asks_first_and_declining_keeps_the_download`).

**Wanted.** Cmd+Q, the Quit menu item and the window's close button run the same question as closing every
tab at once: `end_then("Quit", <all open tabs>, Then::Quit, …)`. Nothing to lose: quit at once (no dialog).
Something to lose: the dialog of `tabs::end_question` lists it ("2 tabs: a transaction on “orders” (open 4
min), pending edits on shop.customers"), with the buttons and the default of `tabs::end_question` as they
are (Enter never confirms a loss, `docs/plans/README.md`): roll back or discard and quit, or Cancel. Rolling
back sends `ROLLBACK` on each open transaction before quitting (the existing `end_then` path does it), with a
3 s limit per session so a dead server does not hold the quit. The update install keeps its own wording.

**Files.** `main.rs`: route `Quit` to the workspace (`cx.on_action` finds the workspace window and dispatches a
workspace action; if no window exists, quit). `workspace.rs`: a `quit` handler calling `end_then` with every
open tab; `on_window_should_close` (GPUI `Window::on_window_should_close`, read its signature in the pinned
gpui) returns `false` and runs the same handler. A `Then::Quit` variant if `Then` has none (look at how the
update path ends the app and reuse it).

**Tests** (`ui_tests/critique.rs`, PostgreSQL and MySQL):
- `quitting_with_an_open_transaction_asks_and_cancel_keeps_it` (C15): manual tab, `insert into
  kerno_ui_quit.t values (1)`, dispatch `Quit`: the dialog names the tab; Cancel: the app is still running,
  `tx_state() == Open`.
- `quitting_with_nothing_to_lose_quits_at_once`.
- `roll_back_and_quit_rolls_back`: confirm; on the server (`on_server`), the row is absent and no session of
  the test's marker holds a transaction.
- Closing the window takes the same path (one test through `on_window_should_close`).

**Acceptance.** No path quits with an open transaction or pending edits without the question. **Capture:**
the quit dialog with a transaction open (`m12-quit-question-{light,dark}.png`).

Done: `Workspace::quit` runs `end_then`'s question ("Quit Kerno?") over every open tab, then `cx.quit()`; the
workspace handles `Quit` when it is on the keyboard's path, and `main.rs` registers the app-wide one
(`workspace::handle_quit`) after the window opens: it hands `Quit` to the workspace (deferred, the window is in
use during a dispatch) and quits directly only without a window. `on_window_should_close` returns `false` and runs the same `quit`. `Then`
is a boxed closure, not an enum: no variant was needed. When quitting, each `ROLLBACK` is limited to 3 s
(`QUIT_ROLLBACK_LIMIT`, a Tokio timeout in `QueryTab::finish_transaction`) and one that fails or gets no answer
does not hold the quit (the server rolls back when the socket closes); a failed `COMMIT` still stops it. The
update install keeps its own wording and its unlimited wait. Unsaved file text is not asked about: the stored
tabs keep it (`TabSnapshot::Query { sql, unsaved }`). A table form with changes not applied (`Loss::Form`) is
closed with the app, so quitting asks about it too (`tabs::quit_question`; disconnecting still does not, the
form survives it). A second Quit while the question is on screen does not ask twice (`Workspace::quit_question`,
a token the dialog holds). Not covered: the Dock's Quit, logout and SIGTERM send `terminate:` straight to
AppKit (gpui has no `applicationShouldTerminate`), so they quit without asking (`docs/ROADMAP.md`, limits). Tests:
`tabs::tests::ending_a_session_asks_once_for_its_transactions_and_pending_changes` (the form),
`ui_tests::critique::{quitting_with_an_open_transaction_asks_and_cancel_keeps_it,
quitting_with_nothing_to_lose_quits_at_once, roll_back_and_quit_rolls_back,
closing_the_window_asks_as_quit_does}` on PostgreSQL and MySQL, and `quitting_from_a_dialog_asks_too` (the
app-wide handler, a dialog having the keyboard, one question for two Quits) on PostgreSQL.

## K2 — Every write can be cancelled; the first page of a table too (C3)

**Now.** No cancel for the data tab's Apply (`data/tab.rs:1350`: `cancel` acts only while loading or
counting), the structure editor's Apply (`structure/editor.rs:983-1013`), navigator Drop / Truncate / Rename
/ Create (`navigator/view.rs:2185`, task detached at 2209). Each waits forever on a lock held by another
session (PostgreSQL: a queued `ALTER` then blocks every reader of the table); closing the tab leaves the
statement running (`bridge.rs:35-36`: dropping a task does not stop the work). A cancel by dropping the future
would not help: inside a transaction block the PostgreSQL session skips the server cancel on purpose
(`kerno-postgres/src/session.rs:420-431`); an explicit `session.cancel()` stops it, block and all. Also, a
table opened for the first time shows Cancel while "Loading rows…" but `DataTab::cancel` does nothing:
`self.session` is set only in `show()` (`data/tab.rs:890`).

**Repro.** `kerno-postgres`: session A `begin; lock table kerno_ui_k2.t in access exclusive mode;`. In
Kerno: edit a cell of `kerno_ui_k2.t`, Apply → "applying…" forever, Cmd+. does nothing. Same with the
structure editor (add a column) and navigator Truncate.

**Wanted.**
- Data tab: keep the apply's session in the tab (`self.applying_session`) and have `cancel` call
  `session.cancel()` while `applying`; the apply then fails with the cancel, rolls back (it is one transaction,
  `sql/edits.rs:224`), keeps the pending edits, and says "Cancelled: nothing was changed. The pending changes
  are kept." (not "Check the filter").
- Data tab first page: store the session in `self.session` as soon as `open_session` returns, before the page
  query starts (move the assignment from `show()` to the load path), so `cancel` reaches it. A cancelled page
  says "Cancelled." with a Reload button, not the filter advice of `data/tab.rs:181-187`.
- Structure editor: a **Cancel** button beside the "applying" state and Cmd+.; keeps the session of
  `run_statements` in the editor and calls `session.cancel()`; on PostgreSQL the transaction rolls back
  ("Cancelled: nothing was changed."); on MySQL (DDL commits per statement) "Cancelled at statement N of M;
  the statements before it were applied." (reuse the existing partial-apply wording at
  `structure/editor.rs:1044-1056`).
- Navigator writes: run in a short-lived "running" state on the node (spinner and "Dropping…" on the row) with
  a **Cancel** entry in the node's context menu and in a notification that stays while it runs ("Dropping
  shop.t… Cancel"); Cancel calls `session.cancel()`.
- Cmd+. (`workspace.rs:2994-3001`) also reaches the active structure tab.
- Closing a tab that is applying cancels first (explicit `session.cancel()` in the tab's drop/close path),
  so nothing keeps running in the background.

No `lock_timeout` is set: a user may legitimately wait; what was missing is the way out. Record that choice
in `docs/DECISIONS.md`.

**Tests** (`ui_tests/critique.rs`, a `Locker` helper beside `Sleeper` that holds `LOCK TABLE` from a session
of the test; PostgreSQL, and MySQL with `LOCK TABLES … WRITE`; wait for the waiting statement with
`running_on_server(engine, marker)` before cancelling, as `docs/plans/README.md` says):
- `a_data_apply_behind_a_lock_is_cancelled_and_keeps_the_edits` (C3)
- `a_structure_apply_behind_a_lock_is_cancelled`
- `a_navigator_truncate_behind_a_lock_is_cancelled`
- `the_first_page_of_a_table_behind_a_lock_is_cancelled` (open the tab for the first time while locked;
  assert the server statement is gone: no row in `pg_stat_activity` with the marker)
- `closing_a_tab_that_applies_stops_the_statement`

**Acceptance.** Every write path has a visible cancel that stops the statement on the server; no statement
of a closed tab keeps running. **Captures:** structure editor applying with Cancel; navigator row "Dropping…".

Done: `DataTab::cancel` acts while applying too (the apply already runs on `self.session`: no second field
was needed); `cancel_requested` makes a cancelled apply say "Cancelled: nothing was changed. The pending
changes are kept." and a cancelled read a new `State::Cancelled` ("Cancelled: the rows were not read", Reload);
the load stores its session as soon as `open_session` returns, and a Cancel that came while it opened stops it
there. `TableEditor` keeps `applying_session` and a cancel flag read between statements, shows "Applying…" with
`editor-cancel`, and its `cancel` is reached by `Workspace::cancel_query` (Cmd+., status bar) and by
`close_tab_at`; `DataTab::close_session` cancels before closing when busy. Navigator Drop/Rename/Create run on
a session of their own (`Navigator::execute`, D-077): a `Write` per statement, "Dropping table t…" on the row
in place of its hint, "Cancel the running statement" first in its menu, a notification with Cancel
(`navigator-write-cancel`) removed when it ends. No spinner (text, as elsewhere). The plan was wrong on three
points: Truncate goes through the maintenance dialog, which already had a cancel on the server (its test is
kept as a pin, a Drop test covers the new path); a data tab's apply was already stopped when its tab closed
(`close_session` → both drivers' `close()` cancel a busy statement): what kept running was a table form's apply
and a first page; a dropped task never reaches the driver at all (`bridge::spawn` detaches the Tokio task), the
PostgreSQL in-block rule only matters for `exec` consumers that drop their execution. On PostgreSQL the first
statement of a first page to wait is the catalog read of the table's indexes (`pg_get_indexdef`), by oid: the
tests wait for a session blocked by the test's own locker (`pg_blocking_pids`, MySQL
`sys.schema_table_lock_waits`), not for a marker in the text. Tests: `ui_tests::critique::{
a_data_apply_behind_a_lock_is_cancelled_and_keeps_the_edits, a_structure_apply_behind_a_lock_is_cancelled,
a_navigator_truncate_behind_a_lock_is_cancelled, a_navigator_drop_behind_a_lock_is_cancelled,
the_first_page_of_a_table_behind_a_lock_is_cancelled, closing_a_tab_that_applies_stops_the_statement}` on
PostgreSQL and MySQL, with a `Locker` helper; `a_slow_page_is_cancelled_and_the_tab_stays_usable` now expects
"cancelled". Not covered here, done by K15 (see its "Done:" line): the structure tab's "Set comment", a cancel
that lands between two statements of a data apply, the table form's own load and the structure tab's load
behind the same lock (Cancel and Cmd+. now stop them, and so does closing the tab). Left with K15's reasons: the
FDW dialog, the admin tab's cancel/terminate.

## K3 — A query tab shows the database and schema its session is really in (C6)

**Now.** The tab's database and schema are applied once when its session opens (`query/tab.rs:1257-1288`:
`open_session(target.database)`, then `SET search_path TO <schema>, public`). After `USE b` (MySQL) or `SET
search_path TO other` (PostgreSQL) the picker and status bar still show the target
(`workspace/toolbar.rs:406-413`, `query/tab.rs:818-824`). Worse, any rebuild of the tab's session
(auto-reconnect `query/tab.rs:1061`/`1106`, read-only toggle, isolation change) silently returns to the
target: the user believes they are in `b`, the next statement runs in `a`. The MySQL driver already follows
`USE` (`kerno-mysql/src/session.rs:430-433`, `Session::database`); the app never reads it for the tab.
`\c` is sent to the server as SQL and fails there (fine: psql meta-commands are not SQL).

**Wanted.** The session's state wins and becomes the tab's target:
- `kerno-core/src/driver.rs` `Session`: add `fn schema(&self) -> Option<String>` beside `database()`
  ("the first schema of the search path on PostgreSQL; `None` where the engine has none").
- `kerno-postgres/src/session.rs`: after a statement for which `touches_session_state` is true (write a
  PostgreSQL one as the MySQL driver has at `kerno-mysql/src/session.rs:497`: `SET [SESSION|LOCAL]
  search_path`, `SET SCHEMA`, `RESET search_path`, `RESET ALL`, `DISCARD ALL`), read `select
  current_schema()` and keep it; `schema()` returns it.
- `query/tab.rs`: after each statement, if `session.database()` or `session.schema()` differs from the
  target, update `target.database` / `target.schema` (the tab's stored snapshot follows, so a restart and a
  reconnect come back to where the user was) and say it in the messages panel: "Now in database b (USE)."
  The picker then shows the truth.
- A `SET LOCAL search_path` inside a transaction is undone at commit or rollback: after `COMMIT`/`ROLLBACK`
  read it again (the same hook covers it if `touches_session_state` also matches `COMMIT`, `ROLLBACK`,
  `END`, `ABORT` when the previous statement set it; simpler: always re-read after a transaction ends).

**Tests.**
- Driver tests: `kerno-postgres/tests/session.rs` `the_schema_follows_set_search_path` (C6), including `SET
  LOCAL` then `ROLLBACK`; `kerno-mysql/tests/session.rs` already covers `USE`: check, add if absent.
- UI (`critique.rs`): `use_in_a_tab_moves_its_picker_and_survives_a_reconnect` (MySQL and MariaDB: run `use
  kerno_ui_k3_b`, the picker shows `kerno_ui_k3_b`; kill the tab's backend with `on_server("kill <id>")`, run
  `select database()`: the result is `kerno_ui_k3_b`), and the PostgreSQL twin with `set search_path`.
- Unit test for the PostgreSQL `touches_session_state`.

**Acceptance.** No sequence of statements, reconnects or toggles makes the tab run somewhere other than what
its picker shows. **Capture:** a tab after `USE`, picker updated, message shown.

Done: `Session::schema()` (default `None`); `PgSession` reads `current_schema()` at connect (in the identity
query, no extra round trip) and again, on the link it holds, after a request `touches_session_state` matches:
a statement starting with `SET` (search path, schema, role and session authorization, which move `$user`),
`RESET`, `DISCARD`, `DO`, `CALL`, or ending a transaction (`COMMIT`, `ROLLBACK` including `TO SAVEPOINT`,
`END`, `ABORT`, `PREPARE`: a plain `SET` inside a block that rolls back is undone too, not only `SET LOCAL`),
or any `set_config` call; not after a closed or let-go statement; a failed read (aborted block) keeps the last
value; NULL gives `None`. `QueryTab` remembers where its session was when it was installed (after its own
`SET search_path`) and, after each statement and after Commit / Roll back (`finish_transaction`, idle rollback
too), moves `target.database` / `target.schema` in place when the session moved and the target differs ("Now
in database b (USE)." / "Now in schema other (search_path)." in the messages), reloads completion and emits
`QueryTabEvent::Moved`, on which the workspace stores the tabs. The session is not rebuilt; other tabs and the
navigator do not change; a `None` (no database after MySQL's `DROP DATABASE`, no schema of the path exists)
leaves the target as it is. (An isolation change does not rebuild the session: `set_isolation` runs on it.)
MariaDB follows `USE` through the same MySQL driver path (`session.database()`,
pinned for both by `kerno-mysql/tests/introspect.rs::introspection_does_not_disturb_the_session`). Tests:
`kerno-postgres/tests/execute.rs::the_schema_follows_set_search_path` (connect, `SET`, `RESET`, `SET LOCAL` then
`ROLLBACK`, plain `SET` in a rolled-back block, `set_config` with a missing first schema, NULL);
`session::tests::statements_that_may_change_the_current_schema`; UI
`critique::use_in_a_tab_moves_its_picker_and_survives_a_reconnect` (MySQL),
`critique::use_in_a_mariadb_tab_moves_its_picker_and_survives_a_reconnect`,
`critique::set_search_path_in_a_tab_moves_its_picker_and_survives_a_reconnect` (with `SET LOCAL` then Roll
back): message, picker, status bar, the stored tab, the other tab unchanged, the tab's backend killed and the
server asked again (`database()` / `current_schema()` and a new backend id), back to the first place from the
picker. Limits: the target holds one schema, so `SET search_path TO a, b` comes back after a rebuild as `a,
public` (`Target` and the stored tab hold one schema; keeping the path would change the stored format and the
picker); `current_schema()` is the first schema of the path that exists (`missing, b` gives `b`); a function
called from a `SELECT` that sets the path (other than `set_config`) is not seen until the next matched
statement; `ALTER ROLE … SET search_path` changes nothing now and only reaches a rebuilt session whose target
has no schema; a session whose path has no existing schema keeps the picker on the old schema until a rebuild
puts it back there. Not K3: a script file bound silently, a deleted connection's tabs (K6). Plan errors:
`kerno-postgres/tests/session.rs` and `kerno-mysql/tests/session.rs` do not exist (the test is in
`execute.rs`; MySQL's `USE` was already covered in `introspect.rs`); one UI test per engine, not a loop over
MySQL and MariaDB: the stored tabs are the app's global storage, and the first engine's workspace, still alive,
stores its own tabs over the second's.

## K4 — An open transaction is visible on every tab, with its age (C15, C21)

**Now.** "transaction open" is in the status bar of the active tab only (`workspace.rs:3955-3961`); the tab
label's dot covers unsaved text and pending edits (`workspace.rs:347-358`); no age anywhere except per
statement inside the transaction log (`query/tx_log.rs:117`). Open a transaction in tab A, switch to B:
nothing says A holds locks.

**Wanted.**
- Tab strip: a query tab with `TxState::Open` shows a small warning-coloured badge after its title (a filled
  circle in `theme.warning`, distinct from the unsaved dot; `TxState::Failed` in `theme.danger`), with a
  tooltip "Transaction open for 4 min: Commit or Roll back (⌘⇧Enter …)" (use the real bindings of Commit and
  Rollback from `actions.rs`). The vertical tab list and the overflow menu (J10) show the same badge.
- Status bar: "transaction open · 4 min" (minutes; seconds under a minute), refreshed by a 15 s timer only
  while some tab has a transaction (no timer otherwise: idle CPU stays at zero).
- The tab records `tx_started: Option<Instant>` when its state goes `Idle → Open` and clears it on `Idle`
  (`query/tab.rs`, where `tx_state` is updated after a statement).
- Readable in both themes (WCAG AA against the tab background): check the contrast with the existing theme
  tests' helper.

**Tests.** `a_transaction_is_shown_on_its_tab_while_another_is_active` (C15): open a transaction in tab A,
switch to B; the strip element `("tab-tx", index_of_A)` exists, B's status bar says nothing, A's shows the age
after the simulated clock moves 2 min. `the_transaction_badge_goes_when_it_ends`.

**Acceptance.** From any tab, the user can see which tabs hold a transaction and for how long. **Captures:**
tab strip with one tab in a transaction, both themes, and the status bar.

Done: the badge and the status text follow the session's own state (`QueryTab::tx_state()`, read on each
frame), so no badge outlives its transaction whatever ended it; only the age depends on hooks.
`QueryTab::tx_started` (the executor's clock, so tests move it) is set by `note_transaction` when the tab sees
its session in a transaction and none was recorded, and cleared once it sees none: after the manual-commit
`BEGIN` (the age starts there, not after the first statement), after each statement (a typed `BEGIN` /
`START TRANSACTION`, `COMMIT`, `ROLLBACK`, MySQL's implicit commit), at the end of a run (a failed source
rolled back), after `finish_transaction` (Commit, Roll back, the environment's idle rule, K1's rollback at
quit), after applying a result's edits; `close_session` clears it (a lost connection, a disconnect, a moved
or rebuilt session, a closed tab). A failed transaction, or one back to a savepoint, keeps its start. The
badge: a 7 px dot in `theme.warning` (`theme.danger` once failed) after the title, outside the environment's
tint and before the close button, `("tab-tx", i)` in the strip, the rows and the side list (where the title
is cut and the dot stays), `("all-tabs-tx", i)` in the list of every tab; its tooltip and accessibility
description are `tabs::transaction_tip` ("Transaction open for 4 min: Commit (⌃4) or Roll back (⌃8).") with
the keymap's shortcuts, the user's file applied (`workspace::shortcut`; none invented when a command has
none). Status bar: `tabs::transaction_status` ("transaction open · 4 min", "transaction failed: roll back · 1
min"). `tabs::age_text` rounds down: "42 s", "4 min", "1 h 05 min". `Workspace::tx_ticker` is armed in
`render` while some query tab holds a transaction and dropped when none does; it only calls `notify` every
15 s. Other tabs hold none between two actions: a table data tab's Apply opens and ends its own (`apply_edits`:
`START TRANSACTION` … `COMMIT` or `ROLLBACK`), a table form's too, the navigator writes on a session of its
own, and all of them report `TxState::Idle` to the status bar; only query tabs get the badge. Restored tabs
have no session: no badge, no timer. Plan errors: ⌘⇧Enter in the plan's tooltip is not a shortcut of Commit or
Roll back (`ctrl-4`, `ctrl-8`, DBeaver's); the strip needed no subscription to redraw when a tab not on
screen changes (GPUI redraws a view when an entity it read in its last frame notifies, and the strip reads
every query tab: a test with the subscription removed still passed, so it was taken out); six checks in existing
tests compared a status bar item with "transaction open" exactly and now match its start (`Fixture::status_says`),
the two negative ones included, which would otherwise have passed for nothing. Not covered: a backend killed
from outside keeps the badge until the tab's next statement finds it gone (K16); a `BEGIN` typed in
auto-commit is not watched by the idle rule (K5, done since); the quit and close questions do not give the age; the
tooltip itself is not hovered in a test (its text is read from the same accessibility description); no test
pins the age of a transaction begun by applying a result's edits. Tests: unit
`tabs::tests::{a_transaction_age_reads_in_seconds_minutes_then_hours, a_transaction_says_its_age_and_how_to_end_it}`,
`theme::tests::non_text_contrast_meets_wcag` (warning and danger at 3:1 on the seven surfaces a tab is drawn
on, both themes); UI `critique::{a_transaction_is_shown_on_its_tab_while_another_is_active,
the_transaction_badge_goes_when_it_ends, a_transaction_ended_behind_another_tab_leaves_the_strip,
a_transaction_begun_in_a_script_is_dated_from_its_begin, a_lost_connection_takes_the_badge_away,
the_idle_rule_takes_the_badge_off_a_tab_not_shown}` on PostgreSQL and MySQL,
`critique::{a_failed_transaction_keeps_its_badge_and_its_age,
the_badge_and_the_unsaved_mark_stay_apart_from_the_close_button, restored_tabs_show_no_transaction}` on
PostgreSQL. Each was seen failing with a change disabled: no badge on the tab (eight of them; not
`restored_tabs_show_no_transaction`, a negative check); the timer never armed (`a_transaction_is_shown…`,
`the_transaction_badge_goes…`); no badge in the list of every tab (`a_transaction_is_shown…`); no note after the
manual-commit `BEGIN`, or after each statement (`a_transaction_begun_in_a_script…`); no clear in
`close_session` (`the_transaction_badge_goes…`). `a_transaction_ended_behind_another_tab_leaves_the_strip`
is a pin: it also passed with the strip's subscription to its tabs removed (see above).

## K5 — Switching to auto-commit or "Set as default" ends the transaction with a question (C15)

**Now.** Switching Manual → Auto with a transaction open only flips a flag (`query/tab.rs:3096-3099`); the
toolbar then says "Auto" but later statements join the open transaction (`query/tab.rs:2454` begins only when
idle) and the idle rollback stops watching it (`query/tab.rs:3107`), so production's 10-minute rule no longer
applies. An explicit `BEGIN` typed in an auto-commit tab has the same effect. Navigator "Set as default"
(`workspace.rs:1928-1937`) calls `tab.set_target` directly, whose `close_session` sends `ROLLBACK`
(`query/tab.rs:1330-1342`) with no question.

**Wanted.**
- Manual → Auto with `TxState::Open`: the end question of `end_then` ("Switch to auto-commit: Commit / Roll
  back / Cancel"), then the switch.
- `idle_period` watches any open transaction that has written, whatever the mode (an explicit `BEGIN` in an
  auto-commit tab included).
- "Set as default" goes through `request_target` (`query/tab.rs:994-1004` → `workspace.rs:4296-4325`), the
  path the toolbar picker uses, so it asks the same question.

**Tests.** `switching_to_auto_commit_with_a_transaction_asks` (Cancel keeps both the mode and the
transaction; Commit commits then switches); `set_as_default_asks_before_rolling_back` (C15);
`an_explicit_begin_in_auto_commit_is_rolled_back_when_idle` (production environment, simulated clock).

**Acceptance.** No action ends or hides a transaction without the question.

Done: `QueryTab::request_manual_commit` is the one path of every commit-mode switch the user makes: the main
toolbar's Auto / Manual menu, the status bar's transaction item, the palette's "Switch to auto-commit" and its
key (`ToggleCommitMode`, all through `Workspace::perform(TabAction::ManualCommit)`), and the tab's own
"Auto-commit / Manual commit" menu, which called `set_manual_commit` directly. To auto-commit with a transaction
open (open or failed) it emits `QueryTabEvent::SwitchToAutoCommit`; `Workspace::switch_to_auto_commit` asks
`end_then`'s question ("Switch “Query 1” to auto-commit?", `tabs::end_question` unchanged: Enter never answers,
Commit greyed for a failed transaction), ends the transaction, then switches; Cancel keeps both. To manual commit
nothing is lost, so nothing is asked: a typed `BEGIN` goes on, with its dot, status text and log. The other ways
the mode changes cannot end or hide a transaction: `set_target` gives a tab its connection's default mode only
on another connection and with no transaction; an isolation change is refused while one is open
(`isolation_why_not`); the read-only toggle, disconnect, reconnect, a connection edited while open
(`ReconnectRequested` → `reconnect`) and delete already went through `end_then`; an environment edited later
does not touch an open tab's mode. The idle rule (`idle_period`) no longer requires manual commit: it watches
any open transaction whose log has a statement that writes, and the log now records the statements of a
transaction in either mode (a `BEGIN` typed in auto-commit included); a statement that begins a transaction (none
dated yet, K4's `tx_started`) starts the log empty, so a transaction ended out of sight (its connection closed
under it) leaves nothing behind for the rule to count. A transaction that only read stays unwatched, as in manual
commit. The navigator's "Set as default" calls `request_target` (the toolbar picker's path): no transaction, it
moves at once as before, running statement or not; one open, `switch_target`'s question ("Switch “Query 1” to
the schema “b”?"). Callers of `set_target`: `request_target` (no transaction) and `switch_target`'s `then`
(after the question); of `QueryTab::close_session`: `set_target`, `close_tab_at` (after the close question), a
lost connection and a connection found closed (`follow_connection`; both say the server rolled back, Kerno's
own disconnects ask first). None rolls back silently outside the paths that ask. Review fix (main session):
switching to auto-commit ends the transaction only (`Asking::Transactions`, beside `Leaving` and `Quitting`, in
place of `ask_then`'s `quitting` flag): the edits waiting in a result are neither asked about nor discarded,
since the session stays (`critique::switching_to_auto_commit_keeps_the_edits_waiting_in_a_result`; the first
version went through `end_then` and discarded them as moving the tab does). Not covered: a MySQL `SET autocommit = 0` typed, which Kerno does not read; a switch made in
manual commit while a first statement waits for its session (before manual commit's `BEGIN` lands) finds no
transaction and does not ask, and the run then opens one in an auto-commit tab (shown, watched, not hidden); a
MySQL `START TRANSACTION` typed inside a transaction commits it and begins another, which keeps the first one's
start and log (K4's dating too), so the idle rule may count writes already committed; the tab's own menu is driven
in the tests through the method it calls, not a click on its entry. Plan error: the idle rule had a second gate,
the log was kept in manual commit only (`statement_ended`), so dropping the mode check of `idle_period` alone
would not have armed it after a typed `BEGIN`. Tests: `ui_tests::critique::{
switching_to_auto_commit_with_a_transaction_asks, switching_to_manual_commit_keeps_a_typed_begin,
set_as_default_asks_before_rolling_back, an_explicit_begin_in_auto_commit_is_rolled_back_when_idle,
a_typed_begin_after_a_closed_connection_starts_its_own_log}` on PostgreSQL and MySQL,
`switching_to_auto_commit_with_a_failed_transaction_only_rolls_back` on PostgreSQL. Each was seen failing with a
part disabled: the switch never asking (the two `switching_to_auto_commit…`), only the palette's path not
asking (the same two), "Set as default" calling `set_target` (`set_as_default…`), the mode check back in
`idle_period` (`an_explicit_begin…`), the log kept in manual commit only (`an_explicit_begin…`,
`switching_to_manual_commit…`, `a_typed_begin…`), no fresh log at a new transaction (`a_typed_begin…`).

## K6 — A script file never binds to a non-local connection silently; a deleted connection's tabs (C6)

**Now.** Opening a `.sql` file binds it to whatever connection the navigator shows, if connected
(`workspace.rs:1989-2002`, used at 2059): select a production connection, open a file, and the script runs on
production with only the colour as a signal. Deleting a connection (`workspace.rs:4250-4261`, then
`connections/manager.rs:466-495`) leaves its query tabs pointing at the dead id (the run message says
"Double-click it in the sidebar", `query/tab.rs:1247-1255`, wrong: there is nothing to double-click) and its
data and structure tabs open.

**Wanted.**
- Open file: if the default target's environment is `local` (or its rules have no confirmation at all), bind
  as today. Otherwise open the tab **unbound** and show in the editor's toolbar "Pick a connection to run this
  script" with the picker open-able in one click, the default connection listed first. The environment test
  is the environment's rules (`confirm_execute || confirm_writes_twice || read_only`), not its name, so a
  user's own environments follow. Recent scripts (`settings.remember_script`) remember the connection a file
  last ran on; reopening it proposes that one first, still without binding when non-local.
- Delete a connection: its query tabs become unbound (keep their text) with the message "Its connection was
  deleted: pick another one to run this script."; its data, structure, admin and ER tabs close (they cannot
  exist without it), after the end question for anything they would lose (they are already in `end_then`'s
  losses).

**Tests.** `a_script_opened_beside_a_production_connection_is_not_bound` (C6);
`a_script_opened_beside_a_local_connection_is_bound`; `deleting_a_connection_unbinds_its_query_tabs_and_closes_the_others`.

**Acceptance.** A script file never runs on a non-local connection unless the user picked it. **Capture:** an
unbound script tab with its "Pick a connection" bar.

Done: every `.sql` file reaches a tab through `Workspace::open_sql_file_from` (File ▸ Open and its palette
entry and the editor's menu entry, all `OpenSqlFile`; the main toolbar's recent scripts), which asks
`script_target`: the connection the file last ran on (`settings.script_connections`, new, beside
`recent_scripts`), else the one the navigator browses when connected; bound only where
`ConnectionConfig::binds_scripts_silently` (kerno-core, pure: none of the environment's `confirm_execute`,
`confirm_writes_twice`, `read_only`, nor the connection's own `read_only`), otherwise the tab opens unbound
proposing it, never bound to another one in its place. There is no other way in: no file drop on the window
(only `DraggedConnection` drops exist), the command line and a running instance take a connection URL only
(`kerno-core/src/instance.rs::parse_args`), no vim `:e`; restored tabs and "Reopen closed tab" keep the
user's earlier binding; "New SQL editor" binds as before. Unbound is the existing `target: None` state (the
one a connection gone at restart leaves): `QueryTab` gained `proposed` and `unbound_why`; every unbound query
tab, a plain new one included, shows the bar `unbound-bar` ("Pick a connection to run this script.", or "Its
connection was deleted: pick another one to run this script.") with `unbound-pick`, the same menu as the
toolbar's `tab-connection` (`connection_menu`, shared), the proposed connection first there, in the main
toolbar's connection picker and in the editor's "Active connection" submenu (`QueryTab::choices`). Run, Run all,
and Explain stop in `QueryTab::launch` before anything (parameters, the
environment's confirmations, the connection) and say "Nothing ran. …" in the messages. A pick goes through
`request_target` (the question when a transaction is open), the bar goes, the environment's confirmations
apply at run, and `set_target` remembers the connection of a file's tab (`remember_script(path,
Some(id))`; `None`, at an unbound open, keeps the one remembered; Save and Save As remember the tab's). The
tab's two pickers and the main toolbar's now accept a connection that is not connected (D-078): the first run
connects it, as for a tab of a closed connection. Deleting: the one path is the sidebar's Delete (its menu, and Delete in the tree) → the
alert → `DeleteRequested` → `Workspace::delete_connection`, whose question is the new `Asking::Deleting`
(`tabs::delete_question`: as disconnecting, plus a table form's changes, which close too; unsaved text is
not asked about, its tab stays); Cancel deletes nothing. Once the question is answered, its query tabs are
unbound with their text, file and unsaved mark (`unbind_deleted` → `set_target(None)`, which stops a running
statement and closes the session, as closing the tab does) before `Connections::delete`, whose disconnect
would otherwise report the statement lost and the connection "opened again by itself"; a deletion the storage
refuses leaves them unbound (a `ponytail:` note). The other tabs follow once the connection has left the list
(`ConnectionsEvent::ListChanged` → `drop_gone_connections`, which also unbinds any query tab left): data,
structure, admin, ER and table-form tabs close (`close_now`: the active tab stays active, a busy one
is cancelled, K2), the closed query tabs that can be reopened lose the connection too, and the tabs are
stored. The edit dialog has no Delete; deleting a folder moves its connections up; an import adds or
replaces by id and never removes one; an unsaved URL connection leaves only through Delete during a run (at
quit or the next start, `without_gone_connection` as before). "Double-click it in the sidebar" stays for a
saved connection that is closed (a double click does connect it); a tab whose connection id is gone now gets
the deleted message from `session()` instead of naming "this tab's connection". The built-in staging has
local's rules, so a file beside a staging connection still binds silently: an environment that should not
gets a rule. Not covered: Library (history, saved queries) opens a query on the connection it was recorded
on, production included (the user's earlier choice, not a file); the editor's "Active connection" submenu
still greys out a connection that is not open (its entries are the editor menu's `Entry` with a reason);
after a restart an unbound file tab has no proposed connection (it is not stored, though
`script_connections` still has it) and a deleted connection's tab shows the usual words; the bar's contrast is not measured (warning wash at 12 % under
the theme's text, the capture will show it); the remembered connection is kept for the last ten scripts
only. Plan errors: there was already an unbound state and a picker (`tabs.rs`), so the bar reuses them; the
messages that name a connection by an id that may be gone are only the run's (`connection_name` is used for
tabs of a connection that still exists); `end_then`'s question did not list a table form, which deleting
closes. Tests: `kerno-core` `config::tests::a_script_binds_silently_only_where_no_rule_asks_first`,
`settings::tests::recent_scripts_keep_the_last_ten_latest_first_once_each` (the connection kept, `None`
keeps it, pruned with the list); UI `critique::{a_script_opened_beside_a_production_connection_is_not_bound,
a_script_opened_beside_a_local_connection_is_bound}` on PostgreSQL,
`critique::deleting_a_connection_unbinds_its_query_tabs_and_closes_the_others` on PostgreSQL and MySQL;
`a_tab_without_an_open_connection_says_what_to_do` now expects the message, not a failed result. Each was seen
failing with a part disabled: binding whatever the rule says (both script tests), the proposed connection
last (production), no guard in `launch` (production, delete), no remembering in `set_target` (production),
the remembered connection ignored (production), no reconcile on `ListChanged` (delete), the end question of
`Leaving` for a deletion (delete: no form line), the other tabs not closed (delete), the closed tabs not
mapped (delete), no deleted message (delete), the pickers greying out a closed connection again
(production), no unbinding before the deletion (delete: the stopped statement reported lost). Found by the
agent and fixed at review (it existed before K6, and D-078 makes it easier to meet): a tab of a closed
connection that connects on its first run never filled its database and schema lists, because the run bumped
`generation` and the listing started on connect was dropped (`QueryTab::start`, `follow_connection`). The
listing now checks `QueryTab::placed`, bumped at a target change only
(`ui_tests::running_in_a_tab_of_a_closed_connection_connects_first` waits for the lists; seen failing with the
old check).

## K7 — Three classifier holes (C21)

**Now** (`kerno-core/src/sql/classify.rs`). Each returns `destructive = false`, so on a local or staging
connection with default settings it runs without the question; read-only and production still guard them
(they are writes).
1. MySQL versioned comments: `collect` (`classify.rs:263-283`) strips the version and always counts the body
   as SQL. `DELETE FROM t /*!90000 WHERE id = 1 */` on MySQL 8.4: the server skips the comment (90000 > its
   version 80400) and deletes every row; Kerno saw a `WHERE`.
2. PostgreSQL `PREPARE p AS DELETE FROM t` (and `… AS UPDATE t SET …` without `WHERE`): falls in `_ =>
   other(true)` (`classify.rs:418-420`); the inner statement is visible text.
3. `MERGE INTO t USING s ON true WHEN MATCHED THEN DELETE` (PostgreSQL 15+, `classify.rs:370`).

**Wanted.**
1. A `WHERE` found inside a versioned comment does not count as a `WHERE` (it may not run). Simplest correct
   rule, independent of the server version: collect the body for every other purpose, but mark its tokens so
   `has_where` ignores them. A statement whose only `WHERE` is in a versioned comment is destructive.
2. `PREPARE name [(types)] AS <statement>`: classify `<statement>` (as `EXPLAIN` does at `classify.rs:497`),
   keep `kind = Other`, `writes` and `destructive` from the inner statement. MySQL `PREPARE p FROM '<text>'`
   and a bare `EXECUTE`: unchanged (a text classifier cannot see them; they stay writes, so read-only and
   production guard them). Say so in a comment.
3. `MERGE … WHEN MATCHED [AND …] THEN DELETE`: destructive (it can delete without a condition the user
   reviews).
`REPLACE INTO` and `INSERT … ON CONFLICT/ON DUPLICATE KEY` stay non-destructive: they act on keyed rows.
`WHERE true` / `WHERE 1=1` stay non-destructive: an explicit `WHERE` is the user's decision (DBeaver does
the same); record it in `docs/DECISIONS.md`.

**Tests** (add to the lists of `classify::tests::statements_are_classified`, MySQL and PostgreSQL rules):
`DELETE FROM t /*!90000 WHERE id = 1 */` and `UPDATE t SET a = 1 /*!999999 WHERE id = 1 */` destructive;
`DELETE FROM t /*!80000 WHERE id = 1 */` destructive too (rule 1 does not look at the version); `/*! … */`
bodies still count for `writes` (a dump's `/*!40000 ALTER TABLE … DISABLE KEYS */` still writes);
`PREPARE p AS DELETE FROM t` destructive, `PREPARE p AS DELETE FROM t WHERE id = $1` not;
`MERGE … THEN DELETE` destructive, `MERGE … THEN UPDATE SET …` not. Update the existing assertion that has
`MERGE … WHEN MATCHED THEN DELETE` non-destructive. One UI test on MySQL: `a_versioned_comment_where_still_asks`.

**Acceptance.** `classify` tests green on both rule sets; the three inputs ask on a local connection.

Done: `classify` reads a statement twice when it holds a versioned comment, with every such comment and
without any (`without_versioned`), and it writes or destroys as soon as one reading does; its kind and
`returns_rows` are the reading with the comments (D-079). Versioned means `/*!NNNNN … */` and every `/*M! … */`
(MySQL skips it; Kerno lexes MySQL and MariaDB alike and never compares versions); a bare `/*! … */` runs on
both servers, so its `WHERE` holds (the existing `DELETE FROM t /*! WHERE id = 1 */` row stays non-destructive).
Each token carries the number of the versioned comment it came from (`Tok::comment`, `collect`), and a keyword
that makes a statement safer counts only beside the one it protects, in the same comment or both outside: the
`WHERE` of an `UPDATE` / `DELETE` (`has_where`), `NOT` / `DEFAULT` after `ALTER … DROP`, `FOR` / `KEY` / `DO` /
`THEN` before an `UPDATE` in a `WITH`. So `DELETE FROM t /*!80000 WHERE id = 1 */` asks whatever the version,
`DELETE FROM t WHERE /*!90000 id = 1 AND */ 1 = 1` does not (the `WHERE` outside is the user's, as `WHERE true`
is), a `LIMIT` in a comment changes nothing, `/*!50000 DELETE FROM t */` is the destructive `DELETE` it holds and
`/*!50000 DELETE FROM t WHERE id = 1 */` is not, `/*!50000 DELETE FROM t */ /*!90000 WHERE id = 1 */` asks, and
`ALTER TABLE t DROP /*!90000 DEFAULT */ c` and `/*!90000 SELECT * FROM */ DELETE FROM t` destroy. Everything
else a versioned keyword decides (`TRUNCATE`, `DROP`, `INTO`, `SET GLOBAL`, the write test) can only make a
statement more dangerous; `single_table_select` takes either reading's refusal. On PostgreSQL rules these are
plain comments (nothing inside ever counted: pinned there too). `PREPARE name [(types)] AS <statement>` is
classified as its statement (first `AS` at the statement's depth; the types sit deeper), kind `Other`, no rows;
no `AS` (`PREPARE TRANSACTION 'x'`, MySQL `PREPARE p FROM '…'`) stays a write, never destructive, and so does
`EXECUTE` (a comment says why). `MERGE` destroys when a `THEN DELETE` pair sits at its own depth (`WHEN MATCHED
[AND …]`, `WHEN NOT MATCHED BY SOURCE`, several `WHEN`s, `RETURNING`, under `WITH`, `EXPLAIN ANALYZE` and
`PREPARE`); `THEN UPDATE SET`, `THEN INSERT`, `DO NOTHING`, and the words in a string, a comment or a quoted
name do not. `WHERE true` / `WHERE 1=1`, `REPLACE INTO` and `INSERT … ON CONFLICT / ON DUPLICATE KEY` unchanged.
The confirmation's explanation (`QueryTab::execute`) now names "a MERGE that deletes". Callers: every editor run (statement, selection, script, stacked results,
parameters, EXPLAIN ANALYZE, and each target of a run on several connections, which runs in a query tab) goes
through `QueryTab::execute`'s `classify`, so the fix reaches them with no other change; applying a routine's
source reads `destructive` and the `BEGIN` kind (unchanged for its statements); `browse.rs` reads kind and
`writes` only; saved script tasks run the engine's client and always confirm (`destructive: true`); no caller
relied on the old verdicts (whole app suite green). Limits: the question is asked at `PREPARE`, never at
`EXECUTE`; a statement whose meaning changes only when some versioned comments run and others do not is judged
on the two extreme readings plus the same-comment rule; kind and `returns_rows` follow the reading with the
comments (`/*!90000 BEGIN */` is a `BEGIN` to the source-apply wording); a `MERGE … ON true … THEN UPDATE`
changes every row without the destructive question (a write: production and read-only guard it). Plan error:
the editor never sent the comments that open or end a statement (`split.rs::push_statement` kept only the
span between the first and last non-comment tokens, and `/*! … */` is a comment to the lexer), so the plan's own
example `DELETE FROM t /*!90000 WHERE id = 1 */` was already asked in the app: it is classified, shown and run
as `DELETE FROM t` (seen with a debug print in `execute`). The hole was real for a comment inside a statement
(`… /*!90000 WHERE id = 1 */ ORDER BY id`) and for `classify` called on whole text. The same trimming means a
trailing `/*! WHERE … */` is never sent even to a server that would run it (once confirmed, `DELETE FROM t /*!
WHERE id = 1 */` deletes every row; the confirmation, which shows the trimmed SQL, is the only guard), and a statement entirely in a
comment (a dump's `/*!40101 SET NAMES utf8 */;`, or `/*!50000 DELETE FROM t */`) is not run at all (a probe of
`split_statements` on MySQL rules gave only `DELETE FROM t` for those two and `DELETE FROM t /*! WHERE id = 1 */`): fixed at review
(main session), since it sent a `DELETE` without the condition the user wrote: `split.rs::runs_as_sql` makes a
`/*! … */` or `/*M! … */` comment statement text on MySQL rules, never trimmed, opaque to the delimiter, and
a statement when it stands alone (`split::tests::mysql_executable_comments_are_statement_text`; the UI test
runs `DELETE … /*! WHERE id = 1 */` and finds one row gone, not all; both seen failing without the fix). Tests: `kerno-core` `classify::tests::statements_are_classified` (19 new rows on the
MySQL list, 22 on the PostgreSQL one, `WHERE true` and `WHERE 1=1` in the common one, the `WITH … MERGE … THEN DELETE` row now
destructive; 24 failed before the fix), `classify::tests::executable_comments_are_mysql_only`; UI
`critique::a_versioned_comment_where_still_asks` (MySQL 8.4: `DELETE FROM kerno_ui_k7.t /*!90000 WHERE id = 1 */
ORDER BY id` asks, Cancel, the two rows still on the server), seen failing with versioned comments not marked.

## K8 — Kerno never vanishes at start; the store is copied before a migration (C16, C14)

**Now.** `main.rs:154-156`: any start error is printed to stderr and the app quits; `logging::init` runs after
the load (`main.rs:174`), so nothing is logged. From the Dock the icon bounces and disappears. Causes: data
written by a newer Kerno (a downgrade, `storage.rs:931-934`), **one** unreadable connection row
(`storage.rs:302-305`: the whole list fails, while tabs skip bad rows at `storage.rs:552-557`), a data
directory that cannot be created. No copy is taken before a migration runs (`storage.rs:927-944`). A settings
save failure is only logged (`state.rs:152-153`), so a change looks applied and is lost at restart.

**Wanted.**
- Start the logger first (it only needs `AppPaths`), so every start failure is in the log.
- A start failure opens a small native window (GPUI, Kerno's theme, no workspace) that says what happened in
  plain words, then the raw error below, and offers what applies: "This data was written by Kerno 0.3 (schema
  14); this is 0.2 (schema 12). Install Kerno 0.3 or newer, or start fresh: the current data is moved aside
  to `kerno-<timestamp>.sqlite3` and nothing is deleted." Buttons: **Open data folder**, **Start fresh (move
  data aside)**, **Quit**. "Start fresh" renames, never deletes.
- One unreadable connection row is skipped and reported once in a notification after start ("1 saved
  connection could not be read and is hidden; it is still in the data file, a newer Kerno may read it"), as
  tabs already do. Never deleted.
- Before running any migration on an existing store (`version > 0 && version < MIGRATIONS.len()`), copy the
  database file with SQLite's online backup (`rusqlite`'s `backup` feature, or `VACUUM INTO`, which needs no
  new feature: prefer it) to `<data dir>/backups/kerno-schema<version>-<timestamp>.sqlite3`, keep the three
  newest. A failed copy stops the migration and reports it (the store stays untouched).
- A failed settings save shows a notification ("Settings could not be saved: … They apply until Kerno
  quits.").

**Tests.**
- Core (`storage.rs` tests, temporary directories): `a_migration_copies_the_store_first` (create a store at
  schema 2 as `storage::tests` already does at line 1725, open it: a backup file exists and opens at schema 2);
  `only_three_backups_are_kept`; `an_unreadable_connection_row_is_skipped_not_fatal` (write a broken JSON
  row with raw SQL).
- App: the start-failure window is a pure function `start_failure(&anyhow::Error) -> StartFailure { headline,
  next_step, raw, can_start_fresh }` unit-tested for the newer-schema, unreadable-directory and other cases;
  one `VisualTestContext` test that renders it.
- `a_settings_save_failure_is_shown` (make the store read-only through a test hook on `Storage`, not a
  process-wide state).

**Acceptance.** No start failure leaves the user without a window and a log line; a migration can always be
undone by hand. **Capture:** the start-failure window for a newer schema (run the release build on a scratch
data directory whose `PRAGMA user_version` is set to 999).

Done: `main()` looks the paths up once (`AppPaths::locate`, only there), hands a URL to a running instance as
before (J4 first), then starts the logger from the paths alone (`logging::start`; a log folder that cannot be
made is no start failure: stderr says so), then loads on a thread (`load(&AppPaths)`, `Startup::load(&AppPaths)`).
Any failure of the load or of `start()` opens `start_failure::open`: a window of its own (kit and Kerno's theme,
no app state, no bridge) with a headline, a next step, the raw error and the buttons that apply, the error
logged and printed. `start_failure(&anyhow::Error) -> StartFailure { headline, next_step, raw, can_start_fresh }`
reads a typed error: `storage::OpenError` / `OpenProblem` (`Newer`, `Damaged`, `InUse`, `NotWritable`,
`CopyFailed`, `MigrationFailed`, `Other`, from SQLite's error code in `storage.rs`), `kerno_core::Error::Io` /
`Storage`, `NoDataDir`. The table of start failures:

| Failure | Headline (short) | Buttons | Start fresh |
|---|---|---|---|
| No home folder (`AppPaths::locate`) | "could not find your home folder" | Quit | no (nowhere to move) |
| Data folder or store file cannot be created, its mode set, opened (permissions, full disk, I/O) | "cannot read or write its data folder" | Open data folder, Quit | no (the rename fails too) |
| Store locked by another program (`SQLITE_BUSY`/`LOCKED` after rusqlite's 5 s) | "Another program is using Kerno's data file" | Open data folder, Quit | no |
| Not a database, corrupt | "damaged, or is not a Kerno data file" | all three | yes |
| Newer schema (a downgrade) | "written by a newer Kerno (data version 14); this is Kerno 0.1.0, which reads up to version 4" | all three | yes |
| Backup copy failed (no space, `backups` not writable, the copy does not check) | "did not update its data: the copy … could not be written" ("Your data is unchanged") | Open data folder, Quit | no (the store is healthy) |
| A migration failed (rolled back, one transaction) | "could not update its data; nothing was changed" | all three | yes (the copy is in `backups/`) |
| Another SQLite error while opening; a list of the store that cannot be read (`Error::Storage`) | "could not open its data file" / "could not read its saved data" | all three | yes |
| After the store opened: theme, fonts, the main window, the Tokio bridge | "Kerno could not start." | Open data folder, Quit | no |
| Settings that do not parse | not a failure: defaults (logged) | — | — |
| The keymap file | not a failure: notifications, as before | — | — |
| A second Kerno on the same data | not a failure (J4 hand-over first when there is a URL; without one it opens the same store, as before) | — | Start fresh hidden while one answers on the socket |
| No display / GPU (before `Application` runs) | no window possible: stderr and the log | — | — |

Start fresh (`storage::move_aside`) renames `kerno.db` with its `-wal`, `-shm` and `-journal` to
`kerno-moved-<UTC time>-schema<N>.sqlite3` (+ suffixes) in the data folder, `N` read through SQLite read-only
("unknown" when unreadable); a target that exists refuses; a failed rename undoes those done; the settings (in
the store) move with it; `known_hosts`, `folder-icons/`, `logs/`, `keymap.json` and the keychain stay. Then the
load runs again off the UI thread, `start()` opens the main window in the same run, the failure window closes,
and the main window says where the data went; a second failure updates the window and says where the data went.
Not offered while another Kerno answers on the data folder's socket, checked when the window opens and again at the
click. The window's restore advice names the three files (a `-wal` left beside a backup put back would be
applied to it). The copy before a migration
(`storage::copy_store`): `VACUUM INTO` an empty 0600 file in `backups/` (0700), the copy opened read-only and
checked (`user_version` and `quick_check`), else removed and the migration not run; only an existing store about
to migrate; the three newest kept by name, only names matching `kerno-<YYYYMMDD-HHMMSS-mmm>-schema<N>.sqlite3`
removed. The pending migrations now run in one transaction. `connections_and_unreadable` skips and counts a row
that does not parse (logged by id and position, never serde's message, which quotes values); the main window says
so once after start (`state::unreadable_notice`); nothing rewrites the row (updates by id, positions never
renumbered; an import with that id fails on the key). A failed settings save pushes one error notification
(`state::show_save_outcome`, id `SettingsNotSaved`, not hidden by itself), not pushed again while saves keep
failing (the kit would restart the toast), removed by the next save that works. D-080. Tests: `kerno-core`
`storage::tests::a_migration_copies_the_store_first`, `a_new_or_current_store_is_not_copied`,
`only_three_backups_are_kept`, `a_failed_copy_leaves_the_store_untouched`,
`a_failed_migration_leaves_the_store_as_it_was`, `a_copy_that_does_not_check_is_removed`,
`a_damaged_or_locked_store_says_which`, `an_unreadable_connection_row_is_skipped_not_fatal`, `starting_fresh_moves_the_store_aside`,
`starting_fresh_moves_every_file_or_none`, `backup_names_are_recognised_exactly`; app
`start_failure::tests::each_start_failure_says_what_happened_and_what_to_do`,
`state::tests::unreadable_connections_are_named`; UI
`critique::a_start_failure_opens_a_window_that_says_what_happened` (schema 999: texts, three buttons, Start fresh
clicked, store moved with its `-wal`/`-shm`, the moved one still holds its connection; the start after it is
recorded by a `cfg(test)` stand-in for `start()`, which would open the keychain, the socket thread and the Dock
icon), `critique::start_fresh_is_not_offered_while_another_kerno_has_the_data` (a Kerno started while the window is up: the
click moves nothing; one running already: not offered),
`critique::a_settings_save_failure_is_shown` (`Storage::refuse_writes`, `PRAGMA query_only` on that store only,
feature `test-support`). Seen failing with a part disabled: no copy (4 core tests), no pruning, one transaction
per migration, an unreadable row fatal, no undo of the renames, no check for a taken name, the copy not checked, a copy that
does not check left in `backups/`, the socket check ignored at open and at the click, Start fresh not moving, the notification pushed again at each failed save, never removed. Not covered by
a test: the logger starting first and the window opened from `main` (only `main` runs them), the push of the unreadable-row notification (in
`start()`; its wording, `unreadable_notice`, is tested). K8 does not cover: saved tasks are still read all or nothing
(`Storage::tasks`: one unreadable task fails the Tasks view and the export/import of tasks, not the start);
settings of the wrong type fall back to defaults and the next save overwrites them; a restored tab bound to a
hidden connection is not checked; history, saved queries, filters and layouts are read with typed columns and
fail per call, never at start. Plan errors: the backup name `kerno-schema<N>-<time>` does not sort by time
(`schema10` before `schema2`): the time comes first; "Start fresh" must move the `-wal` and `-shm` with the
store (a store in use keeps its recent writes in its `-wal`), under names SQLite still pairs; each
migration ran in its own transaction, so "the store stays untouched" did not hold for a failure after the first
pending one; the line numbers date from before K1-K7.

## K9 — The server's detail and hint on every error surface (C14)

**Now.** `impl Display for DbError` (`kerno-core/src/error.rs:38-46`) prints message and code only; detail and
hint reach the query tab (`query/tab.rs:5801-5825`) and maintenance (`maintenance_dialog.rs:460-468`) and are
lost everywhere else. Worst: navigator Drop shows the raw message alone (`navigator/view.rs:2203-2206`) while
PostgreSQL's hint holds the fix ("Use DROP … CASCADE to drop the dependent objects too.").

**Wanted.** One helper in `kerno-core/src/error.rs`: `pub fn describe(error: &Error) -> Described { message,
detail: Option<String>, hint: Option<String> }` (for `Error::Db` it takes the fields; for others, message
only), and one app-side renderer `error_lines(&Described) -> Vec<SharedString>` (message, then "Detail: …",
then "Hint: …"), used by:
- data tab apply failure (`data/tab.rs:1885-1887`): keep "Nothing was changed: statement N failed:" then the
  described lines (PostgreSQL's "Key (id)=(5) already exists." appears);
- structure editor apply (`structure/editor.rs:1044-1056`);
- navigator writes (`navigator/view.rs:2203-2206`, 1481) with a plain first line ("shop.orders was not
  dropped.") then the described lines; and node load failures (`view.rs:665`, `model.rs:1271`): "Could not
  read <kind>: …";
- transfer (`transfer_dialog.rs:213-218`: stop dropping code, detail and hint);
- COMMIT / ROLLBACK failure (`query/tab.rs:3260`): first line says the outcome: "The commit failed: the
  transaction was rolled back by the server; nothing it changed was saved." when the session reports `Idle`
  after the failure, "The commit failed; the transaction is still open." when `Open`/`Failed`;
- the secret-store failure at connect (`connections/manager.rs:928`, falls to `explain`'s generic "The
  connection failed." with no next step, `error.rs:640-646`): add an `explain` case "Kerno could not read the
  password from the keychain. Allow Kerno in the keychain prompt, or choose Ask when connecting in the
  connection's settings.";
- the task run's "connection did not open" (`tasks_view.rs:341-345`): use `explain` like the workspace does.

**Tests.** Core: `describe` keeps detail and hint (`error.rs` tests). UI (`critique.rs`, PostgreSQL):
`a_duplicate_key_on_apply_shows_the_server_detail` (insert a row whose key exists: the message contains
`already exists`); `dropping_a_table_with_dependents_shows_the_hint` (a view on a scratch table, Drop without
cascade: the notification contains `CASCADE`); `a_failed_commit_says_what_was_kept` (a deferred constraint
violated at commit).

**Acceptance.** No surface shows a driver message alone when the server gave a detail or a hint.

Done: `kerno_core::describe(&Error) -> Described { message, detail, hint }` (`error.rs`): the message is the
error's `Display` as before (the server's code in brackets after it: SQLSTATE `[23505]`, MySQL `[1062 (23000)]`),
a detail or hint that is empty, blank or the message again (with or without its code) is left out; every error
not from a server (`Cancelled`, `Closed`, `Io`, `Config`, `Storage`, …) gives its message alone, so K2's
"Cancelled: nothing was changed." and the lost-connection wording keep their own words. `Described`'s `Display`
is the one wording: the message, then "Detail: …", then "Hint: …", one per line; `lines()` gives them apart. No
app-side `error_lines` (D-081): GPUI draws a `\n` as a line break in any text, notifications included, so the
surfaces use the `Display`; the query tab's result panel draws `Failure::described().lines()` as before (message
in the danger colour). `Display` of `DbError` and `Error` is unchanged, so nothing new reaches the log (the quit
path's `ROLLBACK` failures log their first two lines, what was logged before). `crate::error_notification` makes
an error notification that stays until closed when it has more than one line, its first line as the title and
the rest in the selectable text of the connection-failure notification, to copy (D-081); `crate::advice_after` puts
Kerno's advice after the message, before the detail. The surfaces:

| Surface | Before | After |
|---|---|---|
| Query tab result error (panel, stacked results, AI "Explain error") | message [code], Detail, Hint, three copies of the code | the same through `Failure::described()`; nothing repeated |
| Data tab Apply; query tab result Apply (shared `data::tab::apply_failure_text`) | "Nothing was changed: statement N failed: msg [code]. The pending changes are kept." | "Nothing was changed: statement N failed. The pending changes are kept." / msg [code] / Detail / Hint |
| Data tab page read ("The rows could not be read") | msg [code] + advice | msg [code] + advice / Detail / Hint (a filter's "Perhaps you meant…") |
| Distinct values, row counts, isolation level (data and query tabs) | msg [code] | described, notification stays |
| Table form Apply, form load | "Statement N failed: msg [code]" / SQL | Detail / Hint before the SQL; load: advice then Detail / Hint |
| Structure tab: load, properties, DDL, Set comment | msg | described (in the DDL each line a `--` comment) |
| Navigator Drop / Rename / Create / Install / enum values | msg [code] | "The table “t” was not dropped." / msg / Detail / Hint, stays until closed; Cancel and "Cancelled: nothing was changed." unchanged |
| Navigator node loads | msg on the row | "Could not read the objects of shop: msg [code]" on the row, Detail / Hint in its tooltip (`node-failure`) |
| Navigator database list; templates, call, properties, DDL, source | msg | described (list: first line in the detail row, all of it in its tooltip) |
| COMMIT / ROLLBACK (Commit, Roll back, the idle rule, the end questions) | "COMMIT failed: msg [code]" | the outcome first, from the session's `TxState` after the failure (`query::tab::ending_failed`), then msg / Detail / Hint; several tabs one per line, "Nothing else was done." last |
| Transfer (lists, notification, report, log) | `db.message` only: code, detail, hint dropped | msg [code], Detail and Hint on lines of their own (report: indented) |
| CSV import (refused record, report, column read) | `db.message` only, "Nothing was imported: …." | "Nothing was imported." first, then described |
| Export | "The export failed: msg. No file was written." | "The export failed. No file was written." then described |
| Backup / restore wizards (objects, databases, connection's lists) | msg | described (its own `error_text`) |
| Maintenance tools | detail and hint appended unlabelled, on one line | labelled lines |
| FDW dialog, admin tab (panes, cancel / terminate), ER diagram, quick open, AI sample rows | msg | described (FDW: password still masked) |
| Create database dialog | "(msg)" mid-sentence | plain line, then described |
| Connect, the secret store refuses the password | "The connection failed.", no next step | `explain`'s new `FailureKind::SecretStore` |
| A task run's connection did not open | raw message | `explain`'s headline and next step (the raw message is in the window's own notification) |
| Multi-run row, ER "N tables could not be read", data tab read-only reason (`ReadOnly::Unread`) | one-line summaries | unchanged: the full error is in the target's tab or the table's own view |
| Connection failures (test, sidebar, notification), bootstrap statements | `explain` on the message | unchanged (owner's test item 5): the drivers' connect errors carry message and code only |
| Files, storage, keymap, updates, backup and restore tools, SQL the dialect writes | — | unchanged: no server error reaches them |

The secret store at connect: `secrets.rs`'s `secret_error` text, never a secret, starts with `secrets: `;
`explain` reads three cases: the prompt answered Deny or a wrong login password (`User canceled`, `error code
-128`, `passphrase you entered is not correct`, `error code -25293`): "Kerno could not read the saved password: the
keychain prompt was refused." / "Connect again and choose Allow in the keychain prompt, or set Password to “Ask
each time” in the connection's settings."; keyring's `NoStorageAccess` or "interaction is not allowed" (a locked
keychain): "… the keychain is locked or cannot be used." / "Unlock the keychain, then connect again, or …";
anything else: the plan's words. "keychain" on macOS, "secret store" elsewhere. On Linux without a Secret
Service the store falls back to memory at start (`default_store`), so no read fails at connect: the password is
asked. COMMIT / ROLLBACK: PostgreSQL's driver marks a failed `COMMIT` idle (the server rolled the block back), MySQL
pings after a failure and reads the server's in-transaction flag; a `COMMIT` cut by a lost connection says
whether it was saved is not known; K4's badge and K5's question read the same `tx_state()`, and the transaction's
log is cleared when the session is idle after the failure. Tests: `kerno-core`
`error::tests::describe_keeps_the_detail_and_the_hint`, `error::tests::a_secret_store_failure_names_the_next_step`;
UI `critique::a_duplicate_key_on_apply_shows_the_server_detail` (PostgreSQL: "Detail: Key (id)=(1) already
exists."; MySQL: message and `[1062 (23000)]` kept, no Detail line), `critique::dropping_a_table_with_dependents_shows_the_hint`
(PostgreSQL), `critique::a_failed_commit_says_what_was_kept` (PostgreSQL, deferred unique constraint: rolled back,
detail shown, `Idle`, no badge, the row absent), `critique::a_failed_end_of_transaction_says_its_outcome` (unit:
every outcome), `critique::a_refused_keychain_read_says_what_to_do` (a refusing store, no container). Seen
failing with a part disabled: the apply without `describe`, the navigator write without `describe`, the commit's
state read as `Open`, the commit's text without `describe`, `explain` without the secret-store case. Not covered:
a MySQL commit failure in a UI test (InnoDB gives no practical way to make `COMMIT` fail; the outcome function is
unit-tested for both engines' states); MySQL drops a table under a view without error, so the drop test has no
MySQL twin; the detail of a connect-time server error (the drivers keep message and code); the secret store
read when opening a connection's settings (sidebar Edit: "The connection's secrets could not be read: …",
unchanged); the one-line summaries above; the tooltip of a failed node is not hovered in a test. Plan errors:
"choose Ask when connecting" is "Ask each time" in the form; a node label is the bare name (`t`, not
`shop.orders`), so the plain line names the kind and the name as the confirmation does; the transfer's and the
import's core (`transfer.rs::message`, `import.rs::refused`) dropped code, detail and hint too, not only
`transfer_dialog.rs`; the structure editor, maintenance, FDW, backup and export surfaces were not listed; the line
numbers date from before K1-K8.

## K10 — Pin what works but nothing tests (C1, C2, C3, C6, C21)

Small tests for behaviour the audit found correct but unguarded:
- C1, C2: `scripts/bench.sh` exits 1 when a median misses its target (`bench.sh:413` prints MISSED and
  carries on today), after printing the whole table; the owner's idle-memory margin decision stays separate.
- C2: `complete::tests::a_large_catalog_and_a_long_statement_stay_fast` asserts a loose bound (each call under
  20 ms in a debug build: the audit measured 8-26 µs on 1,200 × 20, so the bound only catches an algorithmic
  regression, not machine noise).
- C3: `fetch_all_is_cancelled_on_the_server` (query tab, `generate_series` large enough, wait for the marker
  on the server, cancel, assert gone); `an_explain_analyze_is_cancelled`.
- C6: `renaming_a_connection_renames_its_tabs_picker`.
- C21: `explain_analyze_of_a_delete_without_where_asks` (local, writable); `a_local_target_of_a_multi_run_asks_before_a_delete_without_where`;
  `a_data_apply_on_production_asks_twice`.

Done: no product code changed. `scripts/bench.sh`: the table is printed by a `report` function, which exits 1
after the whole table when a median misses its target (`set -e` makes it the script's status, the scratch still
removed by the trap); `scripts/bench.sh --report FILE` prints the table of a raw file and measures nothing; a run
keeps its raw lines in `target/bench-raw.tsv`. Tested on three hand-written raw files in the real line shape:
all met → 0, the start-up at 450 ms → 1, `docs/BENCH.md`'s last numbers → 1, the table printed each time; with
the line that records a miss removed, the second gives 0. **On the last recorded numbers the bench now fails**:
it measures the bare binary, 152.1 MB idle for a 150 MB target (the bundled app, 149.9 MB, is measured by hand);
no target was changed or exempted, the owner's idle-memory decision stays separate (D-082). Not covered: a
measure whose every run errored has no row and does not fail the run (its "error in …" lines are printed). C2:
`complete::tests::a_large_catalog_and_a_long_statement_stay_fast` asserts under 20 ms a call (the average of
200), measured 0.3 to 1.9 ms in a debug build at a load of 5 to 11; a scan of the catalog per candidate in
`Sink::schema` gives 45 ms and fails. UI tests (`ui_tests::critique`), each seen failing with a one-line
mutation: `fetch_all_is_cancelled_on_the_server` (PostgreSQL, MySQL: first page, Fetch all, the statement seen
on the server, Cmd+., gone from the server, the rows kept as "N rows, incomplete" then "cancelled", the same tab
runs the next statement; fails with `fetch` not marking the tab running, and with `QueryTab::cancel` not calling
`session.cancel()`), `an_explain_analyze_is_cancelled` (PostgreSQL, MySQL, MariaDB; fails with the same
`session.cancel()` line removed: no cancel is particular to a plan), `renaming_a_connection_renames_its_tabs_picker`
(the sidebar's Rename…, then the tab's picker menu, the main toolbar's two pickers and the status bar show the
new name and not the old; fails with `Connections::rename_connection` not storing the name: every place reads
the name live, so no mutation of a view alone makes it fail; it is a smoke check of that chain),
`explain_analyze_of_a_delete_without_where_asks` (three engines; fails with `QueryTab::execute` classifying the
plan without `ANALYZE`), `a_local_target_of_a_multi_run_asks_before_a_delete_without_where` (local PostgreSQL
and MySQL targets asked one at a time, Cancel skips the first and leaves its two rows, the second deletes; fails
with `ask_destructive` off for a script target), `a_data_apply_on_production_asks_twice` (PostgreSQL, MySQL: the
first confirmation turns the button into "Run on Production", Cancel there keeps the row and the edit, two
confirmations apply; fails with `DataTab::review` passing `writes: false`). Taken from what K1-K9 left read,
not run: `the_read_only_toggle_keeps_a_tab_where_it_was_moved` (K3, PostgreSQL and MySQL: after `SET search_path`
/ `USE`, the palette's read-only toggle rebuilds the session, the new one is where the tab was moved and refuses a
write; fails with `follow_session` not moving the target), `a_failed_table_form_apply_shows_the_server_hint`
(K9, PostgreSQL: a text column changed to `int`, the form shows `[42804]` and "Hint: You might need to specify
"USING v::integer"."; fails with the form's message built from the error's `Display`). Left, with the reason: a
MariaDB twin of K7's test (MariaDB 11 runs a `/*!90000 … */` comment, so it tests nothing new); the age of a
transaction begun by applying a result's edits (K4, a result edit flow for one timestamp); the logger starting
first and the window opened from `main` (K8, only `main` runs them); the tooltip of a failed node (K9) and the
tab's own commit-mode menu entry (K5), which need a hover or a click on a menu built by the kit; the Dock's Quit
(K1, no hook). Plan errors: the audit's 8-26 µs a completion call is about a hundred times less than this test
measures (2,000 tables and a 2 kB statement); a cancelled "Fetch all" keeps two results, the rows then the
server's cancellation; MySQL may send the plan of what ran before a killed `EXPLAIN ANALYZE`, then "cancelled";
the tab's connection button exposes no text to the tests, so the test reads its menu. Found while writing them:
waiting for "a statement whose text holds the marker" also matched another test's scratch statements and old
statements of a failed run (a MySQL `SLEEP` runs on after its client is gone), so the cancel could leave before
the tab's own statement reached the server and change nothing; the markers are now per test process and the
slow statements end within a minute. Whether a cancel pressed before the statement reaches the server is lost in
the app is not pinned.

## Below the cut (next milestone)

### K11 — Completion knows the columns of CTEs, subqueries and `LATERAL` (C8, L)

Failing inputs (the audit's probes; each should offer the listed columns, both rule sets unless noted):
`WITH x AS (SELECT id, name FROM t) SELECT x.| FROM x` → id, name; `WITH x(a, b) AS (SELECT id, name FROM t)
SELECT x.| FROM x` → a, b; `WITH x AS (SELECT id, name FROM t), y AS (SELECT name FROM x) SELECT y.| FROM y` →
name; `SELECT s.| FROM (SELECT id AS k FROM t) s` → k; PostgreSQL `SELECT l.| FROM t JOIN LATERAL (SELECT uid
AS lid FROM u WHERE u.uid = t.id) l ON true` → lid; `DELETE FROM t USING u x WHERE x.|` → u's columns.
Causes: `cte_names` keeps names only (`complete.rs:1030`; the column list is skipped at 1047-1048), a derived
table is marked unknown (`complete.rs:1164-1166`), `USING` is missing from the FROM keywords
(`complete.rs:1115-1122`). Approach: give a CTE or a derived table a column list computed from its own select
list (output names: alias, else bare column name, else nothing for an expression; `*` expands through the
catalog of the inner FROM), recursively, bounded by depth. `complete::tests::columns_of_ctes_and_subqueries_are_unknown`
becomes `columns_of_ctes_and_subqueries_are_known`. Keep the C2 bound of K10 green.

Done: a relation of the statement carries either its catalog entry or the output columns of the CTE or
subquery it is (`Relation::derived`, `Out` in `complete.rs`), computed on the same tolerant tokens: `cte_names`
became `with_clause`, which computes each CTE's columns in list order with the ones before it in scope and
pushes them on one stack (outermost `WITH` first, an inner one pushed later and found first, truncated back
after a nested query); `query_columns` reads a `WITH`, a parenthesised first branch, the select list of the
first branch of a `UNION`, or the `RETURNING` list of an `INSERT` / `UPDATE` / `DELETE`, over its own FROM
scope (`collect`, unchanged but for its CTE stack and a depth budget); `select_list` gives each item its output
name (alias, bare or qualified column, `*` and `t.*` expanded, nothing for an expression, PostgreSQL folding
unquoted names) and the type of a catalog column passed through; `rename` applies an explicit column list by
position. `table_reference` fills the columns of `(subquery) alias [(a, b)]`, `LATERAL` ones included, of a CTE
reference, and of `f() AS g(a, b)`; `USING` after `DELETE` or `MERGE` (not followed by `(`) is a FROM list;
`offer_qualified` looks a name up among the CTEs before the schemas, so `WITH x AS (…) SELECT x.` without a
FROM yet offers the CTE's columns; `scope_columns` offers derived columns unqualified with the same ranking and
qualification of shared names as a table's. Matching parentheses are computed once per call (`Query::close`);
the depth bound is eight queries (`DEPTH`). Scope rules and output names: D-083. Every probe above passes on
