both rule sets (`LATERAL` too: MySQL 8.0.14 has it), with the text cut at the cursor, with text after it, and
with an unfinished inner select (what is named is offered, an expression item is not). MySQL's multi-table
`DELETE t FROM t JOIN u x … WHERE x.` and `UPDATE t JOIN u x … SET x.` already worked (checked before the
change) and are pinned. Tests: `complete::tests::{columns_of_ctes_and_subqueries_are_known,
a_derived_relation_has_the_output_names_of_its_select_list, unfinished_ctes_offer_what_is_known,
ctes_and_derived_tables_follow_the_scope_rules, derived_columns_are_offered_unqualified,
delete_using_and_multi_table_statements_name_their_relations}`, a ten-chained-CTE input in
`a_large_catalog_and_a_long_statement_stay_fast` (0.34-0.80 ms a call before, 0.34-0.77 ms after, the chain
0.40 ms; debug build, load 4 to 5), `sql_hard_inputs::{c8_cte_columns, c8_cte_column_list, c8_cte_on_a_cte, c8_subquery_alias,
c8_join_lateral_pg, c8_delete_using_aliased}`, all seen failing before the change; UI:
`critique::{a_cte_typed_in_the_editor_offers_its_columns, a_cte_typed_in_a_mysql_editor_offers_its_columns}`
(typed, the editor's list, Enter; both fail with the CTEs not computed).
Not covered: engine-made names (`count`, `?column?`, MySQL's expression text) are not offered on purpose; a
column alias list on a real table (`FROM t AS x(a, b)`, PostgreSQL) still offers nothing for `x`; a function in
FROM without a column list (`generate_series(1, 3) g`) offers nothing (PostgreSQL names its column `g`); a
`VALUES` without a column list offers nothing (`column1`, `column_0`); `SELECT … IS DISTINCT FROM …` in a select
list ends the list at that `FROM`; a cursor inside a data-modifying CTE's body (`WITH d AS (DELETE … WHERE |)`)
is not a query block of its own, as before; MariaDB was not probed apart from the MySQL rules it shares.
Where the plan was wrong: the line numbers; `USING` as a FROM keyword alone would have made `btree` a
relation in `CREATE INDEX … USING btree (…) WHERE …`, so it counts only after `DELETE` or `MERGE` (pinned, with
`MERGE INTO … USING u x … AND x.`). Seen while writing the UI test, not explained: in the app opened second in
one test context, Enter accepted the item and then also wrote a newline, so the test is one per engine.

### K12 — `BEGIN ATOMIC` and MySQL `BEGIN … END` bodies stay one statement (C9, M)

Failing: `CREATE FUNCTION f() RETURNS int LANGUAGE sql BEGIN ATOMIC SELECT 1; SELECT 2; END; SELECT 3;` →
2 statements (today 4); MySQL `CREATE PROCEDURE p() BEGIN SELECT 1; SELECT 2; END; SELECT 3;` without
`DELIMITER` → 2 (today 4; the `mysql` client also splits it, DBeaver keeps the block); statement at cursor on
`SELECT 2` inside either returns the whole `CREATE`. In `split.rs:75-110`, track a block depth: PostgreSQL
`BEGIN ATOMIC` … `END` after `CREATE [OR REPLACE] FUNCTION|PROCEDURE`; MySQL, after `CREATE … PROCEDURE |
FUNCTION | TRIGGER | EVENT`, `BEGIN` opens and `END` closes (not `END IF`, `END LOOP`, `END WHILE`, `END
REPEAT`, `END CASE`), labels (`lbl: BEGIN`) included. With `DELIMITER` set, unchanged. Completion uses the
same split (`complete.rs:248`) and inside a MySQL body treats `;` as a boundary on purpose: keep that.

Done: `split_statements` tracks blocks in its one pass while the separator is `;` (`Blocks` in `split.rs`; under
a `DELIMITER` the delimiter alone decides, as before), rules in D-084. A block opens only in a statement that
starts `CREATE [OR REPLACE] [DEFINER = user] [AGGREGATE] PROCEDURE | FUNCTION | TRIGGER | EVENT` (`routine_header`,
a few tokens read ahead), or on MySQL rules with `BEGIN NOT ATOMIC` (MariaDB's anonymous block, supported: today
its inner statements after the first ran on their own). PostgreSQL: at `BEGIN ATOMIC`, once. MySQL and MariaDB:
at a `BEGIN` outside parentheses, nested ones and `lbl:BEGIN` included, unless it reads as a name (after a
punctuation other than `)` or `:`, after `SELECT`, `RETURN`, `DECLARE`, `AS`, `ON`, `WHERE` and a few more, or
before a punctuation or `;`). `END` closes when it follows a `;` or an empty block's `BEGIN` / `ATOMIC` and is not
`END IF | LOOP | WHILE | REPEAT | CASE | FOR`: every statement of a body ends with `;` in both grammars, while a
`CASE … END` expression's `END` and `end` as a name (`SELECT start, end`, legal on MySQL) follow something else,
so `CASE` needs no tracking (counting it, with any `END` closing, would close the block at `start, end`).
After the final `END` the statement ends at the next `;` (text between stays in it; the server refuses it on
PostgreSQL); the body never reopens. A body never closed runs to the end of the text as one statement: nothing
after it runs on its own (never an inner `DELETE` without its routine); run script or run at cursor sends it and
the server's syntax error ("syntax error at end of input", MySQL's "near ''", seen on the containers) shows on
that one result. With the cursor on a later, unrelated `SELECT 3` after a `BEGIN` never closed, run at cursor
sends the whole unfinished `CREATE` with that `SELECT 3` in it, and the error is about a statement the user did not
point at: the result shows that statement's text, which starts at the `CREATE`. Timing (`a_long_script_splits_in_linear_time`, 20,000 statements, debug build, load 4 to 7):
about 14 ms before, 14.3 to 15.5 ms after; a never-closed 20,000-line body, 43 to 50 ms. Other users of the
split: completion cuts at `;` inside a statement already, so a body without `DELIMITER` completes statement by
statement as under one (pinned on both engines: an alias of the inner statement before is not offered, `UPD`,
the keywords after `;`, K11's CTE columns as a later inner statement); `classify` already took a `CREATE` with a
body as a write, not destructive, without rows (pinned for `BEGIN ATOMIC`), and now classifies `BEGIN NOT ATOMIC
…` as `Other`, a write, destructive as its statements are, instead of a transaction `BEGIN` (it showed a
transaction that was not there); the destructive question therefore no longer comes for a `DELETE` inside a body
typed without `DELIMITER` (it is stored, not run, as the UI test checks on the server); parameters are found in
the whole `CREATE` as they were found in its pieces (unchanged); the formatter does not use the split; a run on
several connections runs in query tabs (same split); saved script tasks run the engine's own client (unchanged);
`browse::Query::new` refuses a body as "only the result of one SELECT" instead of "several statements"; the
connection's init SQL splits the same way; the MySQL driver's `DELIMITER`-wrapped sources and templates are
unchanged (their tests green). The `mysql` client does split such a body (checked on `kerno-mysql`: it sends
`CREATE PROCEDURE … BEGIN SELECT 1` and the server refuses it), the server takes the whole text as one statement
(text protocol, both drivers); `psql` 17 keeps a `BEGIN ATOMIC` body whole (checked on `kerno-postgres`). The
drivers' `touches_session_state` scans (re-read the current database or schema after a statement that may move
it) now see a body's inner `SET` or `END` and re-read once more: harmless, the body is stored, not run. Tests: `kerno-core` `split::tests::{begin_atomic_bodies_stay_one_statement,
mysql_compound_bodies_stay_one_statement, a_delimiter_still_decides_and_dumps_are_unchanged,
an_unfinished_body_holds_the_rest_of_the_text, statement_at_cursor_inside_a_body}`,
`complete::tests::bodies_without_a_delimiter_complete_statement_by_statement`, three rows of
`classify::tests::statements_are_classified`; `sql_hard_inputs::{c9_begin_atomic_pg,
c9_mysql_procedure_without_delimiter_my, c9_at_cursor_inside_begin_atomic_pg,
c9_at_cursor_inside_mysql_procedure_my, c9_bare_begin_is_a_transaction}`; UI:
`critique::{a_begin_atomic_function_typed_without_delimiter_is_one_statement,
a_mysql_procedure_typed_without_delimiter_is_one_statement,
a_mariadb_procedure_typed_without_delimiter_is_one_statement}` (typed on several lines, run as a script: `done`
then `1 rows`, nothing asked, the scratch row still there, the routine's definition on the server holding both
statements; dropped, then run from the cursor on a line of the body: the whole `CREATE` again), each run five
times. Seen failing before the change: the four split tests and four `sql_hard_inputs` probes that hold a body
(the same inputs gave four statements, or `SELECT 2` alone at the cursor), the two `BEGIN NOT ATOMIC` classify
rows, and the three UI tests (the inner `DELETE` asked for confirmation; with the tracking disabled by a one-line
mutation). Not covered: a routine body that is a bare `IF`, `CASE`, `LOOP`, `WHILE` or `REPEAT` without `BEGIN`
(`… FOR EACH ROW IF … END IF;`) still splits at its inner `;`; MariaDB's top-level `IF … END IF` and the other
compound statements outside `BEGIN NOT ATOMIC`, a labelled `lbl: BEGIN NOT ATOMIC`, and `CREATE PACKAGE`
(Oracle mode) are not tracked; `begin` as an unquoted name in a body after a word not in the list (an alias `FROM t
begin`, `THEN begin`) opens a block and swallows the rest; a body whose last statement lacks its `;` is never
closed; a PostgreSQL `$1` inside a `BEGIN ATOMIC` body (the function's own argument) is still prompted for as a
parameter, as it was; completion does not see a CTE written as the first statement of a body, right after the
header (as under `DELIMITER`, before and after); a MySQL routine written without `DELIMITER` runs in a query tab
but not as a saved script task or "Execute script", which hand the file to the `mysql` client, which splits it. Where the plan was wrong: the line numbers; its "`END` closes" would
also close at the `END` of a `CASE … END` expression and at `end` used as a MySQL name: an `END` closes only after a
`;`; the plan named no `classify` change, but `BEGIN NOT ATOMIC` was a transaction to it.

### K13 — Search in Settings, what overrides what, every menu entry in the palette (C4, C5, C20, M)

A search field at the top of Settings filtering sections and rows by label and keywords (each toggle gets
keywords); one line under each setting that an environment or a connection can override ("Production asks
twice whatever this says", "A connection's read-only flag wins"); the connection dialog's environment field
shows the rules in force ("Production: asks before every statement, twice before writes, read-only by
default, rolls back idle transactions after 10 min"). A test that walks every context menu's builder and the
toolbar's items and checks each action has a `COMMANDS` entry.

Done: **Search.** One declaration per row, `settings_view::ROWS` (kind, section, element id, label, description,
keywords), read by the view (`render_row` draws a row from its kind, with its label, description and override
line; a section is its rows in order) and by `search` (each word typed must start a word of the section's name,
the label, the description or a keyword; the environments' row also reads `environments_view::RULE_LABELS`, the
rule labels the form draws). 25 rows cover every section; Environments, Local clients and AI are one row each,
drawn as their view, and in the results a line with Open that shows the section. The field sits above the tab
bar, Settings opens with the keyboard in it, the body keeps its 400 px (nothing moves while rows hide), the
results are grouped under their section's name and each row works where it is found; "No setting matches “…”"
is the kit's `Empty` with Clear search; Escape empties a search holding text (`clean_on_escape` switched on only
then) and closes Settings on an empty one; ⌘F is the command `search_settings` ("Find a Setting…", palette and
Kerno menu) bound in a new `Settings` key context, carried by `RunCommand` in the menu bar so ⌘F elsewhere keeps
its meaning (D-085). The muted lines are `muted_foreground` on the dialog's `background`, already in
`theme::tests::text_contrast_meets_wcag_aa` for both themes. **What wins** (one shape, "<who> wins: <what>"):

| Setting | Line | What makes it true | Pinned by |
|---|---|---|---|
| Confirmations ▸ Ask before running … without a WHERE | computed from the environments as they stand: "Production wins: its rules ask anyway. With this off, they run without asking in Local and Staging." (with a user's environment that asks: "Staging and Production win: …"; none: "No environment wins: with this off, these statements run without asking."); its description says it covers query-tab runs (statement, selection, script, EXPLAIN ANALYZE, several connections) and that the tree's drop and truncate and the grid's and form's applies always show their SQL | `QueryTab::execute`: `ask_all = from_editor && confirm_execute`, `confirm_writes_twice && class.writes`; `confirm_destructive` is read nowhere else | `settings_view::tests::the_destructive_confirmation_says_which_environments_win`; UI `critique::with_the_confirmation_off_production_still_asks_twice`, `critique::with_the_confirmation_off_local_runs_without_asking` (PostgreSQL) |
| Ask before closing a tab … | "An open transaction wins: closing its tab always asks." (moved out of the label) | `tabs::close_question` (`confirm \|\| loss.is_transaction()`) | `tabs::tests::closing_asks_once_for_what_would_be_lost` |
| Grid ▸ Show the results of a script one under the other | "A result's “Open in its own tab” wins for its query tab, until the next run." | `QueryTab::stacked` (`stack_results && !unstacked`), `unstack`; `unstacked` is reset when the next run starts | `tabs_v11::the_results_of_a_script_stack_in_one_panel_on_every_engine` pins the unstacking; "until the next run" is read from the code, not tested |
| Updates ▸ Check for updates | "KERNO_NO_UPDATE_CHECK wins: when it is set, no check runs, not even from the Help menu." (moved out of the description) | `update::switched_off`; `workspace/updates.rs` returns before any request, manual or not | `update::tests::the_environment_switches_and_a_test_build_make_no_updater` |
| Local clients (its view and its search row) | "A connection's own client wins: its Advanced tab, or “Local client…” in a wizard." | `tools::client_for` (the connection's folder before the default; the dialog adds a folder it picks to the list) | `tools::tests::clients_are_detected_once_per_real_folder` |
| Environments ▸ Auto-commit by default | "A connection's own commit mode wins over this default (its Advanced tab)." | `ConnectionConfig::starts_in_manual_commit` | `config::tests::the_commit_mode_follows_the_environment_unless_chosen` |
| Environments ▸ Confirm every statement, Ask a second time | "This rule wins over Settings ▸ Confirmations: it asks even when that is off." | `QueryTab::execute`; `sql_confirm::ConfirmView::new` (`twice`) | UI `critique::with_the_confirmation_off_production_still_asks_twice` |
| Environments ▸ New connections start read-only | "A connection's Read-only switch wins: this rule only presets it on new ones." | the connection dialog sets the switch from the rule only for a new connection (`editing.is_none()`); writes are refused on `config.read_only` alone | `environments::a_read_only_environment_makes_new_connections_read_only` |

No line where nothing overrides (theme, double click, tab layout, fonts, editor, formatter, page size, NULL
text, time zone, AI: the zoom commands, the View menu and the editor menu change the same setting), pinned by
`settings_view::tests::only_what_can_be_overridden_has_a_line`. Two descriptions were made true while there: the
page size ("the next query result and the next table opened use it; a table already open keeps its size",
`data/tab.rs` reads it once) and the idle rule ("in either commit mode", K5's `idle_period`; it said "in a
manual-commit tab"). **Connection dialog**: under "Environment" (with **Edit environments…**, which opens
Settings on Environments) the chosen environment's rules beside its chips, from `environment::describe`:
"Local: no rule of its own; only Settings ▸ Confirmations asks." (Staging the same), "Production: asks before
every statement run from an editor, twice before any write, starts query tabs in manual commit, rolls back a
transaction left idle for 10 min.", a user's ("QA: asks twice before any write, starts new connections
read-only, rolls back a transaction left idle for 2 min."); it follows the pick and an edit at once. Colour moved
beside Folder so the line takes no row: the General tab was 10 px from the window's bottom in an 800 px window
with the RDS preset (`password_sources::the_rds_preset_connects_with_what_aws_prints_and_says_so` failed on a
first layout). No other place described an environment's rules. **Menus and the palette**: `menu_palette.rs` walks
the data-built menus (tab, tab bar, editor with and without a source, the toolbar's Run list, result tab, every
status bar item, the sidebar's empty area, folder and connection with Tools ▸ and Save connection, the grid's
cell, row number and header menus on a table and a result, Tasks, the toolbar's buttons and its Tools ▸ on both
dialects) and classifies each entry by an exhaustive `match` per kind (per menu where one entry means two things:
Close of a tab and of a result); the tree's node, Filter ▸ and Tools ▸ entries by the same `match` over every
variant (a hand-written list of the variants: a new one is forced into the `match` but its id is checked only once
it is added to the list); the editor's AI entries by their action. 51 commands added, each one line in `workspace.rs`
(`on_tree`, the new `on_subject` / `on_subject_of_list`, `on_grid`, `perform_on_active_result`) and in the menu
bar: tree `open_object_data`, `open_object_structure`, `show_object_ddl`, `edit_object_source`,
`generate_select|insert|update|delete`, `drop_object_cascade`, `create_table|view|schema|database|function|
procedure|sequence|trigger|event|enum`, `add_enum_value`, `rename_enum_value`, `install_extension`,
`export_object`, `import_csv_into_object`, `rename_column`, `drop_column`, `reveal_referenced_table`; result tabs
`close_other_result_tabs`, `close_result_tabs_to_the_left|right`, `close_all_result_tabs`,
`unpin_all_result_tabs`, `rename_result_tab`, `copy_result_statement` (its arm in `perform_result` now copies nothing for a result without a statement, as
its menu greys it); grid `copy_column_name`, `revert_selected_rows`, `show_all_columns`,
`fit_all_column_widths`, `generate_select_by_key|select_in|row_insert|row_update|row_delete`; sidebar
`new_connection_in_folder`, `new_subfolder`, `disconnect_folder`, `paste_connection`, `copy_connection_url`,
`copy_connection_host`; `apply_source`, `show_messages`. Exceptions, each with its reason in the test: the text
field's Undo/Redo/Cut/Copy/Paste/Select all and the grid's Copy/Paste/Copy as TSV (the system's keys); one entry
per connection, database, schema, folder, filter operator or foreign key (lists); the column or value under the
pointer (Order, Filter on column, Pin, Hide, Fit width, Edit cell in place); a status bar item's own text and
"What … means"; the update item's answers (Update, release page, Skip; Turn off is Settings ▸ Updates); a task's
entries (the Tasks view has no selection); the toolbar's connection and database pickers. Left for later, named in
the test's expected list: "Run again" of a result (its menu offers it only on an incomplete result; `run_again`
refuses a write itself) and the grid's "Filter by this value" / "Exclude this value" (their menu greys them on a
binary or JSON value, which the owner's filter does not refuse: commands for them were written, then taken out).
Each new command was checked against what its menu greys: the tree's go through `run_entry` (applicability and
`unavailable`), the grid's through the owner's edit guards (`editing_grid`, read-only) and `rows_sql` (no key, not a
table's rows: refused with a notification), the result tabs' bulk closes skip pinned ones and do nothing when
there is nothing to close, the sidebar's through `Sidebar::perform`'s subject match. Not walked, named in the file: the ER diagram's table and background menus (fit, zoom,
export, refresh would make sense as commands), the administration grid's cell menu, a table form's column row
menu, the Tasks view's New task menu, the query tab's connection picker and the toolbar's lists. Sidebar Connect
maps to `open_connection` (the selected connection); Disconnect, Reconnect and Disconnect others map to the active
tab's connection commands, which act on the selected connection only without a tab on another one. Tests:
`kerno-core` `environment::tests::the_rules_are_described_in_one_line`; `settings_view::tests::{
settings_are_found_by_label_description_and_keywords, nothing_typed_shows_everything_and_nothing_found_is_said,
every_row_is_declared_once_and_found_by_its_label, the_destructive_confirmation_says_which_environments_win,
only_what_can_be_overridden_has_a_line}`; `menu_palette::{every_menu_entry_and_toolbar_button_is_in_the_palette,
every_entry_of_the_schema_tree_is_in_the_palette, the_ai_entries_of_the_editor_menu_are_commands}`; UI
`critique::{typing_in_the_settings_search_filters_the_rows_and_clearing_restores_them,
the_connection_dialog_says_the_rules_of_its_environment, with_the_confirmation_off_production_still_asks_twice,
with_the_confirmation_off_local_runs_without_asking}`, each run five times. Seen failing under a one-line
mutation: the search test with `set_clean_on_escape(false)` (Escape closed Settings) and with `search("")` in the
body (nothing filtered), and with the destructive line's arm moved to `Kind::Theme`; the production test with
`execute` reading local's rules; the local test with `ask_destructive = true`; the dialog test describing local's
rules whatever the pick; the menu test with one command id misspelt (`run_script`), and a `TabAction::Probe`
added to the tab bar's menu does not compile (the `match` in `menu_palette.rs`). Tab from the search reaches the first
result and Space ticks it (in the UI test); the arrows inside a radio group were not checked. Not covered: the search does not look inside
the sub-views' own fields (AI's endpoint, a folder's path in Local clients) beyond their keywords; the menus
built from closures above. Not changed, for the owner: with "Ask before running …" off, Local and Staging run a
`DELETE` without `WHERE` silently (Staging has Local's rules), now said on the setting; a confirmation setting for
the tree and grid does not exist (they always ask); the sidebar's Disconnect / Reconnect commands act on the
active tab's connection first. Where the plan was wrong: its example lines ("Production … read-only by default"
is false, Production does not start read-only; "No rule: statements run without asking" is false while the
confirmation setting is on; "A connection's read-only flag wins" is right, but the rule only presets it); the
connection dialog had no room for a line of its own; "each action has a `COMMANDS` entry" was not true of the 51
entries that became commands, nor of the exceptions above.

### K14 — "Also dropped" list before `DROP … CASCADE` (C11, M)

PostgreSQL: from `pg_depend` (written from the docs: `pg_depend` + `pg_class`/`pg_proc`/`pg_type`,
`deptype in ('n','a')`), list what a cascade would drop, in the confirmation dialog, before the user
confirms. MySQL has no cascade: show the foreign keys that point at the table. A rename shows the views and
routines whose text names the object (a text search, labelled as such). Smart rename of an alias or CTE in
the editor stays on the roadmap.

Done: one driver method, `Session::dependents(&Change) -> Result<Vec<Dependent>>` (`kerno-core/src/driver.rs`;
`Change`, `Dependent`, `Tie`, `like_containing`, `mentions` in `schema.rs`), default `Error::Unsupported` so that
"not read" never shows as "nothing" (D-086). **PostgreSQL** (`introspect.rs` `dependents`, `cascade_of`,
`target_sql`): the target is found by name (a routine by its identity arguments, as the tree names it; a mixed-case
table too), a missing one is an error; a recursive query over `pg_depend` follows every dependent at any depth and
goes up from a part to its owner (`'i'`, `'e'`), and lists what was reached only through normal dependencies or
that way up, which is the server's own "drop cascades to" rule: a table's indexes, own constraints and triggers,
owned sequences, partitions and an extension's members are not listed, a column is not listed apart from its
table, a foreign key of another table is listed as the constraint. Kind and qualified name from
`pg_identify_object` ("foreign key child_t_fk on shop.child", "column shop.holder.row_of", "default value for
shop.t.n"). The same list on a plain Drop, under "The server refuses the drop while these depend on it";
`TRUNCATE … CASCADE` lists the tables it empties too (recursively by foreign key); a rename lists the function and
procedure bodies kept as text (PL/pgSQL, quoted SQL) that name the object, in the current database. **MySQL,
MariaDB** (`introspect.rs` `dependents`, `views_over`, `searched`): a table's plain Drop lists the foreign keys of
other tables that point at it (a key to itself excluded) and the views over it (MySQL `VIEW_TABLE_USAGE`, with
views over views; MariaDB: its view definitions searched, said to be a search); a rename of a table, view,
sequence, event or index lists the views, routines, triggers and events of every non-system database that name
it. Text searches: `LIKE … ESCAPE '!'` then a word-boundary check in Rust. **The dialog**
(`sql_confirm::open_with_reach`, `Reach`, `REACH_WAIT`): under the explanation, each tie under its heading with a
count per kind, 8 objects drawn at most then "and N more", **Copy the list**; empty, failed and slow states as
D-086 says; the button waits (10 s at most) only on Drop with cascade and TRUNCATE … CASCADE. The navigator asks for
it on Drop, Drop with cascade and Rename of every object but a database (`view.rs` `dependents`, on the tree's
session of the database), the Truncate tool when Cascade is on (`maintenance_dialog.rs`, a session of its own).
The rename's explanation no longer claims views have to be changed ("Statements that use the old name of the …
have to be changed too.").

Examples, as drawn. PostgreSQL, Drop with cascade on `kerno_ui_k14_cascade.k14_t`: "Drop table “k14_t” with
cascade?" / "The table, what it holds and every object that depends on it (views, foreign keys, …) are removed for
good. This cannot be undone." / **Also dropped: 1 foreign key, 1 view** / `foreign key child_t_fk on
kerno_ui_k14_cascade.child` / `view kerno_ui_k14_cascade.v` / Copy the list / the SQL / Cancel, Drop with cascade.
MySQL, Drop on the same table: **The server refuses the drop while these depend on it: 1 foreign key** / `foreign
key child_t_fk on kerno_ui_k14_fk.child` / **These stay but stop working: 1 view** / `view kerno_ui_k14_fk.v`
(MariaDB: **These mention its name (a text search: Kerno did not parse them): 1 view**).

Tests. Driver, against the server's own answer: `kerno-postgres` `dependents::{a_cascade_lists_what_the_server_drops,
a_missing_object_is_an_error_not_an_empty_list, a_truncate_cascade_lists_the_tables_it_empties,
a_rename_lists_the_bodies_that_name_it}` (in a transaction rolled back, each shape in a savepoint: Kerno's names
equal the server's "drop cascades to" notice, mapped through `pg_describe_object` ↔ `pg_identify_object`, for a
table with views on views across schemas, a materialized view, another table's foreign key, functions taking and
returning its row type and a column of it; an enum column; a sequence a default reads; a trigger function; an
overload; a mixed-case table; an inheriting table; a partitioned table with a view on its partition; a unique
index a foreign key uses; an extension; a schema; nothing; the truncate's per-table notices; after the rename the
listed bodies fail and the view and the `BEGIN ATOMIC` body still run); `kerno-mysql`
`dependents::a_drop_lists_the_keys_that_refuse_it_and_the_views_it_breaks_and_a_rename_what_names_it` (both engines:
the server refuses the drop naming that key, error 3730 / 1451; the listed views then fail with 1356; a
self-referencing table lists nothing; a routine's drop is Unsupported; after `RENAME TABLE` the listed view, view in
another database, procedure and trigger fail). Unit: `schema::tests::{a_name_is_mentioned_as_a_word,
like_patterns_escape_their_wildcards}`, `sql_confirm::tests::{a_long_list_is_cut_and_counted_by_kind,
each_tie_has_its_heading}`. UI (`critique.rs`): `a_cascade_drop_lists_the_view_and_the_foreign_key_before_confirm`
(confirmed: the table and view gone, the child kept without its key),
`a_cascade_drop_of_an_unused_table_says_nothing_depends_on_it`,
`a_list_that_cannot_be_read_says_so_and_lets_the_user_decide` (the table dropped behind the tree's back),
`a_slow_list_holds_the_button_then_lets_go` (no server: a read that never ends, the clock advanced),
`a_mysql_drop_lists_the_foreign_key_that_refuses_it` (MySQL and MariaDB), `a_rename_lists_what_names_the_object`
(three engines), `a_truncate_cascade_lists_the_tables_it_empties`, `a_long_list_keeps_the_buttons_in_the_window` (no
server: 40 objects over two ties on a production connection, the buttons inside the 800 px window; they were
42 px from its bottom with 12 lines, so 8 are drawn); the new UI tests run five times in a row with
`a_drop_with_cascade_also_drops_what_depends_on_the_table`, which now waits for the list before it clicks (it
clicked at once and passed only because the read was faster). Seen failing under a one-line mutation each: the
walk without its way up to the owner (the first shape's list differs from the notice), an empty target read as found, the
truncate's recursion cut to one level, the rename without the word check (a longer name listed), MySQL views
over views not followed, the self-reference kept, `mentions` without its boundary check, `like_containing`
without escapes, no "and N more", the ties unsorted; in the app: the navigator's cascade flag dropped (no "Also
dropped" heading comes), the rename's read not asked, an empty list drawn as nothing, a failed read shown as an empty
list (each alone), the button never held, MySQL's foreign keys not read, the truncate's read not asked, 100 lines drawn (Cancel at y = 1182 in an 800 px
window). Not
covered: a table with a policy, a rule, a publication, a statistics object or an event trigger (the walk is
generic; not compared with the notice); more than 100 objects (the server's notice stops at 100, so the tests
keep under it); a MySQL view's drop and a MariaDB sequence's rename (driver paths without a test).

Limits. MySQL's and MariaDB's `information_schema` shows only what the user may see: a foreign key on a table the
user cannot see still refuses the drop, a routine's body hidden from a user is not searched; their empty line says
"Nothing this user can see depends on it." (the test user sees everything). In a window shorter than about 690 px
a production drop with a long list and a long statement can push the buttons below the window (the dialog does
not scroll; 440 px is the window's minimum). The rename search is a text search (comments and strings count; a name built in dynamic SQL is missed)
and reads only the current database on PostgreSQL; a view over a listed view is not listed for a rename; a column
rename and a database's drop or rename get no list; MySQL routines, triggers and events that use a dropped table
are not listed for a drop (only views); the truncate list leaves out inheritance children and their foreign keys
(a `TRUNCATE` without `ONLY` empties them too); a slow catalog read holds the tree's loads of that database behind
it (it runs on the tree's session); the list is not read again if something changes while the dialog is open. Not
changed, for the owner: the column rename dialog still says "Statements, views and functions that use the old
name of the column have to be changed too." (`navigator/view.rs:1984`), wrong for PostgreSQL views, which follow a
column rename. Where the plan was wrong: `deptype in ('n','a')` at one level would list a table's own indexes and
owned sequences (automatic) and miss every view (a view is reached through its rule, an internal part of it);
MySQL views come from the catalog only on MySQL (MariaDB has no `VIEW_TABLE_USAGE`); a rename's list on MySQL
needs triggers and events too (they break as well).

### K15 — The other long operations (C3, M)

Navigator loads get a statement timeout (30 s, then "Could not read the tables: the server took longer than
30 s. Refresh to try again.") and a Cancel entry on the "Loading…" row; AI Stop closes the HTTP connection
(the client's agent with a cancellable request, or a thread that drops the socket) and
`ui_tests::ai::a_request_is_stopped` asserts the endpoint saw the connection close; the CSV preview cancels
the previous scan when a field changes (a shared flag checked per 64 KiB); ER layout runs in
`spawn_blocking`; opening a `.sql` file over 20 MB asks "Open in the editor, or run it with Execute script?"
(the editor holds the whole text on the UI thread).

Done: one way to stop a read on a server session, `bridge::Stop` (D-087): the read runs as a Tokio task whose abort
handle is kept; stopping drops it where it waits (so nothing queued behind it on the session is sent) and closes the
session, which stops the statement on the server. MySQL's `close` now kills a statement whose caller dropped it
midway (the server does not see the client leave while it waits for a lock). **Reads behind a lock (part 6).** The
structure tab's read, the table form's read and the ER diagram's read run under a `Stop`; the structure tab and the
form show "Loading the structure…" / "Loading the table…" with a Cancel (`structure-cancel`, `editor-load-cancel`),
Cmd+. reaches them (the structure tab and the diagram were not on `Workspace::cancel_query`'s path;
`TableEditor::cancel` acted only while applying), and closing the tab stops the read (`close_tab_at` did nothing for
a structure tab or a diagram). Cancelled with nothing shown: "Cancelled" / "The structure of s.t was not read." (the
form: "s.t was not read.") with "Read again"; with a structure already shown, it stays. "Set comment" marks the tab
running; Cmd+. stops the `COMMENT ON` / `ALTER … COMMENT` on the server ("Cancelled: the comment was not changed.",
nothing read again). A data apply reads its Cancel before each statement and before `COMMIT`
(`sql::apply_edits_until`): one landing between two statements rolls back instead of letting the next ones run.
**The tree (part 1).** It has sessions of its own (one per database on PostgreSQL, one per connection on MySQL),
no longer the connection's meta session: a read waiting for a lock there also held the meta session's keep-alive
ping (two 5 s timeouts), so the connection was declared lost and reconnected after about 10-15 s (found by reading
`connections/manager.rs`, not run), and a stop could not close it. Every read of the tree goes through
`Navigator::read_on`: a 30 s clock (`LOAD_LIMIT`, the UI's clock) then the same stop as Cancel. A row being read
shows "Loading…" with a small Cancel (`node-load-cancel`; "Loading databases…" too), "Cancel loading" first in its
menu, and Cmd+. with it selected (the tree takes Cmd+. only then). Cancel or the clock stops every read of that
session; their rows say "Could not read the objects of shop: cancelled. Refresh to try again." or "… the server
took longer than 30 s. Refresh to try again."; the next read opens a new session at once, the lock still held. The
one-off reads (Generate, Show DDL, Edit source, Call, Properties) share the clock ("the server took longer than 30
s. Try again." in their notification). K14's list is read on a session of its own; the dialog going drops the
sending end of a channel and the read is stopped and its session closed; its 10 s clock leaves it to finish. A
database's drop closes the tree's session in it first. **AI Stop (part 2)** closes the connection
(`ai::Stopper`, `ureq` 3.5's `Agent::shutdown`, no new dependency); the panel says "Stopped: the request was
closed, nothing was inserted." **Large `.sql` files (part 5)**: over 20 MB (`LARGE_SCRIPT`) nothing is read before
"“big.sql” is 34 MB" / "The editor keeps the whole text in memory and lays it out as you scroll: a file this large
makes Kerno slow. Execute script runs it with the engine's own client (psql, mysql) without opening it, and shows
its progress." with Cancel (opens nothing), "Open in the editor", and "Run with Execute script…" (the Tools wizard
"Execute script…" on the active tab's database, else the default connection's, the file chosen; it asks before it
runs, so K6 holds); Enter answers nothing. **CSV preview (part 3)**: each scan has a flag, set when a field changes
(before the next scan starts) and when the dialog closes; `import::count_records` reads it at each record. **ER
layout (part 4)**: it already ran off the UI thread, on one of the runtime's three async workers; it now runs on the
blocking pool, skipped when the tab was closed or reloaded meanwhile.

Tests, each seen failing under the mutation named: `kerno-mysql`
`session::tests::closing_after_a_dropped_read_stops_it_on_the_server` (MySQL, MariaDB; `close` without its kill
arm: the read stays on the server; a first version with `SLEEP` passed under the mutation, `SLEEP` sees the client
leave, so it waits behind `LOCK TABLES` now); `kerno-postgres` `edits::a_failed_apply_changes_nothing` (a cancel
before a statement: `statement == Some(0)`, rolled back, `Idle`; the per-statement check removed);
`kerno-core` `import::tests::a_stopped_count_ends_at_once` (the check removed); UI (`critique.rs`, PostgreSQL unless
said): `a_structure_read_behind_a_lock_is_cancelled` (button and Cmd+.; `cancel` without `stop`),
`a_table_form_read_behind_a_lock_is_cancelled` (the editor's read not stopped), `closing_a_tab_that_reads_stops_the_read`
(structure, form, diagram; the structure arm of `close_tab_at` off, then the diagram arm off),
`setting_a_comment_behind_a_lock_is_cancelled` (PostgreSQL, MySQL; the write's stop removed),
`a_tree_read_behind_a_lock_is_cancelled_and_the_tree_reads_on` (button, menu, Cmd+.; the session's stop removed,
then the session left in the map), `a_tree_read_that_takes_too_long_is_stopped` (29 s: still waiting, 31 s:
stopped, on the test clock; the timer's stop removed), `closing_the_drop_question_stops_the_read_of_what_it_reaches`
(a lock on `pg_depend` in a database of the test's own, since the list's read does not wait on a table lock; the
channel's branch removed), `a_large_script_file_asks_before_it_opens` (the size check off; the wizard not given the
file), `a_new_preview_of_a_csv_stops_the_scan_before_it` (the reload's flag, then the drop's flag),
`a_database_the_tree_has_read_is_dropped_from_the_tree` (the release's `stop_session` off: the tree's backend
stays in the database and the drop never happens); `ui_tests::ai::a_request_is_stopped` now waits for the
endpoint to see each connection close and no answer to be written (Stop without `stop.stop()`: times out).
`restored_tabs_open_a_session_once_shown_and_structure_tabs_close_theirs` and
`every_owner_of_a_session_closes_it_when_it_goes_away` count one more session for a connected connection (the
tree's): two, not one, then each owner's on top.

Where the plan was wrong: the ER layout already ran off the UI thread; MySQL's and MariaDB's catalog reads do not
wait behind `LOCK TABLES … WRITE` (probed: `information_schema.TABLES`, `COLUMNS`, `STATISTICS`, `SHOW CREATE TABLE`
answer at once, only a read of the rows waits), so the lock tests of the tree, the structure tab and the form are on
PostgreSQL, where `pg_total_relation_size` (the objects of a schema) and `pg_get_indexdef` wait; the row of a schema
reads "the objects of shop", not "the tables"; `session.cancel()` alone does not stop a read (introspection does not
go through the drivers' cancel counter, and a cancel landing between two queries of a read is ignored by an idle
server); the tree did not hang forever on the meta session, it reconnected. Not covered: the FDW dialog's statements
run on after the dialog closes, with no Cancel (`IMPORT FOREIGN SCHEMA` waits for the remote server, bounded by its
connect timeout); the administration's cancel and terminate are not stopped (`pg_cancel_backend`,
`pg_terminate_backend`, `KILL` return at once; they wait only for the tab's own refresh on its session); Settings →
AI "List models" has no Stop (60 s bound); "Revert file" reads the whole file whatever its size; the ER layout, once
started, runs to its end; the between-statements check of a data apply is pinned by the driver test only (no way to
land a click between two statements); nothing shows that the layout runs on the blocking pool; the list of
databases has no timeout test of its own (same stop path), nor have the one-off reads (Generate, Show DDL, Edit
source, Call, Properties: their clock and "the server took longer than 30 s. Try again." are read, not run); two
reads opening the same key's session at once keep one and close the other once read, untested; one more server
connection per connected connection, and the idle memory of D-082 not measured again; other users of the meta
session (the toolbar's and the query tab's reads, the admin tab's picker) were not audited for the keep-alive
coupling.

### K16 — A tab whose own server session died says so before Commit (C15, M)

If only the tab's backend dies (PostgreSQL `idle_in_transaction_session_timeout`, `pg_terminate_backend`,
MySQL `wait_timeout`) while the connection's meta session answers, the tab keeps "transaction open". While a
tab holds a transaction, ping its own session every 30 s (`SELECT 1` is not allowed inside a failed
transaction: use the driver's `is_closed()`/connection state, then a protocol-level ping); on loss, the same
"the server rolled the transaction back" notice as a lost connection.

Done: **No ping (where the plan was wrong).** Any command restarts the server's idle timers, so a ping on the
tab's session every 30 s would have kept a forgotten transaction and its locks past the DBA's guard, forever.
Proved on the three servers with a timeout set on the session itself (PostgreSQL
`idle_in_transaction_session_timeout = '1s'`, MySQL and MariaDB `wait_timeout = 2`, a `COM_PING` restarting it
too): pinged more often than the timeout, the session stayed open long after it. **Before, as verified**: the
drivers learned nothing while idle (PostgreSQL's connection task did read the server's FATAL at once, but only
queued it for the next statement; `tx_state()` stayed `Open` on both engines after the server closed the
session), so the tab kept "transaction open", its badge and its age, Commit and Roll back, and the idle rule and
K1's quit question counted the transaction. Commit then failed with "The connection closed during the commit:
whether the server saved the transaction is not known." The next Run failed with "The connection closed while
this statement ran…" and never said the transaction was lost (the session already said `Idle`); worse, it called
`connection_lost`, which closed every session of the connection and so rolled back the other tabs'
transactions; in manual commit, or through a result's Apply, the dead session met by the `BEGIN` was kept, and
every run after it failed the same way until the connection was reopened.

**Detection: `Session::gone() -> Option<DbError>`** (D-088), one question that sends nothing on the session.
PostgreSQL: the connection task keeps the FATAL with its SQLSTATE in a slot the session holds (`connect::Gone`),
free, and `tx_state()` says `Idle` once it is filled (K4's badge, K5's idle rule and K1's question then agree).
MySQL, MariaDB: `mysql_async` cannot see the close without sending (its socket check is `pub(crate)`), so
`gone()` opens a short connection of its own on the cancel's route and asks `information_schema.PROCESSLIST` for
its id; only an answer of 0 from the same server says gone (`@@hostname`, read at connect: behind a load
balancer the check may reach another node, where the id means nothing); nothing while a statement holds the
session; never the meta session (its keep-alive, K15). The tab asks every 15 s (`SESSION_CHECK`, the UI's clock, advanced by the tests) while it holds
a transaction (its own record, K4's `tx_started`) and nothing runs; a tab without a transaction is not watched,
its next statement finds out. Cost: nothing on PostgreSQL; on MySQL one connection and login per check (an SSH
channel through a tunnel), and one before each Commit.

**On loss** the tab says once (messages and a notification): "The server closed this tab's session while its
transaction was open: the server rolled it back, and nothing of it was committed. Its statements are in the
transaction log. [The pending changes of the results are kept: Apply runs them in a new transaction.] The next
statement opens a new session." then why, in plain words for PostgreSQL's 25P03, 25P04, 57P05 and 57P01 ("It
stayed idle inside a transaction for longer than the server allows (idle_in_transaction_session_timeout).",
"An administrator ended it (pg_terminate_backend), or the server is shutting down.") and "The server said: …";
on MySQL "the server no longer lists this session: it was idle for longer than its wait_timeout, or it was
killed". The session is dropped: badge, "transaction open", Commit and Roll back go. The transaction log keeps
the lost statements under "The server closed the session: it rolled these statements back…" until the next
statement. The next statement opens a new session in the tab's database and schema (the target follows the
session, K3) and commit mode. **A statement that finds its session gone** (a run, the manual-commit `BEGIN`, a
result's Apply) says "This tab's session closed while its transaction was open: …" when the tab held one, drops
that session and asks `Connections::verify` (the keep-alive's ping of the connection's own session): only if it
does not answer is the connection taken for lost. **Commit** asks `gone()` first (3 s at most): on a closed
session the COMMIT is not sent ("The commit was not sent. The server closed this tab's session …"). **A ROLLBACK
that finds the session closed succeeds** (the server rolled back): Roll back, the idle rule and "Roll back and
quit" no longer fail on a dead session. Whichever learns first drops the session and the tab's record, so a
whole connection lost is said once.

Tests, each seen failing under the mutation named: `kerno-postgres`
`execute::watching_a_session_does_not_keep_it_alive` (`gone()` running `SELECT 1` on the session first: the
server never closes it), `execute::a_session_the_server_closes_says_why` (25P04, 57P05, 57P01; the connection
task not filling the slot); `kerno-mysql` `execute::watching_a_session_does_not_keep_it_alive` (MySQL, MariaDB;
`gone()` pinging the session first), `execute::a_killed_session_is_seen_gone` (only a count of 99 taken for gone:
both fail), `session::tests::another_server_behind_the_address_is_not_taken_for_this_one` (the check sent to
MariaDB for a MySQL session; the host comparison dropped: taken for gone). UI (`critique.rs`, PostgreSQL and MySQL unless said):
`a_tab_whose_session_the_server_closed_says_so_before_commit` (the watch disarmed; the session kept after the
report; the lost log not kept), `the_server_s_idle_timeout_still_closes_a_watched_session` (the watch disarmed;
the watch pinging the tab's session before `gone()`: the server's timeout never fires),
`a_session_closed_alone_leaves_the_connection_and_the_other_tabs` (`connection_lost` in place of `verify`: the
other tab's transaction rolled back; the lost run reading the session's state: nothing said; the `BEGIN`'s
failure keeping the session), `commit_on_a_session_the_server_closed_says_it_was_rolled_back` (no check before
COMMIT; a ROLLBACK on a closed session failing), `the_idle_rule_and_quit_ignore_a_transaction_the_server_rolled_back`
(production; the watch disarmed), `an_idle_tab_whose_session_died_runs_on_a_new_one` (auto and manual commit;
`connection_lost` in place of `verify`; the `BEGIN`'s failure keeping the session),
`the_edits_waiting_in_a_result_outlive_a_closed_session` (an Apply's failure keeping the session; the lost run
reading the session's state), `a_lost_connection_found_by_a_statement_is_said_once` (PostgreSQL; `close_session`
not clearing the tab's record: said twice). The existing
`ui_tests::a_lost_connection_says_the_server_rolled_its_transaction_back` fails when `say_if_rolled_back` reads
the session's state again (a PostgreSQL session closed by the server now says `Idle`). Changed expectation:
`ui_tests::a_new_transaction_after_a_lost_connection_starts_an_empty_log` expected the log empty after the loss;
it now keeps the lost statement until the next statement (then holds the new one alone, unchanged).
`a_lost_connection_takes_the_badge_away` passes unchanged, but its "the connection comes back" wait now finds a
connection that was never lost.

Not covered: on MySQL, within the 15 s before the check, a statement still says "The connection closed while
this statement ran… not known" (it was not sent if the server had closed the session, but the client cannot tell
it from a cut mid-statement); a tab without a transaction whose session died runs its next statement into the
failure (it is not run again; the one after runs) rather than being reopened silently; the idle rule can still
say it rolled back a MySQL transaction that the server closed in the last 15 s before the rule's period (its
ROLLBACK finds the session closed and succeeds); on
PostgreSQL the badge and "transaction open" go at the next frame, up to 15 s before the tab's notice; session
settings made with `SET` (and temporary tables) are not put back on the new session; the notification's text is
not read by a test (the kit gives none), only the tab's messages and their detail; the `ptr_eq` guard against a
session Kerno closed itself (or replaced) while a check was under way is not pinned by a test (no way to land
the close between the check's answer and its use); MariaDB is covered by the driver tests, not the UI ones; the
cost of a check through an SSH tunnel was not measured; on MySQL a check whose connection cannot be opened (a
rotated password, an expired IAM token, a network cut) is the last one for that session (review fix:
`MysqlSession::unchecked`, `session::tests::a_check_that_cannot_connect_is_not_tried_again`), so a refused login
is not repeated every 15 s against the server; such a tab is then told by its next statement only. Behaviour changed on the way (D-088): RefreshConnections pings
with the keep-alive's 2 × 5 s limit (it waited without one); a statement that meets a closed session waits for
that ping before the connection is reopened (up to 10 s when nothing answers); `scripts/bench.sh`'s reconnect
measure (J9) runs statements in a loop and was not run again. Seen, not changed: `kill_query`
(`kerno-mysql/src/session.rs`) sends `KILL QUERY <id>` on a fresh connection that, behind a load balancer, may
reach another node, where the id may be another session.

## The owner's requests of 2026-11-18 (proposed, not started)

Not from the critique: what the owner misses when he uses Kerno beside DBeaver. Short entries, to detail in
this file when he names one. K17 before K18 (K18 draws in K17's panel); K19 is independent.

### K17 — A value panel docked beside the grid, following the selected cell (M)

**Now.** A double click or Shift+Enter opens the whole value in a dialog (`query/viewer.rs` `open` and
`edit`, from `query/grid.rs` `open_value`): text, indented JSON, hex dump, editable where the cell takes
typed values. It is a modal: it does not follow the selection, and the grid is hidden behind it.

**Wanted.** DBeaver's "Value" panel, done better: a panel at the right of a results grid and of a table's
data grid, shown and hidden by a button of the grid's bar, a palette entry and a shortcut; its width and its
shown/hidden state remembered. It shows the cell under the cursor and follows it (`TableEvent::SelectCell`,
already listened to in `query/tab.rs` and `data/tab.rs`), reusing the viewer's renderers: JSON and JSONB
always indented and highlighted (DBeaver shows one raw line), long text wrapped, binary as hex, NULL said
as NULL. Editable in place where the dialog is (same pending-edit path, same "Review SQL" and Apply);
invalid JSON is said under the editor and is not written to the pending edits. Nothing is fetched again: the
panel reads the grid's store. The dialog stays for a double click when the panel is hidden.

**Tests.** UI: the panel follows the selection; a JSONB cell shows indented; an edit in the panel becomes a
pending edit and Apply writes it (PostgreSQL `jsonb`, MySQL `JSON`); invalid JSON is refused; hidden state
restored. **Capture:** a JSONB cell in the panel, light and dark.

Done: `query/value_panel.rs` (`ValuePanel`, D-090), one per query tab and per data tab, made when the setting
`value_panel` shows it and dropped when it hides it (hidden it does no work: the 1M-row scroll is untouched, the
setting is off by default). **Showing it**: "Toggle Value Panel" (Data group of the palette, Data menu of the menu
bar, after "Toggle Selection Statistics"; K13's invariant holds), **F7** (DBeaver's key for its panels,
`resultset.grid.togglePreview`; free in Kerno, the kit's inputs and vim mode), and a "Value" button in the result
footer and the data tab's footer, beside "Record", drawn selected while the panel is shown, its tooltip "Show the
selected cell's whole value beside the grid" with the bound key. One switch for every grid; it and the panel's
width (`value_panel_width`, 240-900 pt, 380 by default, clamped on load) are stored with the settings. The panel
sits at the right of a query tab's results area (beside the result tabs and footer, outside the grid's key
handlers, so typing in it never pastes or clears cells) and of a data tab's rows, in the kit's `h_resizable`
(its handle; the width is written when a drag ends, `ResizablePanelEvent::Resized`); the grid keeps at least
240 pt, and in a window too narrow for both the panel is not drawn. **What it shows**: the grid's selected cell,
read from the grid's store and pending values (`Grid::cell`; nothing is fetched): a header "doc · jsonb" with the
status at its right ("NULL", "Empty string", "Empty: 0 bytes", "13 B", "3.2 MB; the first 256 kB are shown");
JSON (a JSON column, or a text holding an object or array when it is only shown) indented and highlighted by
the kit's code editor (`language("json")`, folding), long text wrapped, binary as a hex dump of its first 64 kB,
an editable NULL as an empty editor whose placeholder says NULL and a read-only one as an italic "NULL", J6's
Unix-time line under an integer; arrays as the server prints them, wrapped. It follows the selection through
an observer of the grid, comparing the selected cell and `Grid::generation` first (scrolling costs a
comparison); a value over 64 kB is laid out off the UI thread ("Laying the value out…"), the task dropped when
the selection moves on; at most 256 kB is put in the editor. Empty states (the kit's `Empty`): "No cell
selected / Select a cell of the grid to see its whole value here.", "Several cells are selected / Select one
cell to see its whole value here.", "No rows / Once rows are shown, the value of the selected cell is shown
here.". The renderers and the save rule are `viewer.rs`'s, now functions both use: `views_for`, `view_text`,
`cut_at`, `time_hint`, `hint_line`, `saved_value` (as shown: no change; JSON the parser refuses: "This is not
valid JSON: trailing comma at line 3 column 1."; else `typed_value`) and `json_problem`; the dialog's Save and
Format/Compact go through them too. **Editing**: wherever `Grid::editable` allows the dialog, through the same
path (`Grid::set_value`, which the dialog's apply now calls: one undoable pending change, "Review SQL", Apply):
Set or Cmd+Enter (taken before "Run statement", which the panel's editor never triggers), Set NULL (when the
column takes it), Format / Compact for a JSON column, Discard; Escape drops the text and gives the keyboard
back to the grid. A text column holding JSON is edited as stored (re-indenting a user's text would change it).
Refusals under the editor in the tab's words: the data tab's ("“x” is a read-only connection.", "The rows of a
view are read-only here.", the no-key sentence), a result's `ReadOnly` ("Read-only result: no column comes from
a table.", "Wait a moment: the table of the result is being read."), and `Grid::editable`'s own ("This row is
marked for deletion.", "This column is computed by the server.", "Binary values are not typed in; they can be
set to NULL.", "Matching rows only are shown: turn it off in the find bar to edit."); a cell whose pending value
is a default or an expression: "change it in the cell (Enter)"; a value over 256 kB: "Too long to edit here:
only its start is shown. “Edit value” in the cell's menu shows it whole.". **Typed text is never lost or
half-written**: while it differs from the value, the panel stays on its cell and the selection moving on
changes nothing in the pending edits ("Not set yet: this text is for row 3, not the selected cell. Set it, or
press Escape to drop it."); typed back to the value, the panel follows again; if its rows go (the page read
again, another result on screen, a re-run, the panel hidden) the text goes to the clipboard and a notification
says "The text typed in the value panel was not set: the rows it was for are no longer shown. It is on the
clipboard.". **Double click and Shift+Enter**: with the panel drawn they put the keyboard in its editor (a
boolean is still toggled by its click; Enter and F2 still type in the cell); hidden, or for a value shown in
part, they do what they did (inline editor, dialog). The cell menu's "View value" / "Edit value" keep the
dialog. **Stacked results (J11)**: one panel beside the whole list, following the result that has the focus
(`QueryTab::active_grid`). **Record view**: the panel follows the selected field (the record view selects
through the grid); a double click on a field keeps its inline editor.

Verified: unit `value_panel::tests::values_are_laid_out_as_the_viewer_lays_them_out`,
`viewer::tests::{saving_refuses_broken_json_and_says_where, long_text_is_cut_on_a_character_boundary}`,
`settings::tests::settings_round_trip_and_default` (the two fields); UI (`ui_tests/value_panel.rs`):
`the_panel_follows_the_selected_cell_of_a_result` (PostgreSQL: F7 shows it, header and indented highlighted
JSONB, the read-only reason, empty string versus NULL, the Unix time, Shift+Enter focuses the editor and
Cmd+Enter there does not run the statement again, Escape gives the grid the keyboard, several cells, the
footer's button hides it), `json_edited_in_the_panel_is_pending_then_applied` (PostgreSQL `jsonb`, MySQL
`JSON`, scratch `kerno_ui_k17_edit`: double click focuses the editor, broken JSON said with its line and
column and nothing pending, the selection moving on keeps the text, Cmd+Enter makes one pending `UPDATE`,
Apply writes it), `the_panel_and_its_width_come_back_after_a_restart` (the button, a width of 500 written on a
resize and both there after a restart). Each seen failing under a one-line mutation: the panel not reading the
cell, JSON shown as text, the empty string's status removed, the tab's reason ignored, the Cmd+Enter capture
removed, Set not writing, the JSON check removed, the draft not holding the panel, the double click not
redirected, the width not written, the button doing nothing, F7 unbound. The existing viewer, grid, data
editing, restart, `menu_palette` and `actions` tests pass.

Limits: no Text / JSON / Hex switch in the panel (the dialog keeps it); arrays are not split one element per
line; MariaDB's `JSON` is `LONGTEXT` (shown as JSON when it holds a document, edited as text, not checked);
a tab closed with text not set loses it (the quit question of K1 does not count it); the panel's width is
per tab once made (a drag in one tab reaches the others when their panel is made again); not UI-tested in
stacked mode nor in the record view; no capture taken. To take: `final/m12-value-panel-json-light.png`,
`final/m12-value-panel-json-dark.png`. **For K18**: `ValuePanel::cell()` gives the shown cell (a row of the grid
and a column of the result), `ValuePanel::grid()` its grid (whose `Owner::structure` holds the table's foreign
keys in a data tab), and `render_cell` marks where sections go under the value; the panel's observer and
`follow` already run only when the cell or the grid's generation changes, the place to start and cancel a
read of the referenced row.

### K18 — The row a foreign key points to, previewed in the value panel (M, after K17)

**Now.** A foreign-key cell's menu jumps to the referenced row in a tab of its own (`data/tab.rs` `related`,
E3). Nothing shows that row without leaving the table.

**Wanted.** When the selected cell belongs to a foreign key of the table (the data tab knows them:
`TableStructure::foreign_keys`), the value panel shows, under the value, the referenced row as a short
record: the columns a person reads first (a `name`, `label`, `title`, `email`, first and last name; else the
first text columns), at most about eight, then "Show all columns". The user picks which columns show for a
referenced table (a "Choose columns…" menu of the panel), remembered per table as the grid's column settings
are (J6). One `SELECT … WHERE key = value LIMIT 1` per referenced value, on the connection's meta session,
off the UI thread, cached for the tab, cancelled when the selection moves on; "No row with this key" when
the key dangles (MySQL with checks off), nothing for NULL. "Open the row" keeps the jump of E3. Read-only.
Composite keys use every column of the key. In a query's results (no table structure) the panel shows the
value only: a later step could use the result's column origins.

**Tests.** Driver-free unit test of the column choice; UI on the three engines against the fixtures
(`shop.orders.customer_id` → the customer's name and email), a NULL key, a composite key, the remembered
column choice, no query while the panel is hidden. **Capture:** an FK cell with its preview.

Done: `query/value_panel/fk_preview.rs`, a child module of the panel (D-091). With the panel shown, a cell of a
column in a foreign key of the table the rows are from (`Grid::structure`: a data tab's table, or an editable
one-table query result; mapped through the result's labels, `Grid::table_text`) shows under its value, in a
section above a line, the row it points to headed "customers, by customer_id": a field per line (name muted,
value on one line, cut at 200 characters, NULL in italics, binary as "16 B of binary data"), the columns of
`default_columns` (a name, first and last name, a title, a label, an email, a user name, a code or a slug, matched
whatever the case and `_`/`-`; then any `…name`/`…title`/`…email`; else the text columns, short types first;
never the key, binary or JSON; at most eight), "Show all N columns" / "Show fewer columns", "Choose columns…" (a
menu: "Kerno's choice" and a ticked item per column of the row; kept per connection and referenced table,
`Storage::{preview_columns, save_preview_columns}`, in `table_layouts` under `fk-preview:<J6 key>`, gone with the
connection) and, in a data tab, "Open the row" (the cell menu's `GridAction::Follow`, E3). **The read** is E3's
statement: `data::tab::reference` (extracted from `related`, which uses it) gives the referenced table and the
filter on its key, `page_sql` writes it with a limit of one (`SELECT *`: Kerno's choice, the user's and "Show all"
from one read), the literals typed by the local key columns; on a session of the panel's own, opened at the first
key and closed when the panel goes (a `oneshot` dropped with it), when its connection or database changes, after
a failure or a stop. It starts once the selection has rested 250 ms on the key (`REST`), so a walk through the
cells reads nothing; "Reading the row of customers…" with Cancel appears after 300 ms; a read in flight is
stopped (`bridge::Stop`, the server stops the statement) when the selection moves to another key or off the keys,
and after 10 s on the UI's clock: "Not read: the server took longer than 10 s." / "Not read: cancelled." with
"Try again". Rows and "no row" are cached for the grid (up to 256 keys), cleared when the panel follows another
grid (refresh, Apply, another page, a re-run); another column of the same key, or another row with the same key,
reads nothing. **States**: nothing for a key with a NULL (or binary) column, as the jump; "No row with this key:
parents has no row where id = 99." (and, when the tab's session holds a transaction, "Rows of this tab's open
transaction are not seen here: only committed rows are."); a pending key shows the pending value's row and says
"The row of the pending value, not applied yet."; a pending default or expression says "Its pending value is
computed by the server: the row is shown once the change is applied."; a failure "The row could not be read."
with K9's `describe` lines and Try again. Read-only. No query while the panel is hidden: no panel exists then.

Tests, each seen failing under the mutation named: unit `fk_preview::tests::the_default_columns_are_the_ones_a_person_reads_first`
(the key not skipped), `a_read_row_is_shown_one_line_per_value` (control characters kept),
`storage::tests::preview_columns_round_trip_beside_the_layout` (the choice stored under the layout's own key: a
default layout clears it); UI (`ui_tests/fk_preview.rs`): `the_row_a_foreign_key_points_to_is_shown_under_its_value`
(PostgreSQL, MySQL, MariaDB, fixtures read only: `orders.customer_id` → full_name and email as the server has
them, nothing before the rest, one read for the key shown twice, all columns, "Open the row", a top category's
NULL `parent_id`; the cache never hit; "Open the row" doing nothing), `an_editable_result_previews_its_foreign_keys`
(PostgreSQL; the structure used for data tabs only), `a_pending_or_dangling_key_says_so` (PostgreSQL, MySQL,
scratch `kerno_ui_k18_keys`, the dangling row inserted with `FOREIGN_KEY_CHECKS = 0`; the pending flag never set;
the computed-key branch removed), `a_composite_key_uses_every_column` (PostgreSQL, MariaDB, `kerno_ui_k18_pairs`,
then its row changed on the server and the page read again: read again; the key's first column only; the cache
kept when the grid is new), `the_columns_picked_for_a_table_come_back_after_a_restart` (PostgreSQL, another
order after the restart; the stored choice not read), `nothing_is_read_while_hidden_or_moving` (PostgreSQL,
`kerno_ui_k18_moves`; the panel made while hidden; no rest: every cell read), `a_read_behind_a_lock_is_stopped`
(PostgreSQL, `kerno_ui_k18_lock` locked by K2's `Locker`, made `pub(super)`: the preview's own statement, found
by its text in `pg_stat_activity`, gone after the move and after 10 s, Try again once the lock is gone; the move
not stopping; the time limit removed). A first version waited on `Locker::blocks` and failed about one run in
four: another session waited for the same lock and stayed (likely the tree's read of the schema's sizes,
`pg_total_relation_size`, K15; not identified by a probe).
The E3 jump's, K17's and J6's tests pass unchanged.

Where the plan was wrong or changed: the read is not on the meta session (K15's keep-alive coupling) nor the
tab's, but a session of the panel's own; it is `SELECT *`, not the shown columns; a debounce comes before the
stop (a stop closes the session). Limits: a binary key (`BINARY(16)`, `bytea`) shows no preview, as the jump has
none; one more server connection per tab whose panel has read a key; dropping the panel with a read waiting
closes its session, which stops the statement, but no test pins it (the stop on a move does); the menu closes
after each tick; a choice naming only columns since dropped falls back to Kerno's; a result whose columns were
renamed shows its preview only through the labels the edit target knows; "Open the row" is not offered in a
query's result (no jump there); not UI-tested in the stacked results nor the record view; no capture taken. To
take: `final/m12-fk-preview-light.png`, `final/m12-fk-preview-dark.png`.

### K19 — Split the tab area: drag a tab to an edge (L)

**Now.** One tab area (`workspace.rs`: `tabs: Vec<OpenTab>`, `active_tab`); J10 added the tab list and the
rows / vertical layouts. Two tabs cannot be seen side by side.

**Wanted.** Dragging a tab to the left, right, top or bottom edge of the tab area splits it there (a drop
indicator shows where); "Split right" / "Split down" / "Move to the other group" in the tab's menu and the
palette; a divider to resize; a group that loses its last tab closes. Each group has its own tab strip and
active tab; the focused group is the one the toolbar, the status bar, the environment colour and the
shortcuts (⌘W, ⌘T, ⌘Enter) act on. The layout (groups, sizes, which tab where) is stored and restored with
the tabs. K1's quit question and K2's cancel keep working over every group.

**How (to settle when detailed).** The kit has a dock with split panels (`gpui-component` `dock/`:
`DockArea`, `TabPanel`, drag and drop of panels); Kerno drew its own tab strip instead (J10's rows, vertical
list and overflow menu, the environment tint). Either (a) a pane tree of Kerno's own over the existing tab
strip (`enum Pane { Tabs(group), Split(axis, children, sizes) }`), each `OpenTab` belonging to one group:
keeps J10 and the tint, costs the workspace refactor; or (b) move the tabs into the kit's `DockArea`:
splitting comes for free, J10 and the tint must be redone on its `TabPanel`. Read the kit's `dock/` first
and decide in `docs/DECISIONS.md`. First step either way: every use of `self.tabs` / `self.active_tab`
goes through the focused group.

**Tests.** UI: split by menu and by drop on each edge; closing the last tab of a group; the focused group
drives run, close and the status bar; the layout survives a restart; a production tab keeps its tint in a
split. **Capture:** a query and a table side by side, light and dark.

Done: (a), D-092: a pane tree of Kerno's own, `workspace/panes.rs` (`Layout<K>`: `Pane::Tabs(Group)` /
`Pane::Split { axis, children, shares }` and the focused group; pure, unit-tested), over the existing strip; the
kit's `DockArea` would have taken the order and the drawing of the tabs that J10, K1, K2, K4, K15 and the restore
rely on. The workspace keeps one flat `tabs` list (every tab of every group, put back in the layout's order by
`sync_tabs` after each change, so an index still reads left to right and everything that walks the tabs, K1's
quit question, `tabs_on`, `drop_gone_connections`, the transaction ticker, the restore, still covers every
group); `active_tab` is gone, `active_index()` / `active_open()` are the focused group's shown tab; the tab
subscriptions are keyed by tab id. **Splitting**: "Split right", "Split down", "Move to the other group" (with
three or four groups "Move to the next group" and "Move to the previous group") at the end of each tab's menu;
"Split Right" ⌘⌥⇧L, "Split Down" ⌘⌥⇧I, "Move Tab to the Next Group" ⌘⌥⇧M, "Move Tab to the Previous Group",
"Focus Next Group" ⌘⌥⇧N, "Focus Previous Group" ⌘⌥⇧P (Ctrl+Alt+Shift on Linux; group Tabs of the palette, Window
menu; K20's conflict and reserved-key tests cover them through `K19_DEFAULTS`). With one group, Move splits right.
A tab's title is dragged (GPUI's `on_drag`, a `DraggedTab`): over a group's content the outer 35 % of a side
(the kit dock's rule) shows half of the content in the drop colours with "Split to the left" / "to the right" /
"Split up" / "Split down", the middle "Move to this group"; dropped, it makes the group there, or joins; on a
strip it goes before the tab it is dropped on (in its own strip that reorders: the plan's "reorders as today" was
wrong, a tab could not be moved before), after the tabs last. **Limits said**: four groups at most (any tree;
same-axis splits become siblings, a split of one child folds away): the menu greys Split with "four groups at
most: close the tabs of one first", the commands and a refused drop say it in a notification, a drag over an
edge then only offers "Move to this group"; a tab alone in its group is not split from it ("the tab is alone in
its group"), and dragged to its own group's edge shows nothing. **Divider**: the kit's `resize_handle` with
`on_drag_move` on the split, the shares as fractions (relative flex bases; not `h_resizable`, whose pixel state
cannot take a stored size), 160 px at least each; a window too small shares what it has. A group whose last tab
closes goes and its share goes to its neighbour (the one before, else after), which is focused; the last tab of
the last group gives today's empty state. **Focused group**: the last clicked (capture-phase mouse down on the
group, whatever was clicked) or the one whose tab has the keyboard (checked each frame), so ⌘Enter, Commit, ⌘.,
⌘W never act on another group than the one typed in; marked by the accent line under its shown tab, another
group's shown tab underlined in the muted text colour (no bar). New tabs open at the end of its strip; the
toolbar, the status bar and the chrome's environment colour follow it; a production tab keeps its title tint and
its frame in its own group. Every group's shown tab is revealed (a restored data tab there reads its rows) and
only tabs shown nowhere stop refreshing; the administration tab's auto-refresh, which followed "the tab shown",
now runs while it is shown in any group (`shown_tab` is a list; `admin/tab.rs`). **Per group**: J10's layout
(single row, rows, side list) applies to each strip; each strip keeps its scroll; its "All open tabs" button
lists every group's tabs, group after group (still a way to every tab); Close others / to the left / to the
right / all close within the tab's group (Close All Tabs from the palette: the focused group's); Next / Previous
Tab go round the focused group. **Stored**: setting `tabs.layout` beside `tabs.active`, written with the tabs,
read by `Startup` off the UI thread: the tree with each tab as its index among the stored tabs, each group's
shown tab, the shares; the focused group is `tabs.active`'s. None (before K19) or unreadable: one group;
`Layout::restore` mends one that no longer fits (unknown and repeated tabs dropped, groups left empty closed,
tabs not placed added to the first group, shares that make no sense made even, more than four groups made one);
no start error added. Tests: unit `workspace::panes::tests::{one_group_behaves_as_the_old_strip,
a_split_makes_a_group_at_the_edge_and_focuses_it, a_split_is_refused_for_a_lone_tab_and_past_four_groups,
a_group_left_empty_closes_and_its_neighbour_takes_its_space, a_nested_split_folds_back_when_it_loses_a_group,
a_tab_moves_between_groups_and_reorders_in_its_own, the_focus_goes_round_the_groups,
a_divider_moves_within_its_pair_and_keeps_a_minimum, a_layout_is_stored_and_restored,
a_layout_from_before_k19_or_broken_restores_as_one_group, the_drop_zones_are_the_outer_thirds}`; UI
`ui_tests::split::{split_right_and_down_by_command_menu_and_keys, a_tab_dropped_on_each_edge_of_a_group_splits_there
(native pointer events, the indicator read mid-drag), a_tab_dropped_on_a_strip_moves_there_and_within_a_strip_reorders,
closing_the_last_tab_of_a_group_closes_it_then_the_empty_state, closing_others_and_all_stays_within_the_group,
a_click_in_a_group_makes_it_the_one_keys_act_on, a_production_tab_keeps_its_tint_while_a_local_tab_is_focused,
the_layout_and_its_sizes_survive_a_restart (a divider dragged), an_old_or_broken_stored_layout_restores_as_one_group}`
and, on PostgreSQL, `quitting_lists_the_transactions_of_both_groups`,
`commit_and_run_act_on_the_focused_group_only` (a click in the left group's editor, ⌘Enter runs its text, Commit
ends its transaction only), `a_click_that_takes_no_keyboard_still_moves_the_keys_to_its_group` (the keyboard in
the top group's editor, a click on the bottom group's Run button, greyed while its `pg_sleep` runs, which keeps
the click to itself and takes no keyboard: ⌘. stops the bottom group's statement; this is why the group's click
also gives its tab the keyboard, else the next frame's keyboard rule handed the focus back to the top group). Each seen failing under one mutation, applied alone: closing the shown last tab no
longer stepping back; a split put on the wrong side; the group limit off by one; a closed group's share not
given to its neighbour; same-axis splits not folded; a moved tab's group not focused (the test focused the
other group first: without that the mutation passed); Previous Group going forward; a divider not stopped at its
minimum; even shares stored; tabs a stored layout does not place dropped; zones at 25 %; Split Right splitting
down; the drag's zone ignored; a strip drop always at the end; an emptied group kept; Close All closing every
group; the group's capture listener not focusing it; that listener not giving the group's tab the keyboard; only the focused group's content drawn; the layout not
written; the stored layout ignored at start; the quit question asked over the focused group only;
`active_index` taken from the last group instead of the focused one. Existing tests changed, no assertion relaxed: the `Fixture` reads `tabs.layout` and passes it to
`Workspace::new` as `main.rs` does (its new argument); `menu_palette`'s walk of the data-built menus (`every_entry`)
adds the groups' entries, so K13's check covers them; `the_new_defaults_are_bound_and_take_no_reserved_key` also
loops over `K19_DEFAULTS`. Steps: the group API and the rendering of groups landed together, so the existing suite
was first run green with the split code present and one group in use, not on the API refactor alone. Step 5 is
partial: the focus commands are done; small windows and drops in the rows and side-list layouts are not driven by
a test. A query tab in a group narrower than its toolbar (about 740 px: two side-by-side groups in a 1200 px
window with the sidebar) had the toolbar's last buttons cut off (run, the Run list): fixed at review, the
toolbar wraps to a second line (`flex_wrap` in `QueryTab::render_toolbar`;
`ui_tests::split::the_toolbar_of_a_narrow_group_keeps_run_in_reach`, seen failing without it). Found while there: with
no tab open, the scroll-into-view of the strip asked for an animation frame on every frame (no strip, so no
width to scroll in); it now waits for a tab. Not covered: a drop on a
strip in the rows or side-list layouts (the handlers are the same, only the single row is driven), a divider of
a column split dragged (a row's is), a window too small for the groups, the native menu's key column (the test
platform keeps none), the focused group's grey line against WCAG (it is the text colour the strip already uses);
the split adds no per-frame work beyond a few lookups over the groups, not measured with the bench. **Capture**
(to take): `m12-split-views-{light,dark}`, a query and a table side by side.

## The owner's request of 2026-11-20

"Three modes: dark, light and system, system by default, so the app follows a computer set to light by day and
dark by night. Add a configurable keybinding to switch between dark and light: a colleague does that often. And
a keybindings page in Settings with the list of what is possible, the possibility to change the bindings; having
plenty of keybindings in the app would be really useful." (translated)

### K20 — A theme switch, Settings ▸ Shortcuts, more default shortcuts (M)

**Now.** The three modes exist (`theme::Appearance`, Follow system by default, `Workspace::new` observes the
