window's appearance) with three palette commands, but nothing switches between light and dark. Shortcuts change
only in `keymap.json` (`actions::KeymapFile`, `effective_bindings`, `bind_keys`), read once at start; 145 of the
228 commands have no default key.

**Wanted.** A command that shows the opposite of the theme on screen and keeps it as the choice, with a default
key, in the palette and the View menu. A Settings page listing every command by group with its shortcuts, the
user's marked, a filter (name, group, keywords, the shortcut as written or in words), change by recording the
next keystroke, add, remove, reset, reset all (asked), a conflict question (Replace or Cancel) where two commands
would both answer a key, changes applied at once and stored in the same file (other content kept, an unreadable
file said and never written). More default shortcuts from DBeaver's `plugin.xml` and macOS conventions, none on a
key of the kit, the system, vim mode, or behind Shift or Option on AZERTY.

Done: **Theme switch**: `toggle_theme`, "Theme: Switch Light and Dark", ⌘⇧D / Ctrl+Shift+D, View menu (beside
the three theme entries) and palette; it reads `cx.theme().is_dark()` (what is shown) and stores Light or Dark,
so Follow system showing dark gives Light; it works with Settings open, whose Theme row redraws at once. The OS
appearance is still followed in Follow system only, now `workspace::system_appearance_changed` (pinned by a test
that calls it: GPUI's `simulate_appearance_change` is crate-private). **Settings ▸ Shortcuts** (`shortcuts_view.rs`,
the last section so K13's indices hold; one row of `ROWS`, found by "keymap", "shortcut", "hotkeys"; "Keyboard
Shortcuts…" in the Kerno menu and the palette): "Click a shortcut to change it, or Add for one more." with the
rule ("A shortcut needs ⌘ or ⌃ with a letter, a digit or a sign (⌥ or ⇧ alone would type a character); ⌥ is enough
with an arrow, Enter, Tab or another named key; F1 to F24 work alone. A key the text fields use (⌘C, ⌘Z, ⌥←…) is
kept for them. Escape cancels."), a filter ("Filter by
name, group or shortcut (⌘K, cmd k)", "No command matches “…”" with Clear filter), Reset all… ("Every command gets
its default shortcuts back: N changed commands lose their changes." Reset all / Cancel), then the groups in
palette order: each command's name, "Changed" when the file has its entry, its shortcuts as buttons (click:
"Press a shortcut…" and under the row "Press the new shortcut with its modifiers; Escape cancels."), × to remove
one, Add (Add a shortcut when it has none), Reset to default (tooltip: the defaults). A refused key says why
under the row ("J types a character: hold ⌘ or ⌃ with it."; for a command with no key context, a key the kit
binds, read from the live keymap: "⌘C is the text fields' Copy: a command that works everywhere would take it
from them.", since GPUI ranks a context-free binding with the deepest context and Kerno's come after the kit's)
and recording goes on; showing another section or searching stops it; a taken key asks ("⌘B is
already the shortcut of “Toggle Sidebar” (View). Replace takes it from it and gives it to “About Kerno”." Replace
/ Cancel). The keystroke is caught by `App::intercept_keystrokes` before any binding or field; the page holds the
subscription and itself weakly in it. Every change goes through `actions::apply_keymap` (Kerno's bindings tagged
with a `KeyBindingMetaIndex`, the kit's kept, the menu bar set again) and a background writer (`changed_keymap`
on the file as it is, a temporary file renamed over it, in order); opening Settings reads and applies the file
(a hand edit included) or, while writes are pending, takes its last text; a file that cannot be used shows "The
keymap file cannot be used (…). Kerno keeps the shortcuts it started with and does not change the file: fix it,
then open Settings again." with Open keymap file. The check of the file at start reports one key on two commands
whose contexts can both apply (the page's rule), not only within one context. Open Keymap File stays (Kerno and
Help menus, Settings' footer, now "Shortcuts are changed in Shortcuts, above, or by hand in the keymap file (read
when Kerno starts and when Settings opens)."). **New defaults** (D-089 has the table and the sources): ⌘⌥→ /
⌘⌥← and Ctrl+PageDown / PageUp (tabs), ⌘⌥W (close others), ⌘⇧T (reopen), ⌃⌥⇧K / ⌃⌥⇧R (commit, roll back, beside
⌃4 / ⌃8), ⌃⇧O (messages), ⌃⌥⇧B (administration), ⌘⌥Y (refresh the tree), ⌘⌥N (set NULL), ⌘⌥Z (revert rows),
⌘⌥⇧U (ER diagram); the keymap example's sample moved from `secondary-shift-t` to `secondary-alt-shift-t`, which
⌘⇧T now holds on macOS. Tests: unit `actions::tests::{the_new_defaults_are_bound_and_take_no_reserved_key,
no_two_defaults_conflict_on_either_system, conflicts_follow_the_keystroke_and_the_contexts,
a_recorded_keystroke_round_trips_through_the_file, only_keys_that_do_not_eat_typing_are_recorded,
changing_the_keymap_keeps_the_rest_of_the_file, commands_are_found_by_name_and_by_shortcut}` (and
`the_new_commands_have_their_keys` extended); UI `shortcuts::{the_shortcuts_page_lists_the_commands_and_filters_them,
recording_a_shortcut_binds_it_at_once_and_writes_the_file, a_shortcut_already_taken_asks_and_replace_moves_it,
reset_gives_the_default_back_and_reset_all_asks, an_invalid_keymap_file_is_said_and_never_overwritten,
closing_settings_while_recording_gives_the_keyboard_back, a_key_of_the_text_fields_is_kept_for_them,
another_section_stops_the_recording, enter_is_recorded_once_for_a_command_of_the_grids,
the_global_defaults_over_a_key_of_the_kit_are_known, the_theme_switch_shows_the_opposite_and_stores_it,
the_system_appearance_is_followed_only_in_follow_system, the_settings_search_finds_the_shortcuts_page,
the_new_defaults_win_in_the_editor_and_in_a_text_field}`, each run five times; `a_bad_keymap_file_is_reported_and_the_defaults_stay`
now pins that binding again replaces (it relied on keys stacking). Seen failing under a mutation, one per run:
the reserved-keys test and the editor/field test with the switch on ⌘⇧Z (the kit's Redo; the field test failed
only once it read the live keymap: a "winning binding" check cannot see a kit key, Kerno's global binding always
wins); no conflict among defaults with Reopen on `secondary-shift-t` (Ctrl+Shift+T on Linux); the conflict test
with `QueryTab`/`DataTable` taken out of `NESTED`; the round trip with Shift dropped by `file_form`; the recording
rule with Alt enough before a letter; the file test with defaults written as an entry; the filter test with ⌘ not
read as `cmd`; the bad-keymap test with rebinding keeping the old tagged bindings (the Replace test passes under
that one: the later binding wins anyway); the page's filter not applied; the change not applied live, then not
written; a taken key assigned without asking; Reset sending no shortcut instead of the defaults; an unreadable file
shown as empty; the recorder holding the page strongly (⌘K eaten after Settings closed); the switch reading the
setting instead of the screen; the system followed in Light or Dark; the page drawn whole in the search's results;
the kit check removed; `show` keeping the recording; Enter refused for a grid command too; a key Kerno's defaults
already took from the fields (⌘↵) refused all the same; Kerno's own bindings counted as the kit's; the new Commit
key put last (then shown instead of ⌃4). Two global defaults already win over a key of the kit, by design and now
pinned: ⌘↵ (the kit's Confirm) and ⌘. (the editor's ToggleCodeActions); the page lets another command take them. Not covered: the native menu's
key column (the test platform keeps no key equivalents: by code); a real OS appearance change; a keymap file
changed by hand while Kerno runs applies only when Settings opens or at start (no watcher). The keys added
beside an older default come first, so menus and tooltips (the last key bound) still show ⌃4, ⌃8, ⌃⇥. Not changed, for the
owner: the commands of context `ConnectionList` (Edit F4, Delete, Open Enter on a connection) bind keys in a
context no element declares (a grep finding: only `actions.rs` and `main.rs` name it; whether the tree's own keys
cover connection rows was not checked); several new defaults use `cmd-` (Super on Linux), which a Linux desktop
may take; a keymap file written before K20 may now be reported at start (the wider conflict rule, or a key that
became a default) until its owner settles it on the page. **Capture:** the page with a conflict
question, light and dark.

## The first outside user's feedback (2026-11-20)

A colleague of the owner used Kerno for the first time, beside DBeaver (translated): "I had not understood that
Fill filled the form below." "In the fill, when you go from postgres to mysql the placeholder does not change."
"There is no load more: that is nice in DBeaver." "The table is hard to read, all white" (light theme). And:
"it is ultra fast, what a joy": nothing may get slower.

### K21 — The URL field, the fetch at the end said, a light grid with structure (M)

**Now.** The new-connection dialog's "Fill from a connection URL" (`connections/dialog.rs`) had the fixed
placeholder `postgres://user:password@host:5432/database` whatever the engine (the Database field's followed
it), a "Fill" button and Enter, and nothing saying what Fill does; `ConnectionForm::apply_url` switches the
engine to the URL's, replaces the fields the URL names and keeps the others, the port becoming the engine's
standard one when the URL has none. A query result already fetched its next page when scrolled near its end
(the kit's `load_more`, 20 rows from the end; `Grid::load_more`, since M5), but its footer said only "first
1,000 rows; more on the server" and the same "N rows so far" whether a page was on its way or the statement had
let go. A table's data (`data/tab.rs`) is read by pages: "Page 1" and two small ghost buttons "Previous" /
"Next" at the far right of the footer, after Record, Value and the selection figures, and no stripes at all
(`Grid::render_tr` striped only grids of no table). Light grid tokens: stripe `#FAFBFC` (1.04:1 on white),
lines `#E8EBEF` (1.20), header `#F6F7F8` (1.07).

**Wanted.** The URL example follows the engine; what Fill does is said, and a pasted URL fills by itself; a
result's "more on the server" and "fetching more" readable at a glance, a setting to turn the automatic fetch off
as DBeaver has; a table's next page obvious at the end of a page; rows and columns visible in the light grid,
text AA, brand colours where BRAND.md puts them, the dark theme as it is.

Done: **URL field**: `url_placeholder(engine)` at open (an edited MySQL connection shows `mysql://…:3306`) and on
every engine change (`show_form`); beside its label (on the same line: a first version under the field made the
dialog taller and pushed Save out of a short window, which `environments::a_user_environment_is_added_edited_and_picked_for_a_connection`
caught) "Paste a URL, or click Fill to use the clipboard's: it fills the fields below." (Fill on an empty
field takes the clipboard's URL since the owner's test of the build, D-093), after a fill "Filled from the URL: check the fields below."; "Fill" has the tooltip "Fill the fields below from the URL; the fields it does not name keep their values". A paste fills at once (`fills_on_paste`: one edit of more than one character, from the input's
`InputEditsEvent`, leaving a URL with a host, or text with `://` that cannot be read, whose reason is then
shown); typing never fills by itself. `apply_url` unchanged (D-093 says why its port rule stays). The engine
cards got `test_support()` so a UI test can click them. **Results**: the footer says "1,000 rows fetched, more on
the server", "1,000 rows fetched, fetching more…" while a page is on its way (Stop already cancels it: the
toolbar's `waiting` counts rows on their way), "N rows so far" kept for a statement that let go. The trigger is
the kit's (20 rows from the end, in the list's layout callback); the delegate's `has_more` now also checks the new
setting `fetch_on_scroll` (Settings ▸ Grid, "Fetch the next rows when scrolling reaches the end of a result", on,
found by "load more", "auto fetch", "next page") and that the rows are not sorted in Kerno on an incomplete result
(a `SHOW`: new rows would land after the sorted ones). One page per reach of the end: `Grid::has_more` is false
from the request until the page is here. Keyboard: Page Down and the arrows reach the end by scrolling, so they
fetch the same way (tested with Page Down); End, in the grid's cell mode, goes to the last column (the kit's
`SelectLast`), and Cmd+Down is bound to nothing in the grid: neither reaches the last row, so they fetch nothing.
**Table data**: "Page 2 of 13" ("of about 13" on an estimate, "Page 2" uncounted), "Previous 1,000 rows", "Next
1,000 rows" outlined while there is a next page (ghost and greyed when not; on a counted table the last page's
count, "Next 50 rows", pinned in `a_table_is_read_in_pages_sorted_and_filtered_by_the_server`); the footer wraps in a narrow group of
a split. **Light grid** (tokens only, `assets/themes/kerno.json`; table of values in D-093): stripe `#F4F6F8`
(1.08:1), lines `#DDE1E6` (1.31), header `#EEF0F2` (1.14), hover `#EBEEF1` (1.17, 1.08 on the stripe); dark
unchanged; every grid striped, a table's data and an editable result included (the `self.table.is_none()` guard
removed). Tests: unit `connections::dialog::tests::{the_url_placeholder_follows_the_engine,
only_a_pasted_url_fills_by_itself}`, `theme::tests::the_grid_rows_and_columns_are_visible` (floors per theme, and
the selected cell's frame at 3:1 on the stripe and the hover, since its Lime wash is 1.01:1 on the stripe),
`settings::tests::{settings_round_trip_and_default, missing_and_unknown_fields_do_not_lose_the_rest}` (the field,
on by default); UI `ui_tests/feedback.rs`:
`the_url_example_follows_the_engine`, `editing_a_mysql_connection_shows_a_mysql_example`,
`a_pasted_url_fills_the_form_and_a_typed_one_waits`, `reaching_the_end_fetches_the_next_page_once` (PostgreSQL and
MySQL, `big_table` with a `LIMIT 2500`: the scroll, then Page Down to the last rows),
`a_complete_result_or_a_page_on_its_way_is_not_asked_again`,
`the_setting_off_or_rows_sorted_here_fetch_nothing_by_themselves`, `a_tables_pager_says_where_it_is_and_what_next_shows`
(`shop.orders`, read only, pages of 100). Each seen failing under one mutation: the
placeholder at open fixed to PostgreSQL's; `show_form` not setting it; a paste never filling; any edit filling;
the setting ignored; the sorted-here gate removed; `fetch_more` without its `has_more` guard (2,500 rows instead
of 2,000); the delegate's `has_more` not asking `Grid::has_more`; the old footer wording; the delegate's `has_more` always false; a light stripe back at `#FAFBFC`; MySQL's example on port 5432; `fills_on_paste` ignoring the edit's
size; the pager's "Next" without its row count; the page without "of N"; the last page's count read from an estimate instead of
an exact count; the setting off by default. Re-run after the hint moved: a paste never filling. The summary assertion of the existing `a_result_header_shows_name_and_type_whole_and_keeps_them_when_read_again` follows the new wording.
Not tested: the stripes on a table's grid (styles are not read by the UI tests; the theme test pins the
tokens); Stop during a page fetched by the scroll (by reading: `stop()` finishes every incomplete grid, which
also ends "fetching more…"); the fetch in a narrow split group or with the value panel open, and the scroll
position after a page (the kit moves it only when the row count drops: by reading its code). Limits: the stacked results' headers (J11) still say "first N rows"; the record view does not fetch at its last
row (DBeaver's does); Ctrl+Alt+N (Fetch more) is a query-tab key and does not turn a table's page; the data tab
has no "end of page" line inside the grid, only its pager; the 1M-row and 300-column benches were not run (the
hot path is untouched: nothing new runs before the kit's threshold comparison). **Captures** (to take):
`m12-light-grid-{light,dark}` (a table's data and a result, striped), `m12-connection-url-{light,dark}` (MySQL
chosen, the hint).

## The owner's request of 2026-11-20: legacy MySQL

"We support MySQL but we have no support for legacy MySQL (MySQL 5) as DBeaver has. We need it. It should not be
too long if you look at the logic in DBeaver's repository and do the same." (translated) The first outside user
had connected Kerno to a MySQL 5.1 database and hit an error at once.

### K22 — Legacy MySQL servers (5.1 to 5.7), by server version (M)

**Now.** One MySQL engine, written and tested against 8.4 (and MariaDB 11). Run against real 5.x servers, it
failed at once in several places: on 5.1 the navigator's listing of a database (`information_schema.PARAMETERS`
does not exist before 5.5.3: "Unknown table 'PARAMETERS' in information_schema", most likely the user's error);
on every 5.x the isolation level (`@@transaction_isolation`, 5.7.20: error 1193), what a drop breaks
(`VIEW_TABLE_USAGE`, 8.0.13: 1109), the accounts (`mysql.role_edges`, 8.0: 1146), the settings
(`performance_schema.variables_info` / `session_variables`: 1146) and the lock waits (`data_lock_waits`, 8.0:
1146); before 5.6.5 a read-only connection (`SET SESSION TRANSACTION READ ONLY`: "Unknown system variable
'TRANSACTION'", the connection refused) and EXPLAIN (`FORMAT=JSON`: 1064); before 5.5.3 any literal holding a
backslash (`_utf8mb4 X'…'`: 1064, which reaches every edit, filter and default with a backslash); EXPLAIN
ANALYZE everywhere before 8.0.18. TLS: 5.1 and 5.5 speak TLS 1.0 ("peer is incompatible:
ServerDoesNotSupportTls12Or13", "received corrupt message of type UnknownProtocolVersion"), 5.7.44's official
build TLS 1.2 with DHE only ("received fatal alert: HandshakeFailure"): even `prefer` failed. An account with a
pre-4.1 password hash got the client library's raw "Driver error: `mysql_old_password` plugin is insecure and
disabled by default". The test connection said "Connected to 5.1.73".

**Wanted.** The same MySQL engine connects to, browses, queries, edits and administers MySQL 5.1, 5.5, 5.6 and
5.7 as it does 8.x, tested against real servers, and says in words what an old server cannot do (naming the
release it needs and the server's) instead of a raw SQL error.

Done: **One engine that adapts** (D-094): `Capabilities::of(version, is_mariadb)` in
`crates/kerno-mysql/src/capabilities.rs`, 26 named facts with the release that brought each (DBeaver's version
gates, `MySQLDataSource`/`MySQLUtils`/`MySQLPlanAnalyser`, completed from the manuals), held by the dialect
(`MysqlDialect::for_server`, from the version the session reads at connect) and read by the introspection, the
DDL writer and the administration queries; the version checks scattered before moved into it. Fixes, by layer:
**connect**: `prefer` goes on without TLS when the server's TLS is older than rustls accepts
(`connect::old_server_tls`, the typed TLS error); `require`/`verify-*` say "This server only offers an old TLS that
Kerno does not accept." with the next step (`kerno_core::error::OLD_SERVER_TLS`); a pre-4.1 hash is refused with
"The account “…” has an old password hash that Kerno does not accept." and the administrator's fix
(`OLD_PASSWORD_HASH`); the session's isolation reads `@@tx_isolation` before 5.7.20; literals with a backslash use
`_utf8` before utf8mb4. **Read-only**: `Dialect::read_only_session_sql` is an `Option` (PostgreSQL and the
connector follow); before 5.6.5 the connection opens, Kerno's refusal of writes stays, and the app warns on
connect (`ConnectionsEvent::Warned`, a warning notification) that the server would accept the writes Kerno does
not recognise, suggesting an account that may only read. **Navigator**: routines listed without arguments on 5.1;
generated columns from 5.7.6 (the gate was 5.7.0); what a drop breaks searches the view definitions before 8.0.13,
as on MariaDB. **Structure**: a rename index before 5.7.1, a column renamed alone before 8.0.3, an invisible
column before 8.0.23 are refused in words; imported JSON is `longtext` before 5.7.8. **Plans**:
`Dialect::explain_unavailable` ("A query plan (EXPLAIN FORMAT=JSON) needs MySQL 5.6.5 or later; this server is
MySQL 5.5.62.", "EXPLAIN ANALYZE needs MySQL 8.0.18 or later; …"), checked in `QueryTab::launch` once the session is open (a
warning notification, nothing runs; a plan asked while the tab connects is checked when it is open): the
toolbar, menus, palette and keys all go through it. **Administration**:
accounts read the `mysql.user` columns each release has (plugin 5.5.7, expiry 5.6.6, locked 5.7.6, roles 8.0);
settings from `performance_schema` without the source on 5.7, from `information_schema` before 5.7.6; lock
waits from the `INNODB_*` tables on 5.5 to 5.7, none on 5.1 ("not supported: the locks of this server").
**Edits**: on MyISAM (the default engine before 5.5.5, still there on 8.x) a failed apply's rollback warns 1196;
`apply_edits` and the in-transaction variant then add "The statements before this one stayed applied: the table
does not support transactions (a MyISAM table, for example), so the server could not undo them."
(`kerno_core::sql::edits::KEPT_CHANGES`). Outside strict mode (the default before 5.7) a value too long, out of
range, or with a character the column cannot hold is stored changed with only a warning: `run_each` takes the
warnings 1264, 1265, 1292 and 1366 as the statement's failure, so the apply rolls back and says "the server would
store another value than the one written: Data truncated for column 'c' at row 1". **Naming**: `ServerInfo::named` gives "MySQL 5.1.73" (PostgreSQL's
banner unchanged) for the test line, the status and the AI facts. **Test servers**: compose profile `legacy`,
`kerno-mysql51` (53351), `kerno-mysql55` (53355), `kerno-mysql56` (53356), `kerno-mysql57` (53357), official
`mysql:5.5/5.6/5.7` images pinned by digest under `platform: linux/amd64`, and 5.1 built from Oracle's archived
binaries (`tests/fixtures/mysql51`, MD5 and SHA-256 checked); a fixture every release accepts
(`tests/fixtures/mysql-legacy`: InnoDB tables with keys and foreign keys, views, a function, a procedure with IN
and OUT, a trigger, an event, a table without a key, a MyISAM table with FULLTEXT, every 5.1 type, an account with
a pre-4.1 hash on 5.1 and 5.5, `utf8` text); TLS with a traditional-form copy of the test key (yaSSL reads no
PKCS #8); `scripts/test-env.sh up --legacy`, `kerno_core::testing::legacy_mysql()`, `scripts/check.sh` starts
them. A quirk met on the way: 5.1 and 5.5 drop a NOT PRESERVE event when they restart (under emulation), so
the fixture's event is PRESERVE.

Tests: unit `capabilities::tests::{each_fact_starts_at_its_release, the_tested_servers,
missing_features_are_said_with_both_versions}`, `admin::tests::{every_view_has_a_query_on_both_engines,
each_list_reads_the_table_the_server_has, accounts_read_the_columns_each_release_has}`,
`kerno_core::schema::tests::servers_are_named_with_their_product`,
`session::tests::a_version_without_its_patch_is_the_newest_of_its_release` (Aurora's `8.0.mysql_aurora.…` is not
taken for 8.0.0), two new cases in
`kerno_core::error::tests::each_failure_is_told_in_plain_words`; integration `kerno-mysql` `legacy::{
a_legacy_server_connects_and_runs_what_every_session_runs, a_read_only_connection_opens_and_is_read_only_where_the_server_can,
an_old_tls_is_left_by_prefer_and_explained_by_the_strict_modes, an_old_password_hash_is_refused_in_plain_words,
results_stream_and_a_statement_is_cancelled, the_catalog_is_read_on_every_legacy_server,
every_type_round_trips_through_an_edit, a_failed_apply_says_when_the_table_could_not_be_rolled_back,
a_value_the_server_would_change_is_not_applied,
the_administration_lists_run_on_every_legacy_server, what_a_drop_or_a_rename_reaches,
plans_where_the_server_has_them_and_words_where_it_does_not, the_structure_editor_writes_what_the_server_takes}`,
thirteen, each on 5.1.73, 5.5.62, 5.6.51 and 5.7.44 (about 2 s for the four); UI
`legacy::a_legacy_server_is_named_warned_about_and_spared_a_plan_it_lacks` (5.5, five runs; also a plan asked
while the connection is closed, checked once it opens: the check sits in `QueryTab::launch`, after the connect
on execute). The MySQL 8.4 and
MariaDB 11 suite passes unchanged. Seen failing under a mutation, one per run, each with the server's own error
where a gate was flipped to "every server has it": `transaction_isolation_variable` (1193), `utf8mb4` (1064 on
5.1), `read_only_transactions` (5.1 refuses the connection, 1193), `parameters_table` (1109), `roles` (1146),
`innodb_lock_tables` (1109 on 5.1), `password_expiry` (1054 on 5.1), `performance_schema_variables` (1146),
`view_table_usage` (1109), `explain_json` in `explain_unavailable` (1064 on 5.1), `rename_column` and
`rename_index` (1064: the written statement is run), `json` (1064 on the `CREATE TABLE`); `old_server_tls`
answering false (prefer fails on 5.1); the strict-mode wording arm removed (the bare TLS error); the
`MysqlOldPasswordDisabled` arm removed (the raw "Driver error"); `said_if_kept` never adding the sentence; `VALUE_CHANGED` emptied (the cut value applied on 5.1); the explain
check kept only where the session is already open (the connect-on-execute case sends the plan, 1064);
`ServerInfo::named` returning the raw version; in the app, the `Warned` event not emitted, the explain guard
removed, the status built from the raw version.

Not done (also in `docs/ROADMAP.md`): routine arguments on 5.1; a plan tree from the classic `EXPLAIN` table
before 5.6.5 (the editor still runs a typed `EXPLAIN …` and shows the server's table); a column renamed alone
before 8.0.3 (the producer of `TableChange::RenameColumn` would have to carry the column); the tools: `mysqldump`
8.4 dumps 5.5 to 5.7 (checked in a throwaway `mysql:8.4` container on the compose network, no extra option
needed) but finds no columns in a 5.1 table ("Skipping dump data for table …, it has no fields", exit 2), so 5.1
needs a 5.x client as the local client, which the wizard's version check already flags ("other version") without
saying why; MariaDB before 11 untested. **Capture:** none (no new screen; the warnings are notifications).

## The owner's request of 2026-11-20: the AI assistant's endpoints

The owner showed Settings ▸ AI (a checkbox, an Endpoint field with two example addresses, a Model field with
"List models", an API key, "What was sent") and said (translated): "For now it is not great. First, tell me
what it is for. Then make the connection to local models easier. At work we have two DGX Spark machines with
models on them (Qwen, GLM, DeepSeek…); I have a kit on my computer that configures them, I use the models with
omp, etc. I want the integration and the connection to that to be simple." The models at work are reached
through an OpenAI-compatible LiteLLM proxy on the company LAN, over HTTPS with a key, declared for omp in
`~/.omp/agent/models.yml` by the company's kit (in this repository, `llm.example.lan` stands for it).

### K23 — What it is for, saved endpoints found, imported or typed, a model picker, reasoning left out (M)

**Now.** Settings ▸ AI held one address (two example buttons, Ollama and LM Studio), one model typed or picked
from chips after "List models", one key, and a paragraph about what is sent placed under the switch, nothing
saying what the assistant gives. The client trusted Mozilla's roots only (`webpki-roots`), so a server whose
certificate comes from a company's internal authority was refused even when the computer trusts it, with
"The request to … failed: …". A chat request had 60 s. `chat` took `message.content` whole: a model that writes
its reasoning between `<think>` and `</think>` had it shown, and Insert took the first SQL block, which could be
inside the reasoning.

**Wanted.** The page says what the assistant is for before any field; several named endpoints, one active,
switched in one click in Settings and in the panel; three ways to add one (Detect on this computer, Import from
omp, pi or the environment, by hand), each with Test in plain words; a model picker remembered per endpoint;
reasoning never inserted; a timeout fit for a LAN model, Stop still immediate; today's settings kept; J17's
contract intact.

Done: **What it is for**: `ai_settings_view::PURPOSE` at the top of the page, before the switch ("Turned on, the
assistant does three things, only when you ask: it writes SQL from a sentence (inserted in the editor, never run
by itself), explains a query or its plan, and explains an error with a proposed fix. Find it in the editor's
right-click menu, the command palette and Query ▸ AI Assistant (“AI: …”), and the “Explain … with AI” buttons of
a plan or an error. It sends the endpoint you choose the names of your schemas, tables and columns with their
types; rows only when you tick “Include sample rows” for that request."), checked against the code (editor menu
entries, four palette commands, Query ▸ AI Assistant, the plan's and the error's buttons); `OFF_NOTICE`, what an
"AI: …" command says while off (the panel never opens while off), says the same in one sentence. **Endpoints**
(D-095): `AiSettings.endpoints` (name, base, model, models with labels), the active one mirrored in `endpoint` /
`model` so every request and an older Kerno read what they read before; one endpoint per base, its key
`ai/<base>` as before. Each row: Use / Active, "name · model", "address · this computer / your network / the
internet", Test, Remove (its key removed from the secret store too), and, for a host other than this computer,
the warning worded by case with "Allow requests to …" / "Stop allowing"; under the rows the key of the active
endpoint; "No endpoint yet: Detect one on this computer, Import one from a tool already set up, or Add one by
hand." when there is none. **Detect on this computer** (`endpoints::detect`): 127.0.0.1 on Ollama 11434, LM
Studio 1234, llama.cpp 8080, vLLM 8000, LiteLLM 4000, Jan 1337, in parallel, 1.5 s each, on click only, refused
while off; "Ollama · 4 models · http://127.0.0.1:11434/v1" with Add ("needs a key" for a 401/403). **Import from
omp, pi or the environment** (`endpoints::import`, pure over a given home and environment): `~/.omp/agent/
models.yml` (else `.yaml`, through `serde-saphyr`, already in the tree), `~/.pi/agent/models.json`,
`OPENAI_BASE_URL` / `OPENAI_API_KEY`; `api: openai-completions` only, others listed as skipped with the reason,
a malformed file a note; a checkbox per provider ("Work models (omp) · https://llm.example.lan/v1 · 2 models ·
has a key"), "Import ticked": address, name and labelled models into the settings, the key into the secret store
(an environment variable resolved at import; `!command` never run; an unset variable or a command said: "… was
imported without a key: its key is the environment variable X, which is not set for Kerno."). The page names the
files and ports. **Add by hand**: name, address (with or without `/v1`, a trailing slash, even
`/chat/completions` pasted, no scheme: `https://`, or `http://` for this computer; "Kerno will call …/models and
…/chat/completions."), key. **Test**: one `GET /models`, "Answered in 42 ms · 12 models." or the failure, plain
words first then the client's message: host not found ("Is this computer on the network that serves it, or on
its VPN?"), "refused the connection on port N: is the server running on that port?", "gave no answer within 15
seconds", the certificate ("…presented a certificate this computer does not trust. It names “X” as its issuer,
a name Kerno could not verify…": reworded after the commit's security review, so that the name a server claims
is never a reason to trust it: the user is sent to the administrator for the authority's certificate and told
the connection may be intercepted), 401/403 ("check the API key saved for it in Settings → AI."), 404 ("check the address (most
servers end in /v1). If only the model list is missing, type the model's name."); no chat call (D-095). **Trust**:
the system's certificates (`rustls-native-certs`) instead of Mozilla's when the system gives any; the issuer of a
refused certificate read by a handshake that refuses it (`ai/tls.rs`). **Model**: the field of the active endpoint
with, under it, its list (labels and ids), filtered by what is typed, a click choosing; a typed id is taken;
each endpoint keeps its own; List models, Test and Import fill the list. **The panel**: an "endpoint · model"
dropdown at its top (the endpoints, then the active one's models), disabled while a request is on its way; the
key flag of the preview follows the active endpoint. **Reasoning**: `ai::answer_text` drops a reasoning field
(as before) and `<think>` in its three shapes; only reasoning is said. **Timeout**: chat 5 minutes, List
models / Test 15 s, Detect 1.5 s; Stop unchanged. **Settings search**: "endpoint", "detect", "import", "omp",
"litellm", "vllm", "llama.cpp", "reasoning" find the page. The Settings footer is K20's, on every page: left.

Tests: unit (kerno-core) `ai::endpoints::tests::{an_address_with_or_without_v1_and_a_slash_is_the_same,
the_warning_says_network_or_internet, the_settings_of_before_become_one_saved_endpoint,
switching_removing_and_choosing_a_model_keep_the_mirror, omp_providers_are_read_with_their_keys_and_others_skipped,
pi_providers_the_environment_and_a_broken_file, keys_are_resolved_as_omp_and_pi_do,
detect_lists_the_local_servers_that_answer}`, `ai::tests::{the_reasoning_is_left_out_of_the_answer,
a_certificate_the_system_does_not_trust_is_said_with_its_issuer}` (a TLS server on 127.0.0.1 with the test
certificate `scripts/test-env.sh` generates, `tests/fixtures/generated/tls/`: at review, so that no private key
is kept in the repository); `settings::tests::settings_round_trip_and_default` (the new
field); UI `ui_tests/ai_endpoints.rs`: `the_page_says_what_the_assistant_is_for_before_any_field`,
`import_lists_a_tools_providers_and_keeps_the_key_in_the_secret_store`,
`detect_lists_a_server_on_this_computer_and_adds_it`, `test_says_the_latency_or_the_failure_in_plain_words`,
`switching_the_active_endpoint_switches_where_the_next_request_goes`,
`the_model_is_picked_from_a_filtered_list_and_kept_per_endpoint`,
`the_settings_of_an_earlier_kerno_open_as_one_endpoint`, `an_answer_with_reasoning_inserts_only_its_sql`; and
`ui_tests/ai.rs::a_remote_endpoint_asks_first_and_plain_http_to_it_is_refused` rewritten for the form. Every
server is a local test server, every home a temporary directory. Each new test seen failing under one mutation:
no `/v1` added; only a final `.lan` label; the migration through the new normaliser; removing the active endpoint
leaving the mirror; no exact-name environment lookup; no `OPENAI_BASE_URL`; `!command` taken as a key; Detect
not checking the switch; no reasoning strip (core and UI); the issuer not read; the purpose text gone; the
imported key not stored; Detect without the ports given; the old 401 wording; the picker not switching; the
model list not filtered; `Settings::sanitized` without `adopt_legacy`. Not run: anything against a real LAN
endpoint (the LiteLLM proxy, an internal authority actually installed in the keychain), the container-backed AI
tests of `ui_tests/ai.rs` (their clicks on the settings page now scroll to the element first). **Captures** (to
take): `m12-ai-endpoints-{light,dark}` (two endpoints, one on the network, the Import list, the model list).

## The owner's request of 2026-11-20: the look of Settings

The owner sent a capture of Settings ▸ Appearance in the light theme (a title, the search, a row of ten text
tabs, three plain lists of radio buttons, a large empty area, the keymap line) and said (translated): "improve
the design in Settings, we can make it more beautiful and readable; just for the dark mode and so on, a bullet
list is not great, we can have small card buttons with animations, things like that."

### K25 — The look of Settings: side list, pages, panels, option cards (M)

**Now.** Ten text tabs in one row over a 400 px body; each choice among a few options a vertical list of radio
buttons; checkboxes with their label at the right, descriptions and override lines under them in one column;
pages of two rows ending in a large blank; a fixed body height that cut the footer in a 600 px window.

**Wanted.** A Settings dialog that reads as designed, in both themes, on every page, without changing what any
setting does, stores, or is found by.

**The visual system** (`settings_ui.rs`, D-098). Navigation: a side list of the ten sections (184 px, a glyph
and the name each, the shown one on the accent wash with its glyph in the accent, no bar), the page beside it;
the search above both and the keymap line under both stay in place, only the page scrolls (with the kit's
scrollbar). Spacing: 4 px inside a control, 8 px between a row's lines or a heading and its content, 12 px
between cards and columns, 20 px between sections. Type: a page's name at 1.25× the interface size, semibold,
and one line under it on what the page holds; a section's name semibold; rows at the small size; descriptions,
hints and override lines extra small and muted, an override line marked with an info glyph. Rows: one bordered
panel per run of rows, a line between rows, the name at the left and the control at the right (switches for
on/off settings, chips for the tab width, the formatter's options and the fonts' − / + / Reset), the lines under
the name. **Option cards** for a choice among a few visual options: cards side by side sharing the width, a drawn
preview (64 px, GPUI elements and theme colours only), a round mark and the name; chosen: the accent's frame
(Olive in light, Lime in dark) on its wash with a check in the mark, never colour alone; under the pointer a
stronger frame and a tint. Theme: a light window, a dark window and, for Follow system, the light window's left
half beside the dark one's right half, each in its own theme's colours (`theme::preview`, read from the theme
file whichever theme is on). Double click on a table: a grid, a list of columns with the key first, a tree node
open. Open tabs: one strip, two strips, a list at the side. Motion: 120 ms (the kit's fast duration), colours
and opacity only: a card's frame and fill follow selection and hover, the check fades in, a page fades in from
40 %; nothing moves on its own; with the system's "reduce motion" (read by the kit at start) it all lands at once.
Keyboard: a group of cards is one Tab stop (the chosen card), the arrows move the choice and the keyboard with it
(around the ends), Space and Enter choose, Tab goes on; the focus ring is the theme's ring (Olive / Lime); a
click never takes the keyboard from the search nor draws a ring. Tab from the search reaches the page shown (or
the setting found) before the side list.

**Page by page.** Appearance: three groups of cards under their headings, the double click's hint under its
heading. Fonts, Editor, Grid, Confirmations, Updates: their rows in panels; Editor's formatter a heading, a panel
of its four options and the sample. Local clients and Environments: the page's head says what the row of the
search says, then the view as it was; Environments' "Rules" heading the shared one. AI: the purpose as the page's
line, the switch and its note in a panel, the shared headings; logic unchanged. Shortcuts: the page's head over
the view, unchanged. Search: each section found under its glyph and name, its rows in panels as on the page,
cards working where they are found, then "Show the … page" for a section of rows (after them, so Tab from the
search still reaches the row first); the side list marks no section meanwhile. The footer says "in the list at
the left". The dialog is 860 px wide (the kit narrows it to the window) and as tall as the window allows up to
560 px, the same for every page.

Done: `settings_ui.rs` (new: `page_head`, `section_title`, `panel`, `nav_item`, `OptionCards` on Base's radio
and radio group, the three previews, `height`), `settings_view.rs` (the side list, the pages, the panels, the
cards, the switches, the search's link, the height), `ai_settings_view.rs` and `environments_view.rs` (headings,
the AI purpose and switch), `theme.rs` (`Preview`, `preview`), `kerno.json` (`switch.background`: the kit's
default track was 1.35:1 on white), `engine_logo.rs` (the Keyboard glyph). Every element id the tests and the
search use is kept (`appearance`, `table-double-click`, `settings-tab-layout` with their cards numbered 0 to 2,
`("settings-section", i)` on the side list's items, `settings-body`, `row-…`, `override-…`, `settings-found`,
`settings-open`); new: `<group>-check-<i>` (the chosen card's check), `("settings-found-open", i)`,
`settings-nav`, `settings-scroll`.

Tests: UI `ui_tests/settings_look.rs`: `a_card_applies_its_choice_stores_it_and_is_the_one_marked`,
`the_arrows_move_the_choice_and_tab_leaves_the_group`, `the_search_finds_a_card_setting_and_leads_to_its_page`,
`every_page_fits_the_dialog_and_scrolls_inside_it` (1200 × 800 and 900 × 600),
`no_card_is_cut_at_the_largest_text_size` (18 pt, both themes, 900 × 600); theme:
`the_theme_previews_read_the_theme_file`, and new pairs in `text_contrast_meets_wcag_aa` (text and muted text
on the accent wash and on the hover tint) and `non_text_contrast_meets_wcag` (the accent frame on the
background, the wash and the selected list row, the ring on the wash, the switch's track on the background and
the muted surface, its thumb on the track). Each seen failing under one mutation: no card marked chosen (three
UI tests fail); the arrows not handled; every card a Tab stop; no "Show the … page"; a fixed 560 px height (the
footer leaves a 600 px window); cards of a fixed 240 px; the page without its scroll container; the preview's
first two colours swapped; the kit's track colour (failed before the fix too: 2.86:1 on the muted surface, the
light track darkened to `#7A828E`). `ui_tests/environments.rs` scrolls to the last rule (`tap`): the page's head
moved it below the fold. **Captures**: taken offscreen while working (a temporary test with GPUI's headless
context, Metal's headless renderer and the real text system, not committed: it needs `gpui-pre-apple` as a dev-dependency), every page in
both themes at 1200 × 800 and 900 × 600 and a search; the final ones for `docs/screenshots/final/` are to take:
`m12-settings-{appearance,editor,search}-{light,dark}`.

## The owner's request of 2026-11-20, after 0.2.0: the update dialog

The owner opened his installed 0.1.0 after 0.2.0 was published: the update was offered only as a small text at
the right end of the status bar ("Kerno 0.2.0 is available · Update"). His words, translated: "next times, when
there is an update we should show a popup, because this is not visible enough; we should do as DBeaver does: a
window that opens at every launch unless one clicks to skip the version."

### K24 — A new version opens a dialog at launch, once per run, until updated or skipped (S)

**Now.** A timed check that found a newer version only set the status bar item. "Skip this version" (the
item's menu) stored the version, hid the item and kept the timed checks quiet about it.

**Wanted.** A dialog in the window, once per run, at every launch, until the user updates or skips that
version: what is new and what Update does on this system, Update / Later / Skip this version, a "What's new"
link that sends no request; the item kept in every case; the skip shown and undone in Settings; no key acting
in it, never over another dialog; nothing new sent.

Done: D-097. `Workspace::offer_update` (`workspace/updates.rs`), called by `update_checked` for a timed check that
found a version: nothing if the dialog was shown this run (`Updates::offered`, in memory, for any version), if
the version is `Settings::skipped_update`, or if the item is no longer `Available(that version)`; over another
dialog, confirmation or the palette it waits in `Workspace::pending_update_offer` and `keep_focus` offers it on
the first frame with none open. The kit's dialog: "Kerno 0.3.0 is available", "You have 0.2.0.", and
`what_update_does(&Install, Version)`: for `Install::App` / `Install::Prefix` "Update downloads Kerno X from
GitHub and checks it against the release's SHA-256 checksums, replaces <the bundle> / Kerno's files in <the
prefix> and starts it again. If a tab holds an open transaction or pending changes, Kerno asks before it
quits."; for `Install::Page` "This copy of Kerno cannot replace itself (a package, an AppImage, a build, or a
folder Kerno may not write): Update opens the release page in your browser, to download it from there." Buttons: What's new (ghost, at the left: opens the release page, the dialog stays), Skip this version
(ghost), Later (outline), Update (primary); each closes the dialog first, then Update and Skip run the item's
own `update_action`, so the install is the item's path unchanged. `on_ok` returns false (Enter does nothing);
the keyboard is on the dialog, not a button (Space clicks nothing); Tab reaches the close button and Later first,
then What's new, Skip, and Update last, so Tab then Space closes it as Later; Escape, the close button and the
backdrop are Later. Skip no longer hides the item: a timed check of a skipped version sets it as for any other, with the
tooltip "You skipped this version: Kerno no longer asks about it at launch. Click to update anyway."
(`update_tooltip`, while the item offers it: not during a download, an install or after a failure). Settings ▸ Updates: under the switch, "Kerno X was skipped" and "Ask again" (clears the
skip). Help ▸ Check for Updates… keeps its notification, no dialog. No new request: the release notes are not
fetched.

Tests: UI `ui_tests/updates.rs`: `a_timed_check_that_finds_a_newer_version_opens_the_dialog_once_per_run` (the
texts, What's new opens the page with one request made in all, Later stores nothing, the 6-hour check of the
same version and of a newer one, the manual check, the same and an older version, the check off),
`later_asks_again_at_the_next_run_and_skip_stops_asking_for_that_version_only` (a next run reads the settings
back from the store and gets a new updater), `ask_again_in_settings_clears_the_skipped_version`,
`update_in_the_dialog_takes_the_path_of_the_status_bar_item` (a fake `Kerno.app` checked and swapped; the page
for `Install::Page`), `the_dialog_waits_for_another_dialog_to_close`,
`keys_typed_as_the_dialog_arrives_neither_install_nor_skip` (Enter, Space, typed text: no call, no page, no skip;
Escape gives the editor its keyboard back; at the next run Tab, a word, Space close it as Later); `the_update_item_answers_a_right_click_and_skip_is_remembered`
rewritten (the item stays after Skip, its tooltip). Each seen failing under one mutation: no offer from the
check; no once-per-run guard; the manual check offering too; the skip not read by the offer; Skip not stored;
Later skipping; Ask again doing nothing; the dialog's Update doing nothing; no wait for another dialog; the
waiting offer never made; Enter installing; every button a tab stop; Skip hiding the item; no tooltip. Not run: the real check and
install (the owner's installed app). **Captures** (to take): `m12-update-dialog-{light,dark}`.

## The owner's request of 2026-11-20: a simpler backup

The owner, on the backup wizard (translated): "we could improve the backup system: there are too many things
there; we should have a simplified mode by default, and an 'advanced settings' mode for the version we have now."

### K26 — A database's backup and restore open in a simple view; the wizard is its "Advanced settings…" (M)

**Now.** Back up… opened the three-step wizard (objects, a settings page of about twenty options, progress) for
the most common case, the whole database to a file; Restore… its settings page of options. At 600 px the
wizard's page (520 px) pushed its footer out of the dialog.

**Wanted.** One screen readable in five seconds for the common case, the wizard unchanged behind "Advanced
settings…", nothing weaker than before, the same file name and command in both.

**The simple backup.** Under the wizard's intro, one panel of rows: What ("The whole database “kerno”: 4
schemas, 37 tables and 6 views.", counted from the objects the wizard reads anyway; while they are read,
"(counting its tables…)"), To (the file the backup writes, in the monospace font: the folder of the last backup
that succeeded, else home, and the wizard's pattern `${database}-${timestamp}` with the format's extension;
"Choose…" opens the native save panel), Format (PostgreSQL: option cards "Compressed archive" (default, the
custom format) and "Plain SQL script", drawn previews, with one line on what the chosen one means; MySQL and
MariaDB: no choice, one line said from the options themselves: "A SQL script, run back by the mysql client and
read in one consistent snapshot (single transaction). It holds the tables and their rows, the procedures and
functions, the triggers."), Client (the wizard's client line with its warning, and "Local client…"). Then "Show
command" (the exact command, the password as `•••`). Footer: Advanced settings… at the left, Cancel and Back up
(primary) at the right. Back up and Enter run it; the progress page is the wizard's (log, time, Cancel, Show in
Finder, Back to the simple view).

**The simple restore.** File (the field and "Choose…", the open panel; under it what the file is, read from it:
"Custom archive, restored by pg_restore (made from the database “shop”)"), Into ("The database “x” of “y”" and,
in the warning colour, what a restore does: it writes on top of what the database holds, cannot be undone, is
asked first; one transaction on PostgreSQL, statement by statement on MySQL), Client. A file with client
commands shows the refusal and where to allow it (the wizard's Client commands step). Restore… asks with its
command (twice on production); Enter never starts it.

**Where each opens** (D-099). Simple: a database's Back up… and Restore… from the tree, Tools ▸ on a database or
the connection, the main toolbar, the palette, the menu bar. The wizard: Tools ▸ Back up… on a schema or a table,
Create new task…, a saved task (Run, Edit), the global backup, Execute script. The switch is inside the dialog's
state, one way: `only_tables` / `only_schemas` with objects, `set_purpose` other than a run, `apply`. The main
toolbar's and the menu bar's Tools ▸ use the active tab's database without its schema (a tab on `public` used to
back up `public` alone). "Simple view" in the wizard (a run's backup or restore only) goes back at once while
nothing it hides differs from its defaults, else names what would be reset and asks ("Keep the advanced
settings" / "Reset and go back").

Done: `backup_dialog.rs` (`View`, `show_command`, the switches, Enter through the dialog's `on_ok`, the page's
height from the window, "Simple view" in the wizard's footer, the last folder kept on success, test texts on the
command and the client warning), `backup_dialog/simple.rs` (new: the two simple pages and their footer,
`show_advanced`, `advanced_changes`, `reset_advanced`, `back_to_simple`, `enter`, the save panel and
`set_file`), `settings.rs` (`backup_folder`), `workspace.rs` (`tool_command`) and `workspace/toolbar.rs` (the
tab's schema dropped). Element ids kept: every wizard id, `backup-start` / `restore-start` / `backup-close` /
`backup-local-client` / `restore-file` / `restore-choose-file` / `restore-problem` / `restore-refusal` /
`backup-command-text` used by both views; new: `backup-simple`, `simple-what`, `simple-file`,
`simple-file-problem`, `simple-choose-file`, `simple-format` (cards 0 and 1, `simple-format-check-<i>`),
`simple-format-hint`, `simple-file-kind`, `simple-into`, `simple-restore-warning`, `simple-show-command`,
`backup-advanced`, `wizard-simple`, `simple-stay`, `simple-reset`, `simple-reset-text`.

Tests: UI `ui_tests/backup_simple.rs`: `a_backup_opens_simple_with_each_engines_defaults` (PostgreSQL, MySQL,
MariaDB: the sentence, the default card or the MySQL line, `--format=c` or `--single-transaction --routines`
without `--events`, the file shown is the one written, the command shown on demand with `•••` and no password,
the same masked file name and the same arguments as the wizard opened with its defaults),
`the_wizard_keeps_the_simple_choices_and_the_way_back_asks` (a `.sql` name from the save panel chooses the
script, the archive card gives `.dump`, the wizard opens on the objects with that file and format, the way back
at once, then with "the dump tool's options and the extra arguments" named, Keep, Reset, the objects named too),
`explicit_settings_and_tasks_open_the_wizard` (a table and a schema through `run_tool`, a new task, an applied
task, the global backup and Execute script in the wizard; a database and a restore simple; the toolbar's Tools ▸
Back up… on a tab bound to `shop`: simple, no `--schema`), `a_simple_backup_is_restored_by_the_simple_restore`
(the real tools, PostgreSQL and MySQL, scratch `kerno_test_k26_src` / `_dst` dropped at the end: Enter backs up
into the save panel's folder, which is kept for next time; the restore's file read as a custom archive; a stray
Enter starts nothing; Restore… asks, then three rows are there), `the_simple_views_keep_every_guard` (read-only:
no restore; a file's client commands refused with the way to allow them; production asks twice; an old client
said, Back up and Enter do nothing, "Local client…" opens the list), `the_backup_dialogs_fit_a_small_window`
(900 × 600: the simple backup with its command, the wizard's settings, the simple restore with a file: the
buttons under the page and inside the dialog). `ui_tests/tool_wizards.rs`: `Fixture::backup_dialog` now opens
the wizard explicitly (`show_advanced`, the "Advanced settings…" path) and `simple_dialog` the simple view;
`a_backup_tool_is_found_or_chosen_and_its_output_streams` asserts that the tree's Back up… opens simple. Each seen

## The owner's request of 2026-11-20: PostgreSQL extensions

The owner (translated): "we can improve the PostgreSQL extension system, I am not a fan of our way of doing
it." Asked what bothers him, he named all of: the tree is cluttered (about fifty "not installed" extensions mixed
with the installed ones under each database), nothing says what an extension is for (no description, no
search), too few actions (no schema or version to choose, no update, nothing on what an extension requires). He
approved "an extensions manager per database: a page with a search, each extension's description, filters
Installed / Available / To update, and Install, Update, Drop with the SQL shown first."

### K27 — An extensions manager per PostgreSQL database (M)

**Now.** "Extensions" under each database listed the installed extensions, then every available one muted with
"not installed"; an available one had "Install…" (`CREATE EXTENSION IF NOT EXISTS`, at the server's schema and
version), an installed one Drop. No description, no version choice, no update, no requirement shown.

**Wanted.** The tree keeps the installed extensions with their count. A tab per database lists every extension
the server has, with its description, state and versions; a search and filters with counts; the details of the
selected one (schema, relocatable, versions, requirements, who may install it), read from the catalog; Install…
(schema, version, requirements with `CASCADE`, said first), Update (default or chosen version), Move to
schema…, Drop and Drop with cascade (K14's list), each through the usual confirmation and the connection's rules,
a refusal in plain words first. Ways in: the folder (double click, Enter, its menu), an installed extension's
menu, Tools ▸ on a database, the palette. MySQL and MariaDB: nothing changes.

Done: D-100. Core: `Dialect::create_extension_sql(name, schema, version, cascade)`, `update_extension_sql`,
`move_extension_sql` (`Unsupported` by default); `ExtensionInfo` gains `versions`, `update_targets`, `requires`,
`relocatable`, `fixed_schema`, `superuser`, `trusted`, with `state()` (`ExtensionState::{Installed,
UpdateAvailable, Available}`), `needs_superuser()`, and `schema::compare_versions`. PostgreSQL:
`introspect::extensions` reads `pg_available_extensions`, the matching `pg_available_extension_versions` row,
every version, `pg_extension`, and `pg_extension_update_paths` for installed extensions only; `trusted` from
PostgreSQL 13 (`false` before, `superuser` alone deciding); the dialect writes the four statements. App:
`extensions_tab.rs` (new: `ExtensionsTab`, `Filter`, `Op`, `refusal`, `missing_requirements`,
`who_may_install`); `navigator/model.rs` (installed only, the folder's count once loaded; `NodeKind::Extension`
loses `installed`); `navigator/actions.rs` (`ManageExtensions`, `ShowInExtensions` instead of
`InstallExtension`, not writes; the folder's and an extension's default action; `ToolEntry::Extensions` beside
Foreign data wrappers… on a PostgreSQL database); `navigator/view.rs` (`NavigatorEvent::ManageExtensions`,
`refresh_extensions`, `has_selected_object`, K14's reader moved to the free `read_dependents`, the palette's
command on any node of a database); `workspace.rs` (`OpenTab::Extensions`, `open_extensions`, one tab per
connection and database, not restored, the tree refreshed after each statement, the palette command falling back
to the active tab's PostgreSQL database); `actions.rs`, `menu_palette.rs`, `main.rs` (the command
`install_extension` kept, now "Manage Extensions…", in the Navigator menu); `fdw_dialog.rs` (the new signature, without `IF NOT EXISTS`).
Element ids: `extensions-search`, `("extensions-filter", 0..4)`, `extensions-refresh`, `extensions-missing`,
`extensions-read-only`, `extensions-running`, `extensions-cancel`, `extensions-message`, `extensions-list`,
`("extension-row", i)`, `extensions-empty`, `extensions-error`, `extension-details`, `extension-schema`,
`extension-version`, `extension-install`, `extension-update`, `extension-update-to`, `extension-move`,
`extension-drop`, `extension-drop-cascade`.

Tests, each seen failing under the mutation in brackets: `kerno-postgres` unit
`dialect::tests::extension_and_enum_statements` (install with schema, version, cascade, odd names; update with and
without a version; move; drop) [no `CASCADE` written]; core `dialect::tests::sources_extensions_and_enums_are_engine_specific`
and MySQL's `dialect::tests::source_scripts_replace_what_show_create_printed` (the defaults) [the core default of
`move_extension_sql` answering `Ok`]; `schema::tests::extension_states_versions_and_who_may_install` [an update
offered without a path to the default version]; driver `generate::an_extension_is_installed_and_dropped` (updated)
[the drop writing `SELECT 1`; the installed version read from the default's column],
`extensions_say_what_they_need_install_with_cascade_update_and_move` (scratch database `kerno_test_ext_manager`:
descriptions and states, earthdistance requires cube and is untrusted and installs with it in a schema; citext at
its oldest version, updated along the server's path, moved) [no update paths read; the installed version read from
the default's column]; unit `extensions_tab::tests::missing_requirements_are_followed_through` [no walk through the
requirements' own], `refusals_say_what_happened_first` [no plain words], `who_may_install_is_read_from_the_catalog`
[the trusted branch skipped]; UI `extensions_are_installed_and_dropped_from_the_tree` (updated: installed only,
installed from the manager, dropped from the tree) [the tree listing available ones], and in `ui_tests/extensions.rs`:
`the_tree_lists_installed_extensions_and_every_way_in_opens_the_manager` [the tree listing available ones; no
default action on the folder], `the_manager_searches_filters_and_shows_what_an_extension_needs` (with ⌘F, a row's
right-click, its menu and Copy name) [the search over names only; ⌘F not handled; a right-click that does not
select], `install_with_a_requirement_update_move_and_drop_go_through_their_sql` [no `CASCADE`; the tree not
refreshed; the tree listing available ones], `read_only_refuses_and_production_asks_twice` [read-only not checked;
the row menu not saying why], `a_refusal_says_who_may_install_first` [no plain words],
`the_manager_stays_inside_a_narrow_group` [the details beside the list at any width]. Updated without a mutation of
their own: the `navigator/actions.rs` unit tests (menus, default actions, writes, Tools ▸) and
`ui_tests/tools_menu.rs::the_tools_submenu_says_what_applies_per_engine_and_kind_of_node` (the database's last
groups start with "Foreign data wrappers…, Extensions…": one `database_tail`, two assertions).
**Captures**: taken offscreen while working (the K25 way: a temporary test, `gpui-pre-apple` as a dev-dependency,
not committed): the list, an available and an installed extension selected, the install confirmation, an empty
search, a narrow group, light and dark. Fixed from them: the selected row scrolled out of sight (the list now
scrolls to it), "(default) (installed)" on one version, "picked below" for a picker above, the cascade sentence.
The final ones are to take: `m12-extensions-{list,install}-{light,dark}`.

### K28 — Four defects of daily use (S)

**Now.** Found in real use: (1) the main window always opened centred at 1280 × 800 at most; (2) in the dark
theme a selected grid row hardly differed from its row (1.02:1): the kit paints `table.active.background` and
`list.active.background` at most 20 % opaque, and the dark file wrote them opaque (`#323B20`, `#2A311E`), so the
tint came out as a grey while the dark test read the colour as written; a range of cells, tinted with
`selection.background` (at most 30 %), was as faint; (3) a notification sat at the top right, over the main
toolbar and the active tab's toolbar (Run all, Run); (4) "New SQL editor" (⌘T, toolbar, menu, palette) on a
selected connection that was not open opened a tab bound to nothing: `Workspace::new_query_tab` took
`default_target`, which keeps connected connections only, while the connection's own "New SQL editor" connected
it first through `pending_editor`.

**Wanted.** (1) The window reopens where and as large as it was, maximised or full screen too, on its display; the
centred default when nothing is stored or the place is off every display. (2) Selected rows, cells, ranges and list
or tree items plainly visible in dark, a Lime tint, text and muted NULL text at 4.5:1, measured as painted; light
unchanged. (3) Notifications where they cover no control the user needs (DBeaver's place: bottom right, above the
status bar). (4) The connection connects first, then the tab opens on it; no orphan tab when it fails or is
cancelled; with no connection selected, as before.

Done: D-101. Core (`storage.rs`): `WindowPlacement { display, x, y, width, height, state }`, `WindowState
{Windowed, Maximized, Fullscreen}`, `WindowPlacement::display_among(&[(id, [x, y, w, h])])`. App: `state.rs`
(`WINDOW_KEY` = `"window"`, `Startup::window`, `window_bounds(stored, cx)` → `(WindowBounds, Option<DisplayId>)`
with the old centred default, `window_placement(window, cx)`); `main.rs` (opens with them); `workspace.rs`
(`observe_window_bounds` keeps the last placement and writes it 400 ms after the last move through
`bridge::spawn_blocking`, again in the quit flush; `new_query_tab` → `new_editor_on(id)`, shared with the
sidebar's "New SQL editor", the stale-id case kept unbound); `theme.rs` (`theme.notification.placement =
Anchor::BottomRight`, `margins.bottom = NOTIFICATION_BOTTOM` (68 px); the settings preview paints a selection
written with its opacity); `assets/themes/kerno.json` (dark `table.active.background` and
`list.active.background` `#C8F03C2A`, dark `muted.foreground` `#9AA4B0` → `#A3ADB9`); `query/grid.rs` (a range
tinted with `table_active` instead of `selection`, which stays the text selection's, except where the table
already tints its own selection: `Grid::table_tints`, so the anchor is not tinted twice).

Tests, each seen failing under the mutation in brackets: core `a_window_placement_is_stored_and_read_back`
[`#[serde(default)]` off the state], `a_window_off_every_display_goes_back_to_the_default_place` [the display id
and the bottom edge not checked]; unit `theme::tests::a_selected_row_stands_out_in_the_dark_theme`, rewritten to
measure the painted tint of rows, cells over plain, striped and hovered rows, list items over the list, the
sidebar and a popup (≥ 1.45:1, a Lime hue, text, muted and link at 4.5:1) [the old opaque values: 1.04 to 1.18:1];
`the_theme_previews_read_the_theme_file` updated; UI (`ui_tests/daily_use.rs`): `the_window_is_reopened_where_it_was`
(a simulated resize stored with the display's id; restored on that display, full screen kept; off the display, an
unplugged display and nothing stored all give the centred default) [the bounds observer not storing],
`a_notification_covers_no_control_of_the_tab` (an error notification after its slide-in clear of the main
toolbar, the tab's toolbar buttons and the status bar, at the bottom right) [`Anchor::TopRight`: it covers the
main toolbar], `a_notification_leaves_the_footers_of_a_table_and_a_result_alone` (PostgreSQL: a result's Fetch
more, Fetch all, Export…; a table's pager) [68 px lowered to 40: it covers the fetch and export buttons],
`new_sql_editor_on_a_closed_connection_connects_it_first` (nothing selected: an unbound tab as
before; a refused connection: no tab; a password prompt cancelled: no tab; typed: the tab on the connection)
[the old `new_query_tab`: an unbound tab at once]. The range's token and the anchor tinted once are shown by
the captures only (a `Grid` needs a session to be built).
**Captures**: taken offscreen (temporary test, `gpui-pre-apple` as a dev-dependency, not committed): a selected
row, cell and range and an error notification over a query tab, light and dark. The final ones are to take:
`m12-grid-selection-{row,cell,range}-{light,dark}`, `m12-notification-{light,dark}`. A real window's restore
(its place on a real display, maximised, full screen) is proved in the test context only.

## The roadmap's first engine beyond v1: SQLite

### K29 — SQLite as the third engine family (L)

**Now.** ROADMAP's SQLite item: Kerno spoke PostgreSQL, MySQL and MariaDB only; a SQLite file could not be opened.

**Wanted.** Someone opens a SQLite file and works in it every day: a connection that is a file (chosen, or made
empty with New file…), tested, connected, edited, duplicated, exported; the tree of its tables, views,
triggers, columns and indexes; queries with cancel, errors with their place, history, completion, format; the
grid with every storage class; table data edited and applied atomically; the structure, a table created, the
four alters SQLite does and the others refused in plain words; exports and CSV import; the confirmations and
read-only rules of the other engines. What SQLite has no equivalent of is hidden or refused with its reason.

Done: D-102. Core: `Engine::Sqlite` (`is_file`, port 0, no user), `ConnectionConfig::file` and
`ConnectParams::file`, `validate`'s file branch, `connection_url` (the path), `reaches_another_server` with the
file; `LexRules::SQLITE` and `LexRules::of`, `begin_blocks` (the splitter's rule for trigger bodies);
`Dialect::{begin_sql, transactional_ddl, has_routines}`; `ExecSink::stop_signal`; `error::explain`'s file words
and four `FailureKind`s; `ClientFamily::of` → `Option` with `ClientFamily::required` and `NO_CLIENT_TOOLS`,
`backup_tool`/`restore_tool`/`script_tool` → `Option`, the command builders `Result`; `transfer::map_type`
refusing SQLite; `password::resolve` reading nothing for a file; `testing::available(Sqlite)` true. Driver:
`crates/kerno-sqlite` (owner thread per session, interrupt, affinity and storage classes, `EXPLAIN QUERY PLAN`
parser, pragma-based introspection, `new_database_file`). App: the dialog's file field with Choose… and New
file…, the General and Advanced tabs only, a test that names SQLite's version; the manager never touches the
secret store for a file; the tree without Routines and Events folders; no Tools ▸ for SQLite (`tool_entries`
takes the engine), the dashboard and the administration tab refused; create database and schema greyed; the
plan rows joined by tabs; the table form's type list, auto-increment tooltip and move refusal; the logo.
Element ids: `engine-sqlite`, `conn-file`, `conn-file-browse`, `conn-file-new`.

Tests, each seen failing under the mutation in brackets: `kerno-sqlite` integration
`files_open_as_they_are_and_never_get_created` [`CREATE` added to the open flags and the existence check
removed], `read_only_files_and_connections` [the `is_readonly` check removed], `a_long_statement_is_cancelled`
[no interrupt in `cancel`; no interrupt when the consumer leaves: the test then hangs on the next statement],
`edits_apply_atomically` [the `DEFAULT` arm of `literal` removed], plus `a_locked_file_says_so`,
`rows_stream_in_pages`, `storage_classes_round_trip`, `errors_say_where` [no refusal of an unfilled
parameter], `transactions_are_followed`,
`the_fixture_is_introspected`, `ddl_is_what_sqlite_kept`, `create_and_alter_run_on_the_file`,
`query_plans_become_trees`, `results_export_and_csv_imports_on_a_file` (CSV, JSON, NDJSON, Markdown, XLSX, SQL
INSERT run back, CSV into an existing and a new table), `the_er_diagram_reads_the_file`; 12 unit tests (types,
dialect, plan, introspection helpers). Core: `split::tests::sqlite_trigger_bodies_stay_whole` [`begin_blocks`
off for SQLite], `config::tests::a_database_file_is_a_connection_of_its_own`,
`old_connections_load_and_a_file_s_path_is_exported`, `error::tests::a_file_s_failures_are_told_plainly`. App:
`connections::form::tests::a_database_file_form_builds_from_its_file`; UI `ui_tests/sqlite.rs`:
`a_database_file_connection_is_made_tested_and_opened` [the host row shown for a file],
`the_fixture_file_is_browsed_queried_and_edited`, `an_alter_sqlite_cannot_do_is_refused_with_its_reason`. The
existing suites' engine matches got an arm for SQLite where they run on the test servers;
`dialog::tests::the_url_placeholder_follows_the_engine` skips the file engine, which has no URL field.
**Captures**: offscreen (a temporary test, `gpui-pre-apple` as a dev-dependency, not committed), light and dark:
the dialog, the tree and a data tab, a query with every storage class, the plan, the structure tab, the table
form's refusal to move a column. Fixed from them: the empty Routines and Events folders of the tree; the fourth
engine card wrapped to a second row and pushed the dialog's buttons out of a short window (cards now 152 px).

---

### K30 — Schema compare (L)

**Now.** No way to see how two schemas differ, short of reading both trees or diffing two dumps by hand; item 1
of `docs/ROADMAP.md` ("Beyond v1"), asked in DBeaver's tracker as NAV-57, CM-21, CM-59 and TOOL-22 of
`docs/DBEAVER-PARITY.md` (DBeaver Community has only a simple object compare; the migration script is in its
paid editions).

**Wanted.** Compare a *source* schema (the model) with a *target* schema (what changes), each a saved connection
with its database and schema (the database alone on MySQL and MariaDB), same engine family only; list what is
only in the source, only in the target, different or the same, with the differences in plain words and both
definitions side by side; open the script that brings the target to the source in a query tab on the target,
where it is read and run under the usual rules. Nothing runs from the compare itself.

Done: D-103. Core: `compare.rs` (new): `Family::of` and `refusal` (the family is decided here, with `matches!`,
so a new engine is refused in words until it is handled), `load` (the schema's objects, each table's
`TableStructure` and the others' DDL, eight at a time through `erd::buffered`, the diagram's loop moved out of
`erd::load` and shared; `SET search_path` to the side's schema first on engines with schemas), `compare` (pure:
`Item`s with a `State`, plain-word `differences`, both DDLs, and the script lines per item), `script` (the lines
of the items ticked, in phases: types, constraint drops, new tables without their keys, table changes, constraint
and index adds, foreign keys, routines, views, triggers, then drops of what only the target has, written as
comments unless asked for), `retarget` (the source schema's qualifier rewritten to the target's). A table both
sides have is diffed by building the source's `TableDraft` tied by name to the target's items and calling
`table_draft::changes`; check, unique and exclusion constraints and the indexes a draft cannot hold (partial,
expression, other methods) are compared by name and definition; views, routines, sequences, types and triggers
by DDL text, whitespace aside. `Dialect::add_constraint_sql` / `drop_constraint_sql` (provided, standard SQL);
`default_alter_table_sql` and `table_draft::is_editable` become `pub(crate)`. App: `compare_dialog.rs` (new:
two pickers of the copy dialog's kind, swap, the refusal and what is missing said in words, Compare);
`compare_tab.rs` (new: header with both sides and Swap, Refresh, Open script; filters with counts, search, All /
None, "Also drop what only the target has"; per-side progress with Cancel; the list grouped by kind with a
checkbox and a mark (`+ − ≠ =` and words) per object; the differences and two read-only editors; a row menu Open
in source, Open in target, Copy name, Put in / Leave out of the script; ↑ ↓ Space ⌘F); `workspace.rs`
(`OpenTab::Compare`, `open_compare`, one tab per pair of sides, not restored; the script opens with
`open_query` on the target, Open in source/target with `open_structure` or the DDL in a query tab);
`navigator/actions.rs` (`Action::CompareWith` "Compare with…" on a schema and a database, `ToolEntry::CompareSchemas`
"Compare schemas…" in their Tools ▸); `actions.rs`, `menu_palette.rs`, `main.rs` (the command `compare_schemas`,
in Server ▸ Tools and the palette). Element ids: `compare-connection` (source `0..`, target `1000..`),
`compare-database`, `compare-schema`, `compare-dialog-swap`, `compare-problem`, `compare-dialog-cancel`,
`compare-dialog-start`; `compare-source`, `compare-target`, `compare-swap`, `compare-refresh`,
`compare-open-script`, `("compare-filter", 0..5)`, `compare-search`, `compare-all`, `compare-none`,
`compare-drops`, `("compare-progress", 0..2)`, `compare-cancel`, `compare-read-only`, `compare-list`,
`("compare-row", i)`, `("compare-include", i)`, `compare-identical`, `compare-empty`, `compare-error`,
`compare-differences`, `compare-source-ddl`, `compare-target-ddl`.

Tests, each seen failing under the mutation in brackets: core unit `compare::tests::` (CM-21, NAV-57, TOOL-22)
`only_an_engine_of_the_same_family_is_compared` [any two families accepted],
`every_table_difference_is_said_in_plain_words` [a key's referenced table not retargeted],
`identical_schemas_give_no_difference_and_an_empty_script` [DDL compared without normalising whitespace],
`the_script_runs_in_an_order_that_works` [a new table's keys written inline], `drops_are_comments_until_asked_for`
[drops always written], `names_compare_as_the_engine_compares_them` [MySQL names compared with their case],
`a_column_moves_only_where_the_engine_can_move_it` [a refused move kept as a difference],
`what_cannot_change_in_place_is_a_comment_that_says_why` [a type replaced like a view],
`views_and_routines_compare_by_their_ddl_and_are_replaced` [a changed view not replaced],
`checks_unique_constraints_and_other_indexes` [a changed check taken as the same],
`the_schema_qualifier_is_rewritten_and_nothing_else` [no rewrite],
`a_line_break_in_a_name_never_leaves_sql_out_of_a_comment` (a name with `\n`, `\r`, `\r\n`, U+2028, U+0085
followed by `DROP TABLE kerno_test_victim;`) [comment lines split with `str::lines`, which keeps a lone `\r`]. Driver
`the_script_brings_the_target_to_the_source` in `kerno-postgres/tests/compare.rs` and `kerno-mysql/tests/compare.rs`
(scratch schemas, then databases, `kerno_test_cmp_a` / `kerno_test_cmp_b`: compare, run the script on the target,
compare again: identical; dropped before and after, also on failure) [PostgreSQL: no `SET search_path` (the

added]. UI `ui_tests/compare.rs`: `the_dialog_asks_for_two_sides_and_refuses_two_engine_families` (palette, the
refusal, the same schema twice, swap, the tab) [no refusal], `compare_with_on_a_schema_makes_it_the_source` [the
tree entry doing nothing], `the_tab_counts_filters_and_puts_what_is_ticked_in_the_script` (counts, Same hidden,
search, differences, checkbox, row menu, All / None, drops, ↓ and Space) [Same shown under All differences; the
drops checkbox not wired], `open_script_opens_a_query_tab_on_the_target_and_runs_nothing` [the script bound to
the source], `identical_schemas_say_so` [Open script enabled with nothing to change],
`a_read_only_target_opens_the_script_and_refuses_it_there` [no read-only line]. Updated without a mutation of
their own: `navigator::actions::tests::the_tools_submenu_follows_the_engine_and_the_kind_of_node` and
