### K31 — Export a schema as a document (M)

**Now.** The structure of a schema can be seen in the tree, the structure tabs and the ER diagram, and its
diagram exported (SVG, PNG, Mermaid), but there is no document of it to give a team or a client, to keep in git
or to hand to an AI assistant.

**Wanted** (the owner, 2026-11-21): export the schema of a database, everything or the tables picked, to PDF,
DBML or other formats; the HTML and the PDF in Kerno's style by default, with a plain white one.

Done: D-104; the PDF is K32 (D-105). Core: `schema_doc.rs` (new) and
`schema_doc/html.rs` (new), pure: a `Document` (title, engine, server, database, schema, date, and the objects
read as the compare's `Side`) in, text out; `Format` (HTML, Markdown, DBML, SQL, Mermaid, with `label`,
`extension`, `purpose`), `Style` (Kerno light, Kerno dark, Plain), `Options` (style, diagram), `render`,
`pickable` (the tables and views a document can pick), `Pick` and `load` (the compare's read, narrowed to what is
ticked: `compare::load` is split into `listing` and `read`, both `pub(crate)`), `default_title`, `file_name`,
`server_label`, `today`. SQL is `compare::script` of the objects against an empty schema; Mermaid is
`erd::to_mermaid`; the HTML diagram is `erd::to_svg` in the style's colours. `colours` reads the Kerno styles
from the app's theme file (`include_str!`), the lockups from `assets/brand/`. `erd::to_mermaid` now keeps a
quoted name on one line. App: `schema_export_dialog.rs` (new): the place and connection, a searchable checkbox
list (All / None on what is shown, "n of m ticked"), "Also routines, sequences, types and triggers", the format
and the style as option cards with drawn previews, "Include the diagram", the title, the file chosen with the
save panel (its extension picks the format, a format change renames it), Export with progress and Cancel, then
"Show in Finder" and "Open"; loading, error (Try again), empty and no-match states; Enter chooses the file, then
exports; the options scroll in a short window while the file and the buttons stay; the file is written as
`<name>.part` and put in place only if the export was not cancelled meanwhile. Ways in: `Action::ExportSchema` "Export schema…" on a schema, a database without schemas, a table, a
view (that object ticked alone); "Export document…" in the ER diagram's toolbar and background menu (its tables
ticked); the command `export_schema` in Server ▸ Tools and the palette (the active diagram's tables, else the
active tab's schema, else the browsed connection). Element ids: `schema-export-dialog`, `schema-export-search`,
`schema-export-all`, `schema-export-none`, `schema-export-count`, `schema-export-objects`,
`("schema-export-object", i)`, `schema-export-code`, `schema-export-format`, `schema-export-purpose`,
`schema-export-style`, `schema-export-diagram`, `schema-export-title`, `schema-export-file`,
`schema-export-choose`, `schema-export-problem`, `schema-export-start`, `schema-export-progress`,
`schema-export-cancel`, `schema-export-done`, `schema-export-reveal`, `schema-export-open`,
`schema-export-cancelled`, `schema-export-failed`, `schema-export-back`, `schema-export-close`,
`schema-export-loading`, `schema-export-error`, `schema-export-retry`, `schema-export-empty`,
`schema-export-no-match`; `erd-export-document`.

Tests, each seen failing under the mutation in brackets: core unit `schema_doc::tests::` golden text
`dbml_of_a_small_schema`, `markdown_of_a_small_schema`, `sql_of_a_small_schema_runs_in_order`,
`mermaid_of_a_small_schema`, `html_of_a_table`; the hostile schema (a table `x"><script>alert(1)</script>`, a
comment `ok\nDROP TABLE kerno_test_victim;` with a link, an image and a heading, a column `a|b`c"d`, a default
with a backtick and a line break, a view whose DDL holds a fence) `html_escapes_every_name_comment_and_definition`
[`<` not escaped], `markdown_keeps_hostile_text_in_its_cell_and_its_block` [no Markdown escape; a pipe left in

## Appendix A — proposed text for `CLAUDE.md` §4 K (the owner's file: to paste if he agrees)

```markdown

### K. v1.2: what the general critique of DBeaver shows (owner, 2026-11-17)

`docs/research/dbeaver-critique.md` lists 22 recurring criticisms of DBeaver (C1-C22); `docs/COMPETITOR-ISSUES.md`
holds their verified triage and `docs/plans/M12.md` the plan. Order: K1-K10. Quote the C number in tests.

1. **Quit asks** when a transaction, pending edits or unsaved script text would be lost (Cmd+Q, menu, window
   close), as closing a tab does; Roll back and quit, or Cancel.
2. **Every write can be cancelled**: data apply, structure apply, navigator drop/truncate/rename/create, and the
   first page of a table; the cancel reaches the server; closing a tab stops what it runs.
3. **The tab shows where its session really is**: after `USE` or `SET search_path` the picker follows, and a
   reconnect returns there, never to an older target.
4. **An open transaction is visible on every tab** with its age, not only in the active tab's status bar.
5. **No silent end of a transaction**: switching to auto-commit and "Set as default" ask; the idle rollback
   watches every open transaction that wrote.
6. **A script file is never bound to a non-local connection without the user's pick**; deleting a connection
   unbinds its query tabs and closes its other tabs.
7. **The write classifier** treats a `WHERE` inside a MySQL versioned comment as absent, classifies the
   statement inside `PREPARE … AS`, and confirms `MERGE … THEN DELETE`.
8. **Kerno never vanishes at start**: a start failure opens a window that says why and what to do (start fresh
   moves the data aside, never deletes); one unreadable saved connection is skipped and reported; the store is
   copied before every migration (three copies kept).
9. **The server's detail and hint on every error surface**, and a failed COMMIT says what was kept.
10. **Tests for what works untested**: completion time bound, fetch-all and EXPLAIN cancel, rename, the
    remaining confirmation paths.
```

## Appendix B — proposed `docs/GOAL-3.md` (the owner's file: to create if he agrees)

```markdown
# Goal 3 — the client you can trust with production

Kerno v1.2 closes every hole the general critique of DBeaver (docs/research/dbeaver-critique.md) found in
Kerno: nothing is lost on quit, nothing waits forever without a way out, nothing runs somewhere other than
where the screen says, and every error says what the server said and what to do.

Done when:
- CLAUDE.md §4 K1-K10 are implemented as `docs/plans/M12.md` describes, each with its tests green with
  `KERNO_REQUIRE_CONTAINERS=1` on PostgreSQL 17, MySQL 8.4 and MariaDB 11, its row in `docs/FEATURES.md`,
  and its captures in light and dark;
- `docs/COMPETITOR-ISSUES.md` shows C3, C6, C14, C15, C16 and C21 as answered, each with a test;
- `scripts/check.sh` is green and `scripts/bench.sh core` still meets every target of `docs/BENCH.md`;
- nothing is pushed or published: the owner decides the release.
```

### K32 — The schema document as a PDF (M)

**Now.** K31 writes HTML, Markdown, DBML, SQL and Mermaid; the PDF the owner asked for stopped on `krilla`, which
brings a third copy of `read-fonts` and `skrifa` on Linux (D-104).

**Wanted.** The same document as a PDF, with two small libraries only: `pdf-writer` and `subsetter` without its
`variable-fonts` feature (the coordinator's decision of 2026-11-21, under the owner's "fast and light").

Done: D-105. Core: `schema_doc/pdf.rs` (new), pure, bytes out: `Fonts` (the three font files, given by the app),
`PageSize` (A4, Letter), `Options::page`, `Format::Pdf`; `render` now returns the file's bytes for every format
(`Result<Vec<u8>, String>`, the error being a font that does not parse) and takes the fonts. A two-pass layout
(the first finds each section's page, the second writes the contents' page numbers on the same lines): cover with
the lockup (Kerno styles) and the facts, contents with links, the diagram on its own page scaled to fit it, a
page per table (comment, columns, primary key, constraints, indexes, foreign keys; header rows repeated, rows
that continue across pages, long words broken), views and code in the monospace font, blocks that continue,
"Generated by Kerno" (Kerno styles), a footer with the title and "page n of N". Text: one CID per character
drawn, Type0 fonts over CID fonts of the `subsetter` subsets with a CIDToGIDMap stream and a ToUnicode map;
glyphs, advances and metrics from `schema_doc/pdf/sfnt.rs` (new; `ttf-parser` and `rustybuzz` are unmaintained,
RUSTSEC-2026-0192 and -0206), no kerning, no shaping (a `ponytail:` comment says it). The lockup's
shapes from the brand SVG files' path data (`kurbo`), the diagram's boxes, bands, rows, marks and edges from
`erd::layout` and `erd::route`. Outline (Diagram, Tables and each table, Views and each, the rest and each),
document title, creator "Kerno" for the Kerno styles only; streams compressed with `flate2`. App:
`theme::document_fonts` (the bytes the app already embeds, not a second copy), the PDF card second among six,
the style cards for HTML and PDF, "Paper" A4 / US Letter beside "Include the diagram" for the PDF, bytes
written through the `.part` then rename path. Element ids: `("schema-export-page", 0..2)`.

Tests, each seen failing under the mutation in brackets: core unit `schema_doc::pdf::tests::` (the PDF read back
without a reader: objects split, ToUnicode maps parsed, each shown string decoded)
`a_pdf_is_a_pdf_with_its_pages_outline_and_links` (`%PDF-`, `%%EOF`, 6 pages, the outline's titles, 8 links,
title and creator, compression) [the contents' links not recorded], `the_text_maps_back_to_its_characters`
(names, comments, types, DDL, "page 1 of 6", the contents' page numbers, a character the fonts lack) [the first
CIDs left out of ToUnicode], `plain_names_no_kerno` [the creator written in Plain],
`dark_pages_are_painted_and_light_ones_are_not` [no page painted], `hostile_names_stay_strings` (a table
`a)b\c(d endstream endobj %x` with a line break, a comment `) Tj ET 0 0 1000 1000 re f (`) [an outline title not
put on one line], `nothing_runs_off_the_page` (60 columns of 300-character types, defaults and comments, a
300-character name and title, A4 and Letter: every text inside the margins, the header row on every page with
rows) [the header not drawn again on a new page], `lines_wrap_between_words_keep_their_indentation_and_break_long_words`
[the indentation dropped], `a4_and_letter_media_boxes` [the page size ignored], `an_empty_pick_and_no_diagram`
[the "Nothing was picked" line left out], `the_font_reader_reads_the_glyphs_advances_and_metrics` (the three
fonts' glyphs, advances and metrics as `ttf-parser` read them, every ASCII advance of the monospace font, a
broken font an error) [the PostScript name not read], `sfnt::tests::format4_by_delta_and_through_the_glyph_array`
[the glyph array's delta not added], `sfnt::tests::format12_by_groups` [the group's start not subtracted]. Driver: the read-only tests of K31 now write the PDF too and find each
table in its outline (PostgreSQL, MySQL, MariaDB, SQLite) [the tables' entries left out of the outline: the three
fail]. UI: `ui_tests::schema_export::the_pdf_card_writes_a_pdf_on_the_paper_chosen` [the paper choice ignored];
`each_format_writes_its_file` now writes the PDF too; `the_style_cards_choose_the_look_of_the_html` clicks the
Markdown card at its new place. Readers: `pdftotext` gives back the table and column names, and `pdftoppm`
renders every page with nothing on stderr, for the fixture's `shop` in the three styles (12 pages), the hostile
schema, the 60-column table (17 pages) and an empty pick.
**Captures** (offscreen, temporary test, not committed), light and dark, at 1200 × 800 and 900 × 600: the dialog
on the PDF card and its finished state (the row with Show in Finder and Open); the pages of the PDFs above,
rasterised.

### K34 — A selected row seen at a glance (S)

**Now.** The owner, on the light sidebar with `shop` selected under a connected PostgreSQL connection: "le
selected se voit pas assez". The kit paints `list.active.background` and `table.active.background` at most 20 %
opaque and `selection.background` at most 30 %; the light Lime wash came out at 1.01:1 on the sidebar, the light
grid's tint at 1.10:1, the opaque `accent` (menus, palette, dropdowns, the dialogs' picked rows) at 1.10:1 in
light and 1.11:1 in dark, the text selection at 1.07 and 1.20. K28 had fixed the dark list and grid only.

**Wanted.** Every selected or current state plainly seen in both themes, as one system, hover weaker than
selected, text and muted text on a selection at 4.5:1; tokens first, no fork of the kit, no layout change.

Done: D-107. `assets/themes/kerno.json`: light `list.active.background` and `table.active.background` →
`#4D6B0033` (Olive at the cap), `accent.background` → `#DBE1CC`, `selection.background` → `#4D6B0026`,
`muted.foreground` `#5B6470` → `#505965`; dark `accent.background` → `#374126`, `selection.background` →
`#C8F03C24`. `query/grid_view.rs`: the record view's current field takes `table_active`. `theme.rs`: the
comments that named the Lime wash. `library.rs`, `multi_run.rs`: hover with `list_hover`, not
`accent`. `assets/brand/BRAND.md`: Olive's role, the Lime wash retired.

Tests (`theme.rs`), each seen failing with the old value put back: `a_selection_is_seen_at_a_glance_in_both_themes`
replaces K28's dark-only test (each tint as painted, 1.22:1 from its surface and from the plain and striped rows,

[light list `#F1F8D6`, light table `#B9DC3233`, light accent `#F1F8D6`, dark accent `#22281D`, light muted
`#5B6470`]; `selected_text_is_seen_at_a_glance` (1.22:1 on the editor and its active line, text 4.5:1, syntax

### K33 — Passwords in Kerno's own file, so macOS stops asking (M)

**Now.** Every saved secret is in the system store. Kerno's builds are signed ad hoc, so after each update (and
each rebuild) macOS asks for the login password again, once per keychain item; the owner: "je veux pas qu'on
demande de mot de passe, c'est chiant pour rien". He chose "Fichier Kerno": DBeaver's way, the keychain as a
choice.

**Wanted.** Kerno's own encrypted file by default on macOS, no prompt for anyone; the keychain in Settings;
existing passwords moved without retyping; Linux unchanged; never plain text, never in logs.

Done: D-106 (it says the line of `CLAUDE.md` §1 this changes, which is the owner's to update). Core:
`secrets/file.rs` (new): `FileSecretStore` (`secrets.kerno` sealed with AES-256-GCM, a fresh nonce per write,
`secrets.key` of 256 random bits, both and `secrets.lock` 0600 from creation; every change under an exclusive
`flock`, re-read, replaced through `secrets.kerno.tmp`, `fsync`, rename; `Lookup` value / removed / unknown; a
file that does not decrypt set aside as `.damaged` with its key and said once). `secrets.rs`: `StoreChoice`
(File, Keychain; default File on macOS, Keychain elsewhere), `ChosenStore` (one store in use, the other moved
into it once per key, both ways; with the file in use the keychain is never written or deleted), `app_store`
(replaces `default_store`; `KERNO_SECRET_STORE=memory|file|keychain` gives one store alone), the process-wide
cached system store, `SecretStore::peek` (the store in use only; `has_secrets` uses it) and `name`.
`error.rs`: the file's failures in their own words. App: `Settings::secret_store`; `AppState::secret_stores`
(the builder, so tests give one that never reaches the keychain) and `set_secret_store`; Settings ▸ Passwords
(a section before Shortcuts, `Lock` glyph), the row `settings-secret-store` with chips `secret-store-0/1`, the
honest description and the `KERNO_SECRET_STORE` override line; "Ask each time" reads no store at connect, edit
or duplicate (`connections/manager.rs`); "Password from" named after the place (`form.rs`, `dialog.rs`,
`origin_text`), the AI key lines (`ai_settings_view.rs`), "Use the saved secrets" (`portability.rs`), the
memory-only alert (`workspace.rs`).

Where each secret is read or written, and which store it uses now (every call goes through `AppState.secrets`,
so all follow the setting): a connect (`Connections::connect`, a password typed at a prompt saved after a
successful connect), saving a connection (`persist`), editing (`secrets_of`), duplicating, deleting
(`delete_secrets`), an import (`portability`: `has_secrets` through `peek`, then `save_password` or
`delete_secrets`), the AI key (Settings ▸ AI: save, remove, "List models", "Test"; the AI panel at each
request), the bench (`bench.rs`, memory only). A secret of the URL of `kerno <url>` stays in memory.

Tests, each seen failing under the mutation in brackets: core `secrets::tests::` `no_file_holds_a_secret_in_clear`
[the plaintext written instead of the sealed bytes], `every_file_is_readable_by_its_owner_only` [the 0600 mode
dropped from the lock file; from the replaced files], `a_damaged_file_is_set_aside_and_said_in_plain_words` (one
byte changed, cut short, another key, a key of the wrong size, no key file; the second damage beside the first)
[the file not renamed aside], `a_failed_write_keeps_the_previous_secrets` (a read-only directory, a stale
temporary file) [the file written in place], `two_stores_on_one_directory_keep_each_others_secrets` (two
instances, two threads writing 25 each) [no lock], `a_missing_directory_gets_what_it_needs_at_the_first_write`
[no directory made], `a_keychain_secret_moves_to_the_file_once` (three runs, the fake keychain read once per
key) [the answer not written back], `with_the_file_in_use_the_keychain_is_left_alone` [a delete forgetting
instead of marking], `a_refused_keychain_read_counts_as_nothing_once` [the refusal returned],
`with_the_keychain_in_use_the_file_drains_into_it` [a removal mark reading the keychain],
`a_question_about_saved_secrets_never_reads_the_other_store` [`has_secrets` through `get`];
`the_file_keeps_and_forgets_across_runs` and `unusual_and_long_secrets_round_trip` (NUL, quotes, a line
break, bidi, 1 MB; a non-UTF-8 secret cannot exist) are plain round trips;
`error::tests::a_password_file_failure_says_what_happened_and_what_to_do` [the file's branch removed]. App:
`connections::manager_tests::an_ask_each_time_connection_reads_no_store` (connections of an earlier run, a
counting store) [`|| !config.save_password` removed from `connect`];
`ui_tests::secret_store::the_passwords_setting_shows_changes_and_is_stored` (the row, its chips and text,
Keychain then Kerno's file stored, the status bar's memory warning following the switch, a secret saved after
it found in the file) [the chip only storing the setting; `set_secret_store` not rebuilding the store];
`settings_view` tests updated ("keychain" and "update" find the row; the override line). The real keychain is
never used by a test: `os_store_keeps_and_forgets` stays opt-in.
**Captures** (offscreen, temporary test, not committed), light and dark, 1200 × 800 and 900 × 600: the
Passwords page, and the search "keychain".

### K35 — A restore whose backup names a role the server does not have (M)

**Now.** The first outside user (Kerno 0.3.0 in place of DBeaver) restored a production dump, custom format,
into a local PostgreSQL with the simple restore view. pg_restore 18, speaking French, stopped at once:
`ALTER SCHEMA archive OWNER TO app_owner;` → `role "app_owner" does not exist`, in one transaction. Kerno showed
the raw output and no way on; DBeaver's restore has "Discard object owner" and "Do not restore privileges". The
wizard had the two checkboxes, the simple view neither.

**Wanted.** The failure said in plain words first (the role, what to do), the output below as before; one click
to run the same restore without the backup's owners and privileges where pg_restore can, nothing offered where
it cannot (a plain script); a detection that does not depend on the language of the messages; the choice in the
simple view before a run; nothing else about restore changed.

Done: D-108. Core: `tools/roles.rs` (new): `refused_roles(log, script_line)` reads the SQL the tool printed
(pg_restore's failed command after its translated label, the whole ACL block; psql's `psql:<stdin>:N:` line
read back from the script), lexes it, takes the roles of `ALTER … OWNER TO`, `GRANT … TO`, `REVOKE … FROM`,
`GRANTED BY`, `ALTER DEFAULT PRIVILEGES FOR ROLE` and `SET SESSION AUTHORIZATION`, and keeps those the server's
message above names as a whole name (`RefusedRole`: the name unquoted, owner or privileges); `tools.rs` exports
it. App: `backup_dialog.rs`: `RoleFailure` and `roles`, `check_roles` after a failed PostgreSQL restore (the
output read off the UI thread, then `pg_roles` asked on the connection's metadata session which of the roles it
lacks), `roles_text` (missing, or refused to the user; archive, archive already without owners, a run that went on
past it, script), `RoleFailure::new` (the retry only for an archive restored in one transaction),
`script_line`, `restore_without_owners` (both flags, then `start`: the command shown and asked, twice on
production), the failure view's block `restore-roles` above the status line and the footer's
`restore-without-owners` (primary; Close then plain); the probe clears the pair when the simple view gets a SQL
file. `backup_dialog/simple.rs`: the restore's "Roles" row for PostgreSQL archives, `simple-no-owner` ("Leave out
the backup's owners and privileges", off by default) and `simple-no-owner-hint`; `without_owners`;
`advanced_changes` and `reset_advanced` treat the pair as the simple view's own.

Tests, each seen failing under the mutation in brackets: core `tools::roles::tests::`
`the_owner_case_is_read_in_either_language` (the user's French block verbatim, its English form, a French
server) [only `Command was: ` read]; `the_privilege_case_names_only_the_refused_role` (pg_restore 18's real
five-line ACL block, a `SET SESSION AUTHORIZATION` grantor, a `REVOKE`) [the server's message not checked];
`a_quoted_role_name_is_unquoted` (`"Rôle ""Été"` in default privileges, `"Rôle Été"`) [the quotes kept];
`a_failure_not_about_a_role_names_none` (a table that exists, an owner statement refused for a missing schema,
`kerno` inside `kerno_reader`, `PUBLIC`, verbose lines, psql's command tags) [the name matched as a substring];
`psql_points_at_the_script_line` [psql's line not read]. Unit
`backup_dialog::tests::a_refused_role_is_said_with_what_to_do` (missing; existing, two roles; a run without one
transaction, which went on: no retry; already without them; a script) [the retry offered after a run that went
on]. UI `ui_tests/restore_roles.rs`:
`a_missing_owner_is_said_and_the_restore_runs_again_without_owners` (a stand-in pg_restore printing the user's
block, production: the text, the output below, the retry's command with `--no-owner --no-privileges` and
`--single-transaction`, asked twice, what ran; back in the simple view the checkbox set, the wizard's way back
asks nothing, the checkbox clears both) [the retry setting `no_owner` alone; the retry run without the
confirmation; `check_roles` not called; the pair counted as a hidden change];
`a_plain_script_says_what_to_do_without_a_retry` (a stand-in psql with a French server's message, line 3 of the
script; the checkbox ticked for an archive cleared when a script is chosen) [the button offered for a script;
the pair kept for the script]; `an_archive_from_another_server_restores_without_its_owners` (the
real pg_dump and pg_restore on `kerno-postgres`: a role `kerno_test_k35_<pid>_Rôle "Été"` owning a schema,
scratch databases `kerno_test_k35_src` / `_dst`, the role dropped, the restore fails with the plain words, the
retry restores the schema owned by `kerno` with its two rows; all dropped at the end) [`pg_roles` not asked].
MySQL and MariaDB looked at only (D-108): nothing built.

### K36 — A table's rows keep loading as you scroll; the size of a load changed in place (M)

**Now.** A first user outside the owner, replacing DBeaver by Kerno 0.3.0: a query's result fetches more as
the scroll reaches its end, a table's data turns pages ("Page 2 of 13", Previous, Next); its size is Settings'
alone, while DBeaver shows the fetch size at the bottom of the data and changes it there.

**Wanted.** As DBeaver's data viewer: the next rows read when the scroll nears the end and added under those
there, with everything on screen and pending kept; the footer saying what is loaded against what there is,
the size of a load as a control (a list and a typed size) and a visible way to load more, by hand and by key;
no row twice and none skipped whatever the table; refresh, sort, filter and Apply sensible after several
loads; the pages gone cleanly.

Done: D-109. `data/tab.rs`: `load_size` (Settings' size when the tab opens) instead of `page` and `page_size`;
`fetch_more(all)` and `landed` (a load chained on the tab's session, `LIMIT size OFFSET <rows read>` added to
the grid for a keyed table, `LIMIT <rows read + size>` from the first row in place of the rows for a table
without a key or a view, Fetch all load after load up to 100,000 rows each or one read without a limit; a
failure or a cancel kept in `load_error`, said in the footer, the button "Try again"); `rows_sql` (the one
statement of every read); `reload` reads as many rows as are loaded and keeps the `Place` (selection, first row
in view, record view), `restart` (sort, filter, reset layout) one load; `settle_end` (a counted table whose rows
are all here); the footer: "Rows 1–N of M", `data-fetch-size` (`load_size_button`, `ask_load_size`),
`data-fetch-more`, `data-fetch-all`, `data-fetch-error`; `turn_page`, `has_next`, `page_text` and the pager
removed. `query/grid.rs`: `LoadRows`, `Grid::table_rows` (was `page`: not complete while the table may have
more), `asked`, `took_rows` (adds, or replaces from the first row; a cell typed in an added row kept first),
`has_more` for a table's grid, the delegate's `load_more` deferring to the tab, no load by the scroll while rows
are added; "Go to row" past the rows read. `query/find.rs`: `Find::recount` after rows replaced. `query/value_panel/fk_preview.rs`: `forget_reads`
forgets the key shown too, so a row read again with its selection kept has its foreign key's row read again
(`fk_preview::a_composite_key_uses_every_column` failed without it).
`workspace.rs`, `actions.rs`: Fetch More Rows and Fetch All Rows global, to the active table or query tab.
`settings.rs`: `page_size` (the typed size rule shared by Settings and the footer's dialog); `settings_view.rs`:
"Rows fetched at a time" and the scroll switch's text name a table too.

Tests (`ui_tests/table_loads.rs`, new), each seen failing under the mutation in brackets:
`a_load_adds_rows_under_those_there_and_keeps_what_was_pending` (PostgreSQL and MySQL: the scroll's load once,
the same grid, an edit, a deletion and the selection kept, row numbers on; a row added holds the scroll's loads
back; Fetch more in the record view puts the rows above it, the selection follows it, the record view stays;
the statements address their rows; Apply reads back 250 rows as the server has them) [the delegate not asking
the tab; a load reading from the first row again; the added-row rule removed; the selection not shifted];
`a_fetch_size_changed_in_the_footer_is_read_by_the_next_load` (the list, then "Other size…" refusing 50 and
taking "2,50" with Enter; Settings unchanged) [the size not kept];
`loads_of_a_keyless_table_neither_repeat_nor_skip_a_row` (PostgreSQL, a `json` column, rows moved in the heap
by an `UPDATE` between loads of 100, 120 and 80: every row once; the last load, exactly what is left, ends the
way on) [keyless loads by offset: rows twice and skipped; `settle_end` doing nothing];
`refresh_sort_and_filter_after_several_loads` (300 rows read back with the selection, a sort and a filter from
one load, the next load in the sorted order) [a refresh of one load; the place not kept; a sort keeping the
rows]; `a_failed_load_keeps_the_rows_and_the_edits_and_is_tried_again` (the table dropped: rows, edit, message,
"Try again" that works once it is back) [the failure failing the tab];
`the_fetch_keys_reach_a_table_and_fetch_all_grows_until_cancelled` (Ctrl+Alt+N and Ctrl++ pressed on the grid
of `shop.orders`; on `analytics.big_table` Fetch all past 20,000 rows, then Cancel: nothing more) [the keys
routed to query tabs only; the two commands back in the query tab's key context; Fetch all stopping after one
load]; `fk_preview::a_composite_key_uses_every_column` failed in the whole suite until `forget_reads`. Updated: `a_table_is_read_in_loads_sorted_and_filtered_by_the_server` (was
`…_in_pages_…`), `a_tables_footer_says_what_is_fetched_and_what_comes_next` (was
`a_tables_pager_says_where_it_is_and_what_next_shows`), and the footer ids of `daily_use.rs`, `split.rs` and
the dropped-table test.

Measured (D-109, `docs/BENCH.md`), offscreen, release, `analytics.big_table` with a fetch size of 10,000: loads
of 10,000 rows at offsets 10,000 to 90,000 in 50 to 68 ms; +29 MiB from 10,000 to 100,000 rows; scrolling the
100,000 rows 7.72 ms a frame on average (99th percentile 13.75 ms).

**Captures** (offscreen, temporary test, not committed), light and dark, 1200 × 800: a table after three loads,
the fetch size list open, the "Other size…" dialog, the end of a counted table.

### K38 — A connection's SQL scripts, in its right-click menu (M)

**Now.** The owner: "when I right-click a connection, I want to see in a submenu all the SQL scripts linked to
that connection, like a small folder". DBeaver's connection menu has SQL Editor ▸ Open SQL script / Recent SQL
script. Kerno's saved queries carry their connection but are reached only through the library; its `.sql`
files are in the toolbar's list of ten, which does not say which connection each was used on (C6 kept the last
connection of each of those ten, to propose it).

**Wanted.** A submenu beside New SQL editor with the connection's saved queries by folder and the files last used
on it; a click opens it on that connection, connecting first, never twice; a short list stays a menu; the same
list from the keyboard; the link follows the connection (copy, delete, export); no stored data lost.

Done: D-111. `settings.rs`: `connection_scripts` (id → files, the latest first, `CONNECTION_SCRIPTS` = 10),
written by `remember_script` (all three places that link a file to a connection already call it: opening,
saving, `QueryTab::set_target`, so `query/tab.rs` is untouched), started from C6's map for older settings;
`forget_script` (a file gone, every list) and `forget_connection`. `library.rs`: `SavedQueries`, the saved
queries read off the UI thread at start and after each change (the library's delete too); its checkbox says
"This connection only". `sidebar.rs`: the `SCRIPTS` submenu filled by `with_scripts` (the last
`MENU_SAVED_QUERIES` = 10 saved, by folder, then the files, `file_names` adding the folder to names listed twice;
"All scripts…" when queries were left out, "Open SQL file…", "Saved queries…"; "No SQL scripts yet"), the
actions `OpenScript(Script)`, `AllScripts`, `OpenSqlFileHere`, `SavedQueriesHere` and their events,
`Entry::Note`, a submenu label as `SharedString`, `scripts_of`. `workspace.rs`: `when_connected` (K28's
`pending_editor` now holds what to open), `open_script`, `open_saved_query_on`, `open_sql_file_on` (the file
read first, then bound to the connection picked), `open_sql_file_here`, the library on the connection.
`quick_open.rs`: `open_scripts`, the picker. `connections/manager.rs`: a deleted connection's list goes, the
saved queries are read again. `actions.rs`: "SQL Scripts of Selected Connection…" (`connection_scripts`) and
"Open SQL File on Selected Connection…" (`open_sql_file_on_connection`), both in File ▸ (`main.rs`);
`menu_palette.rs` maps the new entries.

Tests, each seen failing under the mutation in brackets: unit
`settings::tests::each_connection_keeps_its_own_last_files` (fourteen files on one connection, one shared with a
second, one saved unbound; again with the first; a file gone; a connection deleted) [no cap per connection];
`the_settings_of_before_k38_load_with_their_files_under_their_connection` [no start from C6's map]. UI
`ui_tests/connection_scripts.rs`: `the_menu_lists_saved_queries_by_folder_then_files` (a folder of two, one at the
top, another connection's and an any-connection query left out, two `report.sql` in two folders; the real
right-click, SQL scripts ▸, the folder) [no folder after a name listed twice];
`a_connection_without_scripts_says_so_and_offers_how_to_make_one` (and "Saved queries…" opens the library on it)
[no "No SQL scripts yet"]; `a_long_list_shows_the_last_saved_then_finds_the_others` (twelve: the last ten, then
"All scripts…", whose picker lists the first and opens it, typed, on the connection, against `kerno-postgres`)
[every saved query listed]; `a_click_connects_first_opens_on_the_connection_and_never_twice` (against
`kerno-postgres`, closed: the file from the real menu connects it and opens bound to it with its text; the saved
query; each again shows its tab; a tab holding a saved query's text under another title is shown) [the connection
picked ignored for a file; a saved query opened twice; the text not matched];
`a_file_gone_since_is_said_leaves_the_lists_and_connects_nothing` [only the toolbar's list forgetting it];
`a_copy_starts_without_scripts_and_a_deleted_connection_leaves_none` (and an export holds no path) [the deleted
connection's list kept]. `menu_palette::every_menu_entry_and_toolbar_button_is_in_the_palette` walks the new
entries (`with_scripts` with eleven queries and a file).

**Not done** (D-111): the notification of a file gone is the toolbar's, not asserted by the test; an unsaved
connection (J4) leaves its list in the settings under its temporary id; nothing checks a file exists before the
menu shows it.

**Captures** (offscreen, temporary test, not committed), light and dark: SQL scripts ▸ with its folders and
files (two `report.sql` with their folder), a folder open, the empty one, and the picker of "All scripts…". The

### K37 — See where a running script stands, in the editor's margin (M)

**Now.** A first user outside the owner, replacing DBeaver by Kerno 0.3.0: in DBeaver the ruler at the left of
the editor shows where a long script stands, in real time; in Kerno nothing showed it, and the status bar only
said "running…".

**Wanted.** While a run of statements goes (Run script, a selection of several, each tab of a run on several
connections), the margin beside each statement says how it stands, moving as each starts and ends; the running
one plainly alive, its time going up; the marks staying after the run as its trace until the next run or an
edit that makes them wrong; never the user's cursor, selection or scroll; with line numbers off, soft wrap,
folds, zoom, vim mode, both themes, one-line and 300-line statements and 5,000 statements; not colour alone.

Done: D-110. `query/run_marks.rs` (new): `Mark` (running, done, failed, cancelled, refused: a play sign in a
breathing halo, a tick, a cross, a square, a barred circle), `Trace` (the statements of the last run from the
editor by their lines, in text order; `set`, `stopped`, `clear`; `meeting`, two binary searches), `layout` (the
marks of the rows on screen from the editor's latest layout: `visible_row_range`, `line_height`, the scrolled
`text_bounds`, `display_map` across wraps and folds; nothing when the text's length differs) and `overlay` (a
canvas after the editor, painted with paths and quads, clipped to the editor). `query/tab.rs`:
`Statement.origin` becomes `place` (the range read, kept when parameters are bound); the strip reserved once in
`new` (an empty gutter collection, 12 px); the trace made by `start` when the run has places, set at a
statement's start (running), at its first page of rows and its end (done, failed, cancelled), at a refusal
(refused); `stop` turns the running one cancelled, a connection cut off under the run failed; an edit
(`InputEvent::Change`) clears it; `running_text` ("running statement 3 of 12 · 4.20 s") and a 250 ms ticker
while a statement runs; `trace_text` (`Trace::summary`: "statement 3 of 4 failed", "4 of 4 statements done")
once it ended. `workspace.rs`, `tabs.rs`, `menu_palette.rs`: the status bar's running item says it, and a new
item `status-run-trace` (Copy) the words of the marks. `theme.rs`: the marks' contrast.

Tests, each seen failing under the mutation in brackets: unit (`run_marks.rs`)
`a_statement_covers_the_lines_of_its_text` [the line-start rule removed],
`marks_follow_the_run_and_a_stop_cancels_the_running_one` [`stopped` leaving the running mark],
`only_the_statements_of_the_lines_asked_are_met` [the search from the first statement],
`a_failure_or_the_running_mark_is_painted_last` [the running mark on the lowest layer],
`the_marks_are_said_in_words_once_the_run_ended` [another text's length accepted];
`theme::tests::the_marks_of_a_run_read_on_the_editor_in_both_themes` [the running mark given `border`'s colour]; UI
(`ui_tests/run_marks.rs`, PostgreSQL unless said) `a_script_s_marks_move_as_it_runs_and_stay_until_an_edit`
(statement 1 done, 2 running and 3 bare in the middle of the run, drawn beside their rows; the status bar's
"running statement 2 of 3 · "; all done after; cursor, selection and scroll untouched; an edit clears them; the
next run of one statement marks it alone) [the running mark not set; the status without "statement k of n"; the
edit not clearing], `a_failure_marks_where_the_run_stopped` [an error marked done] (its words after the run, and
those of three more tests [`trace_text` saying nothing]),
`with_continue_on_error_the_statements_after_a_failure_are_marked` (J14's `run_script`) [an error marked done],
`a_refused_write_on_a_read_only_connection_is_marked` [the refusal not marked],
`a_cancelled_statement_is_marked_and_the_rest_left_bare` (⌘.) [a cancel marked failed],
`a_statement_cut_off_by_a_disconnect_is_marked_failed` [the cut-off statement not marked failed],
`a_result_read_again_leaves_the_marks_alone` (Run again on a result) [the run marking the tab's trace whatever its
statements], `on_mysql_the_running_statement_is_marked_too` [the running mark not set; the status without
"statement k of n"], `the_marks_sit_beside_their_lines_however_the_editor_draws_them` (no server: line numbers on
and off, zoomed, vim mode, soft wrap, 300 lines, a fold, 5,000 statements there and far down) [a wrapped line
counted as one row; the strip not reserved].

**Timing** (point 6), in the debug UI test harness (a scratch test, not committed): a run of 5,000 one-row inserts
into a `kerno_test_*` table, Run script, each statement's result as today: 1,138 s on the base commit `d06d08a`,
1,159 s with K37 (+1.8 %; one run each, other builds on the machine: within noise). In both the insert rate
fell from about 12 to about 3 a second as the results piled up. The 250 ms ticker does not run under the test
clock. What K37 adds per statement is a binary search; per frame, the margin's
layout with 5,000 marked statements took 5.4 µs (34 marks on screen; a whole headless frame 1.8 ms).

**Not done** (D-110): no tooltip per mark (the status bar says the marks in words after the run); the
run-on-several-connections window does not say "statement k of n"; the view does not follow the running statement
(DBeaver's does: the case is made for the owner).

**Captures** (offscreen, temporary test, not committed), light and dark, 1200 × 800: a run in progress (statement 1
done, 2 running, 3 to come; the status bar's "running statement 2 of 3 · 1.24 s"), with line numbers on and off;
the same cancelled; a run that failed on its third statement, line numbers on and off; a soft-wrapped statement
done and the next running.

### K40 — Three things in a table's data tab, and a tab's close cross (S)

**Now.** Reported on 0.3.1: a sort from a header of a wide table scrolled to the right jumps back to the first
column; the WHERE field proposes nothing while the editor proposes columns; the hover of a tab's close cross is
a pale square too large and against the tab's edge. The owner asks whether a column order is kept.

Done: D-113. `data/tab.rs`: `show` carries the horizontal offset of the grid it replaces to the new one (sort,
filter, refresh, Apply, reset layout; the vertical place unchanged); `raw_filter` an `EditorState` made by
`completion::condition_field` (one line, Enter submits), its provider set in `show` from the table's columns, Tab
captured (accept with J1's setting, else the next control). `query/completion.rs`: `SqlCompletion::prefix`
(`SELECT * FROM t WHERE ` before the text, the ranges moved back), `NOT_A_CONDITION`, `condition_completion`,
`condition_field`, `tab_accepts` (the editor's J1 capture, shared). `kerno-core` `sql/complete.rs`: `ILIKE`
after an operand of WHERE on PostgreSQL. `query/tab.rs`: `next_scroll`/`carried_scroll`, the offset of a result
read again on the server. `workspace.rs`: the close button 16 px, `mx_1`, `rounded_sm`.

`a_sort_a_filter_and_a_refresh_keep_the_columns_scrolled` [offset not carried];
`a_column_moved_keeps_its_place_after_closing_and_a_restart` [`order` not stored];
`the_where_field_proposes_the_tables_columns` [`ILIKE` removed; `NOT_A_CONDITION` not applied]; core:
`after_a_column_in_where_come_the_words_of_a_condition` [`ILIKE` removed].

### K39 — A restore whose archive needs an extension the server does not have (M)

**Now.** The K35 user, after "Restore without owners and privileges": pg_restore 18 stopped again, further on, at
`CREATE EXTENSION IF NOT EXISTS pgaudit` ("extension "pgaudit" is not available"): the production server has an
extension whose files the laptop's PostgreSQL lacks. Two failures in a row, each found only by running.

Done: D-112. Core `tools/archive.rs` (new): `list_database`, `list_extensions`, `missing_owners` (an owner matched
against the whole end of the entry line, so a server role with a space is found), `list_without` (an extension's
`EXTENSION` and `COMMENT - EXTENSION` entries commented out) and `refused_extensions` (the failed `CREATE
EXTENSION` whose name the server's message holds, as K35); `roles.rs` shares its scan (`failed_statements`);
`RestoreOptions::use_list` gives `--use-list` (refused for a script). App `backup_dialog.rs`: `Input::list` (the
`pg_restore --list` K35 already ran for the database name, kept); `check_archive` after the probe asks
`pg_available_extensions` and `pg_roles` on the metadata session, then shows `restore-preflight` (simple view
and wizard) with `restore-leave-out` ticked and the owners' box ticked; `use_list` writes the list as a new file
in the temporary folder before the run and removes it after; `shown` adds to the command "# leaves out of the
archive the extension “pgaudit” (…)"; `check_roles` also reads failed extensions, `extensions_text`, one retry
button whose label names what it leaves out.

Tests, each seen failing under the mutation in brackets: core `tools::archive::tests::`
`the_list_names_its_database_extensions_and_owners` and `leaving_an_extension_out_comments_its_entries` (the
real `pg_restore --list` of pg_restore 18.7 on a pg_dump 17 archive with `pg_trgm` and `uuid-ossp`) [the
comment's entry kept], `a_failed_extension_is_read_in_any_language` (the user's block verbatim, its English
form, `"Ext-Été"`, psql); `tools::tests::each_restore_option_gives_its_argument` [no `--use-list`]; unit
`a_missing_extension_is_said_with_what_to_do` [the retry offered without the list]. UI
`ui_tests/restore_extensions.rs` (stand-in pg_restore, the real server's answers): before the run [the
extensions not ticked; the list file kept], after a failure naming the extension and the owner, one retry
[failed extensions not read; the retry not leaving the extension out]; the real tools on `kerno_test_k39_*`
[`--use-list` not passed]. K35's real-tools test now unticks the box the preflight ticks [owners not ticked].

**Not done** (D-112): a server that truly lacks an extension is not available here: the "not available" answer
is the real server's for `pgaudit`, through the stand-in pg_restore; a missing role whose name holds a space is
named by its last word.

**Now.** After the simple restore's "Faster" (450 s to 219 s on the owner's backup), the owner asked for the same

`parallel_jobs_go_with_the_directory_format_only` [jobs refused with the custom format only]. UI
(`ui_tests/backup_simple.rs`): `faster_backs_up_into_a_folder_and_the_wizard_shows_it` (stand-in pg_dump) [the
format, then the jobs, noted as hidden; the reset dropping the format, then clearing the jobs; an extension
choosing the format while ticked; the archive's card not shown chosen];
`the_simple_restores_faster_is_not_a_hidden_change` [the one-transaction default not adjusted; the reset putting one transaction back; the reset
clearing the jobs]; `a_faster_backup_removes_only_the_folder_it_made` (stand-in pg_dump: failure, a folder
already there, one appearing after Start's check, cancel) [no check before pg_dump starts; the file's wording];
`a_faster_backup_is_a_folder_the_simple_restore_reads_back` (the real pg_dump and pg_restore, `kerno_test_k48_*`,
the open panel offering folders, then the typed path) [the folder's size not summed].

**Measured** (BENCH.md): `kerno` 5.3-5.7 s with, 5.7-5.9 s without (one table is 301 of its 317 MB); eight
tables of 125,000 rows, 1.0-1.4 s with, 3.5-3.6 s without; same sizes.

**Not done**: a hot standby or a server short of connections (pg_dump's message, the failure path tested with a
stand-in); no pass in the real app; captures offscreen in the agent's scratchpad, not committed.

### K49 — A foreign key seen in the grid, followed in one gesture (M)

**Now.** The owner, 2026-11-23: a foreign key's column looks like any other, and the only way to the row it points
to is the cell's menu (E3). DBeaver marks a link cell and opens the referenced rows on a click on the mark or a
Ctrl+click on the value.

Done: D-118. `query/grid.rs`: `links` (per result column, the tooltip of its header's mark, from the structure's
foreign keys, worked out in `with_owner` and `set_edit_target`), `link`, `jump` (the follow number and the jump of
a cell, through `data::tab::follow_at`), `follow` (runs the owner's `GridAction::Follow`), `hovered` and
`shows_arrow`; `render_th` draws the Link2 glyph with `aria_description` and a tooltip, `content_width` counts it;
`render_td` lays the cell out in a row with the arrow (`arrow_element`, also the record view's) only on the
hovered or selected cell, follows the pointer with `on_hover` on a foreign key's cells only, and ⌘-click follows
in its mouse down; `link_text`, `arrow_tip`, `follow_key`. `data/tab.rs`: `reference` worded "Open s.t where c =
v", `follow_at` (shared with `fk_preview::wanted_at`). `query/tab.rs`: the result's `Owner::related` from the
grid's structure once known, `follow`, `QueryTabEvent::OpenData`. `workspace.rs`: `FollowForeignKey` on the active
grid's selected cell (a notification when it holds no key), `OpenData` opens the table's tab. `actions.rs`: "Open
the Rows a Foreign Key Points To", ⌘↓ (Ctrl+Down) in `DataTable`; `main.rs`: the Data menu; the cell editor stops the command.
`grid_view.rs`: the record view's arrow after the value. `engine_logo.rs`: `ArrowUpRight`. `theme.rs`: the
contrast test.

Tests (`ui_tests/k49.rs`, new), each seen failing under the mutation in brackets:
`a_foreign_key_is_marked_and_followed_from_its_cell` (PostgreSQL's `shop.orders` and MySQL's `orders`: the mark
and its tooltip, the arrow on a hovered and on a selected cell, a click, ⌘-click and ⌘↓ each opening what the
menu opens, the key on a cell of no key notified, a double click on the value editing, the command while typing
staying in the cell, one on the arrow opening one tab, the selection kept by a click on the arrow, shift and click) [mark not drawn; arrow never shown;
hover not followed; no arrow on the selected cell; the arrow's press let through; a double click on the arrow
following twice; ⌘-click not followed; the key's handler doing nothing; the cell editor not stopping the command; the tooltip without the click and the
key]; `a_key_of_two_columns_null_keys_pending_values_and_the_record_view` (PostgreSQL and MariaDB on
`kerno_test_k49`: a key of two columns from both its columns, NULL, a pending value and a pending default, the
record view) [an arrow on a NULL key; every jump numbered as the first; the record view without arrows];
`a_result_of_one_table_and_sqlite_follow_their_foreign_keys` (`select * from shop.orders`, MySQL's `orders`, a
SQLite file the test makes, a SQLite result without marks) [marks not worked out in `set_edit_target`; the
result's `Follow` not handled]; the three [the label in the old words]. `theme::tests::foreign_key_marks_meet_wcag`
[the light link at `#9DB070`]. `fk_preview::an_editable_result_previews_its_foreign_keys` now opens the row from
a result (it asserted the opposite), `foreign_keys_lead_to_the_referenced_row_and_back` asserts the new label. A
`stop_propagation` in the arrow's click handler survived its mutation (the press already stops the table's
click): removed.

Frame times, offscreen (release, `HeadlessAppContext`, 1200 × 800, not committed), a table with foreign keys
against the same table without, two runs each: 100,000 rows of 20 columns (five keys), 150 rows a frame, 7.47
and 8.82 ms on average against 9.01 and 8.41 ms; 300 columns (thirty keys) across, one a frame, 8.02 and 7.01 ms
against 8.27 and 7.43 ms; down, 17 rows a frame, 8.47 and 8.02 ms against 8.28 and 7.90 ms (`docs/BENCH.md`).

**Not done.** A primary key referenced by other tables says nothing in its header (D-118). A result that is not
one table's, on a read-only connection or on SQLite, has no marks. A binary key (`bytea`, `BINARY(16)`) has a
mark but no arrow, as the menu has no entry for it. A column in two keys follows the first.

**Corrected by the pass in the real app (2026-11-23).** The hovered arrow stayed where the pointer no longer
was: the cell's hover listener lives in GPUI's state of that element, lost whenever the cell is not drawn for a
frame (its tab hidden, its editor open, scrolled out) and blind after a key, so a cell drawn again never heard
the pointer leave (shown again after following its arrow, it kept the arrow with the pointer in the editor;
after a double click and Escape, until the next click). Now `query/grid.rs` `hover_watch`, on the hovered cell
only (no cost for the others): each frame it is drawn, the pointer is looked for in it (in its visible part),
and until the next frame a move out of it or out of the window takes the arrow away; a pointer that left the
window and has not moved does not hover again (GPUI still places it inside). The arrow is drawn only while the
pointer is over its very cell: not after the tab is shown again, the editor closed, the rows scrolled or read
again under a still pointer. A tab shown again may draw the old arrow for one frame before the check removes
it. Tests `ui_tests/k54.rs`: `the_arrow_is_gone_when_its_tab_is_shown_again` (the owner's path on `select *
from shop.orders limit 50` and MySQL's `orders`) [the frame's check off],
`the_arrow_leaves_after_an_edit_and_under_scrolled_rows` [the move listener off; the exit listener off; the
frame's check off], `the_arrow_leaves_with_the_pointer` (to a cell of no key, another key's cell, the header,
out of the grid, out of the window) [the exited pointer allowed to hover again; and with K49's own clearing,
the move listener and the frame's check all off].

### K50 — The AI assistant's richer context (S)

**Now.** The model was told the names of the schemas, the tables a request names (else the tab's schema) and
their columns with types and foreign keys, from the completion catalog. It did not know which columns can be
NULL, the primary key or the indexes: joins guessed their keys and a plan's advice ignored the indexes there.

**Wanted** (the owner's list of 2026-11-23, item 5 of ROADMAP "The AI assistant, next"): for the tables given,
each column's type and nullability, the primary key, the foreign keys (columns, the table and columns they
reference) and the indexes (columns, unique or not); no comment, default or row count, never a row's value.
Read when the request opens, off the UI thread, bounded, "reading…" in the preview meanwhile; the tables a
plan's or an error's statement names first and in full; a text a person reads; PostgreSQL, MySQL, MariaDB,
SQLite.

Done: D-119. Core `ai.rs`: `Detail` (`Detail::of(&TableStructure)`: columns with nullability, the primary key in
key order, foreign keys on the table's columns, the indexes but the primary key's, each part that is not
exactly a column's name sent as `(expression)` and an index of expressions only left out; nothing else of the
structure) and its text form,
one line per column under the table's name; `read_details(session, tables)` (`table_structure`, 4 at a time,
15 s each, a failure given back per table); `Context::detail_tables` (the first `MAX_DETAILED` = 30 tables
described) and `add_details`; `from_catalog` takes the text that chooses the tables and orders the tables it
names by their first place in it; caps `MAX_COLUMNS` 100, `MAX_INDEXES` 12, `MAX_KEYS` 12 per table, said as
"… N more columns". App `ai_view.rs`: the panel reads the details when it opens and when the sentence names
new tables, each table once; the preview says "(reading the tables' keys and indexes…)" and Send waits.

Tests, each seen failing under the mutation in brackets: core `ai::tests::`
`a_table_read_in_full_says_nulls_keys_and_indexes_and_no_value` [" not null" dropped; the primary key's index
kept; a column's default written after its type; the tables not ordered by their place],
`a_table_read_in_full_stays_within_its_caps` [no column cap]; expression and partial indexes holding literals
(core above, `kerno-postgres` `ai_context::expression_and_partial_indexes_send_no_value`, `kerno-mysql`
`ai_context::expression_indexes_send_no_value` (MySQL; MariaDB has no functional index), `kerno-sqlite`
`the_ai_context_says_nulls_keys_and_indexes`) [index parts sent as written];
`the_context_holds_the_tables_named_else_the_current_schema` [not ordered]; drivers (fixture read only)
`kerno-postgres` `ai_context::the_tables_a_statement_names_are_described_with_their_keys_first` [" not null"
dropped and the primary key's index kept; the default written], `ai_context::the_shop_schema_before_and_after`
(prints D-119's numbers), `kerno-mysql` `ai_context::the_tables_a_statement_names_are_described_with_their_keys_first`
(MySQL and MariaDB), `kerno-sqlite` `the_ai_context_says_nulls_keys_and_indexes` [the default written]; UI
`ai::the_body_is_the_preview_and_holds_rows_only_with_the_tick`, `ai::the_context_is_built_on_mysql_and_mariadb`
[the details never read].

**Not done**: what the navigator or a structure tab already read is not reused (D-119); the details of a table
that cannot be read are skipped, not said in the panel.

### K51 — The AI assistant as a conversation in a side panel (L)

**Now.** Each request was one question and one answer in a panel inside the query tab; to refine, the user
started again. The answer came whole.

**Wanted** (the owner's list of 2026-11-23, item 1 of ROADMAP "The AI assistant, next"): a panel docked at the
side of the window holding a conversation tied to the active query tab; follow-ups sent with the earlier turns
within a budget that says when the oldest are left out; the editor's query as a removable chip; Insert,
Replace and Copy under SQL blocks; the four actions through it; streamed answers if they fit; every state in
words; keyboard first; J17's rules unchanged.

Done: D-120. Core `ai/chat.rs` (new): `Turn`, `Role`, `left_out` (20 turns, 40,000 characters, whole
exchanges), `with_query` (20,000 characters), `build_chat` (the one body builder; system message with the
context once; `"stream": true`), `Events` and `read_stream` (server-sent events cut anywhere, `[DONE]`, an
error event, reasoning fields not read), `segments` / `Segment::sql`. `ai.rs`: `Action::message` (the
instruction in the user's turn), `build_payload` = `build_chat` with no turn, `exchange` (the one socket
function, reading a streamed answer within the same cap; a stop seen after the read), `chat_stream`.
`testing.rs`: `FakeAi::start_answers`, `FakeAi::streaming`. App `ai_view.rs` rewritten: `beside` (the panel
right of the tabs, `ai-dock-split`, width saved on resize), `open(tab, Draft)` (the entry point for K53),
`toggle`, `new_conversation`, `status_item`, `AiPanel` (thread, field, chips, preview, Send/Stop, picker,
streamed pieces through a channel, `insert_sql`, `replace_sql`, the editor's statement re-read when its
selection or length moved). `query/tab.rs`: `ai_request` builds the action then defers `open`; `ai_chat`,
`ai_statement`, `ai_facts`; the in-tab panel removed. `workspace.rs`: `beside` around the tabs, two actions,
the status item. `actions.rs`: `ToggleAiPanel` (⇧⌘J), `AiNewConversation`; `main.rs`: Query ▸ AI Assistant;
`settings.rs`: `ai_panel`, `ai_panel_width`.

Tests, each seen failing under the mutation in brackets: core `ai::chat::tests::`
`a_follow_up_is_sent_with_the_turns_and_the_schema_once` [earlier turns not sent],
`the_budget_leaves_out_the_oldest_exchanges_whole` [no budget; earlier turns not sent],
`a_stream_cut_anywhere_reads_the_same` and `a_stream_stopped_in_the_middle_says_so_after_what_came` [a line
cut between two reads lost], `the_editor_query_is_attached_and_cut_when_long`,
`an_error_event_and_an_answer_of_reasoning_only_are_said`, `an_answer_is_prose_and_code_blocks`; `tests/ai.rs`
`a_streamed_answer_arrives_piece_by_piece`, `a_streamed_answer_is_stopped_in_the_middle` [the stop not checked
after the read: the answer came back whole]. UI `ui_tests/ai_chat.rs` (new):
`a_follow_up_goes_with_the_earlier_turns_and_refines_the_answer` [earlier turns not sent],
`a_long_conversation_leaves_out_its_oldest_turns_and_says_so` [no budget],
`the_editor_query_goes_with_a_message_unless_its_chip_is_removed` [the removed chip still sent],
`insert_and_replace_put_the_sql_where_they_say_and_run_nothing` [Replace inserting],
`a_streamed_answer_shows_as_it_comes_and_stops` [Stop keeping what came; New conversation not closing the
request], `escape_gives_the_keyboard_back_to_the_editor` [Escape not handled],
`the_actions_open_the_panel_with_the_message_ready_and_send_nothing` and
`open_with_a_draft_fills_the_field_and_sends_nothing` [`open` sending at once],
`every_state_of_the_panel_has_its_words` [no status entry],
`switching_tabs_switches_the_conversation` [one panel for every tab]. The J17 tests of `ui_tests/ai.rs` and
`ai_endpoints.rs` run on the new panel (a sent message empties the field, Insert of a fix goes at the cursor,

`m12-ai-empty-*`, `m12-ai-preview-*`, `m12-ai-narrow-*` (light and dark).

**Not done**: dragging the panel's edge is not driven in a test (the saved width is: clamped, remembered);
Shift+Enter making a line is the kit's, not tested here; a late answer of a stopped request is dropped by the
generation check and by the closed connection together, not by one alone in a test; the text of an answer

**Fixed after the first pass in the real app** (D-120, last point), each test seen failing under the mutation in
brackets: the keyboard left in a panel no longer drawn (Close with the assistant off, and every other way) —
`ai_chat::the_keyboard_comes_back_whatever_takes_the_panel_away` [the check skipped]; Insert after a statement

### K53 — The AI assistant asked from a right-click (M)

**Now.** The assistant was asked from the editor only (a sentence, the statement at the cursor, a plan, an
error). A user looking at a table in the tree, its data or its structure, or at a result, had to describe it
in words or open a query tab first.

**Wanted** (the owner's list of 2026-11-23, item 4 of ROADMAP "The AI assistant, next"): on a table, a view or
a materialized view (the tree, a data tab, a structure tab) an AI submenu (Ask about this table…, Explain this
table, Write a query on this table…, Generate test data…), greyed with the reason while off; on columns
(the tree, a structure tab, a grid's headers) Explain these columns; on a result grid Explain this result and
Ask about the selection…, a row's values sent only by a tick that says how many cells go; each entry a palette
command; nothing sent by itself; J17's rules unchanged; PostgreSQL, MySQL, MariaDB, SQLite; both themes.

Done: D-122. Core `ai/ask.rs` (new): `Subject::of` (a table's names, types and the tables its foreign keys
link, nothing else of its structure), `Shown`, `Values` (with the tick's words), `Ask` (seven kinds:
`message(typed, engine, values)`, `chip`, `needs_words`, `tables`, `put_first`), `test_rows` (10, 1 to 100);
`ai.rs`: `pub mod ask`. App `ai_view/ask.rs` (new, a child of `ai_view`): `Entry` (labels, command ids),
`shown`, `caution` (read-only, or an environment that asks twice before a write), `of_table`, `of_grid`
(values read from what was fetched, on the UI thread: 20 rows, 500 cells, 50 columns, binary by size),
`of_data`, `grid_menu`, `with_ai`, `bar_button`, `open`, `Asked`, and `AiPanel::{set_ask, catalog_read,
set_ask_values, …}`. `ai_view.rs` hooks: the `asked` and `new_tab` fields, reset in `prepare`; `message`
returns the ask's; the editor's query not attached with one; `refresh` puts its tables first; `payload` and
Send wait for the catalog; the sent chips; the subject goes once; the values' tick in place of the tables'
sample rows; "Open in a new tab" under SQL blocks after "Generate test data…". `query/tab.rs`:
`tell_ai_catalog` from `load_catalog` (read or failed), `ask_about_result`, the grid owner's `Ai` arm.
`query/grid.rs`: `GridAction::Ai`, its label, AI ▸ added by the three menu builders (`ai_menu`).
`data/tab.rs`: `DataTabEvent::AskAi`, `ask_ai` (the tab's `SELECT` with `?` in place of every literal,
whatever is ticked: `kerno-core` `sql::without_literals`, after an automated review), the footer's AI button,
the `Ai` arm. `structure/tab.rs`:
`StructureTabEvent::AskAi`, the bar's AI button, `ask_ai`, `selected_column`. `navigator/view.rs`:
`NavigatorEvent::AskAi`, `ai_entries`, `ask_ai`, `ask_ai_on_selected`, AI ▸ in `object_menu` before Refresh.
`workspace.rs`: `ask_ai` (a query tab on the table's schema, MySQL's database, titled "Ask · customers"; the
next question on the same connection and database takes it while its editor is empty), `ask_command`, the three
events, seven actions. `actions.rs`: seven commands; `main.rs`: Query ▸ AI Assistant; `menu_palette.rs`:
the grid's `Ai` arm, the AI ▸ walk, the tree's AI entries checked; `ui_tests/sqlite.rs`: `fixture_file`
and `file_config` shared with `ai_ask`.

Tests, each seen failing under the mutation in brackets: core `ai::ask::tests::`
`the_subject_comes_first_and_in_full_whatever_the_words_name` (decoy tables named "data", "table",
