### K52 — Ask the assistant in the editor, and fix an error in one click, both as a diff (M)

**Now.** After K51 the assistant answered in a panel at the side: its SQL went into the editor with Insert or
Replace, unseen against what it replaced; a failed statement's fix was a panel's answer to read, then insert.

**Wanted** (the owner's list of 2026-11-23, items 2 and 3 of ROADMAP "The AI assistant, next"): a shortcut that
opens a field at the cursor; the proposal as a before / after diff in the editor to accept or refuse, Accept one
undo step, Reject leaving everything as it was; refined in place; "Fix with AI" on a failed statement sending
the statement and the error, its fix as the same diff, nothing run; honest states (sending, Stop, a late answer
dropped after a stop, an edit, a tab switch or a close, errors in words, off); J17's rules unchanged.

Done: D-121. Core `ai/inline.rs` (new, declared by one line at the end of `ai.rs`): `Inline` (`Ask` with the SQL
to change or none, `Fix` with the error and its position) and its `message`, `refine`, `proposed_sql` (a closed
SQL block only), `replacement` (line ending, the `;` left after a statement, an insertion on its own line and
ended), `still_there`, `diff` (lines kept, removed, added) and `removed_ranges`. App `query/ai_inline.rs` (new):
`AiInline` (the field, the context with K50's details, the turns, the request through `ai::chat_until` with a
`Stopper` and a generation, the proposal, the range decorations of the place and of the removed lines, Accept,
Reject, Stop, "Open in the panel", "What is sent…"), `OverEditor` (an element laid out in prepaint, deferred,
under its place from the editor's layout of the frame, or at the end of a row), `fix_chip`, `editor_entries`,
`without_tab`. `query/tab.rs`: `ResultPane::source` (the editor's bytes of a refused statement), `error_fix`
(the chip's result and place, set by `mark_error`, cleared by an edit and by `clear_error_mark`), `ask_here`,
`fix_with_ai`, the overlay and the chip after the run marks, "Fix with AI" before "Explain error with AI…" in the
error view, `cancel` stops the field's request first, `hide` closes it. `ai_view.rs`: `record_sent`,
`AiPanel::adopt`. `actions.rs`: `AiAskHere` (⌘⇧H, "secondary-shift-h"), `AiFixError`, their palette commands.
`main.rs`: both in Query ▸ AI Assistant. `workspace.rs`: both run on the active query tab, else say what they need.

Tests, each seen failing under the mutation in brackets: core `ai::inline::tests::`
`an_ask_sends_the_sql_to_change_and_the_sentence` [the SQL to change left out of the message],
`a_fix_sends_the_statement_the_error_and_its_place` [the position left out],
`a_refinement_goes_with_the_turns_so_far` [the refinement's words left out],
`only_a_closed_sql_block_is_proposed` [`sql_block`'s whole answer proposed],
`the_diff_says_lines_kept_removed_and_added` [added lines before the removed ones; a removed CRLF line's range
holding its `\r`], `line_endings_are_alike_and_tabs_count` [lines split on `\n` only],
`the_replacement_fits_the_place_it_goes` [the answer's `;` kept; CRLF not kept; an insertion not ended],
`a_statement_changed_under_the_request_is_seen` [any range of the right size still there],
`a_pure_insertion_and_a_whole_replacement`; app `query::ai_inline::tests::ask_here_has_a_key_of_its_own`
[the key ⌘K, the palette's], `tabs_are_drawn_as_the_editor_draws_them`, `the_entries_are_commands_of_the_palette`;
`theme::tests::a_proposals_lines_read_on_their_tints` [the editor's tint at 60 %; the card's removed tint at 100 %].
UI `ui_tests/ai_inline.rs` (the stand-in endpoint; PostgreSQL, the fixture read only, unless said):
`ask_here_shows_a_diff_that_accept_applies_in_one_undo_step` (the body's SQL to change and sentence, no row, the
log's bytes; the removed line tinted; the text unchanged until ⌘↩ in the field; one undo; no run, no history)
[Accept as two edits], `reject_leaves_the_text_the_selection_and_the_undo_history_alone` (Escape and the button;
the next undo is the user's own edit) [Reject moving the selection],
`a_refinement_goes_with_the_turns_and_replaces_the_diff` (then "Open in the panel") [the turns not sent],
`asking_on_an_empty_line_sends_no_sql_and_writes_at_the_cursor` [the statement taken on an empty line],
`an_answer_without_sql_is_shown_as_text_and_nothing_is_offered` [the whole answer proposed],
`stop_an_edit_or_a_tab_switch_drop_the_answer` (the place framed while waiting; ⌘. closes the connection; an
edit of the statement drops the answer and says so, ⌘⇧H starts again; leaving the tab, and closing it,
close the request) [Stop not closing the connection; an edit dropping nothing; a tab switch dropping nothing; a
closed tab keeping its request],
`fix_with_ai_proposes_the_fix_of_a_failed_statement_and_runs_nothing` (`SELECT id, totl FROM shop.orders`:
from the error view and from the chip by the mark, the statement, `column "totl" does not exist [42703]` and
"character 12" sent; the fix as a diff; accepted, not run, no history) [the refused statement's place not kept;
no chip], `off_nothing_opens_and_nothing_is_sent` (no server; ⌘⇧H is no key of the kit's) [the field opening
while off], `the_field_sits_at_its_statement_however_the_editor_draws_it` (no server: plain, zoomed, soft
wrap, a 300-line statement, vim mode taking no key of the field) [the card over its statement],
`running_while_a_proposal_is_shown_runs_the_editor_text` (⌘↩ in the editor runs `SELECT 1 AS original`; no
mutation: the proposal is never in the buffer, so nothing in the code can be undone to make it fail short of
writing it there).

**Not done** (D-121): an answer of bare SQL without a fence is not offered to accept; the card covers the lines
under its place (the kit's editor has no rows that are not text); Escape in the editor itself does not reject
(the card's field has the keyboard; Reject is there); the code-action menu of the kit is not used (its key is
Kerno's Cancel).

**Captures** (offscreen, temporary test, not committed), light and dark, 1280 × 820: the field at the cursor with
a sentence; a diff with a removed and two added lines, the line removed tinted in the text, Accept and Reject; a
failed statement with "Fix with AI" under the error and by its mark; a fix as a diff; the sending state (the place
framed, "Asking 127.0.0.1…", Stop); the diff zoomed (+4 pt) with soft wrap on.

### K57 — A Kerno left open learns of a new version (S)

**Now.** Two users had to quit and reopen Kerno to be offered an update. The check ran 3 s after the window
opened, then on a 6-hour timer that counts only the time the computer is awake (GPUI's timer stops in a sleep):
on a laptop, days. The dialog showed once per run whatever the version, and a version downloaded but not
installed (`Phase::Ready`) was never replaced by a newer one.

Done: D-123. `update.rs`: `TICK` (a minute); `Updates::clock` (the wall clock, a test sets its own),
`last_check` (stamped when a check is sent, timed or asked for), `Updates::due` (none sent yet, or six hours of
wall time since the last; not at the first look after a sleep, seen as the wall clock ahead of the executor's
clock by more than a minute; a clock set back starts the interval again). `workspace/updates.rs`:
`start_update_checks` looks at 3 s, then every `TICK`, and when the window becomes active again (registered
after the first check); `check_if_due`; `offer_update` once per version (a newer one than the one offered is
offered); `found_newer` (one place for the timed and the About window's answers) replaces an older `Ready`
download and removes it; `install_and_quit` installs only the download still in place.

Tests, each seen failing under the mutation in brackets: app `update::tests::`
`a_timed_check_is_due_after_six_hours_of_wall_time_and_not_right_at_a_wake` [no wake deferral; the clock set
back not reset]. UI (`ui_tests/updates.rs`, the fake runner and an injected wall clock that moves with the test
timer, plus the time asleep): `a_kerno_left_open_checks_after_six_hours_of_wall_time_even_through_a_sleep`
(six hours asleep, then a minute; five hours asleep and the window activated: nothing; a wake seen by the
window's activation waits a minute; activated when due: at once, once; checks off for a day: nothing, on again:
the next look) [no wake deferral; the 6-hour timer instead of the minute's look; no activation observer; the
check not stamped]; `the_timed_checks_run_three_seconds_after_the_window_opens_then_every_six_hours` (now on
the wall clock) [the check not stamped];
`a_timed_check_that_finds_a_newer_version_opens_the_dialog_once_per_version_per_run` (renamed: 0.3.0 found
after 0.2.0 is offered, then neither again, nor a 0.2.0 after it) [once per run whatever the version];
`a_newer_version_replaces_one_downloaded_but_not_installed` [`Ready` kept; its download not removed];
`a_newer_version_found_during_the_quit_question_installs_nothing` (PostgreSQL, a transaction open) [the install
not checking its download is still the one].

**Not done**: a real sleep is not reproduced (the wall clock is injected; on macOS and Linux `Instant`, the
executor's clock, stops in a sleep, so the wake deferral applies there); no pass in the real app; no new screen,
no capture.

### K56 — Seven small defects of dialogs and tabs (S)

**Now.** The passes in the real app of 2026-11-23 found seven small defects, each fixed in a commit of its own.

1. **The connection dialog's buttons in a short window.** Below about 750 px, a test's result pushed Test, Save,
   Save and connect and Cancel under the window's edge. `connections/dialog.rs`: the view is at most the window's
   height less what the kit draws around it; the engines, the tabs and their fields scroll in it (a tab's body
   keeps its 392 px when there is room, so the buttons do not move from tab to tab); the result sits in a box of
   its own, as tall as it is up to 120 px, right above the buttons. The other tall dialogs were checked the same
   way at 1200 × 560 and pass without a change: the folder dialog, the connections' export and import (a dozen
   connections), the CSV import (thirty columns, options and mapping), the simple backup and restore and their
   wizards. Tests (`ui_tests/k56.rs`):
   `the_connection_dialog_keeps_its_buttons_in_a_short_window` (no server: a refused port; every tab, at 560 and
   700 px; the last field reached by scrolling) [the view's maximum height removed];
   `the_other_tall_dialogs_keep_their_buttons_in_a_short_window`. Captures, light and dark, at 1200 × 560.
2. **A path typed in the restore's File field.** It was read only on Enter or when the field lost the focus, and
   nothing said so: Restore… and Show command stayed disabled. `backup_dialog.rs`: the path is taken 400 ms after
   the typing pauses (`take_typed`, a timer replaced at each key; Enter and leaving the field still take it at
   once), `~/` is the home folder (`connections/form.rs`'s `expand_home`, now shared), the field is never
   rewritten under the caret, an emptied field forgets the file; `examine` says "No such file: <path>. Check the
   path, or choose the file." off the UI thread for a path that names nothing (the script run's field too). The
   backup's folder and name fields were already read as typed; the CSV and connections imports have no path
   field. Test: `a_restore_takes_a_typed_path_without_enter` (a stand-in client) [the pause not waited for: the
   path never taken; the existence check removed: the system's `os error 2` instead of the words].

### K55 — Four small defects of the window's chrome (S)

**Now.** Found by the passes in the real app (2026-11-23): the command palette wants the words in order ("theme
light" finds nothing, "sql new" not New Query Tab), and once an Escape only cleared it; with many tabs open the
strip cuts its leftmost tab (a bare "×", "uery 2"); a query tab's bar shifts when Cancel appears and goes; the
running mark's breath (K37) is too small to be seen.

1. **The palette's words in any order.** Done. `workspace.rs`: `palette_folded` (lower case, the accents of Latin
   letters aside, one space between words) and `palette_rank`: every word typed is in the command's name, its
   section or its keywords; the name itself first, then a name that starts with what is typed, then the words in
   the order typed in the name (or the text in a keyword), as before, then the words apart, more of them in the
   name and then a shorter name first ("sql new" gives New Query Tab). The kit no longer filters the list again
   (`filterable(false)`: its filter wanted the text in a row, and hid what Kerno ranked). Escape closes the
   palette at once, with text typed or not (caught before the kit's, which cleared the text first: the pass's
   Escape that "only cleared it"). "Recently used" (K45) and Settings ▸ Shortcuts' search ("cmd k") unchanged.
   Tests (`ui_tests/k55.rs`, new), each seen failing under the mutation in brackets:
   `the_palette_finds_the_words_typed_in_any_order` [the words apart not ranked],
   `the_palette_runs_a_command_typed_in_another_order` [the kit's filter back],
   `escape_always_closes_the_palette` [the capture removed]; `command_palette_finds_and_runs_commands` presses
   Escape once.
2. **The tab strip never starts inside a tab.** Done. Two causes: the strip scrolled only as far as the active tab
   needed, and the kit's reveal of a clicked tab leaves the end of the tab before it showing (its "peek": a bare
   "×"). `tabs.rs`: `settled_offset` (the active tab whole, moving as little as possible, then `whole_start`: a
   tab cut at the strip's start shown whole or scrolled out of view, whichever moves less, the active tab kept
   whole). `workspace.rs`: a strip scrolled to a tab (Kerno's reveal, a click on a tab) is marked, and once it has
   stopped moving for a frame (the kit's reveal is animated) a canvas after it, reading its layout of the frame,
   sets the settled offset; along the strip for one row, down for the rows and the side list (whose scroll across,
   left by the row before, is undone). The room after the last tab of one row is as wide as its widest tab, so
   that the tab before can always be scrolled out. A scroll by hand (wheel, trackpad) is left where the user puts
   it. The overflow list (J10) is unchanged. Tests, each seen failing under the mutation in brackets:
   `tabs::tests::a_strip_never_starts_inside_a_tab` [a cut tab never scrolled out];
   `ui_tests::k55::the_tab_strip_never_starts_inside_a_tab` (forty tabs, in the three layouts: restored, six
   back and one on by key, the first tab in view clicked, a new tab, a closed one) [the strip never settled; the
   active tab not brought into view first, which left the last tab under the rows after the layout changed].
3. **A table tab whose connection was closed.** After a disconnect the tab said "Not connected / Connect" in place
   of its rows while its footer still offered Fetch 100 more and Fetch all and its toolbar Duplicate, Delete, Set
   NULL and Set default; after a restart the same state hid them. `data/tab.rs`: the rows read before stay on
   screen under a bar that says the connection is closed, with Connect (`data-offline`); a tab that never read any
   shows the notice as before. Every action that needs the server is greyed with "(not connected)" in its tooltip:
   Refresh, Add row, Duplicate, Delete, Set NULL, Set default, Export…, Review SQL, Apply, Filter, Clear, Fetch
   more, Fetch all; Count exactly and auto-refresh are not shown. The keyboard and the menus are refused the same
   way, with the reason: `edits` (every edit, typing in a cell included) and `free_to_move` (sort, filter, refresh,
   reset of the layout) say "“X” is not connected: connect it to read or change the rows of t.", as do Export and
   Review. Discard stays (it is local); pending changes are kept. Connect reads the rows again with the selection
   and the scroll kept. Tests: `a_table_tab_whose_connection_closed_offers_nothing_that_needs_it` (fixture
   `shop.customers`, read only) [the toolbar's greying removed; the guard in `edits` removed; the rows replaced by
   the notice], `a_table_tab_restored_closed_offers_connect_only` [the toolbar's greying removed]. Captures, light
   and dark, at 1200 × 800, connected and after the disconnect.

**Corrected by the pass in the real app (2026-11-24, K61).** Part 2 still left a bare "×" at the strip's left
after a click on the first tab in view, when the strip started exactly on that tab. The kit's reveal of a click
starts its animation on the next frame without moving (no time has passed for it) and moves from the frame
after; that still first frame counted as "stopped", the strip already started on a tab, nothing was settled,
and the reveal then scrolled the end of the tab before into view. The test did not see it: its frames were
spaced by sleeps, but the kit's animation reads the executor's clock, which a test only moves by hand, so the
reveal never moved there. Now `settle_strip` forgets the strip's last offset, so the frame after a reveal or a
click never counts as stopped; `strip_settles` (`ui_tests/k55.rs`) advances the test clock by 16 ms a frame,
and the test failed at "one row, tab 32 clicked" without the fix. Proved in the release build too (K61).

### K59 — The result's filter proposes its columns; foreign keys marked in more results (M)

**Now.** K40 made a table tab's WHERE field the query editor's completion over the table's columns; the field
"Filter the result on the server" under a query's result stayed a plain input, and Show Completions (Ctrl+Space,
⌘I) worked in the query editor only. K49 marked a foreign key in a table's data and in a result that is one
table's and editable only.

**Wanted** (the owner's request of 2026-11-23, D-125): (1) the result's filter proposes the result's columns
with their types, then the words of a condition, exactly as the table's field, with its keys, quoted for the
engine; Show Completions opens the list in both fields; one completion path. (2) Foreign keys marked and
followed in every result whose columns the engine says come from a table (a join, a subset, an alias), on a
read-only connection and in a result that cannot be edited too; a key of several columns followed only with all
its columns there, else marked with the missing one named; a column in two keys offers both; a binary key
followed with the engine's binary literal; the structures read once per result and per table, off the UI thread.

Done (part 1): D-125. `query/completion.rs`: `condition_completion` takes `Option<&TableRef>` (`None`: a result,
completed as the end of `SELECT * FROM kerno_q WHERE` over a catalog of its columns, each name once);
`in_a_condition` keeps columns, functions and keywords in a condition field; `condition_input` draws both fields
(24 px, Tab as J1, Enter routed to the open list and stopped). `query/tab.rs`: `result_filter` an `EditorState`
from `condition_field`, its proposals set from the result's columns when it starts showing a result. `data/tab.rs`:
the field through `condition_input`. `kerno-core` `browse.rs`: `QUERY_ALIAS` public. `actions.rs`: Show
Completions under `Input && mode == editor`.

Tests (`ui_tests/k59.rs`, new; PostgreSQL and MySQL), each seen failing under the mutation in brackets:
`the_result_filter_proposes_the_results_columns` (a result with an alias, a name that needs quotes and an
expression: the columns with their types, Escape, Tab, Enter, the words after a column, `ILIKE` on PostgreSQL,
no `kerno_q`, Enter applying with the list closed, Ctrl+Space; Enter that takes a proposal runs nothing) [the
proposals not set; Enter not routed to the list; `in_a_condition` off]; `show_completions_opens_the_list_of_a_tables_where_field`
(`shop.customers`, MySQL's `customers`: Ctrl+Space on an empty field and on a word, Enter taking a proposal
without applying it) [the old key context; Enter not routed to the list].

Done (part 2): D-125. Core `links.rs` (new): `KeyLink` (a key and the result's column showing each of its
columns; `missing`, `holds`), `key_links` (the keys of the tables a result's columns come from, table by table in
the order the result shows them, each in its table's order), `named_origins` and `named` (the table column each
column shows; PostgreSQL's numbers named by one query), `Catalog::foreign_keys`. `dialect.rs`:
`origin_names_sql` (PostgreSQL's in `kerno-postgres`). `kerno-sqlite` `session.rs`: each column's origin from
the statement's column metadata (rusqlite's `column_metadata` feature). App `query/grid.rs`: `keys` (from the
structure of one table's rows, else `found_keys` set by the owner through `set_found_keys`, which widens a
column that gains a mark after the first layout and keeps the others' widths), `find_links` (the header's tooltip, a missing
column said), `references`, `jumps` (every key of a cell), `jump` the first, `arrow_menu`; `arrow_element` takes
the jumps (one: as K49; several: a button and its dropdown menu, the arrow kept while it is open); `cell`,
`session`, `structure` crate-visible. `grid_view.rs`: the record view's arrow from `jumps`. `data/tab.rs`:
`related(grid, row)` (the grid's references, then `referenced_by` for one table's rows), `reference(link, …)`
(by the result's columns, a binary value through `quote_bytes`, `shortened` in the label), `follow_at` gone.
`value_panel/fk_preview.rs`: the jump from the grid's references; a binary key not previewed. `query/tab.rs`:
`origin_columns` (the first run's origins, shared with `edit_check`), `side_session` (shared with
`look_up_table`), `name_origins` (from `open_grid`, whatever the verdict, never for a plan; only the tables not
named yet), `origin_names` and `origin_epoch` (the names kept, cleared by `load_catalog`), `mark_keys` (from the
tab's catalog; again for every pane when the catalog arrives), `ResultPane::origins`; core `editable.rs`:
`ReadOnly::NotOnThisEngine` (a SQLite result's reason); the result's `Owner::related`
and `follow` from `related(grid, row)`.

Tests, each seen failing under the mutation in brackets. UI (`ui_tests/k59.rs`): `a_join_and_an_alias_are_marked_and_followed`
(PostgreSQL's and MySQL's `orders` joined to `customers`: the mark on `customer_id` only, the join not editable,
the arrow's tooltip and click opening the customer, the column widened for its mark; `customer_id as who` marked,
an expression not; read again filtered, marked without naming its tables again) [`name_origins` not called; the
columns not drawn again when the marks come; the names not kept]; `a_read_only_connection_follows_too` (PostgreSQL, `shop.orders` on a read-only connection) [no
names on a read-only connection]; `two_keys_a_missing_column_and_a_binary_key` (PostgreSQL and MySQL, scratch
`kerno_test_k59`: a column in two keys, both in the tooltip and the arrow's menu, the arrow kept while the menu is
open, the second entry opening its table; `(pa, pb)` with `pb` missing named and without arrow; a `bytea` /
`BINARY(16)` key followed with its literal, shortened) [the arrow following the first key only; the missing
column not said; a binary key not followed; the arrow not kept while its menu is open];
`a_sqlite_join_is_marked_and_followed` (a file the test makes; a result of one table says it is edited from the
table's tab) [SQLite's origins dropped; the old reason];
`three_hundred_columns_over_twenty_tables_are_marked` (PostgreSQL, scratch `kerno_test_k59_wide`: 19 keys)
[`name_origins` not called]. `k49::a_result_of_one_table_and_sqlite_follow_their_foreign_keys` now asserts the
SQLite result's mark. Core `links::tests` (4) [the missing column not said; each table's keys not kept in its
order]. Drivers: `edits::a_join_names_the_table_column_of_each_column` (PostgreSQL; MySQL and MariaDB) [on
PostgreSQL, the names' query not asked], `edits::three_hundred_columns_over_twenty_tables_are_named_in_one_query`
(PostgreSQL, timed), `sqlite::a_join_names_the_table_column_of_each_column` [SQLite's origins dropped].

Measured (BENCH.md): 300 columns over 20 tables, naming them 1.57 ms and with the keys 1.68 ms (debug build); in
the app under a UI test, the columns' arrival to the marks 39 to 74 ms (debug, the test's executor).

**Not done.** ⌘-click and ⌘↓ on a column in two keys follow the first. The value panel's preview leaves a binary
key out. A key made elsewhere after the tab's catalog was read is not marked until the catalog is read again. A
table read twice (a self-join) pairs a key's columns from their first showing. The frame times of K49 were not
measured again.

**Captures** (offscreen, temporary test, not committed), light and dark, 1280 × 720: a join of `shop.orders` and
`shop.customers` with the arrow of a hovered `customer_id` and the mark's tooltip; a column in two keys, its
arrow's menu open and its mark's tooltip.

### K58 — The DBeaver import takes jump servers, and says what it leaves out (S)

**Now.** K41 imported an SSH tunnel's host, port, user and authentication and listed "SSH jump server" as not
imported, though Kerno has jump hosts (J2). Its report listed what was left "at Kerno's defaults" in short
words ("SSL mode", "certificates pasted in DBeaver (not files)"), after the import only, all on one line.

Done: D-124. Core `dbeaver.rs`: `jump_servers` reads DBeaver's `jumpServer<i>.` properties as
`SSHUtils.loadHostConfigurations` does (count, `enabled`, host, port, `name`, `authType`, key path, agent
socket; doubles read); `apply_jumps` writes them as the connection dialog saves a jump host
(`[user@]host:port`, a chain in order, OpenSSH), says what a hop logs in with that Kerno cannot give it, and
leaves them out, said, behind a password tunnel or when OpenSSH could not take them; every other note reworded
as what was left out, then what to do (the made-up `jumpServerSettings` key gone). App `dbeaver_import.rs`:
`DbeaverImport::to_check` (the connection's notes and its DBeaver type not created, each once), shown under each
connection of the preview ("To check: …", `dbeaver-note`) and listed connection by connection in the report.

Tests, each seen failing under the mutation in brackets: core `dbeaver::tests::`
`a_tunnels_jump_servers_become_openssh_jump_hosts` (fixtures with the keys `saveHostConfiguration` writes: a
key-file hop, a two-hop agent chain with IPv6 and a third hop off, a password tunnel, a hop OpenSSH refuses, a
hop with no host, a password hop and a key kept in DBeaver) [the hops reversed; no switch to OpenSSH; no
left-out behind a password tunnel; the hop's port dropped; a hop's saved password taken as the tunnel's; the
hops' notes dropped];
`a_port_written_as_a_double_is_read` (the hop's port and the count as doubles) [no switch to OpenSSH; the
hop's port dropped]; the
three tests whose notes were reworded. UI `ui_tests/dbeaver_import.rs`:
`the_preview_and_the_report_say_what_to_check_for_each_connection` (a hop with a key file, two pasted
certificates said once, a type not created) [the type's note dropped; no line under the row; the report on
one line].

**Not done** (D-124): a hop's saved password or passphrase is not imported (Kerno has no place for one, and its
OpenSSH tunnel would not use it); a hop's own key file and agent socket are not given to it (said, with
`ssh-add`); a connection imported before is skipped as before, without its jump host; no real DBeaver export
was read (fixtures written from DBeaver's source); no pass in the real app.

**Captures** (offscreen, temporary test, not committed), light and dark, 1200 × 800: the preview with a jump
server's key file and a pasted certificate under one connection, a jump server left out behind a password
tunnel under another; the report after the import, connection by connection.
3. **Nothing in a query tab's bar moves when Cancel comes and goes.** Done. `query/tab.rs` (the bar only): Cancel
   (Stop while rows are on their way) now sits at the left of the bar's right-hand buttons, before history and
   saved queries, 72 px wide whatever its word; while nothing runs, an empty room of the same width
   (`tab-cancel-room`) keeps its place, so the bar wraps the same in a narrow group too. Test
   `ui_tests::k55::nothing_in_the_query_bar_moves_when_a_run_starts_or_ends` (PostgreSQL, `pg_sleep`): history,
   saved queries, Explain, Run all, Run and its menu at the same place before, during and after a run, Cancel in
   the room [Cancel back between the icons and Explain, no room].
4. **The toolbar's "New SQL editor" and the connection selected in the tree.** It opened on the active tab's
   connection whatever the tree had selected; DBeaver opens on the selected connection. `workspace/toolbar.rs`:
   `editor_connection` is the tree's selected connection (its row, not the one opened last) when the keyboard was
   last in the tree, otherwise the active tab's (or, without one, the tree's); connecting first stays (K28).
   Which one had the keyboard last is noted at each frame (`note_editor_place`, from `keep_focus`), since a
   toolbar button takes the focus when pressed: only the tree and the tabs change it. The kit keeps the tree's
   focus handle to itself: the navigator notes what has the keyboard when a click gives it to the tree
   (`navigator/view.rs`, `focus_tree`, `is_tree_focus`, `selected_connection`). The button's tooltip names the
   connection ("Open a SQL editor on “name”"). Test: `the_toolbar_opens_an_editor_on_the_connection_selected_in_the_tree`
   (two connections to the test server; the second opened from the tree, connected first; the first selected
   then the keyboard back in the tab: the tab's) [the tree's selection ignored; the keyboard in a tab never
   noted].
4. **The running mark seen to breathe.** Done: D-127. `query/run_marks.rs`: `halo` (opacity and width at a point of
   the breath), a breath of 1.5 s (six of the tab's redraws, one every 250 ms), from no halo to 50 % of the info
   colour and from 1.2 to 1.6 times the sign's width; held at its fullest when the system asks for less motion;
   the marks centred on the strip and the kit's gap after it, so that the widest halo is whole (16 px).
   Test `theme::tests::the_running_marks_halo_is_seen_to_breathe`: half a breath apart, 2:1 at least between the
   fullest and the faintest halo on the editor and its active line, both themes (2.06:1 to 2.72:1) [K37's 10 % to
   35 %: 1.42:1 to 1.67:1].

**Captures** (offscreen, temporary test, not committed), light and dark, 1200 × 800, in the session's scratchpad
`k55/shots/`: twelve query tabs, the strip starting on "Query 5" whole, then that tab clicked (no part of "Query 4");
the bar before and during a run, Cancel left of the history button, nothing else moved; the running mark at the

**Not done.** A strip scrolled by hand (wheel, trackpad) may stop inside a tab: snapping it would fight the

5. **Where a tested password came from.** A password typed in the connection dialog and tested was said to come
   from Kerno's file (or the keychain): the test reported where saved passwords are, whatever was in the field.
   `connections/dialog.rs` keeps the password read from the store when the dialog opened on a saved connection
   (`stored_password`), forgotten at the first key in the field; any other password in the field (typed, pasted,
   filled from a URL) was typed here, and `Connections::test` takes it (`typed`): "Password typed here"; the
   others are unchanged (Kerno's file or the keychain for an untouched saved one, the command, `~/.pgpass`,
   `~/.my.cnf`, "Password asked"). Test: `a_tested_password_says_where_it_really_came_from` (Kerno's file in a
   temporary folder, never the keychain: a new connection typed, saved, edited untouched, typed over) [the
   field's value not compared; the first key not forgetting the stored password].
6. **Save panels in the folder last used.** Each "Choose…" of an export opened the native save panel in the home
   folder. `save_panel.rs` (new): `ask(kind, folder, name)` opens the panel in the folder given (the file in use's
   own, for a saved script or a schema document already chosen), else in the folder that kind of save last used,
   else in the home folder, the first that is a folder there now (checked off the UI thread), and remembers the
   folder of the path chosen; `settings.rs` `save_folders` (by kind; the backup's stays `backup_folder`, one memory
   for it). Kinds: rows or a result exported, a backup, a schema document, an ER diagram, the connections' export,
   a query tab's "Save as…", a new SQLite file. Test: `each_save_panel_opens_where_its_kind_last_saved` (the
   connections' export: home, the folder chosen, home once it is gone; "Save as…" keeping to its own) [the folder
   not remembered; the remembered folder not checked].
7. **A short instance socket for a long data directory** (D-128). A data directory whose `instance.sock` path is longer
   than a socket takes (104 bytes on macOS, its end byte included) logged "path must be shorter than SUN_LEN" at
   each start, and `kerno <url>` no longer reached the running instance. `kerno-core` `instance.rs`:
   `socket_path` keeps the data directory's socket whenever it fits; otherwise the socket is `<FNV-1a of the
   data directory, 16 hex digits>.sock` (the same in every build) in a folder `kerno-<uid of the data
   directory's owner>` of `XDG_RUNTIME_DIR` when set, else of the temporary directory (per user on macOS), made
   at 0700 and refused when it is a link, another user's or open to others (no URL is then sent anywhere: the
   window opens as without a socket, and says why in the log); the socket stays 0600. `main.rs` (the hand-off
   and the listener) and `start_failure.rs` find it the same way. No warning for a case that works. Test (core):
   `a_long_data_directory_gets_a_short_socket_of_its_own` (the bind refused at the long path; a short path kept;
   a long one short, stable, one per data directory, 0700 and 0600; a URL handed over; a folder opened to others
   refused) [the data directory's socket kept whatever its length; the folder's permission check removed].

rows (the kit keeps the tree's focus handle to itself): the tree reached by the keyboard alone (Tab) is not
noted, the last place clicked counts.

### K60 — Data compare (L)

**Now.** Two tables that should hold the same rows (a staging copy, a restored backup, a table moved to another
server) could only be compared by hand: two queries, two exports, a diff tool. Item 1 of `docs/ROADMAP.md`
("Beyond v1", TOOL-22); schema compare (K30) left the rows out.

**Wanted** (the owner's request of 2026-11-23): "Compare data with…" from a table (the tree, the data tab's menu,
the palette); a dialog as the schema compare's that picks the other table (same engine family; the same
connection, place and table name by default; a dropdown of the tables there), the key (a primary or unique key,
picked when there are several) and the columns compared (those both tables have, ticked; the others said);
refused without a key; values compared as the engine types them; one ordered `SELECT` per side merged as two
streams in bounded memory, an order both servers agree on whatever the collation, a row counter per side, the
elapsed time and Cancel; a tab with the counts and the rows that differ, filters by kind and by column, the
row's values, copy; "Open script": `INSERT`, `UPDATE` and, ticked, `DELETE` from the pending-edit SQL, in an
order that respects the table's own foreign keys, opened in a query tab on the target, never run; the limits
said; no trait change, no dependency; a run of 1,000,000 rows against 1,000,000 measured.

Done: D-126. Core `data_compare.rs` (new): `refusal` (the family of `compare::Family`, its own words), `Mode`
(`of`, `common`: JSON against text is JSON; `order`, the key order of `sort::compare_cells`; `same`, values as
the engine types them), `keys` (the primary key, then unique NOT NULL indexes without a predicate), `Shape::of`
(keys usable on both sides, shared columns with why one is refused or not written, columns of one side),
`Request`, `Plan::new` (the checked request: key and compared columns as `Col`, the target's foreign keys to
itself), `settings` (read-only, UTC, ISO dates, floats), `select_sql` (`ORDER BY` the key: by value, or `CAST(k
AS text) COLLATE "C"` / `CAST(CONVERT(k USING utf8mb4) AS BINARY)`, NULL last on MySQL too), `Merge` (pure, fed
a batch at a time: `wants`, `feed`, `finish`; each side's keys checked to rise strictly; differences copied
into two `BatchBuilder` arenas, capped by `Limits`, 10,000 rows or 64 MiB), `run` (both sessions told their
settings, both `SELECT`s at once, the merge as they arrive, `progress` after each batch, `cancel`), `Outcome`,
`Counts`, `Row`, `Difference`, `script` (`edit_statements` in the target's dialect; inserts parents first and
deletes children first through `by_references`; `OVERRIDING SYSTEM VALUE` for a key the server always makes;
the partial, time zone and sequence notes, every comment through `compare::commented`). `lib.rs`: `pub mod
data_compare`. App: `data_compare_dialog.rs` (new: the two pickers of the schema compare's dialog with a table
field and Tables ▾, each side's table list and structure read on a session of its own 250 ms after its fields
stop changing, the key (▾ when several), a checkbox per shared column, the notes, the problem in words,
Compare); `data_compare_tab.rs` (new: `Spec`, `DataCompareTab` with its header, filters with counts, Same,
"Any column ▾", "Also delete what only the target has", progress with each side's rows, the time and Cancel,
the partial and read-only notes, `DiffGrid` on the kit's table, the values panel, copy, the snapshot line;
`DataCompareTabEvent::OpenScript`); `workspace.rs` (`OpenTab::DataCompare` in each of its matches,
`open_data_compare`, `compare_data`, `data_tab_place`, the data tab's menu entry and its `perform` arm, the
action, the tree's event, `data_compare_tabs` for tests); `tabs.rs` (`TabAction::CompareData`);
`navigator/actions.rs` (`Action::CompareData` "Compare data with…" in a table's menu); `navigator/view.rs`
(`NavigatorEvent::CompareData`, its arm, SQLite's reason); `actions.rs`, `menu_palette.rs`, `main.rs` (the
command `compare_data`, Server ▸ Tools ▸ Compare Data…). Element ids: `data-compare-connection` (source `0..`,
target `1000..`), `data-compare-database`, `data-compare-schema`, `data-compare-table`, `data-compare-tables`,
`data-compare-dialog-swap`, `data-compare-key`, `data-compare-columns`, `("data-compare-column", i)`,
`data-compare-notes`, `data-compare-problem`, `data-compare-dialog-cancel`, `data-compare-dialog-start`;
`data-compare-source`, `data-compare-target`, `data-compare-swap`, `data-compare-refresh`,
`data-compare-open-script`, `("data-compare-filter", 0..4)`, `data-compare-same`, `data-compare-column`,
`data-compare-deletes`, `("data-compare-progress", 0..2)`, `data-compare-elapsed`, `data-compare-cancel`,
`data-compare-partial`, `data-compare-read-only`, `data-compare-grid`, `("data-compare-cell", row << 12 |
column)`, `data-compare-no-match`, `data-compare-values`, `data-compare-values-empty`, `data-compare-waiting`,
`data-compare-loading`, `data-compare-error`, `data-compare-cancelled`, `data-compare-identical`.

`ui_tests/data_compare.rs` (new): `the_dialog_picks_two_tables_and_says_what_cannot_be_compared` (the palette

**Corrected after the review of 2026-11-24, group A** (the script could change rows it was not written for).
The first version offered the source's keys too, and matched keys by their bytes only: a key only the source
held unique let a `_ai_ci` target holding `Bob@x` and `bob@x` take an `UPDATE` that changed both (F1), and keys

without a key is refused; after the merge `reconcile` asks each kept row of one side of the other side by that
side's own equality (`twins_sql`: `SELECT n, key FROM t WHERE key = literal`, a hundred joined by `UNION ALL`),
`pair` puts a row only in the source together with its kept twin only in the target (`Row::respelled`: its
`UPDATE` writes the source's spelling when the key is text of one type on both sides; the same row otherwise),
and a row whose twin was not kept, or a row only in the target the source holds, is `Row::held`: a comment, no
statement; a temporal key is compared without the zeros of its fraction. The tab shows "key spelled otherwise"
and the key as `source → target`; its partial line says rows beyond the cap were not checked this way. Tests,
each seen failing under the mutation in brackets: core
`the_keys_columns_and_refusals_of_two_tables` [a plan on a key the target does not hold unique; the source's
keys offered], `a_key_spelled_otherwise_is_one_row_never_an_insert_and_a_delete` (`a`/`A`, `b`/`b `, a twin not
kept, a uuid against text) [the key's spelling never written; held rows inserted],
`times_of_two_precisions_are_one_key` [temporal keys compared with their zeros]; driver
`keys_the_target_holds_equal_are_one_row` in `kerno-mysql/tests/data_compare.rs` (MySQL and MariaDB, scratch
databases `kerno_test_dc_ci_a` / `_b`: a `_ai_ci` target with two rows equal for its server and no unique
index, the key not offered and refused; a `_ci` key `a` against `A`; a PAD SPACE key `a` against `a `; a
`DATETIME(3)` key against a `DATETIME(6)` one; each: the script run with deletes, every statement changing one
row at most, compared again, nothing left, the target's row count as expected) [a plan on a key the target
does not hold unique; the source's keys offered; no reconcile; the key's spelling never written; temporal keys
compared with their zeros is not caught here, as the target's equality then pairs the two times: the core test
catches it], `a_citext_key_spelled_otherwise_is_one_row` in `kerno-postgres/tests/data_compare.rs` (a scratch
database `kerno_test_dc_citext` with the extension, dropped with `FORCE`) [no reconcile; the key's spelling
never written].

**Corrected after the review, group B** (values compared or written wrong). `settings`: `extra_float_digits` 3

### K61 — Two defects the pass in the real app found (S)

**Now.** The pass in the real app of 2026-11-24 (release build, one row of tabs, 15 query tabs, a window 1280 px
wide) saw the strip start with the "×" of the tab before after a click on the first tab in view; and with a table
selected in the schema tree, Edit ▸ Copy was enabled and copied nothing, where ⌘C copies the table's name.

1. **The tab strip after a click on the first tab in view.** Done; the cause and the fix are in K55's section
   ("Corrected by the pass in the real app"). Test: `ui_tests::k55::the_tab_strip_never_starts_inside_a_tab`,
   its frames now 16 ms apart on the test clock [`settle_strip` keeping the last offset: fails at "one row, tab
   32 clicked", tab 31 cut]. Real app: the release build of the commit before and of this one, run on a scratch
   data directory, 15 query tabs at 1280 × 800: before, the first tab in view clicked showed the bare "×" (twice,
   in two positions of the strip); after, the strip starts on the clicked tab in three positions.
2. **Edit ▸ Copy where ⌘C copies on its own.** Done. The menu's Copy dispatches the kit's `input::Copy`; on the
   surfaces whose ⌘C is their own nothing took that action, so it reached the window's root (which copies text
   selected in a label: nothing), and stayed enabled. The surfaces with a ⌘C of their own, from the keymap
   (`copy_object_name`, context `Navigator`) and the `on_key_down` handlers: the schema tree, the structure tab,
   the administration tab, a table's grid and a result's grid (K54 already), the data compare tab (its own
   agent). Each new one takes `input::Copy` and does what its key does: `navigator/view.rs` dispatches
   `CopyObjectName` (connections and folders keep going to the sidebar); `structure/tab.rs` and `admin/tab.rs`
   call `copy_selection`, and hand the action on to the window's copy when no cell of theirs is focused. No
   other surface has a ⌘C, ⌘V or ⌘A of its own (the history and saved-queries panels, the ER diagram, the value
   panel, the schema compare and extensions tabs, the logs: none in the keymap or an `on_key_down`), so Paste
   and Select All are unchanged. Copy cannot be greyed: the root keeps it for selected label text (K54). Test:
   `ui_tests::k54::edit_copy_does_what_the_key_does_on_every_surface` (PostgreSQL, fixtures read only: ⌘C then
   the menu's Copy, the clipboard blanked before each, the same text: `orders`, a column's type, a setting's
   value), seen failing under each mutation [the tree's, the structure tab's, the administration tab's
   handler bound to `input::Cut` instead].

### K62 — Choose which databases a PostgreSQL connection shows in the tree (S-M)

**Source.** A user's report relayed by the owner (2026-11-24): an account allowed into one database of a
PostgreSQL server that holds about twenty. DBeaver lists that one database when "Show all databases" is off;
Kerno listed every database of `pg_database`, templates included, and opening one the account may not enter
showed, in red, "Could not read the schemas: could not connect: permission…". Only the size at the right of a
row (read with the CONNECT privilege only) told the dead rows apart, after a click on each. Decision: D-129.

**Done.**

1. **What the catalog says.** `DatabaseInfo` gets `allows_connections` (`datallowconn`) and `can_connect`
   (`datallowconn AND has_database_privilege(oid, 'CONNECT')`, the expression the size already used), both
   `true` by default (`Default` written by hand) and on MySQL, MariaDB and SQLite, which list only what the
   account sees. The admin view of sizes and the dialect's properties query keep their own copies of the
   expression: they show sizes and facts, nothing changes there.
2. **The tree.** A database the account may not open is drawn dimmed (the muted text of `template0`) and its
   children are `Children::Closed`: opening it shows one muted line at once, "This account may not connect
   here." or, for `datallowconn = false`, "This database takes no connection." (short, so the line is whole at
   the sidebar's default width; the longer sentences of the brief were cut by it in the captures). No session
   is opened for it: `expand` loads nothing, `refresh` of the model refuses it, and the view's Refresh on it
   lists the databases again (a privilege granted since then shows).
3. **Its menu, entry by entry.** Kept: Create database… and Drop… (both run on the connection's own session, as
   today), Copy name, Copy qualified name, Filter ▸ (names only), Refresh. Left out: New query here, Create
   schema…, Compare with…, Properties (they read inside the database), and Tools ▸ (every tool works inside
   it). The keys of the tree go through the same list (`run_entry`); Manage extensions, which the keys reach
   from any node, is refused on a closed database too.
4. **What lists databases to act inside one.** The toolbar's database picker, the status bar's and the query
   tab's own selector all read `QueryTab::databases`, which now keeps the server's list and filters it when
   asked: what the tree shows, without the system ones and the ones the account may not open (so a change of
   the choice shows there at once too). The connection dialog's Database list (`Connections::list_databases`)
   leaves out what the account may not open, and keeps the rest (it is how the default is chosen). The global
   backup neither lists a database the account may not open nor dumps it (pg_dumpall would stop there), and
   says so: under its list of databases, in the warning colour, "Left out, this account may not open it:
   billing" ("them", the first three and "and N more", all of them in the tooltip), and the same sentence,
   whole, as the first line of the run's log, so a saved task's run keeps it. They go to pg_dumpall's
   `--exclude-database` for that run only (pg_dumpall itself skips `datallowconn = false`). A saved task
   stores its exclude list as it is (`GlobalBackupOptions::exclude_databases`), and reopening a task reads the
   databases from the server again (`load_databases` at each opening of the wizard): so the task keeps only
   what the user unticked, and the closed ones, held apart (`BackupDialog::closed`), are read again at each
   run. (Review of 7610e54: that version froze them into the user's list, and said nothing.) The data
   transfer lists databases only as MySQL schemas, which the account sees: unchanged. A table's backup and the
   other tools are reached from the tree, where a closed database has no Tools ▸.
5. **A session refused anyway** (pg_hba, a privilege revoked since the list was read): a failed read whose
   error is a connect error goes through `error::explain` with the node's database; `explain` gains SQLSTATE
   42501 ("You may not connect to database “x”.", ask for the CONNECT privilege or choose another database).
   The row shows that line, the tooltip the next step and the server's words. A statement's 42501 (a schema's
   privilege) stays as it was. 28000 (pg_hba) was already "The server refused the login of user …".
6. **The choice.** `ConnectionConfig::databases_shown` (`all`, `available`, `default-only`), serde default All
   and not written when All, so every stored connection and exported file reads as before.
   `DatabasesShown::shown` is the one filter: All; Available is `can_connect` and no template; DefaultOnly the
   database the connection opens on. The default (the session's database: the Database field, else where the
   server put the session) is always shown, dimmed if closed; a default that is not listed makes "only the
   default" show what "those this account can open" shows: never an empty tree.
7. **Where it is chosen.** The connection dialog, PostgreSQL only, a "Databases shown: …" select on the Database
   field's line (beside List…), so the General tab is no taller (K56's short window). MySQL, MariaDB and SQLite
   have no select and save All. Applied on save without a reconnect: the tree applies it when it is drawn, as it
   applies the object filters, from the saved settings.
8. **The cue.** When the choice hides databases, one muted line after the last database, "12 databases hidden";
   a click on its words, or Enter on the line, opens the connection's dialog. No line when nothing is hidden.
   The object filters' own line ("N databases hidden by a filter") stays and counts only what the choice shows.
   The tree's filter box and Collapse all work with it. No menu or palette entry: Edit… is on the connection.
9. **Imported and exported.** The DBeaver import maps the provider properties of a PostgreSQL connection
   (`@dbeaver-show-non-default-db@`, `@dbeaver-show-unavailable-db@`, `@dbeaver-show-template-db@`, read as
   `PostgreDataSource.prepareReadDatabaseListStatement` does, false when absent): the first off is DefaultOnly
   (DBeaver's default), on is Available, or All with either of the other two on. The connections file carries
   the field with each connection; an old file reads as All.

### K63 — A faster backup and restore for MySQL and MariaDB, and an old server's client said (M)

**Now.** A user's request relayed by the owner (his "B1 B2"): PostgreSQL's backup has "Faster" (K48), MySQL
nothing; the user's MySQL 5.1 database holds one table of several gigabytes. mysqldump reads with one
connection; mysqlpump is gone from 8.4; and "mysqldump 8.4 finds no columns in a 5.1 table" was in
ROADMAP.md, not said by the wizard.

Done: D-135. `kerno-core` `tools/mysql_folder.rs` (new): `ranges` (N pieces of equal key span, open at both
ends), `plan` (pure: one file per table, a table of at least `RANGE_THRESHOLD` with a single-column integer key
cut into ranges, then `routines.sql` and `views.sql`), `Manifest` and `read_manifest` (plain names inside the
folder only), `FolderBackup::{piece_command, after_command, sample_command}` (built on `backup_command`:
per-piece consistency, `--where`, `--skip-add-locks`, `--set-gtid-purged=OFF` from MySQL 5.6, `--no-create-info`,
the user's `--where` refused with ranges), `read_catalog`, `can_lock` (RELOAD), `back_up` (the catalog, a new
folder, `FLUSH TABLES WITH READ LOCK` for "lock all tables", N mysqldump through `tools::run`, the manifest last;
any end unlocks and removes only its folder; "it has no fields" is a failure), `restore` (every file checked
first, a table's first file alone then its ranges side by side on InnoDB, the files after the tables in order,
a failure stops the others and says what is in), `dump_client_problem` (the pairs reproduced). `tools.rs`:
`MySqlBackup::jobs`, `guess_format` takes a folder with the manifest for MySQL and MariaDB, `backup_command`
refuses jobs for one file; nothing in detection or the runner. `backup_dialog.rs`: `ToolState::Ready::refused`
(Start refused, said in the client's place), `can_lock` read with the objects, the wizard's Format ("Plain
SQL" / "Folder, one file per table") and Jobs for MySQL, `folder_backup` / `folder_restore` and the run's two
branches (Kerno's own session for the catalog and the lock), the lock confirmed with its statement (a write:
twice on production), RELOAD lacking refuses a lock chosen, "prefer" without TLS for the tools on 5.1 and 5.5,
the restore's Jobs and the open panel's folders for MySQL, the refusal naming the folder's file. `simple.rs`:
"Faster" for MySQL and MariaDB beside the script, the "Writes" row ("Block writes during the backup, so that
every table is read at the same moment", its plain-words hint either way, disabled and said without RELOAD),
the restore's "Faster" for a folder (one transaction stays PostgreSQL's), the way back not a hidden change.

**The review of ccc0b47** (an independent reviewer, three findings confirmed by a run; fixed on top):

| | What was wrong | The fix | The test [mutation seen failing] |
|---|---|---|---|

| F2 | The read lock's session could be closed by the server (idle timeout, proxy) mid-run, and the backup still said it succeeded | That session gets a long `wait_timeout`, its `CONNECTION_ID()` is checked every 2 s and at the end; a lost session, another id or a failed `UNLOCK TABLES` fails the run ("The lock that kept writes out was lost at 21:04:12 …") and removes the folder | driver `a_lost_read_lock_fails_the_backup` (8.4 and 5.7: Kerno's session killed during the run) [the end check off] |
| F3 | mysqldump 5.7 (the client the old-server message recommends) talks `utf8` and wrote a 4-byte character as `?`, exit 0; the plain backup too | `--default-character-set=utf8mb4` for the dump (plain and folder) and the restore's client when the server has utf8mb4 (5.5.3 on) and the client knows it (MySQL 5.5.3, MariaDB 5.5): checked accepted by 5.7.44, 26.10, 8.4.13 and MariaDB 11.8.9 | core `the_tools_talk_utf8mb4_where_the_server_and_the_client_have_it`; driver round trip with a 4-byte character and every byte value, compared with `HEX()`, on every server [`utf8mb4()` false: 5.5 to 5.7 restored `?`] |
| F4 | Each range of a cut table was its own snapshot: a row whose key changed between two ranges' starts could be lost or doubled | The ranges of a table start together while Kerno's session holds `LOCK TABLES t READ`, released once every range's snapshot is taken (mysqldump's "Retrieving table structure" line, which follows its transaction on every client tried), 30 s at most; a table whose lock does not come within 10 s, or is refused, is dumped whole by one process and the log says so; only InnoDB tables are cut (amended by G1 below) | driver `every_range_of_a_table_reads_one_moment` (8.4, MariaDB, 5.1: the second range starts a second late, a row's key moves from the first range to the second in between: restored once) [the table's lock removed: the row doubled]; core plan test (a large MyISAM table not cut) |
| F5 | A pool task that panicked was neither done nor failed | A task's join error is a failure of its file, with its label | core `a_pool_task_that_panics_fails_its_file` [the join error skipped] |
| F6 | The plain backup ignored mysqldump's "it has no fields" (exit 0, no table) | The same check in the plain backup: such a run fails and removes its file | UI `a_plain_backup_that_read_no_column_fails_and_removes_its_file` [the check off] |
| F7 | `-w…` or `--wher=` in the extra arguments came after a range's `--where` and won | The folder backup refuses, in every spelling mysqldump takes (any prefix, `skip-`/`loose-` forms, short letters), the options it sets per file: `where`, `tab`, `tables`, `no-data`, `no-create-info`, `single-transaction`, `lock-all-tables`, `lock-tables`, `add-locks`, `databases`, `all-databases`, `triggers`, `routines`, `events`, `xml`, `opt`, `set-gtid-purged`; `-w -T -d -t -x -l -B -A -X -E -R`. Found: the general list already refuses `--result-file`/`-r` and their prefixes; `-T`/`--tab`, `-w`, `-d`, `-t`, `-x`, `-l`, `-B`, `-A` were not refused | core `the_folder_backup_refuses_the_options_it_sets_itself_in_every_spelling` [the check off] |
| F8 | The catalog and the key bounds were read before the global read lock: a table made in between was missed | The lock first, then the catalog, as `--lock-all-tables` | driver `the_read_lock_holds_writes_until_the_end` checks in the log that the lock comes before the tables are listed (no mutation: the race itself, a table made between the two, is not reproduced) |
| F9 | A read lock waiting behind a long statement stalled every write, also after Cancel | The lock session's `lock_wait_timeout` is 10 s (5.5 on; Kerno's own timer on 5.1): a lock that does not come stops the start, in plain words, before any folder; the confirmation names the statements running for 5 s or more ("A query has been running for 4 min 12 s (session 1234): …") and its words say what Cancel does and does not end | driver `a_read_lock_that_does_not_come_stops_the_start` (8.4, a 16 s read) [the wait raised to 30 s]; unit `the_read_lock_s_confirmation_names_the_long_statements` |
| — | Nothing proved the lock was held | | driver `the_read_lock_holds_writes_until_the_end` (8.4 and 5.1: a write during the run lands after `UNLOCK TABLES`) [the FLUSH removed] |
| — | A restore into a database that is not empty | Said before the run: the backup's tables it already holds are dropped and created again, its other tables stay (or, without DROP TABLE statements, the restore stops at the first) | UI `a_faster_mysql_backup_is_a_folder_the_restore_loads_on_several_clients` (target with `one` and `extra`) [the sentence off] |
| — | The size threshold's real path | | driver `the_sizes_are_read_fresh_and_only_a_table_above_the_threshold_is_cut` (8.4: sizes cached while empty, 170,000 and 110,000 rows loaded: the first cut, the second not) [the stats expiry left at the default] |

Release notes material: F3 also fixes the plain MySQL backup with a client that defaults to `utf8` (mysqldump 5.7 and
older): 4-byte characters (emoji, some CJK) were written as `?`.

**The second review** (of 7424377, 1bbd132 and 53e35c0; two findings confirmed by a run; fixed on top; G1's rule
is the coordinator's). "53e35c0's core" in brackets: the new driver test run against `tools.rs` and
`mysql_folder.rs` as they were at 53e35c0.

| | What was wrong | The fix | The test [mutation seen failing] |
|---|---|---|---|
| G1 | On MySQL 5.1 (no metadata locks) `LOCK TABLES t READ` let an open transaction that had already written to `t` COMMIT between two ranges' snapshots: a row doubled, backup and restore Ok | A table is cut only where its ranges read one moment (D-135's table): from MySQL 5.5.3 (and every MariaDB) an InnoDB table under Kerno's table lock; on any version and any engine (MyISAM, MEMORY too) under "Block writes", where the COMMIT waits until the end; never under "No locks" (G7). The wizard's hint says that "Block writes" is what cuts a large table on 5.1 | core `a_table_is_cut_only_where_its_ranges_read_one_moment` [the version check removed]; driver `every_range_of_a_table_reads_one_moment` (8.4 and MariaDB autocommit, cut; 8.4 open transaction, read whole after the lock's 10 s; 5.1 open transaction and autocommit, not cut; 5.1 and 8.4 open transaction with "Block writes", cut, the COMMIT returned after the release) [53e35c0's core: on 5.1 with the open writer, 1001 rows, the row twice]; `under_the_read_lock_a_myisam_table_is_cut_too` (8.4, 5.1: the MyISAM table in four ranges, rows, checksums and bytes equal) [53e35c0's core: not cut] |
| G2 | `--source-data`, `--master-data`, `--flush-logs`, `--dump-replica`, `--dump-slave` made each range wait 30 s behind Kerno's table lock; `--log-error` and `--skip-verbose` hid the lines Kerno reads (the ranges' snapshot, "it has no fields") | The folder refuses the replication position (it writes none, with or without "Block writes": use the plain backup to seed a replica) and `--flush-logs`; both backups refuse `--log-error` and `--verbose` turned off in any spelling (5.7.44, 26.10 and mariadb-dump 11.8 have no other such option) | core `a_backup_refuses_what_would_hide_mysqldump_s_lines` [`--verbose` off let through], `the_folder_backup_refuses_the_options_it_sets_itself_in_every_spelling`; driver `arguments_that_stall_or_blind_the_backup_are_refused` (the reviewer's two runs on 8.4: refused before any mysqldump, no folder, no log file) [53e35c0's core: `--source-data=2` waited 30 s, "its parts did not all start within 30 s"] |
| G3 | The restore's warning said "dropped and created again" for a backup of the rows only | The words follow the backup's content and DROP TABLE: rows added (a table holding some stops on a duplicate key, or ends with rows twice without a key), created again, created again empty, or a stop at the first; the confirmation says it too | UI `the_restore_says_what_happens_to_the_tables_already_there` (five manifests: data only, all with and without DROP TABLE, definitions only, no table there; the wizard and the confirmation) [the data-only words off; the words left out of the confirmation] |
| G4 | The lock's watcher sent its check on the session the catalog read used, and took 10 s without an answer for a lost lock | The watcher starts once the catalog is read; one check at a time, never dropped half-way; no answer is no evidence, only an error or another id is; the last check waits 60 s and says so if the session does not answer; the statement that does not answer is then cancelled and the lock released, 10 s at most each, so the run always ends (a follow-up after the re-run) | driver `a_slow_session_is_not_a_lost_lock` (a recording session holds the catalog's read 12 s and the watcher's first check 12 s: the backup ends well; no check asked for before the catalog was read) [53e35c0's core: "The lock that kept writes out was lost"]; `a_session_that_never_answers_ends_the_backup` (every check after the first never answers: the failure within 85 s, the stuck check cancelled, UNLOCK sent last, the session closed within 10 s, a write then lands) [the cancel left out] |
| G5 | A cut table waited for an empty pool, and the files behind it waited too | The first item of the queue that can start does: single files fill the processes a cut table waits for, which starts once it has them | core `single_files_start_while_a_table_waits_for_its_processes` [only the queue's first item may start]; BENCH (one cut table among eight, and its key skewed: the same within the noise, as the cut table comes first in both queues) |
| G6 | "it has no fields" failed any plain run (restores, scripts, pg_dump) and the folder restore, on the bare phrase | Only a MySQL / MariaDB backup, on mysqldump's own line | core `only_mysqldump_s_own_line_says_no_fields` [the bare phrase]; UI `only_a_mysql_backup_fails_on_no_fields` (a restore whose client writes the line ends well) [the dialog's check on any run]; the backups' checks still pass (`a_plain_backup_that_read_no_column_fails_and_removes_its_file`, driver `a_client_that_cannot_read_5_1_is_refused_and_its_empty_backup_fails`) |
| G7 | "No locks" still cut, with no moment at all | Nothing is cut; the wizard's Consistency says what each choice does to the folder | core `a_table_is_cut_only_where_its_ranges_read_one_moment` ["No locks" cutting InnoDB tables]; UI `the_consistency_choice_is_said_and_needs_reload` (each choice's sentence in the wizard) [the sentence off] |
| G8 | The long statements' list missed `Execute`, could not see other accounts' statements without PROCESS, and read an error as none | `Execute` too; PROCESS read as RELOAD is; without it the confirmation says Kerno cannot see other accounts' statements; an error is said | unit `the_read_lock_s_confirmation_names_the_long_statements` (the account without PROCESS, a failed read) [the PROCESS sentence off]; driver `a_long_prepared_statement_is_listed` (a `SLEEP` sent with the binary protocol, `Execute` in the process list) [`Query` only] |
| G9 | `--set-gtid-purged=OFF`, `--skip-lock-tables`, `--skip-add-locks`, `--skip-triggers`, `--skip-opt` were refused | `--set-gtid-purged=OFF` and `--skip-add-locks` taken as typed; `--skip-lock-tables` dropped (the folder sets each file's lock: kept, it came last and won, and a MyISAM table was read unlocked); `--skip-triggers` read as Triggers off (plan, files, manifest). `--skip-opt` stays refused, with `--compact`, `--skip-set-charset`, `--skip-add-drop-table`: `mysqldump … --skip-opt --help` (5.7.44) shows it turns off `add-drop-table`, `add-locks`, `create-options`, `disable-keys`, `extended-insert`, `lock-tables`, `quick` and `set-charset`, which the files and the restore's words rely on. Each refusal names the argument and what the folder sets instead | core `the_folder_backup_refuses_the_options_it_sets_itself_in_every_spelling` [`--skip-lock-tables` kept] |
| G10 | A plain backup of 5.5.3+ now starts with `SET NAMES utf8mb4`, refused by 5.1; `--ignore-table` emptied a table's files the manifest still listed; the held test compared with a line said after UNLOCK; F8 checked log lines | The restore wizard reads the file's first 4 KB (a folder: its first file) and says so on a server before 5.5.3; `--ignore-table` and `--ignore-table-data` refused for the folder; the write's instant compared with "releasing the read lock", said before UNLOCK; the order checked on what Kerno's session sent (a recording session) | UI `a_utf8mb4_backup_for_an_old_server_is_said` (a plain file, and a folder's first file) [the file's head not read; the folder's first file not read]; core refusal test; driver `the_read_lock_holds_writes_until_the_end` (FLUSH before `information_schema.TABLES` and `@@lower_case_table_names`, UNLOCK last) [53e35c0's core: no instant before UNLOCK] |

The driver tests that take a lock run under a guard (after the re-run: a test of the stuck check, run with its
fix taken out, held `FLUSH TABLES WITH READ LOCK` on 8.4 for 1 h 41 min, its own `DROP DATABASE` waiting behind
it): each test body has 120 s; a thread with its own runtime and its own connection, opened first, kills every
connection the test opened and every one working in a `kerno_test_k63_*` database when the body ends or its time
is up, whatever the code under test does; the test then fails, and the drop of its databases waits 60 s at most.

Release notes material (G10): a plain MySQL backup of a 5.5.3 or later server, in utf8mb4 since F3, stops at its
first statement when restored into MySQL 5.1 (ERROR 1115); the restore wizard says so before it runs.

**Measured** (BENCH.md): on MySQL 8.4 with 8 processes, `kerno_analytics` (one 238 MB table cut in 8)
backed up in 0.81–0.83 s against 1.79–1.88 s and restored in 6.5–8.4 s against 14.3–17.1 s; eight tables of 30 MB
in 0.98–1.23 s against 1.80–2.85 s, restored in 6.5–7.5 s against 14.8–17.2 s; on 5.1 (one run) 0.68 s against
1.18 s and 3.69 s against 7.20 s. The threshold from a table cut or not at 8 to 238 MB; `--skip-add-locks` 6.7–9.3 s
against 16.3–29.6 s for the ranges' restore.

**Not done / not verified**: a server with GTIDs; MySQL 8.0's and MariaDB's older clients on old servers; an
SSH tunnel; a read-only connection's lock; no pass in the real app; captures offscreen (committed as
`m12-mysql-faster-*`).

### K64 — Windows, step 1: the workspace builds and `kerno-core`'s tests pass there (M)

**Now.** The owner's word of 2026-11-24: releases for macOS, Linux and Windows from CI; "step 1" first. A probe
on a `windows-2025` runner that morning: every dependency compiles for `x86_64-pc-windows-msvc` (GPUI
included), and `kerno-core` stops at 27 errors, all Unix sockets and Unix file modes. This Mac cannot compile
for Windows (ring and SQLite need a Windows C toolchain), so a GitHub runner is the compiler: the probe workflow
`.github/workflows/windows.yml` runs on pushes to the `windows` branch only and is not for `main` (the real CI
gets a Windows job of its own). D-131 holds the decisions.

1. **Private files and folders.** Done: `kerno_core::private` (`file_options`, `create_dir`, `restrict`,
   `mode`), a Unix and a Windows function each; the password file, the store, the logs, the backup wizard's
   object list, the update's and the OpenSSH tunnel's folders go through it. Windows sets and reads nothing
   (inherited access lists, D-131); `mode` answers `None`, so the `.pgpass` and `.my.cnf` checks are not made
   there. Test: `private::tests::files_and_folders_are_made_for_their_owner` [`file_options` without its
   0600: fails on macOS].
2. **The running instance (J4).** Done: `instance.rs` keeps the command line and the URL rules; the channel is
   `instance/unix.rs` (today's socket code, moved) or `instance/windows.rs` (a named pipe; its random name and
   a random token in `instance.pipe`; both ends prove they can read the token, `instance/proof.rs`; the URL
   sent without its password; D-131). `instance::Listener`, `instance::running` and `instance::forget` (the
   file removed at quit on Windows, nothing on Unix) replace the app's `UnixListener` and `UnixStream`. Tests:
   `instance::tests::a_second_endpoint_hands_its_url_to_the_first` (both systems; on Windows the line arrives
   without its password); on every system `a_handed_over_url_loses_its_password_and_nothing_else` [the
   removal skipped: fails on macOS] and `instance::proof::tests::only_the_token_s_holder_proves_itself_once`
   [both checks always true: fails on macOS]; on Windows `instance::windows::tests::{only_a_name_kerno_makes_
   is_opened, a_url_crosses_once_both_ends_proved_themselves, a_pipe_without_the_token_gets_nothing}`. A failed
   hand-off other than "nobody listens" is logged by `main.rs`, never with the URL.
3. **The system OpenSSH tunnel (J2).** Unix only for now: the running tunnel moved to `openssh/unix.rs`;
   `openssh/windows.rs` has an empty `Forward` and an `answer_askpass` that answers nothing; `detect` returns
   `None` on Windows and `NOT_FOUND` says "not available on Windows yet" (the dialog and `error::explain` read
   it). Test: `openssh::tests::openssh_is_not_offered_on_windows_yet` (Windows).
4. **The SSH agent.** Done: `ssh::connect_agent`, Unix sockets on Unix, russh's named-pipe client on Windows
   (the connection's pipe, `SSH_AUTH_SOCK`, then `\\.\pipe\openssh-ssh-agent`), a pipe of this machine only
   (`ssh::is_local_pipe`, D-131). Test: `ssh::tests::the_windows_agent_is_a_pipe_of_this_machine_only`, on
   every system [the prefix check dropped: fails on macOS]. Never run against an agent.
5. **The app.** `windows_subsystem = "windows"` in release builds (no console; `--version` prints to a pipe or a
   file); PostgreSQL's Unix-socket cancel gated to Unix.
6. **Tests that need Unix**, gated with the reason beside each: the `/bin/sh` scripts of the password command
   and the client tools, modes, symbolic links, the OpenSSH tunnel's integration tests, the password-source
   driver and UI tests (a fake `aws`), SQLite's read-only file.

### K65 — The tests on Linux, and what they showed broken there (M)

**Now.** The workspace's tests ran on Linux for the first time on 2026-11-24, on GitHub's `ubuntu-24.04` runner
with the `kerno-*` containers (legacy MySQL included) under `xvfb-run`: the app crate ended 932 passed, 13
failed, and cargo stopped there. With `--no-fail-fast` the driver crates then showed four more. The probe

`test-linux` job is the lasting one). Each failure is one of three kinds (D-132).

every system); on macOS `kerno` 945 passed, fmt, clippy and `cargo deny` clean.

1. **Defects of the Linux app.**
   - Query History's Ctrl+Y (`secondary-y`, a key with no context) took the kit's only Redo key on Linux from
     every text field and the editor. Its default is now Ctrl+Alt+E on Linux (DataGrip's Browse Console History;
     DBeaver has none), ⌘Y on macOS. Test: `ui_tests::shortcuts::the_global_defaults_over_a_key_of_the_kit_are_known`,
     now per platform [on 4aeff7e it listed `show_history secondary-y Redo` on the runner].
   - Linux stamps file times with a coarse clock, a tick behind `SystemTime::now()`. A cancelled backup into a
     new file compared the file's time with the run's start and could keep the incomplete file; it now compares
     with the file's own time before the run (a file that was not there is the run's). Test:
     `ui_tests::tool_wizards::a_running_backup_is_cancelled_from_the_progress_page`, whose fake `mysqldump` now
     dates its file 2020 [the clock comparison put back: fails on macOS, "The incomplete file was removed" not
     said].
   - A folder picture written just after a prune was called could be removed by it (`manager_tests::
     renaming_a_folder_keeps_its_look` and `deleting_a_folder_drops_its_look_and_its_unused_image` on the
     runner). `folder_dialog::write_icon` now writes the picture under another name, sets its time from the
     clock, then renames it; the dialog and the tests' helper write through it. The macOS file clock is fine, so
     no mutation shows there: the runner is the proof (both failed on 4aeff7e, pass now).
2. **Tests that assumed macOS**, now saying what is true on each system, no assertion weakened:
   `k44::cmd_left_collapses_the_tree_only_from_the_tree` (Ctrl+Left moves a word in Linux text fields),
   `k44::a_key_of_the_text_fields_is_taken_for_a_command_of_the_tree` (the refusal names `MoveToNextWord`),
   `the_editor_adds_a_cursor_above_and_below` (Shift+Alt+Up / Down and Ctrl+Home on Linux, the kit's keys),
   `the_editor_searches_and_replaces` (`secondary-f`, not `cmd-f`),
   `folder_dialog::tests::only_kernos_unused_and_older_pictures_are_removed` (read a removed file's time; the

   `tasks::a_task_keeps_no_password_and_asks_the_override_s_at_each_run` and
   `tool_wizards::the_options_page_changes_the_command` (the wizards' `tap` clicked the middle of a field cut at
   the page's edge: it now scrolls until the field is whole), `kerno-core` `openssh::an_unknown_host_key_is_
   shown_then_trusted_then_remembered` (Ubuntu's OpenSSH hashes the host names it records: `ssh-keygen -F`

3. **What the runner lacked**, given through the scripts: PostgreSQL 17's client
   (`ui_tests::a_table_is_backed_up_alone_from_the_tree` waited on "old client": Ubuntu's `pg_dump` is 16),

One of the owner's picks for the next release ("A1 A2"): the Windows preview finds the user's files, its updates

`KERNO_K67_RUNNER=1` (`testing::k67_runner`: skipped everywhere else).

   folder, on Windows), `known_hosts()`, `expand()` (`~/` and, on Windows, `~\`), `display()`, `pgpassfile()`

   Unix: fails], a_path_under_the_home_is_shown_from_the_tilde [the separator dropped: fails],
   pgpassfile_wins_and_unix_has_no_other_default}`; `password::tests::the_home_files_are_unix_ones_and_
   pgpassfile_is_read_everywhere` [the home files read on Windows only: fails on macOS; `PGPASSFILE` again
   needing a home: fails]; on the runner `kerno-core/tests/windows.rs`
   `the_home_is_the_profile_folder_and_a_tilde_goes_under_it` and
   `libpq_s_password_file_is_in_appdata_and_no_my_cnf_is_read`.
2. **The update check.** `update::curl()` is `%SystemRoot%\System32\curl.exe` on Windows, never `PATH`; the
   arguments are the same; the system runner starts it without a console window. Tests:

   runner `update::tests::windows_k67_the_check_finds_the_latest_release_with_system32_curl` (one request to

3. **The local clients.** `tools.rs`: `.exe` names (`EXE_SUFFIX`), `ClientRoots::system` per system (Windows:
   `%ProgramFiles%\PostgreSQL` as the EDB installer's root, `program_files` for `MySQL\<product>\bin` and

   `tools::tests::the_windows_installers_folders_are_read_not_assumed` [the Program Files loop skipped: fails on
