### K75 — The tree keeps no closed session, and Reconnect mends it (S)

The real-app pass of 2026-11-25 (PostgreSQL 17 test server, a saved connection): after about 25 minutes every
schema of the tree not read yet showed "Could not read the objects of <schema>: connection closed: the session
was closed"; the schemas already read were fine; Refresh and Reconnect did not help, only Disconnect then
Connect. Before it, the connection's Read-only switch had been turned on, then off, while it was open; a
query tab had run `select pg_sleep(30)` and cancelled it. Done: 2026-11-25, D-144.

**Reproduced first** in a UI test against the test servers (`ui_tests/k75.rs`,
`the_tree_reads_on_after_every_way_a_connection_is_opened_again`), with the user's exact row. Each way to open
an open connection again was run alone, first: the toolbar's Read-only button (on, a `pg_sleep` run and
cancelled, off), the connection menu's Read-only entry, the connection's settings saved with "connect", and
the menu's Reconnect. Each one alone failed the same way on PostgreSQL; on MySQL the toolbar's switch, run
first, failed the same way (`kerno_analytics`). The cancel and K71's change to `PgSession::close` play no
part: one switch of Read-only is enough.

**Cause.** Reconnect, the Read-only switch (`Connections::set_read_only`), saving an open connection (through
Reconnect) and a lost connection (`connection_lost`) each close the connection and open it again in one update
of `Connections`: `close_all` closes every session opened on it, the tree's included. GPUI delivers the
`StatusChanged` events after the update, when the status already reads `Connecting`: the tree's
`follow_status` never saw `Disconnected` (which forgets the tree), and on `Connected` it kept its tree because
it had one. It went on with the sessions of its `Browse`, closed: what was read stayed, every new read failed,
and Refresh read again on the same closed session.

**What changed.**

| Part | Change |
|---|---|
| The cause | `Browse` keeps the connection's own session it was read through; on `Connected`, a tree read through another one is read again from the databases (`load_databases`: a new browse, new sessions, folders closed). Whatever the order the events come in, a new connection means a new tree (`navigator/view.rs`) |
| A session lost | A read of the tree whose session reports itself closed or lost (`Error::is_disconnect`) lets that session go, so the next read opens another; a load of the tree (schemas, objects, columns, extensions) is read once more on a new session without asking, and the connection is checked (`Connections::verify`: opened again if its own session does not answer). The confirmed statements that go through the same `read_on` (drop, rename, truncate…) are never run again |
| The words | A read that fails again says "The connection to “name” was lost." and "Reconnect it (its menu in the sidebar), then try again.", then the driver's words: `kerno_core::error::explain` classifies `connection closed: …` as `FailureKind::Lost` (servers and files), and the tree's `read_failure` passes it `Error::Closed` as it did `Error::Connect` (K62) |
| A data tab | Reading its rows when its connection closed, the tab kept the session it was opening on the old connection: closed by then (seen: the tab never showed its rows again), or, by the code and not seen, open and outside the connection's list, with the old settings. It drops a read that was started before the connection closed and reads again once it is open (`data/tab.rs`) |
| A query tab | Unchanged, checked: a reconnection with a transaction open asks first (`read_only_is_toggled_saved_and_reopens_the_sessions` and `disconnecting_ends_open_transactions_only_as_answered` in `ui_tests.rs`), a lost connection says the server rolled it back (`critique::a_lost_connection_found_by_a_statement_is_said_once`); a tab never carries a transaction over to a new session. After a reconnection its next statement opens a new session (the K75 test runs one after each way) |

**Tests**, each seen failing with its part taken out:
`the_tree_reads_on_after_every_way_a_connection_is_opened_again` (PostgreSQL, MySQL: after each way the tree
is read again on a new session, a schema not read yet reads, the data tab shows its rows, a query tab runs;
without the second read and the reread of the tree the user's row; with the second read and without the reread
the tree keeps its old session, timed out); `a_tree_session_ended_by_the_server_is_replaced_and_read_again`
(PostgreSQL, MySQL: the tree's own session ended with `pg_terminate_backend` / `KILL`; without the second read,
or without letting the dead session go, the schema's row shows the failed read);
`a_tree_read_when_the_server_went_away_mends_once_it_is_back` (PostgreSQL, MySQL, through `testing::Proxy`:
without the reread the tree keeps its old session once the connection is back);
`a_data_tab_reading_while_its_connection_opens_again_moves_to_the_new_one` (PostgreSQL, MySQL: a reload and the
Read-only switch in the same moment; without the change the tab never shows its rows again);
`a_database_file_tree_reads_on_after_it_is_opened_again` (SQLite: Read-only on, Reconnect, Read-only off;
without the reread the tree keeps its old session); unit tests
`navigator::view::tests::a_lost_session_is_said_in_plain_words_first` and `kerno-core`
`error::tests::a_lost_connection_is_told_in_plain_words`.

**Not done.** The administration tab opens its session as the data tab did and keeps one opened at the moment
its connection opens again (read, not reproduced): its next read fails once, then it opens a new session; left
as it is. When the server is gone, the tree's rows are not shown while the connection is reconnecting: the
plain words of a second read that fails show only while the connection still answers: covered by the unit
tests, not seen drawn in the tree. No capture: the row is K62's row with other words.

### K74 — Restoring a MySQL folder backup again into a full database deadlocks (M)

**Now.** The pass in the real app of 2026-11-25 (MySQL 8.4): a database of a 20,000-row table with a view and a
trigger, a parent with a UNIQUE column and a child with a foreign key to it, backed up with "Faster" into a
folder, restored with "Faster" into an empty database (right), then the same folder again into that database,
now full: 1 run in 2 failed with "ERROR 1213 (40001) at line 22: Deadlock found when trying to get lock" on the
parent or the child. "Partly loaded: big, small_a, small_b" while `big` held 0 rows, `small_b` 0 and `small_a`
its old 300; the trigger gone. The wizard's "Into" and its confirmation said "on top of what it holds", word for
word an empty database's; on MySQL 5.1 the backup's Speed hint spoke of "Block writes below", a row hidden
until "Faster" is ticked.

Done: D-143.

1. **Reproduced, the cause measured.** `kerno-mysql/tests/folder_backup.rs`
   `a_folder_restored_again_into_a_full_database_never_deadlocks` (under `with_scratch`, the guard now killing
   `kerno_test_k74_*` connections too; scratch databases `kerno_test_k74_src` and `_dst`): the pass's shape,
   restored with 8 clients into an empty database then 30 times again. Before the fix: MySQL 8.4 failed 5 runs
   in 21, then 2 in 31 (ERROR 1213 at line 22, the parent's or the child's `DROP TABLE IF EXISTS`); MariaDB 11
   failed 11 in 31 (ERROR 1205 at the same line of the parent's file); MySQL 5.7 none in 31. `SHOW ENGINE
   INNODB STATUS` held no deadlock after a 1213: the server's metadata locks, which in MySQL 8.0 follow a foreign
   key to the tables it links. Into an empty database (each file's DROP finds nothing): none in 93.
2. **Fixed at its cause** (`tools/mysql_folder.rs`). Dropping every table first (tried first) left MariaDB failing
   2 in 31 with ERROR 1100: the child created by another client between the parent's `LOCK TABLES … WRITE` and
   its INSERT. Now `find_definition` reads where each table's first file has its definition (from its `DROP
   TABLE` to the `SET character_set_client = @saved_cs_client` line mysqldump writes after every CREATE TABLE);
   `define` runs every definition first, one after the other on one `mysql` client fed parts of the files
   (`tools::StdinPart`, no temporary file), each followed by a `SELECT 'kerno-defined:N'` the client prints
   back, so that Kerno knows which ran; then each first file is fed again without its definition, N clients at
   once, its ranges after it. A file whose definition is not found runs whole, on a client of its own (after
   the review, F2: first in that run). A backup of
   the rows only loads as before; the plain one-file restore is not concerned (one client, in order). No retry.
3. **Said before it happens** (`backup_dialog.rs`, `simple.rs`). The target's tables are read again after each
   run (the pass had read them while the database was empty, and Back kept that) and Start waits for the read;
   the wizard and the confirmation say "A restore drops and replaces the backup's tables that “db” holds" and
   "“db” already holds 2 of the backup's 3 tables (…): each is dropped and replaced with the backup's table and
   rows; the tables only in “db” stay", and what a stopped restore leaves; the shown command says the
   definitions come first. Production and read-only: the same confirmation as every restore. A plain dump into a
   database that is not empty: said to replace the tables it has, unnamed (naming them means reading the whole
   file).
4. **A true failure message.** Once every client has ended, one client reads which of the backup's tables exist
   and which hold a row (`tables_now`, 30 s per read): Loaded, Partly loaded (some of their rows), Empty now,
   Missing now, Not loaded (as they were before), the routines and views not loaded, and "Run the restore again:
   it drops each of the backup's tables and loads it from the start"; a database that cannot be read is said,
   the tables not finished named as possibly empty, partly loaded or missing. A failure in the definitions' run
   is said at the line of the table's own file.
5. **The Speed hint** says "“Block writes”, offered once this is ticked" while the row is hidden, "below" once it
   shows, on every server version.

Tests, each seen failing under the mutation in brackets: core (`tools::mysql_folder::tests`)
`a_table_s_definition_is_found_where_mysqldump_writes_it` (the fixtures of `tests/fixtures/mysqldump/`: mysqldump
26.10 from 8.4 and MariaDB 11, 5.7.44 from 5.7; a partitioned table, an empty one, `--skip-comments`, no DROP
TABLE, definitions only, `--replace`, `--insert-ignore`; four shapes that run whole) [rows before a definition
not stopping the search], `a_line_of_the_definitions_run_is_said_in_its_own_file` [the client's line left as
it is], `a_failed_restore_says_which_tables_it_left_empty_or_missing` [the files' states read instead of the
database]; driver
`a_folder_restored_again_into_a_full_database_never_deadlocks` (8.4, MariaDB 11, 5.7: 31 restores each, then the
child's file broken after its DROP: the message names it missing, every other table where the database has it,
and the next run loads everything) [the definitions' run off: MySQL 8.4 failed 3 in 31, then 2 in 31, ERROR
1213 at line 22]; K63's `a_failed_restore_says_what_is_in_and_a_refused_one_loads_nothing` (new words). UI
(`ui_tests/mysql_folder.rs`) `a_restore_again_says_what_it_replaces_and_a_failure_what_it_left` (real mysqldump
and mysql on 8.4: restored, then again from the same wizard after Back, then with the child's file broken, then
mended) [the target not read again after a run: the words never came; the confirmation's "dropping and
replacing" off; the files' states read instead of the database: "Not loaded: “child”, “parent”", the pass's
lie], `the_speed_hint_points_only_at_a_row_that_shows` (8.4 and 5.1 versions) ["below" either way];
`the_restore_says_what_happens_to_the_tables_already_there` takes the new words and a plain dump [the plain
dump's sentence off]; `a_faster_mysql_backup_is_a_folder_the_restore_loads_on_several_clients` the new words.

**The independent review** (of 5598f78, rebased as 6d51f94; two regressions confirmed by a run; fixed on top in
one commit). Each test seen failing under the mutation in brackets.

| | What was wrong | The fix | The test [mutation seen failing] |
|---|---|---|---|
| F1 | Every part of the client's input was opened before the child started: more than about 250 tables failed on macOS, "Too many open files" (256 for an app started from Finder), blaming the wrong table | `tools::run` checks each file before the start and opens each part when the feed reaches it, then closes it; a part that cannot be read kills the child and fails the run; a definition is in only when its marker came back, even after an exit 0; lists in a message stop at ten ("… and N more") | core `tools::tests::parts_are_opened_one_at_a_time` (600 files fed under `ulimit -n 64`, in a child of the test binary; Unix only) [every part opened up front: "Too many open files"]; `a_part_that_cannot_be_read_kills_the_child` [the feed's error ignored: the child ended 0]; `a_failed_restore_says_which_tables_it_left_empty_or_missing` (25 tables) [the cut at 10 raised to 1,000] |
| F2 | The end line was taken inside a quoted NAME (mysqldump writes names raw): the cut fell inside CREATE TABLE, ERROR 1064 at every run, tables dropped | `find_definition` follows quotes (backticks, `'` and `"` with doubling and backslashes) and comments from the file's start; a line counts only when it starts in code. A file whose definition is not found runs whole on a client of its own, after the definitions' run, never in it | core `a_table_s_definition_is_found_where_mysqldump_writes_it` with real mysqldump files of a table and a column named with a newline and the end line (8.4, MariaDB 11) [quotes ignored]; driver `names_that_hold_a_newline_restore_again_and_again` (8.4, MariaDB 11: restored three times, `CHECKSUM TABLE` equal; the third time the parent's file without its end line runs alone and replaces a row added) [quotes ignored: ERROR 1064; the file run alone skipped: 3 rows where 2 are wanted] |
| F3 | A cancel said "what ran before it stopped is kept" with every table of the backup empty | The summary read from the database goes to the dialog: "Cancelled: mysql was stopped. Loaded: none. Empty now: …" (`FolderEnd::said`) | UI `a_cancelled_restore_says_what_it_left_and_output_options_are_refused` (the real mysql behind a stand-in whose rows' runs wait; cancelled once the rows' clients start; Unix only) [the dialog's old sentence] |
| F4 | `--table`, `--xml`… changed the client's output: the markers and the read afterwards broke, naming the wrong table | Refused for a folder restore before Start and in the run, in every spelling the client takes (`restore_refusal`), said under the extra arguments; not stripped (the user's TLS and plugin options must still reach every client) | core `a_folder_restore_refuses_what_changes_the_client_s_output` [the refusal off]; the UI test above (`--force --table` refused, `--force` taken) [the dialog's check off] |
| L1 | A server error quoting several lines was said by its last line | The last `ERROR …` line wins (`Said`) | core `a_line_of_the_definitions_run_is_said_in_its_own_file` [the last line kept] |
| L2 | A table created whose marker never came back was "as they were before" while empty | Missing, Empty, then rows not started "as before", then partly loaded | core `a_failed_restore_says_which_tables_it_left_empty_or_missing` [the old order] |
| L3 | The progress stood still during the definitions and never reached the total | The definitions' bytes are counted, every byte of the folder once (`StdinPart::File::counted`) | driver `a_folder_restored_again_into_a_full_database_never_deadlocks` (bytes counted equal `total()`) [the definitions not counted: 662,637 of 663,892] |
| L4 | Unread target: "on top of" and "replaced" together; a plain dump's confirmation likewise; names compared without case on a server that tells them apart | One sentence each ("every table of the backup that exists there is dropped and replaced"; "where the dump's DROP TABLE statements, if it has any, replace the tables they name"); `lower_case_table_names` read with the tables | UI `the_restore_says_what_happens_to_the_tables_already_there` (a database that does not exist; a plain dump; `Two` against `two` on 8.4) [the unread case not replacing; the plain clause off; names always folded] |
| L5 | A manifest table with no file shifted the other tables' cuts | One entry per table | driver (a hand-edited manifest whose first table has no file) [the shift put back: ERROR 1064 in `big`] |
| L6 | The tests' guard killed every `kerno_test_k63%` / `k74%` connection, another agent's included | Only connections in the databases the test named (and its own sessions) | none (the tests' harness) |

Left: L7, `--force` in the extra arguments makes `mysql` exit 0 after errors (before K74, plain restore too).

**The second review** (of d605bde: F1 to F4 and L1 to L6 confirmed fixed by its own runs: 300 and 1,200 tables
under `ulimit -n 256`, the newline names, 7 option sets by `CHECKSUM TABLE`, 31 runs on three servers).

| | What was wrong | The fix | The test [mutation seen failing] |
|---|---|---|---|
| N1 | A failed feed dropped the client's input at once: the client saw its end before the kill (a statement cut short could run), and an unbiased `select!` could return its exit 0 | The error comes back with the pipe; `run` kills the client, then drops it; the `select!` is biased, the feed first; a feed that failed as the client ended still wins | core `a_feed_that_fails_never_ends_the_child_s_input` (200 runs of a stand-in that writes `EOF` when its input ends: never; 500 runs of one that ends on its own as the feed fails: an error each time) [the pipe dropped before the kill: `EOF` written at run 0]. d605bde itself passed these 200 runs here (the race is too narrow on this machine to catch), and the second loop was not seen failing with `biased` and the check taken out (0 in 500): it guards, it does not prove |
| N2 | `--compatible=ansi` quotes names in double quotes, which the tracker reads with backslashes | Refused for a folder backup, in plain words | core `the_folder_backup_refuses_the_options_it_sets_itself_in_every_spelling` [the entry renamed: "kept"] |
| N3 | `--print-defaults`, `--version`, `--help` (`-V -? -I`) exit 0 having run nothing: "Done" | Refused for every MySQL / MariaDB tool (the shared list) | core `options_that_print_and_stop_are_refused` [the check off] |
| N4 | A task's refused arguments were said only by the field, on another page or nowhere | Every reason Start cannot run is said above the buttons ("Cannot start: …") in the simple view and on the wizard's last page | UI `a_task_with_refused_arguments_says_why_where_it_starts` (a saved task with `--force --table`, run from the Tasks view: Start disabled, the reason shown, no client run) [the note off] |

Left, as the review asks: the read of the database after a stop cannot be cancelled (two reads of 30 s at
most, about 10 s in practice); `NO_BACKSLASH_ESCAPES` was reasoned about, not run.

A trap met on the way, for the next agent: the cargo target is shared with other worktrees, and a `touch` before
`cargo test` is not enough while another agent's build waits for the same lock: it may rebuild `kerno-core` from
its own tree after the touch, and the run then takes that build as fresh (one mutation run here showed 16 core
tests where this tree has 19). Setting this tree's `lib.rs` / `main.rs` to a time in the future (`touch -t`)
before each run made every run here build from this tree.

**Not done / not verified**: the restore of a database of hundreds of tables (the definitions now run one after
the other: not measured); files written by MariaDB's own `mariadb-dump` (none installed here; where the end of a
definition is not found, the file runs whole on a client of its own); an SSH tunnel; the real app (no GUI
tonight). Captures offscreen, not committed (a temporary test, `gpui-pre-apple` as a dev-dependency, the real
mysql client on 8.4), light and dark: the wizard into a database holding 2 of the backup's 3 tables, its
confirmation, a failure in the definitions' run, the backup's Speed hint unticked.

### K73 — The macOS disk image opens on a designed window (S)

The owner's request of 2026-11-16 (ROADMAP, "Limits found on the way"), picked on 2026-11-24 for the next
release. D-142.

1. The picture: `packaging/macos/dmg-background.svg`, 640 × 400, from the brand (a grey-olive surface that
   keeps Finder's labels readable black or white, an arrow in lime made of the mark's three disks and its
   chevron, the lime mark below); `packaging/macos/render-background.swift` renders it with AppKit into
   `dmg-background.png` and `dmg-background@2x.png`, committed.
2. The layout: `packaging/macos/make-dmg-layout.sh` mounts a scratch image with the release's names and runs
   `dmg-layout.applescript` (icon view, no toolbar, sidebar or status bar, bounds holding the 640 × 400
   picture under the title bar, 128-point icons, Kerno's centre at 160,180 and Applications' at 480,180, the
   picture as background); the `.DS_Store` Finder writes is kept as `packaging/macos/dmg-window.dsstore`.
   It refuses to run while a volume named Kerno is mounted and gives up after two minutes if Finder does not
   answer. The committed file was made on GitHub's `macos-15` runner.
3. `scripts/bundle-macos.sh`: the image is made read-write (the app, the Applications link, the picture as a
   retina TIFF by `tiffutil`, `.VolumeIcon.icns`), mounted out of Finder's sight to receive the layout and
   the custom-icon flag of its root (`xattr`), converted to `UDZO` and verified; each hdiutil step that creates,
   mounts, unmounts or converts keeps five tries. Then the compressed image is mounted read-only and checked: the layout byte for byte, the
   picture, the volume icon and its flag, `codesign --verify --deep --strict` on the app inside and its
   binary compared with the one launched. Any miss fails with a line naming it; there is no plain fallback.
   The binary is taken from `$CARGO_TARGET_DIR` when it is set.
4. `.github/workflows/macos-dmg-probe.yml`, on the `macos-dmg` branch only (not for `main`): the generator on
   the runner, then the script's image opened in the light and the dark appearance, the screen and the
   window kept with the run.

Done: 2026-11-24. Proved on GitHub's `macos-15` runner (run 45452073025): the script built, signed, launched
and packed the app with the committed layout; its image, opened, shows the designed window in both
appearances (`docs/screenshots/final/m12-dmg-window-{light,dark}.png`, cut from the runner's screen) and
Kerno's icon on the desktop; the generator ran there in about 11 s. The disk image step takes 15 s instead
of 7. Locally (macOS 27): the script's full run passes (signature, launch check, the image's checks), the
image is `UDZO` and verifies; each check was seen failing with its part removed (no layout copied, no
flag, no volume icon, the picture elsewhere, the app changed after signing with and without the signature
check); the generator's two-minute guard was seen ending a Finder that never answers. Not proved: on the
owner's Mac the window had its size and places but no picture, because an image of 0.1.0 was mounted under
the same volume name (D-142); the same image under another volume name showed it. The light appearance was
not captured locally: switching it from a script means asking for a permission, a dialog left on the owner's
screen.

### K72 — Generate test data for a table (M)

**Now.** Test rows could only be typed, imported from a file, or asked of the AI assistant (K53: it needs the
assistant, writes what a model imagines and knows no bound or key). ROADMAP TOOL-23 ("Mock data generator"; item
5), the owner's pick for the next release. DBeaver's generator is not in its Community sources: nothing ported.

**Wanted.** "Generate test data…" on a table (the tree, a data tab's menu, the palette), on PostgreSQL,
MySQL/MariaDB and SQLite: how many rows (default 100, at most 1,000,000) and a seed shown and editable; one line
per column with its kind of values and their settings, guessed from the type, the keys and the name, changeable;
each type's bounds kept; unique keys and primary keys never repeated; foreign keys picked among the keys that
exist (a composite key whole; an empty referenced table said before anything runs); a share of NULL per nullable
column; what Kerno cannot check said; a preview of the first rows that follows the settings, made off the UI
thread; "Open script" (a query tab, nothing run) and "Insert" (one transaction, progress, Cancel, the
environment's rules, a failure rolled back whole and its row said).

Done: D-141. Core `test_data.rs` (new): `Draw` (SplitMix64 keyed by seed, stream, row, attempt), the word lists,
`Shape` (nine name shapes, `of_name`, `width`), `Kind` (`label`, `params`), `ColumnSettings`, `Settings`,
`Facts` (`reference`, `unique_sets`, `unique_alone`) and `read` (the structure; `COUNT(*)` and the `MAX` of each
whole-number key alone; per foreign key `SELECT DISTINCT … ORDER BY … LIMIT 10000`; on SQLite whether the
table's SQL holds a CHECK), the type bounds (`integer_bounds`, `decimal_bounds`, `text_limit`, `bytes_limit`,
`bits_width`, `has_zone`, `element` of an array), `kinds`, `guess`, `defaults`, `Plan::new` (every refusal in
words, the unique sets with their capacity), `Plan::values` (`Values`: rows drawn again until their unique
values are new), `preview`, `script` (heading through `compare::commented`, `Batches` of 500 rows or 1 MiB),
`first_statement`, `shown`, `insert` (`Report`, `Failure`: begin, a savepoint per batch, row by row to name the
refused row, rollback), `notes`, `grouped`, `new_seed`; `lib.rs`: `pub mod test_data`; `xlsx.rs`:
`days_from_civil` shared with the core. App: `test_data_dialog.rs` (new: the table read on a session of its own,
the rows and seed fields, New seed, one line per column with its kind (a menu of the kinds offered), its two
settings and its NULL %, the notes, the problem in words, the preview grid, the read-only line, Open script
written off the UI thread into a query tab, Insert… through `sql_confirm` (`writes: true`), progress, Cancel,
done, failed, cancelled); `workspace.rs` (`generate_test_data`, the data tab's menu entry and its `perform` arm,
the tree's event, the action; `imported` shared); `tabs.rs` (`TabAction::GenerateTestData`);
`navigator/actions.rs` (`Action::GenerateTestData` "Fill with test data…" beside Compare data, reads only);
`navigator/view.rs` (`NavigatorEvent::GenerateTestData`); `actions.rs`, `menu_palette.rs`, `main.rs` (the
command `generate_test_data`, Server ▸ Tools ▸ Generate Test Data…). Element ids: `test-data-dialog`,
`test-data-rows`, `test-data-seed`, `test-data-new-seed`, `test-data-columns`, `("test-data-kind", i)`,
`("test-data-from", i)`, `("test-data-to", i)`, `("test-data-nulls", i)`, `test-data-notes`,
`test-data-problem`, `test-data-preview`, `test-data-read-only`, `test-data-close`, `test-data-insert`,
`test-data-open-script`, `test-data-progress`, `test-data-cancel`, `test-data-done`, `test-data-failed`,
`test-data-cancelled`, `test-data-back`.

Tests, each seen failing under the mutation in brackets (one at a time, by a script that restores the file):
core unit `test_data::tests::` (13) `the_same_seed_gives_the_same_rows_and_another_seed_others` [the seed
ignored], `numbers_stay_in_their_range_and_their_type` [`unsigned` ignored],
`unique_keys_never_repeat_and_impossible_ones_are_refused` and `a_junction_table_takes_each_pair_of_keys_once`
[taken values accepted; the capacity check off], `nulls_come_in_their_share_and_only_where_allowed` [the share
ignored], `the_script_writes_batches_with_the_dialect_s_literals` [the heading written as one comment line;

`the_guess_follows_types_keys_and_names` [a sequence from 1 instead of after the largest key],
`a_composite_reference_is_one_key_of_the_parent` [each column of a composite key picking its own parent];
`a_table_the_server_fills_alone_gets_rows_of_defaults` and
`numbers_dates_and_seeds_are_read_and_written_exactly` without a mutation of their own. Driver, on scratch
objects `kerno_test_k72_*` dropped before and after, also on a failure:
`a_thousand_rows_of_every_type_insert_and_keep_the_rules` and
`an_empty_parent_is_refused_and_a_failing_row_keeps_nothing` in `kerno-postgres/tests/test_data.rs` (schemas

timestamp, time, uuid, jsonb, json, an enum and an array of it, bytea, `text[]`, `integer[]`, `bit(4)`,
interval, an identity, two foreign keys to a scratch parent, a stored generated column; 1,000 rows inserted,

empty table refused; a CHECK broken at a known row: the row named, nothing kept) [the final ROLLBACK turned into
COMMIT: caught on MySQL, MariaDB and SQLite; PostgreSQL rolls an aborted transaction back on COMMIT itself, so
its test passes under it], `kerno-mysql/tests/test_data.rs` (MySQL and MariaDB, scratch databases: the same with
`SMALLINT UNSIGNED`, `TINYINT`, `TINYINT(1)`, `DATETIME`, `TIMESTAMP`, `YEAR`, `CHAR(36)` named `public_uuid`,
JSON (MariaDB's checked text), `ENUM`, `SET`, `VARBINARY`, `BINARY(16)`, `BIT(4)`, an `AUTO_INCREMENT`),
`kerno-sqlite/tests/ test_data.rs` (a file in a temporary directory; a column held to a list by a CHECK, said
and left to its default). UI `ui_tests/k72.rs` (new):
`generate_test_data_is_asked_from_the_tree_a_data_tab_and_the_palette` [the tree's entry emitting nothing; no
entry in the data tab's menu; the palette's command not wired],
`the_preview_follows_the_settings_and_open_script_runs_nothing` (ids 1-10, a range of one price, another seed
and back, NULL names, a range the column cannot hold said, 10,001 rows: Open script off; 25 rows: the tab, its
target, no outcome, the table still empty) [the preview made from the guess, not the settings; the script's tab
on no connection], `insert_on_a_local_connection_fills_the_table_and_its_data_tab` [the data tab not refreshed],
`read_only_refuses_production_asks_twice_and_cancel_keeps_nothing` (read-only: the line, Insert off, the script
still opens; production: one click arms, Cancel inserts nothing, two clicks insert; 1,000,000 rows cancelled
after the first batches: nothing kept) [read-only not refused; the confirmation with `writes: false`; Cancel
doing nothing], `a_refused_row_keeps_nothing` [a refused row shown as done], `a_sqlite_table_gets_its_rows` (a
file's table from its data tab and the palette; 100 rows; the data tab shows them) [the palette's command not
wired; the data tab not refreshed]. Updated without a mutation of their own: `navigator::actions::tests`
(Generate test data reads only), `menu_palette`'s walk of the tree's entries. **Captures** (offscreen, temporary
test, not committed), light and dark, 1200 × 800: `docs/screenshots/final/m12-generate-data-dialog-*` (replaced
by K76's `m12-test-data-dialog-*`), `m12-generate-data-insert-*` (the confirmation), `m12-generate-data-script-*`
(the query tab; its heading has K76's two more lines since).

**Not done**: values from a file or another table; CHECK rules read and kept; values tied across columns; the
settings saved as a task; types without a kind (geometry, ranges, `inet`: NULL where allowed, else refused); the
SQLite path from the tree is not driven by a UI test (the tree's rows move while a file connection opens; the
data tab's entry is), its code is the PostgreSQL one.

**K76: after the pass** (2026-11-25; D-141 amended). A pass in the real app (captures `n4-c*`, `n4-b1-*`) found:
(1) on every text column's line the max length field ran under NULL % at 1280 × 800; (2) a disabled NULL %
looked enabled in the dark theme; (3) a second run with the same seed inserted unique values that carried on
after the rows there, so "the same settings give the same rows" was wrong; (4) a SQLite `created_at TEXT DEFAULT
CURRENT_TIMESTAMP` got random words; (5) the seed and the settings were new at each opening; (6) the tree's "N
databases hidden" tooltip stayed over the connection's dialog its click opened. The CHECK a note names stays the
catalog's raw text (what the server says). Done:

- (1) `render_lines`: the column's name takes a fifth of the line (`NAME_SHARE`) and is cut first, the kind 220
  points (`KIND_WIDTH`, "A key of the referenced table" whole), the two settings share what is left (each label
  whole, each field `flex_1`), NULL % 48 points; at 720 × 440 (the window's smallest size, `main.rs`) the body
  was cut below the columns with the buttons, so the body (`test-data-body`) scrolls and the buttons stay below
  it (`DialogContent` `min_h_0`). (2) A disabled NULL % gets the theme's muted background and its input border
  at half, as a disabled button (the kit's disabled field is lighter than an enabled one in dark).
- (3) Core: `Written::unique` (in a unique set) and `Plan::before` (the table's rows): a unique column's value,
  NULL share and key pick are drawn for row + rows there (a sequence keeps its start), and only unique columns
  take the attempt number, so a retry for a repeated key no longer redraws the other columns; a unique text's
  number counts from 1 at that shifted row (the same numbers as before). The sentence (`SAME_ROWS`, the
  script's heading, GUIDE): "the same settings give the same rows, except values that must be unique, which
  continue after the rows already in the table".
- (4) Core: `kinds` offers Date, Date and time and Time of day on every SQLite text column; `text_time` reads
  the default (`CURRENT_TIMESTAMP`, `CURRENT_DATE`, `CURRENT_TIME`, `datetime(`, `date(`, `time(`, a literal
  date) then the name (`*_at`, `timestamp`, `*_date`, `date`, `time`); `guess` gives such a column "Leave to the
  default" when it has a default (K72's entry, not renamed), else that kind. Narrow on purpose: other engines'
  date columns have date types, and a default elsewhere still keeps the type's guess (K72's tests unchanged).
- (5) `Kept`, a GPUI global: per (connection, database, table; a place without a database takes the
  connection's own, so every entry finds the same settings) the last valid settings, the column names and
  each column's starting settings for its kind; `new` takes the rows and the seed from it, `take_facts` the
  columns when the names match and each kind is still offered; a from/to left as it started takes today's
  start (a sequence after the largest key there now). Memory only, for the run of the app. New seed unchanged.
- (6) `tip.rs` (new): `tip(text)` builds the tooltip as a small view that draws nothing once a dialog is open
  and stays so. GPUI's `.tooltip` does not cancel its pending show on a click (it clears only one already
  shown), then keeps the tooltip while the pointer is inside the element's bounds, occluded or not: a click
  inside the show delay that opens a dialog put the tooltip over it (seen by a probe: shown before the click,
  cleared; waiting at the click, shown over the dialog). The six plain tooltips of the tree
  (`navigator/view.rs`) use it; the other plain tooltips (tab titles, grid marks, result tabs, the status bar)
  open no dialog and are unchanged, and the kit's components manage their own.

Tests, each seen failing under the mutation in brackets (one at a time, by a script that restores the file):
core `test_data::tests::unique_values_continue_after_the_rows_there_and_the_rest_repeats` (a uuid key, a unique
number, a junction pair: an empty table twice gives the same rows; with 50 rows there, no key repeats and the
plain column is drawn as before) [unique values not shifted; the other columns drawn again on a retry],
`a_sqlite_text_column_of_dates_is_guessed_from_its_default_and_its_name` [the default not guessed first; names
not read]; UI `k72::the_same_seed_gives_the_same_rows_and_the_dialog_keeps_its_settings` (the sentence; 20 rows
inserted with seed 4242, the table emptied, the dialog opened again on the same rows, seed and qty range, the
same rows; again on the full table: 40 distinct ids, numbers and codes, the sequence from 21; the tree's entry
opens on the same seed; New seed changes it) [the old sentence; unique values not shifted; nothing kept; a kept
sequence start not refreshed],
`k72::a_sqlite_text_column_of_dates_gets_dates_or_its_default` (a file in a temporary directory: Server, Default,
Date, Words guessed; 100 rows with the server's timestamps and drawn dates) [the default not guessed first;
dates not offered on SQLite text], `k76::a_tooltip_does_not_come_over_the_dialog_its_row_opened` (the tooltip
shows on a rest; a click inside the delay opens the dialog and no tooltip comes over it, nor after it closes)
[the tip ignoring an open dialog; the tip not staying hidden]. **Captures** (offscreen, temporary test, not
committed), light and dark: `m12-test-data-dialog-*` (1280 × 800), `m12-test-data-small-*` (720 × 440, the
first columns, a key of the referenced table, the widest decimal), `m12-test-data-small-kinds-*` (a sequence
from 2,147,483,000, a random number, bytes, a date and time), `m12-test-data-small-end-*` (the body scrolled to
the preview, the buttons in view).

### K71 — The tests that failed now and then (M)

The owner's pick C1 for the release of 2026-11-25: the CI must not go red by chance. Since 2026-11-24 every push
to `main` runs the workspace's tests on GitHub (macOS without the containers, Linux with them), and seven tests
had failed there or on this Mac under load, then passed on a rerun. Each was looped until it failed, its cause
found in the code, fixed where the wrong thing happens, and looped again. The loops: on this Mac one copy at a
time (the tests that share a scratch schema or a role cannot run side by side) beside the other agents' builds
and tests on the same servers; on GitHub, the probe workflow `.github/workflows/linux-tests-probe.yml` on the
branch `linux-tests` (not merged): each test in a loop with a CPU hog per core, then the whole workspace once.
Done: 2026-11-24, D-140.

| Test | Seen before | Cause | Fix | After |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `fk_preview::a_read_behind_a_lock_is_stopped` | 1 in 30 on the Linux runner; 2 in about 500 here | App. PostgreSQL parses a statement before it runs it, and the parse waits for the table's lock. `PgSession::close` sent its cancel outside the session's count of cancels: the parse was cancelled, then the statement was sent all the same and waited for the lock on the server, unseen, while the panel said it was stopped (both of the test's messages: the read moved away from, and the read stopped after 10 s) | `close` cancels as `Session::cancel` does, counted: a statement whose describe step a cancel ended is not sent (`kerno-postgres` `session.rs`) | 0 in 600 here; 0 in 100 on the Linux runner |
| `every_owner_of_a_session_closes_it_when_it_goes_away` | 1 in 30 on the Linux runner; 2 in 96 here | Test. After a read of `information_schema.TABLES`' sizes (the database list, a table's structure) MySQL 8.4 can keep an InnoDB transaction on the idle session, autocommit on and the statement's transaction committed for `performance_schema`, until that session's next statement: the connection's 5 s keep-alive in the app; never in the test, whose clock stands still. The test read `innodb_trx`, and counted it as a session left in a transaction | The test counts the transactions the server's SQL has open (`performance_schema.events_transactions_current`, `ACTIVE`), which a transaction Kerno began and left still shows; its message also says how each open transaction began | 0 in 96 here; 0 in 40 on the Linux runner |
| `updates::update_installs_the_checked_release_and_starts_it` | 1 in 30 on the Linux runner; 0 in 200 here | Test. The fake runner lists a call before it runs it, on another thread: the test read what the new Kerno found in the store as soon as `open` was listed. The app saves the tabs before it installs (`install_and_quit` awaits it) | The test waits until the new Kerno has read the store | 0 in 30 on the Linux runner |
| `transfer::copy_tables_opens_from_tools_and_from_the_palette` | twice here under load (the dialog blocked 30 s); 0 in 30 on the runner | App. A schema's listing asks each table's size, which opens the table and waits behind an ACCESS EXCLUSIVE lock held or asked for elsewhere (here another test's `Locker`; in a user's hands a long ALTER or a migration): Copy tables reads every schema and stayed on "Reading the tables…"; the tree and the backup dialog wait the same way. Shown in one run: a lock on a table of another schema, the dialog still reading after 5 s | A table locked that way is listed without its size (`pg_locks` read in the same query; `kerno-postgres` `introspect.rs`) | 0 in 30 on the Linux runner; a second cause below |
| `critique::a_local_target_of_a_multi_run_asks_before_a_delete_without_where` | 7 in 200 here | App. The target's tab set its stage to "asking" without saying it changed: the run reads it when the tab notifies, so it showed "Starting…" while the question was open, until something else redrew the tab | `cx.notify()` with the stage (`query/tab.rs`) | 0 in 300 here; 0 in 40 on the Linux runner |
| `editor_v11::a_folded_region_still_runs_and_saves_in_full` | not reproduced (0 in 30 on the runner, 0 in 200 here with the old retry) | Test. The kit parses for 2 ms as the text is set and, when that is not enough, again 150 ms later on the test clock, which stands still. The test set the text again before each click (a parse again) and retried the click until it folded: a mark that needed two clicks would have passed | The test waits for the marks with the clock moved on, moves only the cursor, and one click must fold | 0 in 200 here; 0 in 30 on the Linux runner |
| `dbeaver_import::a_dbeaver_project_is_previewed_then_imported_once` | 2 in 100 on the `macos-15` runner; 2 in 300 here | App. The preview judged "already here" from the connection list in memory, which an import reads back from the store a moment after it says it is done: importing again at once could copy a connection a second time | The preview judges from the store, plus the connections opened from a URL (memory only) | 0 in 400 here; 0 in 100 on `macos-15`; 0 in 30 on the Linux runner |

Copy tables had a second cause, met in the full check on `main` after these fixes ("The tables could not be
read: could not open relation with OID … [XX000]"): a listing reads what its snapshot holds, then each
object's size, last value or definition from the catalog's present, and an object another session dropped
meanwhile failed the whole statement (the tree and the backup dialog too). `PgSession::query`, which every
catalog read of `introspect.rs` goes through, reads again (twice at most, never inside a transaction block) on
that error and on "cache lookup failed": the new snapshot no longer lists the object. The administration views
run as statements and are left as they were, not read again (their size functions open a table with
`try_relation_open` and give no size for a dropped one, by PostgreSQL's code, not run; their
`pg_get_function_identity_arguments` would still fail on a function dropped meanwhile). MySQL 8.4 and MariaDB 11 showed no error in 200 listings each (tables' sizes, the databases' sums,
indexes) beside 400 tables created and dropped. And since a table's lock no longer holds a listing, the two
tests of the tree's Cancel and 30 s bound (`critique::a_tree_read_behind_a_lock_is_cancelled_and_the_tree_reads_on`,
`a_tree_read_that_takes_too_long_is_stopped`) failed; they now hold a sequence (an `ALTER SEQUENCE` left in its
transaction: the read of its last value waits) in a database of their own, so that no other test's read of
`kerno` waits behind them.

The last round on GitHub, on the final code (run 45468100079): each looped test 0 failures (the FK preview in
50 runs, the others and the tree's two lock tests in 20; the DBeaver import 0 in 100 on `macos-15`), then the
whole workspace on the Linux runner with the containers: 2,120 passed, 3 ignored, none failed. On this Mac:
`kerno` 953 passed, `kerno-postgres` 152 passed (1 ignored); fmt, clippy and `cargo deny` clean.

The folding test's cause was seen in a run (a temporary test, not committed): a text over the kit's 256 KB
limit for the 2 ms parse had no fold mark until the test clock moved 200 ms, then had it.

Tests added, each seen failing with its part of the change taken out:
`kerno-postgres` `execute::closing_stops_a_statement_waiting_for_a_lock_as_it_is_described` (the old `close`:
the close itself waited 5 s for the statement, which still waited for the lock);
`introspect::a_locked_table_is_listed_at_once_without_its_size` (the old query: the listing waited the 10 s
the test gives it); `introspect::an_object_dropped_while_it_is_listed_is_left_out` (the listing waits for a
sequence, which its holder then drops: without the second read, "could not open relation with OID"); `ui_tests::dbeaver_import::a_connection_just_written_is_already_here` (a connection saved
to the store behind the list's back: "Shop Import(true)" without the fix). The update test was proved by a
50 ms delay put in the fake runner's `open` (not committed): the old test failed at once, the new one passed;
the multi-run fix by the 7 in 200 before against 0 in 300 after.

Not done: `portability::open_import` (J15's import of Kerno's own file) plans from the list in memory as the
DBeaver import did. A second import at once of the same file mostly fails in the store (the id is there
already: "The import stopped"), but a connection whose password is typed anew gets a fresh id and could be
copied. Not one of the seven, not reproduced: left for its own item. The probe workflow stays on its branch.

### K68 — Windows, step 3b: the real test suites against real servers, and the app at work (M)

The owner's pick "A3" for the next release: Windows was a preview nobody had used. Before K68 the Windows CI ran
check, clippy and `kerno-core`'s tests; the driver crates' suites and the app's GPUI tests had never run there.
D-137 holds the decisions. A probe workflow, `.github/workflows/windows-tests-probe.yml` on the branch
`windows-tests` (not for `main`), runs the whole workspace on `windows-2025`; the lasting job is `ci.yml`'s
`windows`.

1. **The servers, seeded as the containers.** `scripts/windows-test-servers.sh up|down` (Git Bash) uses the
   image's own installs, nothing downloaded: EDB's PostgreSQL 17 (`initdb` with `kerno` as the bootstrap
   superuser, UTF-8, `en-US`; port 55432 on 127.0.0.1; TLS with the generated certificates and the `hostssl
   kerno_cert cert` line of `00_tls.sh`; `pg_ctl` starts it) and the MySQL Server 8.4 MSI (a data folder of its
   own, `--initialize-insecure` with root from any host, `docker-compose.yml`'s options, `skip-name-resolve`,
   `lower_case_table_names=2` so that names keep their case as on the container's Linux; started as a Windows
   service, since what a step starts is stopped when the step ends). Both are seeded with `tests/fixtures/`
   in order, the 1M-row tables included (about 15 s and 1 min), plus `kerno_test_k67` for K67's runner tests.
   `scripts/test-env.sh generate` makes the certificates and keys alone. The EDB install has every extension the
   fixture and the tests use (`pgcrypto`, `pg_trgm`, `hstore`, `ltree`, `postgres_fdw`): none skips for one.
   `testing::KERNO_REQUIRE_SERVERS` (a comma-separated list of container names) fails a test whose server
   is down, for the named ones only: the workflow names `kerno-postgres,kerno-mysql`. Test:
   `testing::tests::a_list_of_required_servers_names_whole_container_names`.
2. **The first run** (45450298678): the app crate 905 passed, 43 failed; the driver crates 10 more failed;
   `kerno-core` green. 13 of them are the update's tests (`update::tests` 2, `ui_tests::updates` 11): K70's
   files, where Windows has no release file yet; the probe skips those 13 by name, `ci.yml` does not. The 40
   others, by kind:

   | Kind | Tests | What was wrong | Fix |
   |---|---|---|---|
   | Windows app | `connection_feedback::a_refused_connection_says_so_in_plain_words`, `sidebar_tree::a_failed_connection_shows_its_error_and_try_again_under_it`, `visual4::a_failed_connection_shows_its_whole_message_in_details` | Windows words a refused connection "actively refused (os error 10061)", a missing host "No such host is known (os error 11001)": no plain words, no next step | `error.rs` knows Windows' codes: refused 10061, not found 11001, 11002, 11004, timed out 10060, unreachable 10051, 10065 [`each_failure_is_told_in_plain_words` with Windows' texts; 10061 removed: fails] |
   | Windows app | `kerno-mysql` `tools::a_genuine_dump_passes_the_check_and_restores_both_ways` | `mysql.exe --version` starts with its own path, `C:\Program Files\MySQL\MySQL Server 8.4\bin\mysql.exe  Ver 8.4.13`: the version was read as 8.4.0 from the folder name, so the client's own switch for client commands was not used | `parse_version` takes the word after `Ver` [`versions_are_read_from_each_tool` with that line; the `Ver` rule removed: fails] |
   | Windows app | `backup_simple::a_faster_backup_is_a_folder_the_simple_restore_reads_back` | pg_dump given `\\?\C:\…\k48` for a folder writes `\\?\C:\…\k48/toc.dat`, which Windows refuses (that form takes no `/`) | `tools::plain_path` drops `\\?\` before a drive letter in `--file=` (pg_dump, pg_dumpall), pg_restore's input and `pg_restore --list` (the same test failed next on reading the folder back) [`a_verbatim_drive_path_reaches_the_tools_plain`; on the runner the backup test] |
   | Windows app | `tool_wizards::a_global_backup_writes_the_roles`, `tool_wizards::a_restore_asks_with_its_command_and_restores` | the dialogs said "pg_dumpall.exe finished", "pg_restore.exe failed" | `backup_dialog::program_name` drops the `.exe` |
   | Everywhere | `kerno-postgres` `execute::{a_running_statement_can_be_cancelled, a_cancel_stops_the_statement_waiting_for_its_turn_too, transaction_state_follows_the_server}`, `connect::tls_modes_without_verification` | a PostgreSQL server on Windows ends the cancel connection with a reset (our FIN unread): the statement was cancelled, Kerno said "could not send the cancel request" | `UntilClosed::poll_shutdown` takes a reset on its read after the request as the close, as libpq ignores that read's errors; an error before or while writing the request stays one [`connect::tests::a_reset_after_the_cancel_request_is_the_server_closing`; the reset not accepted: fails; the four runner tests prove the statement stopped] |
   | Test assumed Unix | `kerno-{postgres,mysql}` `transfer::the_million_row_table_exports_line_by_line`, `…exports_to_a_workbook` | `ps -o rss=` | `testing::resident_kb`: `tasklist`'s working set on Windows |
   | Test assumed glibc | `kerno-postgres` `data_compare::the_script_makes_the_target_like_the_source`, `dependents::a_cascade_lists_what_the_server_drops` | the collation `"en_US"` exists on glibc only; `kerno_test_k14.v1` sorts after `kerno_test_k14b.v2` only where the dot is ignored | `"en-US-x-icu"`, in every PostgreSQL built with ICU; the second schema is `kerno_test_k14z`, after the first in every collation |
   | Test assumed the container | `tools_menu::foreign_data_wrappers_create_a_server_a_mapping_and_import_a_schema` | the loopback server was given port 5432, the container's inside port | the port the server says (`SHOW port`) |
   | Test assumed Linux | `the_editor_adds_a_cursor_above_and_below` | the kit's keys on Windows are Ctrl+Alt+Up / Down (Shift+Alt on Linux) | the Windows keys in the test |
   | Test assumed Unix | `open_url::urls_handed_over_through_the_socket_open_in_the_same_window`, `critique::start_fresh_is_not_offered_while_another_kerno_has_the_data` | on Windows a line that is not a URL is refused before it is sent and a URL crosses without its password; a pipe proves itself only once served (D-131) | each system's behaviour asserted; the other Kerno's pipe is served, as a running Kerno serves it |
   | Test assumed Unix | the 17 tests whose stand-in client tools are `/bin/sh` scripts (list below) | Windows runs a client tool only as an `.exe` | `tool_wizards::fake_tools_run()`: on Windows they print why and stop |
   | Race | `critique::a_local_target_of_a_multi_run_asks_before_a_delete_without_where` | the question was drawn before its target's line said "Waiting for your confirmation" | K71's fix (`query/tab.rs`, the same night): unchanged here |

   Also: `kerno-postgres`'s tool tests looked for `pg_dump` and `psql` on `PATH` only and skipped (EDB's
   installer adds nothing to `PATH`): they now look in the installs Kerno detects too and run.
   `a_key_puts_the_keyboard_in_the_tree_filter` failed once (the first run) and passed in every later run: its
   message now lists the tree; not reproduced, not otherwise addressed.
3. **Found by reading, not by a test.** Settings ▸ Passwords named the Secret Service on Windows, where
   `keyring` uses Windows Credential Manager: the name and the description now say so
   (`secrets::SYSTEM_STORE_NAME`, `settings_view.rs`; `ui_tests::secret_store::the_passwords_setting_shows_
   changes_and_is_stored` checks each system's sentence). The guide says Windows shares the Linux keys.
4. **What skips on Windows, and why** (the final run's `SKIPPED` lines):
   - MariaDB (169 lines), the SSH bastion and jump host (27), the legacy MySQL 5.1-5.7 servers (15 each): no
     such server on the runner, as when a container is down.
   - The 17 tests built on `/bin/sh` stand-in tools: `backup_simple::{the_simple_views_keep_every_guard,
     a_faster_backup_removes_only_the_folder_it_made}`, `restore_extensions::{what_the_server_lacks_is_said_and_
     left_out_before_the_run, a_failed_extension_and_owner_are_said_and_one_retry_leaves_out_both}`,
     `restore_roles::{a_missing_owner_is_said_and_the_restore_runs_again_without_owners, a_plain_script_says_
     what_to_do_without_a_retry}`, `script_progress::a_script_shows_how_much_was_sent_and_cancels_at_once`,
     `tasks::{a_backup_task_is_saved_listed_run_again_edited_duplicated_and_deleted, a_task_keeps_no_password_
     and_asks_the_override_s_at_each_run}`, `tool_wizards::{a_backup_tool_is_found_or_chosen_and_its_output_
     streams, a_restore_asks_twice_on_production_and_is_refused_on_read_only, a_running_backup_is_cancelled_
     from_the_progress_page, an_older_client_warns_before_running, a_connection_overrides_the_local_client,
     settings_lists_local_clients_and_sets_the_default}`, `visual4::{the_local_client_panel_says_what_the_
     server_asks_and_disables_older_clients, restore_and_script_find_their_client_before_the_file_and_take_a_
     typed_path}`. Windows' real tools are run by K67's runner tests and by the tests that use the installed
     client (backup, restore, global backup, Execute script, the faster folder backup).
   - `no pg_dump older than the server (17)`: one PostgreSQL client on the image.
   - Compiled out on Windows since K64: the fake `aws` (`password_sources`, `tool_passwords`), modes, links,
     the OpenSSH tunnel. Ignored on every system: the three measurements. K67's six runner tests skip in the
     suite and run in their own step, after the profile's files are written.
5. **Pictures of the app at work.** `scripts/windows-app-pictures.ps1` starts the release `kerno.exe` with
   `postgres://kerno:…@127.0.0.1:55432/kerno` on a scratch data folder and the memory store, maximises it and
   sends keys as a person would (SendKeys): `docs/screenshots/final/m12-windows-connection.png` (the
   connection from the URL, `kerno` default with `analytics`, `public`, `shop`, the other databases with their
   sizes), `m12-windows-query.png` (Ctrl+T, a `select` typed, Ctrl+Enter: the grid with 500 rows, types in the
   headers), `m12-windows-table.png` (Ctrl+P `shop.products`: the data tab, 200 rows, the foreign key's mark),
   `m12-windows-connection-dialog.png` (Ctrl+N: the engines' logos, the `%APPDATA%\postgresql\pgpass.conf`
   hint), `m12-windows-settings.png` (Ctrl+,). Inter and JetBrains Mono drawn, icons and logos present, nothing
   cut but what a 1024 × 768 screen cuts (a long connection name in the tree, the last grid column). The title
   bar is Windows' (minimise, maximise, close); there is no menu bar, as on Linux: GPUI draws none outside
   macOS, the toolbar and the palette (Ctrl+K) hold every command.

**Not verified.** No person used the Windows app: the pictures are of keys sent by a script. MariaDB, the SSH
tunnel and the legacy servers are not on the runner. The 17 tests of stand-in tools do not run on Windows.

### K70 — Windows, step 3c: an installer for this user, and the update installed from the app (M)

The owner's pick "A4" for the next release (2026-11-24): on Windows an update only opened the release page, and
the zip was unpacked by hand. CLAUDE.md §3 item 12: updates "as in the owner's other apps Recto and Vigie".
D-139.

1. `packaging/windows/kerno.iss` (Inno Setup 6, as Recto and Vigie): per user (`PrivilegesRequired=lowest`),
   into `%LOCALAPPDATA%\Programs\Kerno`, Start menu entry, optional desktop shortcut (off), uninstall entry
   with version and publisher, both licences on one page, "Launch Kerno"; `CloseApplications=yes`,
   `RestartApplications=no`, `kerno.exe` replaced first. For an update (`/relaunch=1`): waits for the asking
   Kerno (`/pid`, `OpenProcess` / `WaitForSingleObject` imported by the script), stops when another Kerno holds
   `{app}\kerno.exe`, starts the new Kerno, or the asking one (`/kerno`) when it did not install. The
   uninstaller keeps `%APPDATA%\Kerno` and says so.
2. `scripts/bundle-windows.sh`: after the zip, `LICENSE.txt` (both texts) and `ISCC` make
   `dist/Kerno-<version>-windows-x86_64-setup.exe`; an executable built with `test-update-without-click` is
   refused; the install check (silent install, files, shortcut target, uninstall entry, a launch from the
   installed folder with a picture, silent uninstall, data folder kept); it refuses to run where Kerno is
   installed or has data for the user.
3. `kerno_core::update::artifact(version, "windows", arch)` names the installer.
4. `crates/kerno/src/update.rs`: `Install::Setup(exe)` for a `kerno.exe` with `LICENSE-MIT` beside it (the
   zip's folder or the installed one); `install_setup` writes `update-pending.txt` in the data folder and
   starts the installer with fixed arguments; `after_install` reads the note at each start (any system),
   removes it and the download (only a `kerno-update-*` folder of the temporary folder), and says when this
   Kerno is older than the version it named; `standard_user` decides from `whoami /groups /fo csv /nh` by the
   integrity SID (Medium alone runs the installer); K67's rule for `curl.exe` made one helper,
   `system32(root, name)`, for `whoami.exe` too (both through `System::output`, no console window);
   `downloads_dir` puts the download in `%LOCALAPPDATA%\Kerno\data\updates` on Windows (the temporary
   folder elsewhere); `open_checked` checks the SHA-256 through a handle opened with the share mode "read"
   only, kept by `install_setup` until the installer's process exists; `CURRENT` is the version, 0.0.0 in
   the test build. `kerno-core` `AppPaths::local_data_dir`. `workspace/updates.rs`: the download is kept while the installer runs; `not_installed` says a
   failure (also from `main.rs` at start); the test build takes a found update without the dialog.
5. `crates/kerno/Cargo.toml`: the feature `test-update-without-click` (none by default).
6. Tests: core `artifacts_are_named_as_the_release_script_names_them`; app
   `on_windows_the_zip_and_the_installed_folder_update_through_the_installer`,
   `only_a_process_at_medium_integrity_runs_the_installer` (the runner's captured `whoami` output in
   `tests/fixtures/windows/whoami-groups-runner.csv`, the Medium, Low, System and French rows built from the
   documented form), K67's `windows_curl_is_always_system32_s_by_an_absolute_path` (with `whoami.exe`),
   `the_installer_is_started_after_a_note_and_the_note_says_what_happened`,
   `the_installer_is_held_shut_from_its_check_to_its_start`; UI
   `on_windows_update_starts_the_checked_installer_and_the_next_start_says_if_it_failed`. Each seen failing:
   the integrity read from the label text instead of the SID (the French Medium row); `levels.next().is_none()`
   dropped (two rows); any `SystemRoot` accepted (a relative one); no `LICENSE-MIT` check (a bare build);
   the note without its folder line; the installer's handle dropped before the start (on Windows: a write
   and a delete from inside the start succeed); the download's folder no longer required to be in the temporary folder
   (a folder elsewhere kept); `after_install` saying a failure for a newer Kerno; the download removed after
   the installer started (the UI twin); the core's Windows arm removed.
7. The update tests on Windows (12 failed there before, K68's run 45450298678): `curl.exe` was not recognised
   by the tests (`program.ends_with("curl")`): `update::tests::is_curl` (the file stem); the release's shape
   had no Windows artifact; the macOS bundle swap test (an `Install::App` exists only on macOS) gated to
   macOS. No assertion changed.
8. On GitHub's runner (`.github/workflows/windows-installer-probe.yml` on the branch `windows-installer`, not
   merged): clippy of the workspace and of the test build; the install check; then, in a second job from those
   packages, `scripts/windows-update-check.sh` against a fake `github.com` on that runner (hosts file, a
   certificate trusted for the step, `scripts/fake-release.py`): the shipped build as administrator keeps to
   the release page; a wrong `SHA256SUMS` line refuses; another Kerno running stops the installer and the old
   Kerno says why; 0.0.0 installed is updated to this version by the shipped installer.

Done: 2026-11-24 (rebased on K67 and K73). Proved on GitHub's `windows-2025` runner by the probe of the branch
`windows-installer`: clippy clean on Windows (the workspace, and the kerno crate with
`test-update-without-click`); the update tests on Windows: `kerno-core` 24 passed, `kerno` (filter `update`) 34
passed, `the_installer_is_held_shut_from_its_check_to_its_start` among them (a write and a delete refused while
held; seen failing in run 45463424968 with the handle dropped before the start); the install check ("installed
in …\Programs\Kerno, Start menu shortcut to kerno.exe, uninstall entry 2026.11.3", "uninstalled …: folder,
shortcut and entry gone; data kept: instance.pipe kerno.db …", a picture of the installed Kerno); the update
check's four cases ("1: the shipped Kerno, as administrator, checked and keeps to the release page", "2: a
wrong SHA256SUMS line: downloaded, refused, nothing kept, run or installed", "3: another Kerno running: the
installer replaced nothing, the old Kerno started again and said why", "4: 0.0.0 updated to 2026.11.3 by the
shipped installer: uninstall entry 2026.11.3, kerno.exe the shipped one, the new Kerno started, the note and
the download removed"); the installer's and the uninstaller's pages pictured, clicked through with the keyboard
(licence, tasks, ready, finished, the uninstall question and its last message). Locally (macOS): fmt, clippy,
`cargo deny`, `kerno-core` and `kerno` suites. Not proved: a standard user's update on a real Windows (the
runner gives an administrator only; the test build leaves the elevation check out, takes the update without the
click and says it is 0.0.0); a person clicking the installer; what Restart Manager does to a running Kerno when
installing over it by hand; SmartScreen's words.

The independent review of 9b308b6 and 29c3624 (2026-11-24, evening), its ten findings and what each became
(D-139 has the reasons):

| Finding | Change | Proof |
